# Jobs to be done, third list: tactical and executable

Built by `app/jtbd/build_v3.py` from `app/jtbd/v3/*.json`. Brief: `BRIEF-v3.md`. Seed: `NOTES-KEVIN-29SEP.md`.
Total = 4 x money + 2 x satisfaction + 2 x speed + 2 x ease, out of 50. Start now = inputs ready (4 or 5 of 5) and first output in hours or days.

## Start now: top 30 (from 332 start-now jobs, at most 3 per screen)

1. **Mark every live ad scale, watch or cut each Monday, name the part that made it win or lose (hook rate, hold rate, click rate or page), and move ads under 3 times target spend to 'not read yet'** (09-creative, 48, days). Enablers: creative-archive, creative-tags.
2. **Rank every ad set each Monday by dollars spent above target, and mark each cut, hold or scale with a dollar amount, judging only ad sets that spent at least 3 times target cost per purchase** (11-paid-social, 48, days). Enablers: spend-rules, margin-view.
3. **Check checkout settings against a short list (guest checkout on, 7 fields or fewer, express wallets first, address autocomplete on, shipping cost shown in the cart) and change the ones that fail** (06-store-health, 46, hours). Enablers: change-log.
4. **When conversion drops more than 20 percent in a day, check in order: tracking, traffic mix, device and browser, then the page, before anyone touches the design** (07-landing-pages-cro, 46, hours). Enablers: behaviour-map.
5. **Compare Meta-reported purchases and value with store orders from paid social each week, per campaign, and show the gap and the store-side cost per new customer** (11-paid-social, 46, days). Enablers: margin-view, funnel-mix.
6. **Check checkout completion by payment method each week (card, Apple Pay, Google Pay, PayPal, Shop Pay, pay later) and place one real order on any method that falls 10 points behind cards** (06-store-health, 46, hours). Enablers: checkout-probe.
7. **The morning after any change, compare add to cart, checkout start, discount use and orders with the 14 day average for that weekday, and name the change if a step fell** (06-store-health, 46, days). Enablers: change-log.
8. **Put 5 new openings on the body of a worn out winner within a week, each changing one lever (angle, offer, avatar or format) across all 3 hook layers: sound, on-screen text and the first picture** (09-creative, 46, days). Enablers: variant-stack, script-builder.
9. **Tell the buyer for each new ad after 2 days whether to kill it or wait, by showing how far it is from the read spend and whether its hook and click rate are already below the kill line** (11-paid-social, 46, hours). Enablers: spend-rules.
10. **Match tracked purchases to store orders every morning (Meta, Google Ads and GA4 purchases, each divided by store orders) against their 28 day range, and alert when one falls out** (22-measurement, 44, hours). Enablers: tracking-check.
11. **Cost every proposed discount before it runs: gross profit per order at that depth, extra orders needed to break even, and a yes or no against the margin floor** (04-offers-pricing, 44, hours). Enablers: offer-math, margin-view.
12. **Write discount rules the founder signs: deepest discount per product, a never discount list, how often, who may make codes, and check every live code against them each week** (04-offers-pricing, 44, hours). Enablers: code-watch.
13. **Write the Friday copy batch: 10 headline candidates for each chosen avatar and pain pair, cut to 3 truly different angles, each with a written reason and a pass line** (10-copy, 44, hours). Enablers: copy-decomposer, principle-matcher.
14. **Compute days of stock left for every product and size against supplier lead time plus a 2 week buffer, and list what must be ordered this week, ranked by gross profit at risk** (19-supply-inventory, 44, days). Enablers: stock-cover.
15. **Set the ad spend line from real margin: compute the break-even and target ratio of total sales to total ad spend, check it weekly, and answer the agency's next budget ask with it** (21-finance-cash, 44, hours). Enablers: margin-view, funnel-mix.
16. **Turn on 2-step login with an app or a key for every admin on the 8 critical accounts, and replace every shared admin login with a named login at the lowest role each person needs** (24-security, 44, hours). Enablers: access-vault.
17. **Every morning compare live Meta ads, admins and daily spend against yesterday and the work board, and alert on any ad, spend jump or admin not made by a known person** (24-security, 44, days). Enablers: access-vault, work-board.
18. **Write the weekly profit line: sales minus product cost, shipping, payment fees, discounts, returns and ad spend, with any cost marked unavailable when its source is missing** (26-reports, 44, hours). Enablers: margin-view, number-book.
19. **Turn each approved sale or bundle into one offer line (what, how much, until when) and check it against the live code, the price and the end date in the store before it ships** (10-copy, 42, hours). Enablers: claim-guard.
20. **When revenue drops, split the change into visits, buy rate and order size by channel and device, check stock and late returns, and name the part that moved in 1 line** (22-measurement, 42, hours). Enablers: metric-contract.
21. **Rank the top 5 money leaks each morning, from ad view to repeat order, with dollars at stake against each step's own 4 week median** (00-today, 42, days). Enablers: leak-ranker, margin-view.
22. **Pick the 3 things that matter most this week every Monday from the leak list and the board, each with 1 owner and the number it should move** (00-today, 42, hours). Enablers: work-board, leak-ranker.
23. **Show the funnel mix every week: new vs returning revenue, prospecting vs retargeting spend on Meta, generic vs brand search on Google, with a flag when rebuying old customers passes 40 percent of spend** (00-today, 42, days). Enablers: funnel-mix.
24. **Walk the founder through the drafted record shelf by shelf and get each section marked checked, fixed or not true, with a name and a date** (05-brand-brain, 42, hours). Enablers: brain-graph, review-log.
25. **When an offer ends or a policy changes, find every live ad, email flow and page still saying the old thing, and hand each owner a fix list** (05-brand-brain, 42, hours). Enablers: fact-watch, brain-graph.
26. **Rewrite the pack picker so the bigger pack shows its saving in dollars and per unit, preselect the pack most buyers should take, and test it against today's picker** (07-landing-pages-cro, 42, days). Enablers: test-reader.
27. **Make the product image, price and buy button the first things to paint on mobile product pages: preload the main image at phone size, stop lazy loading it, and show text while fonts load** (08-site-speed, 42, hours). Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
28. **Duplicate the live theme, apply one speed change to the copy, run the same speed tests on both themes 3 times, walk the buying flow on the copy, and merge only if the copy wins on both** (08-site-speed, 42, hours). Enablers: theme-twin, speed-lab.
29. **After every speed change, check the Meta pixel, Conversions API and GA4 still record add to cart and purchase, with a test order and 3 days of counts against store orders** (08-site-speed, 42, hours). Enablers: event-check.
30. **Refresh a tiring winner's words without losing its appeal: keep the promise and the proof, and change only the first line, the headline and the form (list, story or question)** (10-copy, 42, hours). Enablers: variant-stack, copy-decomposer.

## 00-today Today

### T1. When I sit down in the morning with 20 minutes before the team starts asking me things, help me see the one place the business is losing the most money right now, in dollars, and the fixes ranked by what each is worth, so I spend today on that and not on whatever is loudest

Metric: dollars at stake at the weakest step from visit to repeat order, 7 day, against its own 4 week median

- **00-01 Rank the top 5 money leaks each morning, from ad view to repeat order, with dollars at stake against each step's own 4 week median** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every weekday at 7am, after the overnight data pull. Enablers: leak-ranker, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders, customers and refunds by day (connected, Shopify); sessions, add to cart and checkout by day and device (connected, GA4); spend, clicks and purchases by day (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); margin per order (enabler, margin-view).
  - Steps: 1) Pull yesterday and the last 28 days for each step of the chain 2) Compute each step's rate for the last 7 days and its 4 week median 3) Turn each drop into dollars: lost step rate x traffic x average margin per order 4) Drop leaks under $200 a week and leaks caused by known events 5) Rank the rest by dollars and write 1 plain line each: step, drop, dollars, start date
  - Output: a ranked list of 5 leaks, each with step, dollars a week, start date and 1 line. Next: feeds 00-02 (rank fixes) and 00-14 (Monday top 3).
  - Watch: dollars at stake at the weakest step (7 day, Shopify, GA4 and ad platforms), then gross profit recovered per week, then gross profit.
- **00-02 Attach 1 to 3 ranked fixes to each leak with a dollar value, and let the founder approve, send to a person or park each one in a click** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: each morning after 00-01. Enablers: leak-ranker, work-board.
  - Inputs: the ranked leak list (enabler, leak-ranker); fix ideas filtered by bottleneck (have, idea bank ideas/v1); approval rules and budget caps (brain, decisions and goals shelf); team and agency list with roles (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) For each leak, filter the idea bank by its bottleneck and store type 2) Score each idea in code with the brand's numbers: dollars at stake x likely share recovered 3) Keep the top 3 per leak and write what it takes: hours, cost, who 4) Show the 3-button row: let the agent do it, send to a person, not now 5) Log each decision with date and reason to the decisions log
  - Output: a ranked fix list with dollar values and a logged decision on each. Next: approved fixes become cards in 00-13 (work board).
  - Watch: fixes decided within 1 day of being shown (7 day, decisions log), then gross profit recovered per week, then gross profit.
- **00-03 Run the day one sequence for a new brand: build the brain core page first, then show 3 things the founder did not know in dollars, then pick the creatives and pages to audit first** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, on onboarding day. Enablers: brain-graph, leak-ranker.
  - Inputs: store, ads, GA4, Search Console, helpdesk and reviews accounts (connected, day one kit); founder interview answers (founder, founder interview); site crawl and product catalogue (brain, products and site shelves); top 10 ads by spend in the last 90 days (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Connect the day one kit and check each account returns data 2) Fill the core page from the crawl, the data and the interview; founder signs it 3) Run the leak ranker on 12 months and pick the 3 biggest surprises in dollars 4) List the top 10 ads and top 5 landing pages by spend and pick 3 of each to audit first 5) Write the first week plan on 1 page and put each item on the board
  - Output: a signed core page, 3 findings with dollars, and a first week audit list of 3 ads and 3 pages. Next: feeds 00-13 (work board) and the creative and page audits on 09 and 07.
  - Watch: founder signs the core page and approves the first week plan (7 day, decisions log), then time to first useful decision, then revenue.
- **00-04 Flag any key number that has slipped 2 weeks running, even by a little, with the dollars it will cost over a quarter if it keeps sliding** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: number-watch, leak-ranker.
  - Inputs: weekly conversion, order value, repeat rate, refund rate, cost per new customer (connected, Shopify and ad platforms); margin per order (enabler, margin-view); slide rule: 2 weeks worse, change over 3 percent (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute each key number weekly for the last 12 weeks 2) Mark numbers worse than the prior week 2 weeks in a row 3) Project the quarter cost if the slide holds, in dollars 4) Check the slide is not a data gap or a season, against last year's same weeks 5) Write 1 line per slide and add it to the leak list
  - Output: a short slide list: number, weeks sliding, quarter cost in dollars. Next: feeds 00-01 (leak list) and 00-14 (Monday top 3).
  - Watch: slides caught at 2 weeks, not after the month (12 week, Shopify and ad platforms), then gross profit protected per quarter, then gross profit.
### T2. When yesterday's orders are in and bills are coming, help me see whether yesterday made money after every cost and whether cash covers the next 4 weeks, so I know if I can keep spending today

Metric: yesterday's contribution after product cost, shipping, fees, returns and ads, and weeks of cash cover

- **00-05 Post yesterday's contribution by 9am: revenue minus product cost, shipping, payment fees, discounts, returns and ad spend, with a keep, hold or cut call on spend** Score 46, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every morning at 9am. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: yesterday's orders, discounts and refunds (connected, Shopify); product cost per SKU (brain, finance shelf); shipping cost per order and payment fees (setup, Shopify and carrier invoices); ad spend by platform (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); floor: lowest contribution per order (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull yesterday's orders and ad spend 2) Take off product cost, shipping, fees, discounts and a return reserve per product 3) Compare contribution per order with the floor and with the 28 day average 4) Mark any cost line that is estimated and not actual 5) Write the call: keep spend, hold, or cut, with the 1 number behind it
  - Output: a 1 line daily profit card: contribution in dollars, per order, against floor, and the spend call. Next: feeds 00-08 (month pace) and 00-07 (cash cover).
  - Watch: daily contribution after ads (1 day, Shopify, ad platforms), then contribution margin per month, then contribution margin.
- **00-07 Show weeks of cash cover for the next 13 weeks, with the first tight week and the spend or stock move that fixes it** Score 38, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: every Monday, and the day a supplier bill or payout hold lands. Enablers: cash-runway.
  - Inputs: bank balance (founder, founder or bank export); payout schedule (connected, Shopify and marketplaces); bills, payroll and supplier payment dates (setup, finance shelf or a Google Sheet); open purchase orders (brain, operations shelf); cash floor (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) Load balance, payouts due and bills due, week by week 2) Add planned ad spend and stock payments 3) Find the first week under the floor 4) Test 3 moves: cut ad spend 20 percent, delay a purchase order, ask for supplier terms 5) Write the 1 line answer: covered for N weeks, tight in week X, best move
  - Output: a 13 week cash line with the first tight week and the best move. Next: feeds 21 finance screen and 19 purchase order timing.
  - Watch: weeks of cash cover (13 week, bank and Shopify payouts), then cash on hand, then cash.
- **00-08 Show month to date pace against the revenue and gross profit goal on the 10th, 15th and 20th, with the 2 moves that close the gap** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 10th, 15th and 20th of each month. Enablers: margin-view, leak-ranker.
  - Inputs: month to date orders and gross profit (connected, Shopify); monthly revenue and gross profit goals (brain, decisions and goals shelf); same month last year by day (connected, Shopify); planned sends and launches for the rest of the month (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Project month end from month to date plus last year's shape for the remaining days 2) Compute the gap to goal in revenue and gross profit 3) List moves that fit the rest of the month: an email send, a budget shift, a bundle 4) Size each move from past results and keep the top 2 5) Put the 2 moves on the board with an owner
  - Output: a pace card: projected month end, gap, and 2 moves with owners. Next: feeds 00-13 (work board).
  - Watch: projected month end against goal (month to date, Shopify), then monthly revenue and gross profit, then revenue.
- **00-20 Send a 1 screen read the evening of a big day, such as a launch, a sale day or a big email, against plan, with tomorrow's move** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at 9pm on any day in the calendar marked big. Enablers: margin-view, number-watch.
  - Inputs: the plan for the day: orders, revenue, spend (brain, calendar shelf); orders and revenue by hour (connected, Shopify); ad spend by hour (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the day's orders, revenue, spend and top products by hour 2) Compare with plan and with the last similar day 3) Name what beat plan and what missed 4) Write tomorrow's move: extend, add budget, change offer or stop 5) Send to the founder and the owner
  - Output: an evening card: day against plan and tomorrow's move. Next: feeds 04-04 (sale review) and 26 reports.
  - Watch: revenue against plan on big days (1 day, Shopify), then revenue from big days, then revenue.
- **00-06 Show what last week's orders are really worth after returns, cancellations and card disputes, split by channel and first vs repeat order** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and after any sale week. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders, cancellations and refunds (connected, Shopify); card disputes and chargebacks (connected, Shopify Payments or Stripe); return reasons (setup, helpdesk and returns app); order channel and first or repeat flag (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pull orders from 5 to 8 weeks ago so most returns have landed 2) Take off refunds, cancellations and disputes for each order 3) Split net value by channel and by first vs repeat order 4) Flag any channel whose net value is 15 percent or more under its gross 5) Carry the net rate forward as a return reserve for recent weeks
  - Output: a table of gross vs net order value by channel, plus the return reserve per channel. Next: feeds 00-05 (daily profit uses the reserve) and 11 paid social budget calls.
  - Watch: net to gross order value by channel (28 day, Shopify), then real revenue per channel, then revenue.
### T3. When something breaks during the day, like checkout failing, a campaign spending double, a best seller running out or a pixel going quiet, help me know within the hour and whether we caused it, so I stop the loss before tomorrow's report

Metric: minutes from a break to the founder seeing it, and dollars lost per break

- **00-09 Ping the founder within the hour when checkout breaks, the site goes down or a campaign spends at double pace, with the dollars lost so far** Score 42, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every hour, all day. Enablers: number-watch.
  - Inputs: orders per hour (connected, Shopify); spend per hour by campaign (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); uptime and checkout test (setup, an uptime checker such as a free monitor); who to ping and on which channel (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compare orders this hour with the same hour on the last 4 same weekdays 2) Check spend pace per campaign against its daily budget 3) Run a test add to cart and checkout load every 30 minutes 4) When 2 signals agree, send 1 message with what broke, since when and dollars lost 5) Log the break, the fix time and the cost
  - Output: an alert within the hour, and a break log with cost per break. Next: feeds 06 store health and 00-12 (cause check).
  - Watch: minutes from break to founder alert (per event, number-watch log), then dollars lost per break, then risk.
- **00-10 Mark each morning which numbers cannot be trusted today, such as pixel purchases far from store orders or a missing feed, and say why** Score 40, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every morning before the Today view loads. Enablers: number-watch.
  - Inputs: pixel and GA4 purchases by day (connected, Meta Ads and GA4); store orders by day (connected, Shopify); last refresh time per account (brain, trust and access shelf); known tracking gaps and their start dates (brain, audits shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compare platform purchases with store orders for yesterday 2) Flag a gap over 20 percent against its usual level 3) Check each account refreshed in the last 24 hours 4) Grey out every tile that uses a flagged number and say why 5) Add the flag to the numbers-not-to-trust list with a start date
  - Output: a trust strip: numbers to trust today, numbers not to trust, and why. Next: feeds 22 measurement and every tile on Today.
  - Watch: days with an untrusted number shown as trusted (28 day, number-watch log), then decisions made on good numbers, then risk.
- **00-11 Warn when a best seller has fewer days of stock than the reorder lead time, and list the ads still sending buyers to it** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every morning. Enablers: number-watch.
  - Inputs: stock on hand per SKU (connected, Shopify); sales rate per SKU, last 14 days (connected, Shopify); reorder lead time per SKU (brain, operations shelf); ads with landing URLs and product sets (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Compute days of cover per SKU from stock and 14 day sales rate 2) Flag SKUs where cover is under lead time plus 7 days 3) List live ads and product sets that point to each flagged SKU, with daily spend 4) Suggest: reorder now, move spend to an in stock item, or cap spend 5) Send the reorder note to the person who buys stock
  - Output: a stock risk list: SKU, days of cover, lead time, ads pointing to it, daily spend. Next: feeds 19 supply and 11 paid social budget moves.
  - Watch: ad spend on SKUs with under 14 days of cover (7 day, Shopify and ad platforms), then revenue kept by not selling out, then revenue.
- **00-12 When a number jumps or drops, check outside causes first, such as a holiday, a platform outage, a rival sale or the weather, and say us or outside** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: whenever number-watch fires, or the founder asks why. Enablers: number-watch, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: the number that moved and when (enabler, number-watch); holiday and sale calendar (brain, calendar shelf); platform status pages and policy news (setup, public status pages); rival sales and new ads this week (enabler, competitor-feed); our own changes: launches, price, budget, site edits (brain, decisions log).
  - Steps: 1) List our own changes in the 3 days before the move 2) List outside events in the same window 3) Check if the move shows on all channels or one 4) Write the likely cause as us, outside or unclear, with the evidence 5) Say what to do: act, wait 3 days, or ignore
  - Output: a cause note: us, outside or unclear, with evidence and a next step. Next: feeds 00-01 (drops leaks with outside causes) and 26 reports.
  - Watch: moves with a named cause within 1 day (28 day, number-watch log), then fewer wrong calls on luck, then risk.
### T4. When people are waiting on me across Slack, email, WhatsApp and the agency's threads, help me see every decision and task in one board, decide each in a minute, and hand it off with the numbers, so no one is blocked by me

Metric: decisions waiting on the founder longer than 2 days

- **00-14 Pick the 3 things that matter most this week every Monday from the leak list and the board, each with 1 owner and the number it should move** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday at 9am. Enablers: work-board, leak-ranker.
  - Inputs: ranked leaks and fixes (enabler, leak-ranker); the board (enabler, work-board); weekly goals (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Take the top 5 leaks and the oldest 5 cards waiting 2) Pick 3 by dollars at stake and by who is free 3) Write each as: action, owner, due date, number to move 4) Post to the team channel and pin to the top of the board 5) On Friday mark each done, slipped or dropped
  - Output: a Monday note with 3 priorities, owners and target numbers. Next: feeds 26 reports (weekly note).
  - Watch: share of Monday priorities done by Friday (7 day, work board), then team focus on the top leak, then gross profit.
- **00-16 Send the founder 1 phone message each morning with every pending approval, so each can be answered yes, no or later in 1 reply** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every weekday at 8am, and when a card waits over 24 hours. Enablers: work-board.
  - Inputs: cards waiting on approval (enabler, work-board); founder's phone channel (brain, trust and access shelf); approval rules (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick cards waiting on the founder, oldest and costliest first 2) Write each in 1 line with the dollar value and the default 3) Send as a numbered list 4) Read the reply and move each card 5) Log the decision with date and reason
  - Output: a morning approval message and the decisions logged. Next: feeds 00-13 (board moves).
  - Watch: median hours a card waits on the founder (7 day, work board), then work not blocked by the founder, then customer satisfaction.
- **00-15 Send each handed-off card to the team or agency with the numbers, the reason, the result expected and the date to check it** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each time a card moves to a person. Enablers: work-board.
  - Inputs: the card and its leak or job (enabler, work-board); the numbers behind it (connected, Shopify and ad platforms); who owns what (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the 3 numbers that show the problem, with dates 2) Write why it matters in dollars 3) Write the result expected and the check date 4) Send to the owner in their channel and log it on the card 5) On the check date, show result against expected
  - Output: a hand-off note per card and a check on the due date. Next: feeds 25 experiments when the card is a test.
  - Watch: hand-offs sent back with questions (28 day, work board), then fewer wrong fixes, then customer satisfaction.
- **00-13 Set up the kanban board: pull every open task and decision from Slack, email, WhatsApp and agency threads into to do, doing, waiting on approval, live and measured** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then new cards daily. Enablers: work-board, leak-ranker.
  - Inputs: open tasks and asks from Slack and email (setup, Slack and email export or forward); team and agency list with roles (brain, operations shelf); approval rules (brain, decisions and goals shelf); approved fixes from the morning leak list (enabler, leak-ranker).
  - Steps: 1) Forward or import last 30 days of open asks and list each as a card 2) Give each card an owner, a due date and the number it should move 3) Put cards in 5 columns: to do, doing, waiting on approval, live, measured 4) Sort the approval column by age and dollars at stake 5) Close duplicates and dead asks with the founder in 1 sitting
  - Output: a live board with every open card, owner, due date and target number. Next: feeds 00-14 (Monday top 3) and 23 team ops.
  - Watch: cards waiting on approval over 2 days (7 day, work board), then work shipped per week, then customer satisfaction.
### T5. When revenue is up and the agency says the account is doing well, help me see whether new customers are growing or we are rebuying the same people, so I do not mistake a loyal base for growth

Metric: new customer revenue share and cost per new customer, 28 day

- **00-17 Show the funnel mix every week: new vs returning revenue, prospecting vs retargeting spend on Meta, generic vs brand search on Google, with a flag when rebuying old customers passes 40 percent of spend** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: campaigns and audiences with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); campaigns and search terms with spend (connected, Google Ads); new vs returning orders and revenue (connected, Shopify); campaign naming rules for prospecting and retargeting (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each campaign and ad set as prospecting, retargeting or existing customers 2) Split Google spend into brand and generic search 3) Compute share of spend per stage and new customer revenue share 4) Compare with the last 12 weeks and flag the trend 5) Write the 1 line call: add top of funnel spend, hold, or cut retargeting
  - Output: a weekly mix tile: spend by stage, new customer share, trend and the call. Next: feeds 11 prospecting budget split and 12 brand search spend.
  - Watch: share of spend on prospecting and generic search (28 day, Meta Ads and Google Ads), then new customers per week and cost per new customer, then revenue.
- **00-19 Check the agency's claim each month by comparing platform reported revenue with new customer revenue in the store for the same weeks** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and before each agency review call. Enablers: funnel-mix, margin-view.
  - Inputs: platform reported purchases and value (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); new customer orders and revenue by day (connected, Shopify); agency fee and scope (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull platform revenue and store new customer revenue for the month 2) Compute cost per new customer from store data, not platform data 3) Compare with the prior 3 months 4) Write 3 lines: what the platform says, what the store says, the gap 5) Send the lines to the agency before the call
  - Output: a 1 page agency check with 3 lines and the numbers under them. Next: feeds 26 reports and the agency review.
  - Watch: cost per new customer from store data (28 day, Shopify and ad platforms), then money paid for real new customers, then gross profit.
- **00-18 Show share of ad spend by awareness level on Today, and when unaware plus problem aware ads fall under 20 percent, send a card to creative with the gap** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every Monday after the ad pull. Enablers: awareness-map, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: last 90 days of our ads with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); awareness rubric with 5 examples per level (enabler, awareness-map); awareness tags on each ad (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) Read spend share per awareness level from the heat map 2) Compare with the last 4 weeks and the floor set by the founder 3) If top of funnel share is under the floor, write the gap in dollars of spend 4) Send a card to creative asking for 4 top of funnel ads 5) Track new customer cost for the new ads against the old mix
  - Output: a Today tile with spend by awareness level and a card to creative when the gap opens. Next: feeds 09 creative briefs for top of funnel ads.
  - Watch: share of spend on unaware and problem aware ads (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.

## 01-market-research Products and research

### T1. When sales dip for a quarter and I cannot tell if it is us or the market, help me see whether our category is growing or shrinking quarter by quarter, and where we grow slower than it line by line, so I know whether to fix us or ride it out

Metric: our growth minus category growth, per product line, quarter against the same quarter last year

- **01-01 Pull 12 quarters of search demand for 10 to 20 category terms and chart each quarter against the same quarter last year** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then in the first week of each quarter. Enablers: market-pulse.
  - Inputs: 10 to 20 category terms, not brand names (brain, market shelf); monthly search volumes for the terms (connected, Google Ads Keyword Planner); 5 year interest trend per term (setup, Google Trends); longer volume history and more terms (buy, an SEO data source such as OpenSEO).
  - Steps: 1) List category terms with the founder: what a buyer types before they know us 2) Pull monthly volumes and 5 year trend per term 3) Sum by quarter and compute change against the same quarter last year 4) Mark terms growing 3 quarters in a row and terms falling 3 in a row 5) Write 1 line: the category is growing, flat or shrinking, by how much
  - Output: a quarterly demand chart for the category, with growth against last year and the 1 line call. Next: feeds 01-02 (our growth vs category) and 01-05 (seasonality).
  - Watch: category search demand, quarter against last year (quarterly, Google Ads Keyword Planner and Trends), then the right fix: us or the market, then revenue.
- **01-02 Compare our revenue growth with the category's growth each quarter, product line by product line, and name the lines where we lose share inside our own growth** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: first week of each quarter. Enablers: market-pulse.
  - Inputs: revenue by product line by quarter, 3 years (connected, Shopify); category demand by quarter (enabler, market-pulse); marketplace revenue if we sell there (setup, Amazon Seller Central); which category terms map to which line (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Sum our revenue per line per quarter, all channels 2) Compute our growth against the same quarter last year 3) Match each line to its category terms and take the category growth 4) Subtract: our growth minus category growth, per line 5) Flag lines with a gap worse than minus 10 points for 2 quarters 6) Write 1 line per flagged line: growing but losing share, or shrinking with the market
  - Output: a table: line, our growth, category growth, gap, 2 quarter flag. Next: feeds 01-04 (line diagnosis) and 26 reports.
  - Watch: our growth minus category growth per line (quarterly, Shopify and market-pulse), then share of category demand, then revenue.
- **01-05 Map category demand month by month for each market we sell in, and set the start date for stock, creative and cash before each peak** Score 34, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once a year in January, checked each quarter. Enablers: market-pulse.
  - Inputs: monthly search volume, 3 to 5 years (connected, Google Trends and Keyword Planner); our orders by week, 3 years (connected, Shopify); countries sold in (brain, core page); supplier lead times (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Index demand by month for each market, average of the last 3 years 2) Lay our own weekly orders over it 3) Mark peaks, dead zones and where we under sold a peak 4) Count back lead time from each peak to set stock, creative and cash dates 5) Put the dates on the calendar shelf
  - Output: a 12 month demand map per market with prep dates on the calendar. Next: feeds 00 Today alerts and 19 purchase orders.
  - Watch: orders in peak weeks against last year (yearly, Shopify), then revenue in peak months, then revenue.
- **01-03 Split our search clicks into brand name searches and category searches each quarter, to see if the brand grows faster than the category** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: first week of each quarter. Enablers: search-feed, market-pulse.
  - Inputs: queries with clicks and impressions, 16 months (connected, Search Console); brand terms and misspellings (brain, core page); category demand by quarter (enabler, market-pulse).
  - Steps: 1) Pull Search Console queries by month 2) Tag each query as brand, category or other with a word list 3) Sum clicks and impressions per quarter for each group 4) Compare brand search growth with category demand growth 5) Write the call: brand pulling ahead, keeping pace or falling behind
  - Output: a quarterly brand vs category search table with the call. Next: feeds 02 brand health and 12 brand search spend.
  - Watch: brand search clicks against last year (quarterly, Search Console), then brand demand, then revenue.
- **01-04 Before cutting a product line that slid 2 quarters, check whether the market, the product or our ads are to blame** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: when a line falls 2 quarters in a row. Enablers: market-pulse, review-miner.
  - Inputs: line revenue, conversion and repeat rate by quarter (connected, Shopify and GA4); category demand for the line (enabler, market-pulse); review rating and themes by quarter (connected, reviews app); ad spend, reach and cost per purchase for the line (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Check category demand for the line: down, flat or up 2) Check the product: rating trend, new complaint themes, return rate 3) Check our push: spend, reach and cost per purchase against last year 4) Name the likely cause with the 2 numbers that show it 5) Propose: fix product, fix ads, reprice, or wind down, with cash freed
  - Output: a 1 page line diagnosis with a cause and a proposal. Next: feeds 19 supply (stock plan) and 09 creative if ads are to blame.
  - Watch: line revenue against category trend (quarterly, Shopify), then revenue kept from good products, then gross profit.
### T2. When a supplier quote, freight rate or tariff moves and margin keeps slipping, help me see which input costs are rising on my top products and who can squeeze my margin, in plain words, so I act before margin shrinks

Metric: landed cost per unit for the top 10 SKUs against last quarter

- **01-07 When a tariff or duty change is announced, compute the cost per unit for each product it hits and the 3 options: reprice, reroute or order early** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: the day a tariff or trade rule is announced. Enablers: margin-view, market-pulse.
  - Inputs: country of origin and customs code per SKU (brain, operations shelf); current and new duty rates (setup, public tariff tables); landed cost per SKU (enabler, market-pulse); stock on hand and open orders (connected, Shopify and operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List SKUs by country of origin and customs code 2) Apply the new rate and compute extra cost per unit and per month 3) Check stock on hand and open orders: how many units land before the date 4) Cost 3 options: raise price, move supplier, place an order early 5) Write the pick with the cash it needs
  - Output: a tariff hit table per SKU and a costed choice of 3 options. Next: feeds 04-15 (price response) and 19 supply.
  - Watch: gross margin on hit SKUs (90 day, Shopify and invoices), then gross margin per unit, then gross profit.
- **01-06 Build an input cost tracker for the top 10 SKUs: factory price, freight, duty, packaging and fulfilment per unit, each quarter, and flag any rise over 5 percent** Score 38, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: first week of each quarter, and when a quote or freight invoice lands. Enablers: market-pulse, margin-view.
  - Inputs: unit cost per SKU (connected, Shopify cost field); supplier quotes and invoices (founder, founder or finance); freight and 3PL invoices (setup, a Google Sheet kept by ops); public freight and raw material indices (setup, free public indices).
  - Steps: 1) List the top 10 SKUs by gross profit 2) Split landed cost per unit into 5 parts from the latest invoices 3) Compare each part with last quarter and last year 4) Flag parts up over 5 percent and the gross profit at stake per month 5) Check public indices to say if more rises are coming
  - Output: a landed cost table for the top 10 SKUs with flags and dollars at stake. Next: feeds 04-15 (raise price, change pack or absorb) and 21 finance.
  - Watch: landed cost per unit, top 10 SKUs (quarterly, invoices and Shopify), then gross margin per unit, then gross profit.
- **01-09 Check whether the cost to reach a buyer is rising for us or for the whole category, by comparing our CPM and cost per click with the category benchmark each month** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and when cost per purchase rises 2 months running. Enablers: market-pulse.
  - Inputs: our CPM, cost per click and cost per purchase by month (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); category benchmark for the same months (connected, Meta industry benchmark and Google auction insights); our category label for benchmarks (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull our monthly CPM, cost per click and cost per purchase for 12 months 2) Pull the category benchmark for the same months 3) Index both to 100 at the start and plot 4) If ours rose faster, point to our creative and page; if both rose, it is the category 5) Write the 1 line call and the next job
  - Output: a 12 month chart of our cost against the category's, with the call. Next: feeds 09 creative (if it is us) or 21 finance (if it is the category).
  - Watch: our CPM against category CPM (monthly, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then contribution margin.
- **01-08 Write the five forces for our category in plain words: who can squeeze our margin (suppliers, marketplaces and ad platforms, rivals, cheaper substitutes, new sellers), each with evidence and a 1 to 5 pressure score** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: market-pulse, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: supplier count and terms (brain, operations shelf); marketplace fees and ad cost trends (connected, Amazon and Meta Ads); rival list and their prices (brain, competitors shelf); substitutes buyers use instead (brain, competitors shelf); new sellers in the category this quarter (enabler, competitor-feed).
  - Steps: 1) Write 1 plain line per force: who, how they squeeze us, the evidence 2) Score each force 1 to 5 and compare with last quarter 3) Name the force that rose the most 4) Write 1 push back per force: dual source, raise price, own the channel, own a claim 5) Founder checks the scores and signs
  - Output: a 1 page forces sheet: 5 lines, scores, the rising force and the push back. Next: feeds 04-15 (pricing) and 03 competitors.
  - Watch: pressure score of the top force (quarterly, forces sheet), then margin protected, then gross profit.
### T3. When sales on our own site flatten, help me see where buyers in my category are moving and which nearby categories they also buy are growing, so we follow the buyer instead of fighting it

Metric: share of category demand by channel (brand sites, Amazon, TikTok Shop, search, AI answers), quarterly

- **01-12 Find uses buyers found for our product that we never planned, count them in reviews and chats, and size each use as its own search market** Score 30, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: twice a year. Enablers: review-miner, market-pulse.
  - Inputs: all our reviews and chats (connected, reviews app and helpdesk); search volume for each use (connected, Keyword Planner); current product uses we sell for (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Search reviews and chats for use words: for my, I use it to, works for 2) Group uses and count mentions 3) Drop uses we already sell for 4) Pull search volume for each new use 5) Pick 1 use to test with 2 ads and a page block
  - Output: a list of unplanned uses with mentions and search volume, and 1 test. Next: feeds 09 creative (new angle) and 02-17 (unserved group).
  - Watch: sales from the new use angle (60 day, Meta Ads and Shopify), then new buyers from new uses, then revenue.
- **01-13 Catch search terms and needs in the category that grew 3 months in a row, from Google, Search Console, site search and Amazon** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: first Monday of each month. Enablers: search-feed, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: search terms with spend (connected, Google Ads); queries by month (connected, Search Console); site search terms (connected, GA4 or store search); Amazon search terms report (setup, Amazon Brand Analytics).
  - Steps: 1) Pull terms from each source for the last 6 months 2) Keep terms up 3 months in a row and over a minimum volume 3) Group them by need 4) Check if we have a product, page or ad for each need 5) Send the top 3 needs to product, SEO and creative
  - Output: a monthly list of rising needs with our coverage for each. Next: feeds 17 SEO briefs, 09 creative and 01-14 (kill test).
  - Watch: clicks on pages built for rising terms (90 day, Search Console), then early demand captured, then revenue.
- **01-10 Track where buyers in the category are moving each quarter: brand sites, Amazon, TikTok Shop, search and AI answers, with a share for each** Score 28, ready 2/5, weeks.
  - When: first week of each quarter. Enablers: market-pulse, search-feed.
  - Inputs: Search Console and Google category volume (connected, Search Console and Keyword Planner); Amazon category search volume (setup, Amazon Brand Analytics); TikTok Shop category sales estimates (buy, a TikTok Shop data tool); what AI assistants say for 20 buyer questions (enabler, search-feed); our own orders by channel (connected, Shopify and marketplaces).
  - Steps: 1) Pull category demand for each channel for the last 8 quarters 2) Convert each to a share of the total 3) Compare with where our own orders come from 4) Name the channel gaining the most share and the one we are thin in 5) Write 1 move: list there, test ads there, or hold
  - Output: a channel share table with our mix next to the category's and 1 move. Next: feeds 20 marketplaces and 18 answer engines.
  - Watch: our share of orders from the fastest growing channel (quarterly, Shopify and marketplaces), then revenue from where buyers are going, then revenue.
- **01-11 List 5 to 10 allied categories our buyers also buy, check which are growing, and rank the top 3 to grow into** Score 26, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: twice a year, before range planning. Enablers: market-pulse, customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: what else buyers buy (setup, post-purchase survey); frequently bought together in the category (setup, Amazon listings); demand trend per allied category (connected, Google Trends and Keyword Planner); buyer jobs and moments (enabler, customer-jobs).
  - Steps: 1) Collect allied categories from the survey, marketplace pairings and reviews 2) Pull 8 quarters of demand for each 3) Score each: growth, fit with our buyer's job, fit with our supply 4) Rank and keep the top 3 5) Write 1 test per category: a bundle, a partner or a small run
  - Output: a ranked list of 3 allied categories with a first test each. Next: feeds 01-14 (kill test on each) and 04-11 (bundles).
  - Watch: revenue from allied category tests (90 day, Shopify), then new revenue lines, then revenue.
### T4. When I am about to put $20k or more into a new product, or an idea excites the team, help me check demand from more than one source and margin after every real cost in an hour, and force a go or no-go, so I do not sink cash into a product that will not sell

Metric: share of launches that hit their 90 day plan

- **01-14 Run a 1 hour kill test on any new product idea: demand from 2 sources, margin after every cost, room for ad cost, seller count and rules, then call go, no-go or drop** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: whenever an idea is raised, before any samples. Enablers: launch-scorecard, margin-view.
  - Inputs: search volume and trend for the idea (connected, Keyword Planner and Google Trends); marketplace sales signals (buy, Amazon best sellers or a marketplace data tool); landed cost quote (founder, supplier); category cost per purchase (connected, Meta Ads and benchmark); idea checks for product research (have, idea bank ideas/v1 01-PRD).
  - Steps: 1) Check demand in 2 sources: search trend and marketplace sales 2) Compute margin: price minus landed cost, fees, shipping, returns 3) Compute ad room: margin minus the category's cost per purchase 4) Count sellers and check rules and certifications 5) Score and call go, no-go or drop; log the date and reason
  - Output: a 1 page scorecard with a go, no-go or drop call. Next: feeds 01-16 (product meeting) and 04-17 (launch price).
  - Watch: ideas decided within 1 week (90 day, launch-scorecard log), then cash kept from bad launches, then cash.
- **01-16 Bring a ranked idea list with a go or no-go note to the monthly product meeting, and force a decision on any idea in research for over 4 weeks** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, 2 days before the product meeting. Enablers: launch-scorecard, work-board.
  - Inputs: scorecards for all open ideas (enabler, launch-scorecard); the board with research cards (enabler, work-board); 12 month goals and range limits (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every idea with its score and age 2) Rank by score and fit with goals 3) Mark ideas over 4 weeks old as decide today 4) Write 1 line per idea: go, no-go, drop, and the reason 5) Log decisions and close dropped cards
  - Output: a ranked list of ideas with decisions logged. Next: feeds 19 supply (samples and orders) and 00 Today board.
  - Watch: ideas older than 4 weeks without a decision (monthly, work board), then team time on ideas with a chance, then gross profit.
- **01-15 When a product in our category goes viral, check if it is 1 clip or real demand: 3 or more videos over 1 million views, a search rise and marketplace rank moving** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: within 48 hours of the team spotting it. Enablers: launch-scorecard, market-pulse.
  - Inputs: the clip and similar videos (setup, TikTok and Instagram search); search trend for the product words (setup, Google Trends); marketplace rank history (buy, a marketplace rank tool such as Keepa); our fit and supply (brain, products and operations shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Count videos on the product over 1 million views from different creators 2) Check search trend for the last 90 days 3) Check marketplace rank for the top 3 listings 4) Call it noise, a fad or real demand 5) If real, run the kill test in 01-14
  - Output: a 1 line call with the 3 checks behind it. Next: feeds 01-14 (kill test).
  - Watch: viral ideas taken to kill test that pass (90 day, launch-scorecard log), then cash kept from fads, then cash.
- **01-17 Compare each new product's first 90 days with its launch plan: units, price, margin, ad cost and repeat rate, and fix the launch math for next time** Score 28, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 90 days after each launch. Enablers: launch-scorecard, margin-view.
  - Inputs: launch plan and scorecard (enabler, launch-scorecard); units, revenue and returns for 90 days (connected, Shopify); ad spend and purchases for the product (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 90 day actuals for the product 2) Put actual next to plan for each line of the scorecard 3) Name the 2 biggest misses and why 4) Change the scorecard rule that caused the miss 5) Log the result in the history shelf
  - Output: a plan vs actual sheet and 1 changed rule. Next: feeds 25 experiments (win and loss ledger).
  - Watch: launch plan error on units and margin (90 day, Shopify), then better launch bets, then gross profit.
### T5. When I am shaping a product or a price, help me see the price bands my category buys in, the price buyers call fair and the complaints no one has fixed, so we build and price for what buyers want

Metric: share of our revenue from products priced inside the busiest band

- **01-19 Mine reviews of the top 10 category products for the price buyers call fair or too much and the complaints no seller has fixed** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: before a new product or a price change, and twice a year. Enablers: review-miner, price-bands.
  - Inputs: reviews for the top 10 category products (setup, Amazon and rival sites); our own reviews (connected, reviews app); price bands (enabler, price-bands).
  - Steps: 1) Pull up to 500 reviews per product, 1 to 5 stars 2) Tag each for price words (worth it, overpriced) and complaint themes 3) Count themes and mark ones every seller has 4) Pull the price where worth it turns to overpriced 5) Write 3 build notes and 1 price note
  - Output: a review sheet: unfixed complaints ranked, fair price line, 3 build notes. Next: feeds 04-17 (launch price) and 19 supply (product spec).
  - Watch: rating of the product built from the notes (90 day, reviews app), then products buyers rate higher, then revenue.
- **01-20 Pick the next product for the best selling line from what buyers ask for in search, emails and reviews, checked against market demand** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: twice a year, before range planning. Enablers: customer-jobs, review-miner, launch-scorecard.
  - Inputs: site search terms with no result (connected, GA4 or store search); tickets and reviews asking for something we do not sell (connected, helpdesk and reviews app); demand for each ask (connected, Keyword Planner); supplier options (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Collect every ask for a product, size, color or pack we do not sell 2) Group and count the asks 3) Check search demand for the top 5 4) Run the top 2 through the kill test 5) Write why it differs from what we have and who it is for
  - Output: a shortlist of 2 next products with asks, demand and a kill test each. Next: feeds 01-16 (product meeting).
  - Watch: revenue from the next product in its first 90 days (90 day, Shopify), then new revenue from the best line, then revenue.
- **01-18 Track which price bands the category's best sellers sit in each quarter, and whether buyers are trading down or up** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: first week of each quarter. Enablers: price-bands.
  - Inputs: top 50 category best sellers with prices (setup, Amazon best sellers and rival sites); our prices per unit (connected, Shopify); unit rule for the category, like per pair (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull prices and pack sizes for the top 50 sellers 2) Convert to price per unit and set 4 to 5 bands 3) Count best sellers per band and compare with last quarter 4) Place each of our products on the band chart 5) Write the call: trading down, up or steady, and what it means for our range
  - Output: a band chart with our products placed and the quarter's call. Next: feeds 04-17 (launch price) and 03-15 (price index).
  - Watch: share of our revenue in the busiest band (quarterly, Shopify and price-bands), then revenue from well priced products, then revenue.

## 02-customers-avatars Brand and customers

### T1. When I brief an ad, a page or a product and cannot say who buys from us and why, help me see the real buyers and the job they hire us for, backed by orders, reviews and their own words, so money stops going to a customer we made up

Metric: share of orders that map to a named avatar with evidence

- **02-02 Add a 3 question post-purchase survey: where did you first hear of us, what were you using before, what nearly stopped you** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then read weekly. Enablers: customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: a post-purchase survey app (setup, Shopify app store, free or cheap tier); the 3 questions with answer choices (have, idea bank ideas/v1 02-AVT); channel list for the first question (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Install the survey on the thank you page 2) Write the 3 questions with 6 choices and an other box 3) Check the answer rate after 1 week; aim for 20 percent or more 4) Each Monday, count answers by channel and by first product 5) Send new old-way and doubt answers to the customer jobs map
  - Output: a live survey and a weekly count of answers by channel and product. Next: feeds 02-01 (jobs map), 02-06 (objection ladder) and 22 measurement.
  - Watch: survey answer rate (7 day, survey app), then better reads on what brings buyers, then revenue.
- **02-04 Draft up to 4 avatars with evidence: share of orders, first product, trigger to buy, doubts, words and where they spend time, each claim linked to a source, and have the founder sign them before any creative or page is chosen** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once after 02-01, then re-signed each quarter. Enablers: brain-graph, customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: the customer jobs map (enabler, customer-jobs); orders with first product and region (connected, Shopify); age and gender of buyers from ads (connected, Meta Ads breakdowns); founder view of who buys (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) Group buyers by job and first product and take the share of orders 2) Fill each avatar's 6 fields from the jobs map, quotes and ad breakdowns 3) Link each field to its source; mark guesses as guesses 4) Show the founder where data and belief disagree 5) Founder signs; file on the customers shelf and core page
  - Output: up to 4 signed avatars, each with share of orders and sourced fields. Next: feeds every brief on 09 creative, 10 copy and 07 landing pages.
  - Watch: share of briefs that name a signed avatar (30 day, work board), then fewer ads for a made up buyer, then revenue.
- **02-01 Map why customers hire the product: code 500 reviews, 200 tickets and all survey answers by the moment, the progress wanted, what they used before and what nearly stopped them, and rank the top 5 jobs by share** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: customer-jobs, review-miner.
  - Inputs: reviews with text and product (connected, reviews app); support tickets and chats (connected, helpdesk); post-purchase survey answers (setup, post-purchase survey); coding sheet: moment, progress, old way, doubt (have, idea bank ideas/v1 02-AVT).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the last 12 months of reviews, tickets and survey answers 2) Code each for the 4 parts: moment, progress wanted, old way, what nearly stopped them 3) Check 30 codes by hand and fix the coding rules 4) Group into jobs and count the share of each 5) Write each job as 1 line: when I, I want to, so I can, with 3 quotes
  - Output: a ranked list of 5 customer jobs, each with share, 3 quotes and the old way. Next: feeds 02-04 (avatars), 10 copy angles and 09 creative hooks.
  - Watch: share of new ads built on a named customer job (90 day, ads shelf), then cost per new customer on job-led ads, then revenue.
- **02-03 Book 8 customer calls in 2 weeks, split by first product and repeat vs one time buyers, with a $25 gift card, and add notes to the brain** Score 32, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once at onboarding, then 4 calls a month. Enablers: customer-jobs, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: list of recent buyers split by product and repeat (connected, Shopify); gift cards for thanks (buy, store gift cards, about $200); a 20 minute call script (have, idea bank ideas/v1 02-AVT); someone to run the calls (founder, founder or team).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 30 buyers across 4 groups and send a short invite 2) Book 8 calls of 20 minutes 3) Ask about the moment before buying, the old way and what nearly stopped them 4) Write notes the same day with quotes marked verbatim 5) File notes on the customers shelf and tag the job and avatar
  - Output: 8 call notes with verbatim quotes, filed and tagged. Next: feeds 02-01 (jobs map) and 02-04 (avatars).
  - Watch: calls done per month (30 day, customers shelf), then founder close to the buyer, then customer satisfaction.
- **02-05 Split gift orders from self purchases, and write who buys and who uses for the gift season** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once a year, 10 weeks before the main gift season. Enablers: customer-jobs, buyer-cohorts.
  - Inputs: orders with gift notes, gift wrap or a different ship to name (connected, Shopify); reviews that mention a gift (connected, reviews app); gift dates per market (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Flag gift orders from notes, wrap and ship to name 2) Compute gift share by month and by product 3) Read gift reviews for who gave and who got it 4) Write 2 lines: the giver's worry and the receiver's delight 5) Hand both to creative and email before the season
  - Output: a gift share table and a giver and user note. Next: feeds 09 creative and 14 email gift campaigns.
  - Watch: gift season revenue against last year (gift season, Shopify), then gift season revenue, then revenue.
### T2. When plenty of people visit and few buy, or returns climb, help me name the top reasons they hold back or send it back and what changed the mind of those who bought, so the page answers the doubt instead of shouting louder

Metric: conversion rate on the top 5 product pages, and not as expected return rate

- **02-06 Build the objection ladder: every doubt from reviews, tickets, chats and pre-sale questions, ranked by sales lost, with the answer, the proof and where the answer lives today** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then monthly. Enablers: question-matrix, review-miner.
  - Inputs: pre-sale tickets and chats (connected, helpdesk); reviews that mention a doubt before buying (connected, reviews app); what nearly stopped you answers (setup, post-purchase survey); product page and FAQ text (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every doubt and group into themes 2) Rank themes by count and by the order value of the people who raised them 3) Write the best answer and the proof for each 4) Mark where it is answered: product page, FAQ, ad, email, or nowhere 5) Send the top 3 blanks to the page and copy teams
  - Output: a ranked ladder of doubts with answer, proof and a coverage mark. Next: feeds 07 landing pages, 10 copy and 04-13 (offer around the doubt).
  - Watch: conversion rate on pages where a top doubt was answered (28 day, GA4), then conversion rate, then revenue.
- **02-07 Code not as expected returns into who the product is wrong for, and write a not for you if line for each product page** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: return reasons with comments (setup, returns app or helpdesk); 1 and 2 star reviews (connected, reviews app); return rate per SKU (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pull returns and low reviews for the top 10 SKUs 2) Code each by the mismatch: size, feel, use, expectation 3) Name the buyer each mismatch fits 4) Write a not for you if line per product 5) Put it on the page and track return rate for 60 days
  - Output: a mismatch table per SKU and a not for you if line per page. Next: feeds 07 product pages and 15 CX macros.
  - Watch: not as expected return rate (60 day, Shopify and returns app), then returns cost, then gross profit.
- **02-08 Split every too expensive comment into cannot afford vs do not see the value, and pick the fix: proof and value for the second, a smaller pack or pay in parts for the first** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: quarterly, and when price complaints rise. Enablers: review-miner, customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: reviews, chats and survey answers with price words (connected, reviews app, helpdesk, survey); pack sizes and price per unit (brain, products shelf); rival price bands (enabler, price-bands).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every mention of price, cost, expensive, worth 2) Code each as cannot afford, no value seen, or price vs a named rival 3) Count each and check the price band 4) Pick the fix per group 5) Hand value fixes to copy and pack fixes to offers
  - Output: a price objection split with counts and 1 fix per group. Next: feeds 04-11 (bundles and packs) and 10 copy.
  - Watch: price mentions in chats per 100 sessions (28 day, helpdesk), then conversion without deeper discounts, then gross profit.
- **02-09 Ask 300 visitors who leave the top product page without buying 1 question, what stopped you today, and add the answers to the objection ladder** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, run for 2 weeks, then each quarter. Enablers: question-matrix, behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: an on-page survey tool (setup, Clarity, Hotjar or similar); top product pages by traffic (connected, GA4); objection ladder (enabler, question-matrix).
  - Steps: 1) Set an exit question on the top 2 product pages 2) Collect answers for 2 weeks 3) Code answers into the ladder themes 4) Compare what non-buyers say with what buyers said nearly stopped them 5) Update the ladder ranks
  - Output: 300 coded exit answers and an updated ladder. Next: feeds 07 landing pages.
  - Watch: answers collected per week (14 day, survey tool), then conversion rate, then revenue.
### T3. When I or the copywriter sit down to write, and when a new wave of reviews lands, help me use the exact words customers use and see what they love and hate as it changes, so the copy sounds like them and a quality slip does not hurt sales first

Metric: share of live ads and pages that use a language bank phrase, and review rating trend

- **02-13 Alert the same day when complaints on 1 topic spike in comments, tickets or reviews, with the count, the first post and a draft reply** Score 40, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every day at noon and 5pm. Enablers: review-miner, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: tickets and chats (connected, helpdesk); ad comments (connected, Meta Ads); new reviews (connected, reviews app); reply rules and tone (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Count mentions per topic in the last 6 hours 2) Compare with the same hours over the last 4 weeks 3) Flag a topic at 3 times its usual count 4) Draft a reply and a fix owner 5) Send to the founder and support lead
  - Output: a same day spike alert with a draft reply. Next: feeds 15 CX and 00 Today.
  - Watch: hours from spike to first reply (per event, helpdesk), then trust kept on a bad day, then risk.
- **02-10 Build the language bank: the top 50 phrases customers use for the problem, the product and the result, with counts, and the words we use that they never do** Score 38, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then monthly. Enablers: review-miner, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: reviews, tickets, call notes and survey answers (brain, customers shelf); our live ads, pages and emails (brain, ads, site and email shelves); phrase rules: verbatim only, forum quotes never as testimonials (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull phrases of 2 to 6 words from the customer corpus 2) Group by problem, product and result and count each 3) Pull our own phrases from ads and pages 4) List our words they never use 5) Hand the top 20 to copy with 1 source quote each
  - Output: a language bank of 50 phrases with counts and a list of our words to drop. Next: feeds 10 copy, 09 creative hooks and 17 SEO.
  - Watch: share of new ads using a bank phrase (30 day, ads shelf), then click through and conversion on new copy, then revenue.
- **02-12 Send a weekly review pulse: themes customers love, hate and wish for, per product, with rating trend and any theme that doubled** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: new reviews with rating and product (connected, reviews app); marketplace reviews (setup, Amazon Seller Central); theme list (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull last week's reviews 2) Tag each with themes and sentiment 3) Compare theme counts with the 8 week average 4) Flag themes that doubled and ratings down 0.2 or more 5) Send quality flags to ops and praise quotes to copy
  - Output: a weekly pulse: love, hate, wish lists, rating trend and flags. Next: feeds 19 supply (quality) and 10 copy (proof).
  - Watch: average rating per top product (28 day, reviews app), then conversion and repeat rate, then customer satisfaction.
- **02-11 Split every live ad and top page into avatar and pain point, count spend and purchases for each pair, and list the pairs we never speak to** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly, after the ad pull. Enablers: copy-decomposer, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: ad copy with spend and purchases, 90 days (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); signed avatars and pains (brain, customers shelf); landing page text (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each ad and page with 1 avatar and 1 pain 2) Check 20 tags by hand and fix the rules 3) Sum spend, purchases and cost per purchase per pair 4) Mark pairs with good results and low spend, and pairs with no ads 5) Brief 3 ads for the best unspoken pair
  - Output: an avatar by pain grid with spend, results and 3 briefs. Next: feeds 10 copy briefs and 09 creative.
  - Watch: cost per purchase for ads on new pairs (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then revenue.
### T4. When I decide where to spend to win new buyers, help me see which buyer groups by age, gender, placement, first product and channel make the most gross profit over 6 months and which are getting pricier, so budget goes to buyers worth having

Metric: 6 month gross profit per new customer, by buyer group

- **02-14 Build the best segment map: pull 90 days of Meta breakdowns by age, gender, placement and region, compute cost per new customer and purchase value per cell, and name the top 3 and bottom 3 cells** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every 2 weeks. Enablers: segment-map.
  - Inputs: breakdowns by age, gender, placement and region with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); new vs returning purchases (connected, Shopify); minimum spend per cell to trust (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull breakdowns for 90 days 2) Drop cells under the minimum spend 3) Compute cost per purchase and value per purchase per cell 4) Rank cells and name top 3 and bottom 3 5) Write who the top cell is in 1 line for the writer
  - Output: a segment grid with top and bottom 3 cells and a 1 line brief. Next: feeds 10 copy and 09 creative (tailor to the top cell) and 11 placements.
  - Watch: cost per purchase in the top cell (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then revenue.
- **02-15 Show 6 month gross profit per new customer by first product, channel, first code and region, not just first order value** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: buyer-cohorts, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders and customers, 24 months (connected, Shopify); margin per order (enabler, margin-view); first touch channel per customer (connected, GA4 and survey).
  - Steps: 1) Group new customers by first order month and by each split 2) Sum gross profit per customer at 30, 90 and 180 days 3) Rank groups at 180 days 4) Mark groups with a small first order but high 180 day profit 5) Write the max cost per new customer each group can take
  - Output: a buyer group table with 180 day gross profit and a max cost per new customer. Next: feeds 11 paid social budget and 04-09 (loyal vs new offers).
  - Watch: 180 day gross profit per new customer (180 day, Shopify), then gross profit per customer, then gross profit.
- **02-16 Track cost per new customer for each segment cell each quarter, and move budget from cells getting pricier to cells still cheap** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: quarterly, and when cost per new customer rises 2 months running. Enablers: segment-map, buyer-cohorts.
  - Inputs: segment grid (enabler, segment-map); cost per new customer by month (connected, Meta Ads and Shopify); 180 day value per group (enabler, buyer-cohorts).
  - Steps: 1) Compare each cell's cost this quarter with last quarter 2) Mark cells up 20 percent or more 3) Set each cell's cost against its 180 day value 4) Propose a budget shift and the ads that fit the cheaper cells 5) Founder approves the shift
  - Output: a quarterly shift plan by cell with the value case. Next: feeds 11 paid social.
  - Watch: blended cost per new customer (28 day, Meta Ads and Shopify), then cost per new customer, then contribution margin.
- **02-17 Find a buyer group no rival speaks to: list every angle rivals run, map each to an avatar, name the group none serve, and test it with 3 ads** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: twice a year. Enablers: copy-decomposer, competitor-feed, customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: rival ads with angles (enabler, competitor-feed); our avatars and jobs (brain, customers shelf); reviews that mention uses and buyers we do not target (connected, reviews app).
  - Steps: 1) Tag every rival ad with the avatar it speaks to 2) Count ads per avatar across all rivals 3) Find groups with buyers in our reviews and few or no rival ads 4) Brief 3 ads for the best one 5) Launch as 1 test and read cost per new customer
  - Output: an avatar count across rivals, 1 unserved group and a launched test. Next: feeds 09 creative and 11 paid social.
  - Watch: cost per new customer in the unserved group test (28 day, Meta Ads), then new buyers at lower cost, then revenue.
### T5. When a small group of customers makes a big share of our profit and some of them go quiet, help me know them by name, treat them quietly well and hear why any stop, so they stay and bring others

Metric: 12 month repeat rate of the top 5 percent of customers

- **02-18 Rank customers by 12 month gross profit and recency, pick the top 2 percent, and send each a personal letter with quiet early access, telling no one else** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: vip-list, buyer-cohorts.
  - Inputs: customers with orders, 24 months (connected, Shopify); margin per order (enabler, margin-view); letter template and budget (founder, founder); what we can offer quietly: early access, a private product (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Rank customers by 12 month gross profit, then by recency 2) Pick the top 2 percent and remove anyone with an open complaint 3) Write a short personal letter signed by the founder 4) Offer quiet early access to the next launch with a private link 5) Log who got what and track their next 90 days
  - Output: a VIP list, letters sent and a log of what each did next. Next: feeds 02-19 (VIP early read) and 14 email.
  - Watch: repeat rate of VIPs against a held back group (90 day, Shopify), then repeat revenue from top customers, then revenue.
- **02-19 Show a new product or message idea to 20 VIPs before any ad spend and collect a yes, maybe or no with 1 reason each** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: before each launch or big message change. Enablers: vip-list, customer-jobs.
  - Inputs: VIP list (enabler, vip-list); the idea as 1 image and 3 lines (founder, creative team); a reply form (setup, a Google Form).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 20 VIPs who fit the idea's avatar 2) Send the idea with 1 question and the form 3) Collect answers for 5 days 4) Count yes, maybe and no and group the reasons 5) Write go, change or stop
  - Output: 20 answers with reasons and a go, change or stop call. Next: feeds 01-14 (kill test) and 09 creative.
  - Watch: share of launches tested on VIPs first (90 day, work board), then fewer paid lessons, then gross profit.
- **02-20 Ask good customers who went quiet why, in their own words: email the top customers overdue by 2 times their usual gap, and code the answers** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: vip-list, buyer-cohorts.
  - Inputs: customers with their usual gap between orders (connected, Shopify); an email tool to send a plain email (setup, email platform); coding list of leave reasons (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List top 10 percent customers now 2 times past their usual gap 2) Send a plain email from the founder with 1 question 3) Code replies: product, price, rival, life change, service 4) Count reasons and name any rival they moved to 5) Send product and service fixes to the owners
  - Output: a monthly list of leave reasons with counts and named rivals. Next: feeds 15 CX, 03 competitors and 14 win back flows.
  - Watch: reply rate and win back orders (60 day, Shopify and email platform), then repeat revenue, then revenue.

## 03-competitors Competitors and market

### T1. When I catch myself scrolling rival sites and Instagram at night, help me see only the rival moves that touch our sales this week, like a sale, a launch, a new policy or a stock out, so I can act on those and stop watching the rest

Metric: rival moves flagged per week with our sales touched in dollars

- **03-03 Alert the same day a rival starts a sale, with the share of our sales it touches and 3 choices: match, hold or answer with value** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: daily check at 10am. Enablers: rival-site-watch, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: rival site banners and prices (enabler, rival-site-watch); new sale ads from rivals (connected, Meta Ad Library); our sales by product, 28 days (connected, Shopify); discount rules (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Spot a new sale banner, price cut or sale ad 2) Match to our products and compute our sales touched 3) Cost match, hold and answer with value using margin per order 4) Send the 3 choices to the founder 5) Log the call and read our sales for 7 days after
  - Output: a same day alert with 3 costed choices and a logged call. Next: feeds 04-20 (answer without matching).
  - Watch: our sales on touched products during rival sales (7 day, Shopify), then revenue kept during rival sales, then gross profit.
- **03-02 Send a Monday rival digest with only the moves that touch our products making over 10 percent of sales: price, offer, shipping, returns, new products** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: rival-site-watch, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: rival site and listing diffs (enabler, rival-site-watch); rival new ads this week (connected, Meta Ad Library); our sales by product (connected, Shopify); which rival products match ours (brain, competitors shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull last week's diffs and new ads 2) Match each move to our products 3) Drop moves on products under 10 percent of our sales 4) Rank the rest by our sales touched in dollars 5) Write 1 line each with a respond, watch or ignore call
  - Output: a Monday digest of 3 to 7 moves with dollars touched and a call each. Next: feeds 04-20 (answer a rival sale) and 00 Today.
  - Watch: hours from a rival move to our call (7 day, digest log), then founder peace of mind and fewer missed moves, then customer satisfaction.
- **03-04 Flag when a rival is weak, out of stock on a best seller, pulling ads or in bad press, and propose a push to win their buyers that week** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: weekly, and on a stock out or a drop in rival ads. Enablers: rival-site-watch, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: rival stock state (enabler, rival-site-watch); rival active ad count by week (connected, Meta Ad Library); news and review spikes about rivals (setup, public news and reviews).
  - Steps: 1) Check rival stock on their top 10 products 2) Check rival active ads against their 8 week average 3) Check news and rival review spikes 4) If weak, propose a push: budget up, a comparison ad, a search campaign on their name 5) Founder approves and the push runs for 2 weeks
  - Output: a weak rival flag with a 2 week push plan. Next: feeds 11 paid social and 12 Google ads.
  - Watch: new customers during the push (14 day, Shopify), then new customers won from rivals, then revenue.
- **03-01 Set up the rival list: 5 to 8 rivals with Meta page ids, social handles, site URLs and Amazon listings, plus 2 substitutes buyers use instead, and have the founder confirm it** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, reviewed each quarter. Enablers: competitor-feed, rival-site-watch.
  - Inputs: rivals the founder names (founder, founder interview); brands running ads on our category terms (connected, Meta Ad Library search); brands bidding on our brand name (connected, Google Ads auction insights); rivals named in reviews and chats (connected, reviews app and helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) List rivals from the founder, the Ad Library, auction insights and reviews 2) Find page ids, handles, site URLs and Amazon listings for each 3) Add 2 substitutes: what buyers do instead 4) Founder confirms 5 to 8 rivals and ranks the top 3 5) File on the competitors shelf and start the weekly pulls
