  - Output: a confirmed rival list with ids, handles, URLs and a top 3. Next: feeds every job on this screen and 09 creative.
  - Watch: rivals with a working weekly pull (7 day, competitors shelf), then a clean base for every rival read, then risk.
### T2. When our creative team runs dry, help me see how rivals spread their ads across awareness levels and which angles they keep paying for, turned into principles we can test in our own words, so we reach new buyers without copying anyone

Metric: tests launched from rival principles each month and their hit rate

- **03-06 For the awareness level where rivals push and we are thin, pull 10 examples from rivals and the swipe file, and write 3 briefs for our own ads at that level** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: after each heat map update that shows a gap. Enablers: awareness-map, swipe-file, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: the heat map gap (enabler, awareness-map); saved ads tagged by level (enabler, swipe-file); signed avatars and pains (brain, customers shelf); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Filter rival ads and the swipe file by the thin level 2) Pick 10 that ran 30 days or more 3) Write the principle each follows in 1 line 4) Write 3 briefs in our voice for our avatar, never copying text, logos, products or faces 5) Send to creative as 1 test
  - Output: 3 briefs at the thin awareness level with the principles behind them. Next: feeds 09 creative and 11 paid social test.
  - Watch: cost per new customer for ads at the new level (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **03-05 Tag every rival ad and every one of our ads by awareness level (unaware, problem aware, solution aware, product aware, most aware), weight rival ads by run length and variant count, and show our share next to 3 rivals** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every Monday after the ad pull. Enablers: awareness-map, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: last 90 days of our ads with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); active and past ads of 3 to 5 rivals (connected, Meta Ad Library by page id); rival page ids (brain, competitors shelf); awareness rubric with 5 examples per level (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) Pull our ads and rivals' ads 2) Tag each with 1 awareness level and 1 funnel stage using the rubric 3) Check 20 tags by hand and fix the rubric 4) Weight ours by spend; weight rivals by days live times variant count, since their spend is hidden 5) Lay the 5 levels by 4 brands as a heat map 6) Write the gap: the level where we are thin and rivals are thick
  - Output: a heat map of 5 awareness levels by 4 brands, with the gap line. Next: feeds 03-06 (fill the gap) and 09 creative briefs.
  - Watch: our share of spend on unaware and problem aware ads (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **03-07 Turn rival ads and posts that ran 30 days or more into principles: what they do, why it likely works, and the test to run in our own words** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every 2 weeks. Enablers: competitor-feed, principle-matcher, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: rival ads with days live (connected, Meta Ad Library); rival organic posts with views (setup, Instagram and TikTok public profiles); idea bank and knowledge cards to check principles against (have, ideas/v1 and data/library-v3); tag list (enabler, creative-tags).
  - Steps: 1) Pull rival ads live 30 days or more and posts with 3 times the account's median views 2) Tag hook, angle, format, proof and awareness level 3) Match each to a principle in the idea bank or cards 4) Write 1 line why it likely works and 1 test for us 5) Mark each as a signal, not a proven winner 6) File in the swipe file with the note
  - Output: a list of principles, each with the rival examples, a why line and a test. Next: feeds 09 creative (scripts and briefs) and 10 copy.
  - Watch: hit rate of tests built from rival principles (60 day, win and loss ledger), then creative hit rate, then revenue.
- **03-08 Pull each rival's top organic posts from the last 90 days on TikTok and Instagram, tag hook and format, and name the formats that get 3 times their usual views** Score 30, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: competitor-feed, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: rival handles (brain, competitors shelf); public posts with views and dates (setup, Instagram and TikTok public profiles); a social data tool for view history (buy, a social listening tool).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 90 days of posts per rival 2) Compute each rival's median views 3) Keep posts at 3 times the median 4) Tag hook, format, length and first frame 5) List formats that win for 2 or more rivals
  - Output: a table of breakout formats across rivals with examples. Next: feeds 16 organic content and 09 creative.
  - Watch: views per post on our tests of the format (30 day, Instagram and TikTok), then organic reach, then revenue.
- **03-09 Map the main claim and look each rival owns, and find a claim buyers care about that no rival makes** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: twice a year, and before a big brief. Enablers: competitor-feed, review-miner.
  - Inputs: rival ads, home pages and product pages (connected, Meta Ad Library and rival sites); what buyers praise in the category (enabler, review-miner); our allowed claims with proof (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write each rival's main claim and visual style in 1 line 2) List claims buyers praise in reviews 3) Mark claims no rival owns 4) Keep the ones we can prove 5) Brief the team on what to avoid and what to own
  - Output: a claim map with 1 open claim we can prove and a do not look like list. Next: feeds 10 copy and 05 brand brain positioning.
  - Watch: click through on ads using the open claim (28 day, Meta Ads), then a claim we can own, then revenue.
- **03-10 Find which creators carry each rival and whether it is paid, from partnership ad labels and tagged posts** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: quarterly. Enablers: competitor-feed, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: rival partnership ads (connected, Meta Ad Library); posts tagging rivals (setup, Instagram and TikTok); our creator list (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull rival partnership ads and the creators named 2) Pull posts tagging rivals and note paid labels 3) Count creators per rival and how long each runs 4) Mark creators who fit our avatar and do not work with a rival now 5) Add them to our candidate list
  - Output: a creator map per rival and a candidate list for us. Next: feeds 09 creative and 27 wild bets.
  - Watch: creators contacted from the list (90 day, creators shelf), then reach through creators, then revenue.
### T3. When shoppers compare us with a named rival and some leave for them, help me learn from rival reviews and lost buyers what they fail at and what they win on, so we pitch against their weakness with proof

Metric: conversion rate of chats and pages where a rival is named

- **03-11 Mine 1 to 3 star reviews of each rival's top 3 products on Amazon and their site, count the complaint themes, and pick 3 we beat with proof** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: rival reviews (setup, Amazon and rival sites); our reviews on the same themes (connected, reviews app); our proof files (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull up to 300 low reviews per rival product 2) Tag themes and count them 3) Check our reviews on the same themes 4) Pick 3 themes where we clearly win and can prove it 5) Write 3 ad angles and 3 page lines, naming no rival
  - Output: a rival complaint table and 3 angles we win on with proof. Next: feeds 10 copy, 09 creative and 07 product pages.
  - Watch: cost per purchase on the new angles (28 day, Meta Ads), then buyers won on rival weakness, then revenue.
- **03-13 Write a battlecard per rival for support and pages: the true, checked difference, the proof link and the line to use when a shopper asks how are you different** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when a rival changes product, price or policy. Enablers: review-miner, rival-site-watch.
  - Inputs: rival products, prices and policies (enabler, rival-site-watch); rival complaint themes (enabler, review-miner); chats where a rival was named (connected, helpdesk); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull chats that name each rival and the questions asked 2) Write 3 true differences per rival with proof 3) Write the reply in our voice 4) Founder checks each line for truth 5) Load into support macros and FAQ
  - Output: a battlecard per rival loaded into support. Next: feeds 15 CX macros and 07 comparison blocks.
  - Watch: order rate from chats that name a rival (28 day, helpdesk and Shopify), then chats won, then revenue.
- **03-12 Mine 4 and 5 star reviews of rivals for what buyers love that we never say, and check if we can say it truthfully** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: review-miner, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: rival reviews (setup, Amazon and rival sites); our language bank (brain, customers shelf); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull top reviews for rival products 2) Tag praise themes and the words used 3) Mark themes our pages and ads never mention 4) Check if our product does the same and we have proof 5) Hand true ones to copy with the buyer words
  - Output: a list of praised benefits we under say, with proof checked. Next: feeds 10 copy and 07 landing pages.
  - Watch: conversion on pages that add the benefit (28 day, GA4), then conversion rate, then revenue.
- **03-14 Ask cart abandoners and exit visitors which brand they chose instead and why, and count wins and losses per rival** Score 30, ready 4/5, weeks.
  - When: run for 4 weeks, then each quarter. Enablers: customer-jobs, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: an exit question on product and cart pages (setup, Clarity or Hotjar survey); an abandoned cart email with 1 question (setup, email platform); rival list (brain, competitors shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Add the question: did you buy something else instead, which and why 2) Collect answers for 4 weeks 3) Count rivals named and code the reasons 4) Name the top reason we lose to each rival 5) Send the top fix to the owner
  - Output: a win and loss count per rival with the top reason. Next: feeds 04 offers, 07 pages and 03-13 (battlecards).
  - Watch: answers collected (28 day, survey tool), then fewer buyers lost to rivals, then revenue.
### T4. When I set prices or plan a sale, help me see where our price and discounts sit against rivals per unit and when they run their big sales, so we are not priced out, not training buyers to wait and not drowned out

Metric: our price per unit against the rival median, per product

- **03-15 Show our price per unit against the rival median for each product, pack size matched, and flag any product over 15 percent above or below** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: price-bands, rival-site-watch.
  - Inputs: our prices and pack sizes (connected, Shopify); rival prices and pack sizes (enabler, rival-site-watch); the unit rule, like per pair (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Match each of our products to 3 to 5 rival products 2) Convert all to price per unit 3) Compute our index against the rival median 4) Flag products over 15 percent off 5) Check the flag against our proof and rating before any price move
  - Output: a price index table per product with flags. Next: feeds 04-15 (price moves) and 04-16 (price test).
  - Watch: price index against rival median (weekly, rival-site-watch), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **03-16 Compare our discount depth and how often we discount with each rival over 12 months, and say if we discount more while claiming to be premium** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: quarterly. Enablers: rival-site-watch, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: our discount history (connected, offers shelf and Shopify); rival sale ads with start dates (connected, Meta Ad Library); rival site sale banners over 12 months (enabler, rival-site-watch); our price position (brain, core page).
  - Steps: 1) Count our sale days and average discount for 12 months 2) Do the same for each rival from ads with sale words and site banners 3) Lay them side by side 4) Write the call: more, less or in line 5) Propose a cap if we discount more
  - Output: a 12 month discount table vs rivals and a cap proposal. Next: feeds 04-02 (discount rules).
  - Watch: share of revenue on a discount (90 day, Shopify), then gross margin, then gross profit.
- **03-17 Build a rival sale calendar from 12 months of ad start dates and site banners, and mark the weeks to avoid or to answer** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once a year, updated monthly. Enablers: competitor-feed, rival-site-watch.
  - Inputs: rival ad start dates with sale words (connected, Meta Ad Library); rival site sale history (enabler, rival-site-watch); our calendar (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 12 months of rival ads and find sale windows 2) Add site banner dates where we have them 3) Plot all rivals on 1 calendar 4) Mark weeks where 2 or more rivals run sales 5) Plan our own dates around them and put them on the calendar
  - Output: a rival sale calendar with our planned dates. Next: feeds 04-19 (big sale plan) and 14 email calendar.
  - Watch: our revenue in weeks with rival sales (quarterly, Shopify), then sale revenue, then revenue.
### T5. When I set next quarter's budget, help me see how loud we are next to rivals in ads, brand search and Amazon, and who is growing faster, so I know if we are being outshouted or paying a toll on our own name

Metric: our share of active ads and of brand search impressions

- **03-19 Find which rivals bid on our brand name and what share of brand searches they take, and decide how much to spend to defend it** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: auction insights on brand campaigns (connected, Google Ads); brand search clicks (connected, Search Console); brand campaign spend and orders (connected, Google Ads and Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pull auction insights for brand terms 2) List rivals and their impression share 3) Compare brand clicks from ads with organic brand clicks 4) Estimate orders lost if we stop bidding 5) Set the defend spend and send it to Google ads
  - Output: a brand toll table with the defend spend call. Next: feeds 12 Google ads brand campaigns.
  - Watch: rival impression share on our brand terms (28 day, Google Ads), then cost of brand search, then contribution margin.
- **03-18 Count active and new ads per rival each week against ours, and track public growth signals: ad count, follower growth, review count growth** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: competitor-feed, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: active ads per rival (connected, Meta Ad Library); our active ads (connected, Meta Ads); follower counts over time (setup, Instagram and TikTok public profiles); review counts over time (setup, rival sites and Amazon).
  - Steps: 1) Count active and new ads per brand 2) Compute our share of all active ads 3) Pull follower and review count growth for 90 days 4) Rank rivals by growth 5) Write the call: outshouted, even, or louder
  - Output: a weekly share of voice table and a growth rank of rivals. Next: feeds 11 paid social budget and 26 reports.
  - Watch: our share of active ads (28 day, Meta Ad Library), then reach against rivals, then revenue.
- **03-20 Track our Amazon rank against rivals on 10 core category search terms each month** Score 26, ready 1/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: market-pulse, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: 10 core search terms (brain, seo shelf); organic and sponsored rank per term (buy, a marketplace rank tracker); our listings (setup, Amazon Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 10 terms buyers use on Amazon 2) Pull our rank and each rival's rank per term 3) Compare with last month 4) Mark terms where we fell 5 places or more 5) Send listing and ad fixes to marketplaces
  - Output: a rank table by term and brand with fall flags. Next: feeds 20 marketplaces.
  - Watch: average rank on core terms (30 day, rank tracker), then marketplace sales, then revenue.

## 04-offers-pricing Offers and pricing

### T1. When someone on the team or at the agency wants to run a discount or change an offer, help me see what it costs in gross profit, how many extra orders it needs, the new most we can pay per purchase, and whether the ad, page and cart all show the same offer, so I do not give away the month's profit

Metric: gross profit per order during offers against normal weeks

- **04-01 Cost every proposed discount before it runs: gross profit per order at that depth, extra orders needed to break even, and a yes or no against the margin floor** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every time someone proposes a discount, before it goes live. Enablers: offer-math, margin-view.
  - Inputs: margin per SKU (enabler, margin-view); baseline orders per day for the products in the offer (connected, Shopify); the proposal: products, depth, dates, channels (founder, the person asking); margin floor and deepest discount allowed (brain, offers and finance shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Take the products, depth and dates from the proposal 2) Compute gross profit per order at full price and at the offer 3) Compute the extra orders needed to make the same gross profit 4) Compare with lift from past offers of the same depth 5) Write yes, no or change, with the numbers, and log it
  - Output: a 1 page offer cost sheet with break even orders and a yes, no or change. Next: feeds 04-03 (new max cost per purchase) and 04-04 (sale review).
  - Watch: gross profit per order during offers (per offer, Shopify), then gross profit kept per month, then gross profit.
- **04-02 Write discount rules the founder signs: deepest discount per product, a never discount list, how often, who may make codes, and check every live code against them each week** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then every Monday. Enablers: code-watch.
  - Inputs: all live codes with creator and depth (connected, Shopify); founder's limits (founder, founder interview); rival discount habits (brain, competitors shelf); rule ideas for discount policy (have, idea bank ideas/v1 03-OFF).
  - Steps: 1) Draft rules from margins, rival habits and the founder's limits 2) Founder signs and the rules go on the offers shelf 3) List live codes and check each against the rules 4) Flag breaking codes with who made them and margin given away 5) Turn off or fix breaking codes with the founder's yes
  - Output: signed discount rules and a weekly list of codes that break them. Next: feeds 04-06 (leaks) and 14 email offers.
  - Watch: codes breaking the rules (7 day, Shopify), then margin given away, then gross profit.
- **04-03 Send the media buyer the new most we can pay per purchase, per product, the day an offer or price changes** Score 42, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: the day any price, offer or cost changes. Enablers: offer-math, margin-view.
  - Inputs: new price or offer (brain, offers shelf); margin per SKU (enabler, margin-view); 180 day value per new customer (enabler, buyer-cohorts).
  - Steps: 1) Recompute gross profit per first order at the new price 2) Add expected repeat profit where the founder allows it 3) Set the most we can pay per purchase, first order and with repeat 4) Send both numbers to the media buyer and the ad screens 5) Log the change date
  - Output: a cost per purchase target per product with the date it changed. Next: feeds 11 paid social and 12 Google ads targets.
  - Watch: ads judged against the current target (7 day, ad platforms), then contribution after ads, then contribution margin.
- **04-14 Check every week that each live ad's offer matches its landing page, cart and emails, and fix mismatches before buyers see them** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday and before any offer launch. Enablers: congruence-check.
  - Inputs: live ads with copy and URLs (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); page, cart and checkout text (brain, site shelf); live email offers (setup, email platform); live offers and codes (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List every offer claim in live ads 2) Open each landing page and cart and read the offer shown 3) Check emails sent this week for the same offer 4) Flag mismatches with spend on the ad 5) Send fixes to the owner the same day
  - Output: a weekly offer match list with mismatches fixed. Next: feeds 07 landing pages and 11 paid social.
  - Watch: ads with offer mismatches (7 day, congruence-check log), then trust at checkout, then customer satisfaction.
- **04-04 Review every sale 3 weeks after it ends: the 2 weeks before, the sale and the 3 weeks after against a normal baseline, and record whether to run it again** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 3 weeks after each sale ends. Enablers: offer-math, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: orders and gross profit by day, 8 weeks around the sale (connected, Shopify); ad spend by day (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); the offer's cost sheet (enabler, offer-math); offer archive (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Set a baseline from normal weeks 2) Sum gross profit above baseline in the sale and below baseline before and after 3) Split new vs returning buyers in the sale 4) Write the true lift and whether to run it again 5) File in the offer archive and the ledger
  - Output: a sale review with true lift, new buyer share and a run again call. Next: feeds 04-19 (big sale plan).
  - Watch: gross profit per sale against baseline (per sale, Shopify), then gross profit from sales, then gross profit.
### T2. When sales only come during promotions and every visitor gets a code, help me see how much revenue depends on discounts and which discounts go to buyers who would have bought anyway, so I stop training buyers to wait

Metric: share of revenue on a discount code, new vs returning

- **04-05 Show promo dependence each month for 24 months: share of revenue and orders on a code, split new vs returning** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: code-watch, buyer-cohorts.
  - Inputs: orders with codes and discounts, 24 months (connected, Shopify); new vs returning flag (connected, Shopify); known sale dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Sum revenue on a code and at full price per month 2) Split each by new and returning buyers 3) Plot share on a code over 24 months 4) Mark months where full price sales fell as code sales rose 5) Write the trend in 1 line
  - Output: a 24 month chart of discount share, new vs returning. Next: feeds 04-02 (rules) and 04-09 (loyal vs new offers).
  - Watch: share of revenue on a code (30 day, Shopify), then full price revenue, then gross profit.
- **04-06 Find codes leaked to coupon sites and browser extensions from the referrer on each order, shut them and swap in unique codes** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: code-watch.
  - Inputs: orders with code, referrer and UTM (connected, Shopify and GA4); list of coupon sites and extensions (setup, a public list, kept in a Google Sheet); which codes are meant to be public (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List codes used by orders from coupon sites or with no source 2) Mark codes not meant to be public 3) Sum discount given away on those orders 4) Turn them off and issue unique codes for their true purpose 5) Check next week for new leaks
  - Output: a leak list with dollars given away and codes swapped. Next: feeds 04-05 (dependence falls).
  - Watch: discount dollars on leaked codes (7 day, Shopify), then margin given away, then gross profit.
- **04-07 Test the welcome pop-up offer: hold back 20 percent of new visitors from the discount for 4 weeks and compare conversion and gross profit per visitor** Score 36, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once, then each year. Enablers: offer-math, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: pop-up tool with a hold back option (setup, email platform); orders and margin per visitor group (connected, Shopify and GA4); sample size rule (have, idea bank ideas/v1 09-CRO).
  - Steps: 1) Set a 20 percent hold back with no discount, email capture kept 2) Run 4 weeks or until the sample size is met 3) Compare conversion, order value and gross profit per visitor 4) Write keep, cut the depth, or swap for a non cash reward 5) Log in the ledger
  - Output: a pop-up test result with gross profit per visitor for each group. Next: feeds 14 email welcome flow.
  - Watch: gross profit per new visitor (28 day, Shopify and GA4), then gross profit from new visitors, then gross profit.
- **04-08 Test the first cart reminder with and without a discount, and keep the discount only if it adds gross profit** Score 32, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once, then twice a year. Enablers: offer-math, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: cart and checkout reminder flows (setup, email platform); recovered orders and margin (connected, Shopify); current reminder offer (brain, email shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Split the first reminder: no discount vs current discount 2) Keep later reminders the same 3) Run 4 weeks 4) Compare recovered orders and gross profit per cart 5) Keep the winner and log it
  - Output: a reminder test result and the kept version. Next: feeds 14 email flows.
  - Watch: gross profit per abandoned cart (28 day, Shopify), then recovered gross profit, then gross profit.
- **04-09 Stop giving loyal buyers new customer codes: count returning buyers using welcome codes, and replace their discount with early access or a gift** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: quarterly. Enablers: buyer-cohorts, vip-list, code-watch.
  - Inputs: orders with codes and new vs returning flag (connected, Shopify); VIP list (enabler, vip-list); allowed perks (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Count returning buyers who used welcome or new customer codes 2) Sum the discount given to them 3) Limit welcome codes to first orders 4) Offer returning buyers early access or a gift instead 5) Track repeat rate for 90 days
  - Output: a count of discount to loyal buyers and a new loyal offer. Next: feeds 14 email and 02-18 (VIP letters).
  - Watch: discount dollars to returning buyers (90 day, Shopify), then gross profit per repeat order, then gross profit.
### T3. When order size is stuck and shoppers hesitate, help me raise order value and remove the biggest doubt with thresholds, bundles, gifts and guarantees that still clear the margin floor, so each order earns more without a deeper discount

Metric: gross profit per order and average order value

- **04-10 Set the free shipping line from the order value spread and shipping cost per order, pick the line that lifts order size and pays for itself, and test it** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when shipping rates change. Enablers: offer-math, margin-view.
  - Inputs: order values, 90 days (connected, Shopify); shipping cost per order (setup, carrier invoices or Shopify); rival free shipping lines (brain, competitors shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Plot the order value spread and find the peaks 2) Test 3 lines 15 to 30 percent above the median order 3) Cost each: shipping paid vs extra gross profit from buyers moving up 4) Pick the best and run it for 4 weeks 5) Compare order value and gross profit per order
  - Output: a chosen free shipping line with a 4 week result. Next: feeds 07 cart messages.
  - Watch: gross profit per order (28 day, Shopify), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **04-11 Build 3 bundles from products bought together, price each to keep gross profit per order above the floor, and launch on the top product pages** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: twice a year. Enablers: offer-math, margin-view.
  - Inputs: baskets with 2 or more products (connected, Shopify); margin per SKU (enabler, margin-view); pack and price per unit issues from buyers (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Find the top pairs and sets in baskets 2) Pick 3 bundles that fit a buyer job 3) Price each so gross profit per order beats the average 4) Add to the top product pages 5) Read attach rate and order value for 4 weeks
  - Output: 3 live bundles with prices and a 4 week read. Next: feeds 07 product pages and 14 email.
  - Watch: bundle attach rate and order value (28 day, Shopify), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **04-13 Build an offer around the biggest doubt from the objection ladder, such as a fit guarantee or a trial, and cost it at the expected claim rate** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: after each objection ladder update. Enablers: offer-math, review-miner.
  - Inputs: top doubt from the objection ladder (brain, customers shelf); return and claim rates (connected, Shopify and returns app); current guarantee terms (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the doubt that costs the most sales 2) Write 2 offers that remove it 3) Cost each at the claim rate and at 2 times that rate 4) Put the best on the product page and in ads 5) Read conversion and claims for 6 weeks
  - Output: a costed doubt offer, live, with a 6 week read. Next: feeds 07 product pages and 10 copy.
  - Watch: conversion rate on the product (42 day, GA4 and Shopify), then conversion without deeper discounts, then revenue.
- **04-12 Test a free gift at an order value tier against 10 percent off, and keep the one with more gross profit per visitor** Score 34, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once, then each season. Enablers: offer-math, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: gift options with cost (brain, operations shelf); order values and margin (connected, Shopify); a split test tool or a split by week (setup, store app or schedule).
  - Steps: 1) Pick a tier just above the most common order value 2) Pick a gift that costs less than the discount it replaces 3) Run gift vs 10 percent off for 4 weeks 4) Compare conversion, order value and gross profit per visitor 5) Keep the winner and log it
  - Output: a gift vs discount result with the kept offer. Next: feeds 04-19 (big sale plan).
  - Watch: gross profit per visitor (28 day, Shopify and GA4), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
### T4. When a tariff, freight or supplier cost rise lands, or a new product needs a price, help me choose between raising price, changing the pack or absorbing it, using real costs and the rival range, so margin holds without losing buyers

Metric: gross margin percent on the top 10 SKUs

- **04-16 Test a price rise of 5 to 10 percent on 1 product for 4 weeks and read conversion and gross profit per visitor** Score 40, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once a quarter, on 1 product. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, price-bands.
  - Inputs: product page traffic and conversion (connected, GA4 and Shopify); price test tool or a before and after plan (setup, store app); price band position (enabler, price-bands).
  - Steps: 1) Pick a product with steady traffic and room in its band 2) Raise the price on all channels at once 3) Run 4 weeks 4) Compare conversion and gross profit per visitor with the 4 weeks before 5) Keep, roll back, or move to the next product
  - Output: a price test result and a keep or roll back call. Next: feeds 25 experiments and 04-15.
  - Watch: gross profit per visitor on the product (28 day, Shopify and GA4), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **04-15 When a cost rise lands, compare raising price, changing the pack and absorbing it for each SKU hit, in gross profit per month at 3 levels of buyer loss** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: the week a cost rise lands. Enablers: offer-math, margin-view, price-bands.
  - Inputs: new landed cost per SKU (enabler, market-pulse); sales and margin per SKU (connected, Shopify); price band position (enabler, price-bands); pack options (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List SKUs hit and the cost change per unit 2) Model 3 options for each: raise price, change pack, absorb 3) For each, compute gross profit per month at 0, 5 and 10 percent fewer buyers 4) Check each new price against the rival band 5) Founder picks; log the reason
  - Output: an option table per SKU with a founder pick. Next: feeds 04-16 (price test) and 04-03 (new cost per purchase target).
  - Watch: gross margin on SKUs hit (90 day, Shopify), then gross margin, then gross profit.
- **04-17 Price a new product from 3 numbers: landed cost floor, the rival band, and what 100 buyers say is cheap, fair and too much** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: before each launch. Enablers: price-bands, launch-scorecard, margin-view.
  - Inputs: landed cost and fees (enabler, launch-scorecard); rival band (enabler, price-bands); a 4 question price survey to 100 buyers (setup, email platform or Google Form); target margin (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the floor price for the target margin 2) Read the rival band for the product 3) Survey 100 buyers: too cheap, a bargain, getting pricey, too much 4) Pick a price in the overlap of all 3 5) Set the cost per purchase target at that price
  - Output: a launch price with the 3 numbers behind it. Next: feeds 04-03 (cost per purchase target) and 01-17 (90 day review).
  - Watch: gross margin and conversion in the first 30 days (30 day, Shopify), then launch margin, then gross profit.
### T5. When Black Friday or a rival's big sale is coming, or an offer has gone stale, help me plan an answer that adds profit and does not just pull orders forward or match their price, so the season lifts the year

Metric: season gross profit against normal weeks, counting the dip after

- **04-19 Plan the Black Friday offer 8 weeks out: read the last 2 years, cost 3 offer shapes, pick one that adds profit after the dip, and check stock** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 8 weeks before Black Friday. Enablers: offer-math, win-loss-ledger, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: last 2 years of sale results (connected, Shopify and offers shelf); rival sale calendar and offers (enabler, competitor-feed); stock by SKU and lead times (connected, Shopify and operations shelf); margin floor (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Read true lift from the last 2 sales, counting the dip 2) Cost 3 shapes: sitewide percent off, gift tiers, bundles 3) Pick the shape with the most gross profit above baseline 4) Check stock covers the plan and order now if not 5) Set dates for creative, emails and page, and put them on the board
  - Output: a costed sale plan with stock check and dates. Next: feeds 09 creative, 14 email and 00 Today board.
  - Watch: gross profit in sale weeks against baseline (sale window, Shopify), then season gross profit, then gross profit.
- **04-20 Answer a rival's big sale within 24 hours without matching their price: add a gift, a bundle or a longer guarantee that costs less than their discount** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: within 24 hours of a rival sale alert. Enablers: offer-math, rival-site-watch.
  - Inputs: rival sale alert (enabler, rival-site-watch); margin per SKU (enabler, margin-view); allowed offers (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Read which of our products the rival sale touches 2) Cost a gift, a bundle and a guarantee against their discount 3) Pick the one with the lowest cost per order 4) Brief 1 ad and 1 email with the answer 5) Read our sales on those products for 7 days
  - Output: a live value answer with its cost and a 7 day read. Next: feeds 09 creative and 14 email.
  - Watch: our sales on touched products (7 day, Shopify), then revenue kept at full price, then gross profit.
- **04-18 When an offer stops working, refresh it in order: new creative, then new copy, then a new wrapper or name, then new length, before any deeper discount** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: when an offer's conversion falls 20 percent from its first 4 weeks. Enablers: offer-math, variant-stack.
  - Inputs: offer results by week (connected, Shopify); the offer's ads (connected, Meta Ads); offer archive (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Confirm the drop is the offer, not traffic or stock 2) Swap creative first and read 2 weeks 3) Then copy, then the name or wrapper, then the end date 4) Only then cost a deeper discount 5) Log what brought it back
  - Output: a refreshed offer and a note on which step worked. Next: feeds 09 creative and 25 experiments.
  - Watch: offer conversion rate (14 day, Shopify), then gross profit kept, then gross profit.

## 05-brand-brain Brand brain

### T1. When I bring a brand onto ecomOS, help me turn its site, ads, reviews, tickets and my own answers into one record filed on the right shelves and linked like a graph, so I can trust every agent, freelancer and screen to start from the same checked facts

Metric: record sections signed by the founder in the last 90 days, share

- **05-04 Walk the founder through the drafted record shelf by shelf and get each section marked checked, fixed or not true, with a name and a date** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once in week 1, then a 20 minute re-sign each quarter. Enablers: brain-graph, review-log.
  - Inputs: the drafted core page and shelf indexes (enabler, brain-graph); 60 minutes of the founder (founder, founder); interview answers filed on shelves (brain, founder records).
  - Steps: 1) Order the sections by how many jobs read them: products, claims, offers, voice first 2) Show each fact with its source so the founder judges the fact, not the wording 3) Founder marks each section checked, fixed or not true 4) Write fixes back and turn each gap into an open question 5) Sign the core page with the date and lock it so agents read only the signed version
  - Output: a signed core page with 18 or more of 22 sections signed and each open item named. Next: every brief pack in 05-07 loads only the signed version.
  - Watch: record sections signed by the founder, share (7 days, brain sign-off log), then drafts sent back for a brand reason, then gross profit.
- **05-03 Run the 45 minute founder interview and file each answer on its shelf, with the question number as its source** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once in week 1, after the first ingest. Enablers: brain-graph.
  - Inputs: the interview script with 25 core questions (have, app/brain/FOUNDER-INTERVIEW.md); 45 minutes of the founder and 30 minutes for the written follow-ups (founder, founder); shelves already filled from connected sources, so we skip what we know (enabler, brain-graph).
  - Steps: 1) Mark which questions the ingest already answered and drop them from the call 2) Record the call and write the transcript within 24 hours 3) File each answer on its shelf with tier founder and the question number 4) Turn every 'I would have to check' into an open question with an owner and a date 5) Send the founder a 1 page readback and fix what they correct
  - Output: founder records on the goals, finance, claims, voice, history and calendar shelves, plus an open question list. Next: feeds 05-04 (sign-off) and 05-11 (forbidden claims from question 17).
  - Watch: open questions answered within 14 days, share (14 days, brain open question list), then record sections signed by the founder, then gross profit.
- **05-06 Score every live ad, landing page and email flow against the signed record and sort them into keep, fix and retire before any new work starts** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once after sign-off, then each quarter. Enablers: brain-graph, claims-register, congruence-check.
  - Inputs: the signed record (enabler, brain-graph); live ads with spend and results (connected, Meta Ads); live landing pages and their ad traffic (connected, store platform and GA4); live email flows with copy (setup, email platform); claims in three tiers (enabler, claims-register).
  - Steps: 1) List every live ad, page and flow with its spend or sends in the last 30 days 2) Check each against the record: wrong price, banned claim, old offer, off voice, wrong product fact 3) Mark keep, fix (with the exact line to change) or retire 4) Sort fixes by spend so the work that reaches most shoppers is fixed first 5) Founder approves the retire list before anything is paused
  - Output: a keep, fix and retire list across ads, pages and flows, sorted by spend, with the line to change. Next: feeds 07-07 (fix pages for top ads), 14-flow fixes and the 09-creative brief queue.
  - Watch: live assets breaking a signed fact, count (14 days, brain check against Meta, site and email platform), then drafts sent back for a brand reason, and ads rejected for claims, then risk.
- **05-01 Crawl the store, pull 12 months of ads, reviews and tickets, and file every fact onto the 22 brain shelves with a source, a date and a tier** Score 34, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then weekly for the site and daily for ads and reviews. Enablers: brain-graph.
  - Inputs: every page, product, price and policy on the live site (connected, store platform); 12 months of ads with copy, media and spend (connected, Meta Ads); all reviews with rating, text and product (connected, reviews app); 12 months of tickets with tags (connected, helpdesk); the 22 shelves and what goes on each (have, app/brain/BRAIN-CONTENTS.md).
  - Steps: 1) Crawl every page and product and save the text, a screenshot and the date 2) Pull ads, reviews and tickets into one pile with their source ids 3) Split each item into single facts (a price, a claim, a doubt, an angle) and file each on its shelf 4) Tag every fact with source, date and tier: site, data, public, inferred 5) List facts where two sources disagree, like a price on the site and a different one in a live ad 6) List empty shelves and the interview question that fills each
  - Output: 22 shelves with an index each, plus a list of conflicts and a list of empty shelves. Next: feeds 05-02 (links), 05-03 (interview skips what is already known) and 05-04 (sign-off).
  - Watch: shelves with at least 1 sourced fact, share of 22 (first 7 days, brain index), then drafts sent back for a brand reason, then gross profit.
- **05-05 Split every fact that changes by country into its own field per market: currency, price, tax line, shipping promise, return window, payment methods and peak dates** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then when a new country is switched on. Enablers: brain-graph.
  - Inputs: revenue and orders by shipping country (connected, store platform); prices, shipping and return pages per market (connected, store platform); where the founder wants to grow and refuses to sell, and what changes by market (founder, founder interview questions 8 and R3); peaks and dead zones per market (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List the countries that bring any revenue and their share, computed from orders 2) For each, fill currency, price, tax line, shipping days, return window and payment methods from the site 3) Add the peaks per market from the calendar shelf 4) Leave a field empty and marked unavailable where the site does not say, never guess 5) Founder checks the 2 biggest markets line by line
  - Output: a market table: one row per country, with each field sourced or marked unavailable. Next: feeds 06-20 (test orders per new market) and 14-email send rules per country.
  - Watch: per-market fields filled and sourced, share (7 days, brain market shelf), then orders from non-home markets that fail or get a wrong promise, then risk.
- **05-02 Link the record like a graph: each product to its claims, each claim to its proof, each proof to the avatars, ads, pages and emails that use it** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once after the first ingest, then on every new product, claim or ad. Enablers: brain-graph, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: filed facts on the products, claims, customers and messaging shelves (enabler, brain-graph); live and past ads with angle and avatar tags (enabler, creative-tags); landing pages and email flows with their text (setup, site shelf and email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Name the nodes: products, claims, proofs, avatars, angles, ads, pages, emails 2) Draw the links from text matches, like the claim 'no slip heel' on the product page and in 14 ads 3) Spot check 20 links by hand and fix the matching rule where it is wrong 4) List orphans: claims with no proof, products with no ad, avatars no ad speaks to 5) Show one product as a map so the founder can see it in a minute
  - Output: a linked record with an orphan list: claims without proof, products without ads, avatars without ads. Next: feeds 05-11 (claims register) and the 09-creative briefs for products no ad speaks to.
  - Watch: claims with a proof link, share (14 days, brain graph), then claims in live work with proof on file, then risk.
### T2. When I hand a brief to an agent, a freelancer or an agency, help me give them the facts, voice, customer words and proven standards I would give, so I stop rewriting drafts that should have been right the first time

Metric: drafts sent back for a brand reason, share of all reviewed drafts, 30 days

- **05-07 Build one brief pack per job type (ad, email, landing page, creator) that loads the core page plus only the shelves that job needs, under 3,000 words** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.

  - Steps: 1) Pull every review and ticket and tag each with the moment, the doubt and the result wanted 2) Count each moment and doubt, computed in code 3) Lift 50 exact phrases for the product, the problem and the result, with source 4) Mark our own words customers never use 5) Founder checks the top 10 moments against what they hear on calls
  - Output: a language bank of 50 sourced phrases and a ranked list of 10 buying moments with counts. Next: feeds 07-12 (answers in the customer's words), 14-10 (welcome emails) and the 10-copy briefs.

- **05-08 Collect 20 liked and 20 disliked pieces from the founder with one line on why each, and turn them into voice rules per channel with a do and a do not example** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once in the first 2 weeks, then each time the founder rates 10 new drafts. Enablers: review-log.

  - Steps: 1) Ask for the 40 pieces across ads, emails, pages and support replies 2) Group the why lines into patterns: sentence length, humour, how we name the product, words we never use 3) Write 8 to 12 rules, each with 1 liked and 1 disliked example 4) Split rules that change by channel: ads, email, support, marketplace 5) Run 3 new drafts through the rules and have the founder mark which rule they broke
  - Output: a voice shelf: 3 words, 8 to 12 rules, 40 rated examples and a banned words list. Next: loaded by every brief pack in 05-07; ratings keep feeding 05-18.

- **05-09 Promote every test winner to the brand standard (T0) that new briefs start from, write a fail note for every loser, and set a date to recheck each one** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.

  - Steps: 1) Pull every test closed since the last run with its result 2) For a win, write the standard in one line with the proof, like 'show the saving in dollars on packs, +9% profit per visitor, May' 3) For a loss, write the fail note with why and 'do not rerun unless' 4) Add standards to the brief packs and fail notes to the idea filter 5) On each recheck date, test the standard again or mark it expired
  - Output: a T0 list of standards with proof and recheck dates, and a fail list new ideas are checked against. Next: feeds 05-07 brief packs, 07-19 page standards and 14-08 email standards.

  - Steps: 1) Pull every sentence that promises a result, a number, a material, a test or a comparison 2) Merge repeats so each claim appears once with every place it lives 3) Match each claim to a proof file; unmatched ones go to the founder 4) Founder marks each unmatched claim approved with proof, allowed as worded, or banned 5) Publish the register and flag live assets that carry a banned or unproven claim

  - Steps: 1) List every review, quote, photo and clip in live ads, pages and emails 2) Match each to its source and to a permission record 3) Mark usable, needs permission (with who to ask), or pull 4) Check creator usage windows and flag any past their end date 5) Send permission requests and pull the rest
  - Output: a proof and rights list with each item marked usable, needs permission or pull. Next: feeds 05-11 register and the 09-creative UGC library.

### T4. When the site, a product, an offer or a policy changes, help me find every ad, email and page still saying the old thing the same day, so I never have shoppers see a promise we stopped keeping

Metric: facts that differ between the record and the live site, count, daily

- **05-16 When an offer ends or a policy changes, find every live ad, email flow and page still saying the old thing, and hand each owner a fix list** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.

  - Steps: 1) Crawl the pages that carry promises 2) Compare each promise with the record 3) Name each difference with the page, the old and new value, and when it changed 4) Ask the founder: is the site right or the record right 5) Update whichever is wrong and date it

- **05-17 Before a product launches or changes fabric, formula or supplier, add its facts, claims and proof to the record, and mark every old claim that no longer holds** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.

  - Steps: 1) Collect the spec sheet and proof for the new or changed product 2) Write its product facts and allowed claims into the record 3) Find every claim linked to the old version and mark it retired or still true 4) List live ads, pages and emails with retired claims 5) Founder signs the product entry before any launch brief goes out
  - Output: a signed product entry and a retired claims list, ready before launch briefs. Next: feeds 05-07 brief packs for the launch and 14 launch sends.
  - Watch: launch assets with a claim not in the record, count (launch week, claims-register), then returns and tickets for 'not as described', then risk.
### T5. When the team keeps asking 'can we say this' or 'what did we decide', help me let them find the answer and the reason without me, so I get my day back and decisions stick

Metric: brand questions answered from the record without the founder, share, 30 days

- **05-18 Log every draft review with its reason, and when the same reason repeats 3 times in 30 days, write it as a rule with the draft that caused it** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.

## 06-store-health Store health

### T1. When the store or checkout stops taking orders at night, on a weekend or on one payment method, help me know within minutes and where it broke, so I stop paying for ads that send shoppers who cannot buy

Metric: minutes from checkout breaking to someone being told, per incident

- **06-01 Run a scripted test shopper through product, cart, discount and checkout every 15 minutes, and alert a named person when a step fails twice in a row** Score 46, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every 15 minutes, always on. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: store product, cart and checkout pages (connected, store platform); a hidden test product and a test discount code that zeroes the price (setup, store admin); a browser check tool that clicks through checkout from 2 regions (buy, a checkout monitor service); who gets the alert at night and on weekends (brain, decisions and goals shelf (approval rules)).
  - Steps: 1) Write the path: open product, pick a size, add to cart, apply the test code, reach payment 2) Run it every 15 minutes from 2 regions on a phone-sized screen 3) Alert by text when a step fails twice in a row, naming the step and a screenshot 4) Check the alert against orders per hour so one bad run does not wake anyone 5) Review false alarms monthly and tune the rule
  - Output: a live check with an alert that names the broken step, and a monthly false alarm count. Next: incidents feed 06-07 (was it a change) and 06-02 (revenue lost).
  - Watch: minutes from checkout breaking to someone being told (per incident, checkout-probe log), then revenue lost to downtime, 30 days, then revenue.
- **06-03 Check checkout completion by payment method each week (card, Apple Pay, Google Pay, PayPal, Shop Pay, pay later) and place one real order on any method that falls 10 points behind cards** Score 46, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and after any checkout or payment app change. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: checkouts and orders by payment gateway, 7 days (connected, store platform); a real card and wallet to place and refund test orders (founder, founder or ops lead); recent checkout and app changes (enabler, change-log).
  - Steps: 1) Compute completion rate per method from checkouts started and orders, in code 2) Flag any method 10 points or more behind cards 3) Place a real order on that method on an iPhone and an Android, then refund it 4) Match the fall with the change log to name the likely cause 5) Fix or hide the broken method and recheck in 7 days
  - Output: a weekly table of completion by payment method, with any broken method fixed or hidden. Next: feeds 06-07 when a change caused it.
  - Watch: checkout completion rate by payment method (7 days, store checkouts by gateway), then checkout to order rate, then revenue.
- **06-05 Check checkout settings against a short list (guest checkout on, 7 fields or fewer, express wallets first, address autocomplete on, shipping cost shown in the cart) and change the ones that fail** Score 46, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter and after a platform checkout update. Enablers: change-log.
  - Inputs: checkout settings and field list (connected, store platform); checkout step drop-off on mobile (connected, GA4); checkout ideas with steps (have, idea bank ideas/v1 05-SET and 09-CRO).
  - Steps: 1) Screenshot each checkout step on a phone 2) Tick each item on the list and note what fails 3) Pull mobile drop-off per step to see where shoppers quit 4) Change one setting at a time and log it in the change log 5) Read checkout to order rate 14 days before and after each change
  - Output: a checkout with guest on, fewer fields and wallets first, and a before and after read for each change. Next: feeds 07-17 test reads when traffic allows a real test.
  - Watch: checkout to order rate, mobile (30 days, store checkouts and orders), then orders, then revenue.
- **06-02 Compare orders per hour with the same hour over the last 4 weeks and flag any trading hour with none where 3 or more are normal, with an estimate of sales lost** Score 44, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every hour. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: orders with timestamps, 8 weeks (connected, store platform); ad spend by hour (connected, Meta Ads); average order value, 30 days (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the normal orders for each hour of each weekday from the last 4 weeks 2) Flag an hour with 0 orders where the normal is 3 or more, while ads are spending 3) Estimate lost sales in code: normal orders minus actual, times average order value 4) Send the alert with ad spend running in the same hour 5) Log each incident with cause once found
  - Output: an hourly zero-order alert and a monthly total of sales lost to gaps. Next: feeds 06-01 tuning and the 00-today morning line.
  - Watch: hours with no orders in normal trading hours (30 days, store orders), then revenue lost to downtime, then revenue.
- **06-04 Send a test order through every order, shipping and refund email each week, and a test message through the contact form and chat, and flag any that did not arrive** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and after any email, helpdesk or domain change. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: order, shipping and refund notification settings (connected, store platform); contact form and chat routing (connected, helpdesk); a test inbox on Gmail, Outlook and Yahoo (setup, Google Sheet plus free inboxes).
  - Steps: 1) Place a test order, mark it shipped, then refund it 2) Check each email arrived in the inbox, not spam, with working links and the right logo 3) Send a message through the contact form and chat and check it lands in the helpdesk 4) Flag any missing or broken message with the step 5) Fix and retest the same day
  - Output: a weekly pass or fail sheet for 6 emails and 2 contact paths. Next: failures feed 14-17 (sender domain health).
  - Watch: 'where is my order' tickets per 100 orders (14 days, helpdesk), then support tickets per 100 orders, then customer satisfaction.
### T2. When a developer, an agency or I change the theme, add an app or edit a setting, help me make the change on a copy, walk the buying flow and check the numbers the next morning, so I never lose a week of sales to one change

Metric: days a break stays live before it is found, per incident

- **06-07 The morning after any change, compare add to cart, checkout start, discount use and orders with the 14 day average for that weekday, and name the change if a step fell** Score 46, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every morning after a logged change. Enablers: change-log.
  - Inputs: the change list (enabler, change-log); add to cart and checkout start by page and device (connected, GA4); orders and discount code use (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Take yesterday's changes from the log 2) Compute each step rate against the same weekday average over 14 days, in code 3) Flag any step down 15 percent or more on the pages the change touched 4) Name the change and the person, with the rollback steps 5) Founder or developer rolls back or fixes the same day
  - Output: a morning note: 'add to cart on product pages fell 22 percent after the upsell app went in, roll back?'. Next: feeds 06-09 (rollback) and settles blame between ads and site in 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: add to cart rate and checkout start rate, day after change vs 14 day average (1 day, GA4 and store orders), then days a break stays live, then revenue.
- **06-08 Duplicate the live theme before every app install or theme edit, make the change on the copy, walk the full buying flow on a phone, and publish only when it passes** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before every theme edit or app install. Enablers: theme-twin, change-log.
  - Inputs: theme access and the duplicate theme feature (connected, store platform); a 12 step flow checklist: product, size, add to cart, cart drawer, discount, shipping, checkout, pay, emails (have, idea bank ideas/v1 05-SET); an iPhone and a mid-range Android (setup, team).
  - Steps: 1) Duplicate the live theme and name it with the date and change 2) Make the change on the copy only 3) Walk the 12 step flow on the copy on both phones, and run the same speed test on both themes 4) Fix anything the copy broke, then publish it 5) Keep the old theme for 14 days as the way back
  - Output: a change that went live only after passing the flow and speed check, with the old theme kept for rollback. Next: speed results feed 08-12; publishes feed 06-06 change log.
  - Watch: changes that broke cart, discount or checkout after publish, count (30 days, change-log), then days a break stays live, then revenue.
- **06-06 Log every theme edit, app install or removal, setting, price and discount change with who, when and what, from the store's activity feed and a form for outside developers** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on every change, always on. Enablers: change-log.
  - Inputs: store activity feed, app install and uninstall events, theme publish events (connected, store platform); a 3 field change form for agencies and developers (setup, Google Form); who works on the store (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the activity feed and app events into one dated list 2) Send outside developers the form: what changed, which pages, how to undo it 3) Match each change to a person 4) Mark changes that touch product pages, cart or checkout as high risk 5) Post a daily digest to the founder
  - Output: one dated change list with owner, pages touched, risk and the way back. Next: feeds 06-07 (morning after check) and 08-15 (speed budget check).
  - Watch: changes logged with an owner and a way back, share (30 days, change-log), then days a break stays live before it is found, then revenue.
- **06-09 Keep a nightly backup of the theme, products, collections and settings, and practise a rollback to yesterday once a quarter** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: nightly, and a quarterly drill. Enablers: theme-twin, change-log.
  - Inputs: theme, product, collection and settings data (connected, store platform); a backup app that restores products and themes (buy, a store backup app); who may run a restore (brain, decisions and goals shelf (approval rules)).
  - Steps: 1) Install the backup app and check it covers products, images, metafields, theme and pages 2) Confirm the nightly backup ran each morning 3) Once a quarter, restore one product and one theme file on a copy and time it 4) Write the 5 step restore guide and store it with the change log
  - Output: nightly backups, a tested restore time in minutes, and a written guide. Next: used by 06-07 when a change must be undone.
  - Watch: minutes to restore after a bad import or wipe (per incident, backup app log), then days lost to a bad import or click, then risk.
### T3. When app bills stack up and old app code stays in the theme after the app is gone, help me see which apps earn nothing and what they cost in fees and lost sales, so I cut both

Metric: monthly app bill, and seconds of load time added by apps on product pages

- **06-10 List every installed app with its monthly bill, when it was last used, which pages load its script, and the orders it touched in 90 days, and mark keep, replace or remove** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: app-audit.
  - Inputs: installed apps and app charges (connected, store platform); scripts and app embeds per page (connected, store platform theme code); orders or revenue each app reports (upsells, reviews, bundles) (connected, store platform and app dashboards); which apps the team still uses (founder, founder or ops lead).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the app list with monthly charges 2) Map each app to the pages its script loads on 3) Pull what each app touched in 90 days: orders, reviews shown, emails sent 4) Ask the team which apps they open each week 5) Mark keep, replace with a native feature, or remove, and total the monthly saving
  - Output: an app table: bill, pages, what it touched, and a keep, replace or remove call with the monthly saving. Next: feeds 06-11 (clean leftover code) and 08-07 (script list).
  - Watch: monthly app bill (30 days, store app charges), then operating cost per month and product page speed, then gross profit.
- **06-12 Put a dollar figure on each app: its bill plus the sales lost to the load time it adds, using our own conversion rate by page load time** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each quarter, and before any new app is approved. Enablers: app-audit.
  - Inputs: sessions with page load time and orders (setup, GA4 with a load time event); load time with and without each app on a theme copy (enabler, theme-twin); app bills (connected, store platform); average order value and sessions per page (connected, store platform and GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Bucket sessions by load time and compute conversion per bucket, in code 2) Measure each app's added seconds with a with and without test on a theme copy 3) Estimate lost sales per month: sessions times the conversion drop for those seconds times average order value 4) Add the monthly bill 5) Rank apps by total cost and show it next to the sales each app claims
  - Output: a ranked table: app, bill, seconds added, estimated sales lost per month, and sales it claims. Next: feeds 08-08 speed revenue model and the 21-finance-cash app line.
  - Watch: monthly cost of apps, bill plus estimated lost sales (quarterly, app-audit), then apps kept only where they earn more than they cost, then gross profit.
- **06-11 Search the theme for code left by apps that were removed, delete it on a theme copy, walk the buying flow, and measure the speed and sales before and after** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after each app removal, and once as a sweep at onboarding. Enablers: app-audit, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: apps removed in the last 2 years, from billing history (connected, store platform); theme code (connected, store platform); page speed on mobile before and after (setup, PageSpeed Insights API).
  - Steps: 1) List every app ever billed but no longer installed 2) Search the theme for their names, script domains and snippet files 3) Delete the leftovers on a theme copy 4) Walk the buying flow on the copy and run the same speed test on both themes 5) Publish if it passes and log seconds saved
  - Output: a clean theme with leftover app code removed, and seconds saved per page type. Next: feeds 06-12 (dollars per app) and 08-09.
  - Watch: mobile load time on product pages (7 days lab, 28 days field, PageSpeed Insights and Chrome field data), then conversion rate on product pages, then revenue.
### T4. When paid orders stall, stock is wrong or money leaks out after the sale, help me find them before the customer writes in, so I never forget a paid order or lose margin I cannot see

Metric: paid orders not shipped past the promised ship date, count, daily

- **06-13 List every paid order past its promised ship date each morning, with the reason: payment pending, fraud hold, address problem, failed warehouse sync or out of stock** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every morning. Enablers: order-watch.
  - Inputs: orders with payment, fulfilment status and tags (connected, store platform); promised ship days per product and market (brain, operations shelf); warehouse shipment confirmations (setup, warehouse or 3PL feed).
  - Steps: 1) Pull paid orders not fulfilled 2) Compare each with its promised ship date 3) Tag the reason from status, tags and the warehouse feed 4) Hide known preorders so the list stays real 5) Send the list to ops with the oldest first, and to support for a heads-up email
  - Output: a morning list of late paid orders with reason and age. Next: feeds 15-customers-cx heads-up emails to delayed buyers.
  - Watch: paid orders not shipped past the promised ship date, count (daily, store orders and fulfilments), then on time ship rate, then customer satisfaction.
- **06-14 Compare store stock with warehouse stock per SKU every day and stop selling any SKU where the store shows more than the warehouse holds** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: daily. Enablers: order-watch.
  - Inputs: inventory per SKU (connected, store platform); stock on hand per SKU (setup, warehouse or 3PL feed); SKUs allowed to sell on backorder (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull both stock counts per SKU 2) Flag SKUs where the store count is higher than the warehouse count 3) Skip SKUs allowed on backorder 4) Set the store count to the warehouse count or pause the SKU, with ops approval 5) Count orders cancelled for no stock each month
  - Output: a daily stock gap list and a monthly count of orders cancelled for no stock. Next: feeds 19-supply-inventory reorder alerts.
  - Watch: orders cancelled for no stock, count (30 days, store orders), then review rating and refunds, then customer satisfaction.
- **06-15 Put the three legs of reversed orders side by side each month (returns, return to origin where cash on delivery exists, chargebacks) with cost per leg and the top 3 causes each** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 1st of each month. Enablers: order-watch, margin-view.
  - Inputs: refunds and returns with reasons (connected, store platform); chargebacks and disputes (connected, store platform payments); return reasons from the returns app (setup, returns app); what a return or failed delivery really costs (founder, founder interview question 7).
  - Steps: 1) Count each leg and its value, computed in code; return to origin reads unavailable for US stores 2) Cost each leg: shipping both ways, restocking, write-offs, fees 3) List the top 3 causes per leg 4) Name the leg that cost most this month 5) Hand the top cause to its owner: product, CX, fraud or delivery
  - Output: a one page table: 3 legs, count, value, cost, top causes, owner. Next: fit causes feed 07-13 (fit finder) and 14-03 (fit email before delivery).
  - Watch: cost of reversed orders, share of revenue (30 days, store refunds and disputes), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **06-16 Hold orders that look like card testing or fraud before they ship, and answer the chargebacks we can win with proof of delivery within the deadline** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: daily for holds, and within 5 days of each chargeback. Enablers: order-watch.
  - Inputs: orders with fraud risk flags, IP, address and payment attempts (connected, store platform); chargebacks with reason and deadline (connected, store platform payments); tracking and proof of delivery (setup, shipping app or carrier).
  - Steps: 1) Flag bursts of small orders, many declines on one session, and billing and shipping far apart 2) Hold flagged orders and have ops approve or cancel within 24 hours 3) For each chargeback, pull tracking, delivery proof and the customer's messages 4) Answer the winnable ones before the deadline and log win or loss 5) Count chargebacks from confusion, like an unclear billing name, and fix the cause
  - Output: a daily hold list and a chargeback file per case, with a win rate per month. Next: confusion causes feed 15-customers-cx.
  - Watch: chargeback rate, share of orders (30 days, store platform payments), then payouts held and fees paid, then risk.
- **06-17 Find products live without a cost, weight or SKU, and the shipping zones and weights where what the shopper pays is below the label cost** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: weekly, and after any catalog import. Enablers: order-watch, margin-view.
  - Inputs: products with cost, weight and SKU fields (connected, store platform); shipping charged per order (connected, store platform); label cost per shipment (setup, shipping app).
  - Steps: 1) List live products missing cost, weight or SKU 2) Send the list to the catalog owner to fill 3) Match shipping charged with label cost per order 4) Group the loss by zone and weight band, computed in code 5) Propose a rate change or a free shipping threshold for the worst 3 bands
  - Output: a list of products to fix and a table of zones and weights that lose money on shipping. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing (free shipping threshold) and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: shipping loss per order (30 days, store orders and shipping app), then gross profit per order and trusted margin reports, then gross profit.
### T5. When a sale, a launch, a new country or a peak day is coming, help me prove the store is ready with real orders and timed checks, so I do not watch it fail on the best day

Metric: minutes from scheduled promo start to confirmed live, per promo

- **06-19 Two weeks before Black Friday or a drop, run a readiness list: freeze theme changes, test every discount stack, check the plan and app limits for 10 times traffic, confirm stock and carrier cutoffs, name who is on call** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 2 weeks before each peak or drop. Enablers: checkout-probe, theme-twin, change-log.
  - Inputs: peak dates and what went wrong last year (brain, calendar shelf); discount codes and automatic discounts (connected, store platform); stock per SKU and carrier cutoff dates (setup, store platform and shipping app); team on call (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Freeze theme and app changes from 7 days out, logged in the change log 2) Test every discount combination shoppers could stack 3) Check host and app limits for 10 times normal orders per hour 4) Confirm stock cover for the top 20 SKUs and the last ship date 5) Name who is on call each hour of the peak and share the rollback steps
  - Output: a signed readiness list with each item passed or fixed, and an on-call rota. Next: feeds 08-16 (peak speed test) and 14 peak send plan.
  - Watch: checkout errors and zero-order hours during the peak (peak days, checkout-probe and store orders), then peak revenue against plan, then revenue.
- **06-20 Before a launch, relaunch or new country, place real orders end to end per market (price, tax, shipping, payment, emails, refund) and fix every step that fails** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before each launch or new market. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: market settings per country (connected, store platform); per-market facts: currency, tax line, shipping promise, payment methods (brain, market shelf); a real card and a local address per market (founder, founder or ops lead).
  - Steps: 1) List each market and the payment methods shown there 2) Place a real order per market and per main payment method 3) Check price, tax, shipping cost, delivery promise and emails against the market shelf 4) Refund each order and check the refund email 5) Fix failures and rerun until all pass
  - Output: a pass sheet per market and payment method, signed before ads go live. Next: none.
  - Watch: failed or abandoned first orders in the new market (first 14 days, store checkouts), then revenue from the new market, then revenue.
- **06-18 Check at the minute a sale starts and ends that prices, codes, banners and scheduled emails changed as planned, and message the founder 'live' or what is not** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: at each scheduled promo start and end. Enablers: checkout-probe.
  - Inputs: scheduled discounts and price changes (connected, store platform); the promo plan: products, prices, codes, banner text, start and end (brain, offers shelf); scheduled campaign sends (setup, email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Read the plan and list every thing that should change 2) At start plus 5 minutes, open each product, apply each code and read each banner 3) Check the scheduled email went and links land on the sale 4) Message the founder 'live' or name what is not 5) Repeat at the end and confirm the sale is gone everywhere
  - Output: a live or not-live message at start and end, with each item ticked. Next: failures feed 06-06 (log the cause) and 05-16 (sweep ended offers).
  - Watch: minutes from scheduled promo start to confirmed live (per promo, checkout-probe), then promo day revenue against plan, then revenue.

## 07-landing-pages-cro Landing pages and CRO

### T1. When I am paying for clicks that do not buy, help me see from scroll depth, recordings, polls and GA4 the one block where shoppers stop and what it costs, so I fix that first instead of redesigning everything

Metric: conversion rate from paid sessions on the top 10 landing pages, mobile, 30 days

- **07-04 When conversion drops more than 20 percent in a day, check in order: tracking, traffic mix, device and browser, then the page, before anyone touches the design** Score 46, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when conversion drops 20 percent day over day. Enablers: behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: purchases in GA4 and orders in the store, by day (connected, GA4 and store platform); sessions by source and campaign (connected, GA4); ad spend and clicks by campaign (connected, Meta Ads); yesterday's store changes (connected, store platform activity log).
  - Steps: 1) Compare GA4 purchases with store orders; if only GA4 fell, it is tracking 2) Compare traffic share by source; a new cold campaign lowers the average 3) Split conversion by device and browser; one broken browser shows here 4) Only then check the page and the change log 5) Write the cause in 1 line with the numbers
  - Output: a 1 line cause with the numbers behind it, sent the same day. Next: tracking causes go to 22-measurement; page causes go to 06-07.
  - Watch: hours from the drop to a named cause (per incident, GA4 and store orders), then days lost to fixing the wrong thing, then revenue.
- **07-01 Install Clarity on every money page, tag sessions by ad campaign and device, and show how far down each of the top 10 paid landing pages shoppers scroll, block by block** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then read every Monday. Enablers: behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: Clarity (free) installed through the theme with consent (setup, Microsoft Clarity); top 10 landing pages by paid sessions (connected, GA4); campaign and ad names per URL (connected, Meta Ads); page blocks in order: hero, price, proof, reviews, FAQ, footer (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Install Clarity and pass campaign and device as custom tags 2) Wait for 7 days or 1,000 sessions per page, whichever is later 3) For each page, compute the share of mobile shoppers who reach each block 4) Mark where the buy button, the reviews and the guarantee sit on that curve 5) Write 1 line per page, like '64 percent never reach the reviews; the guarantee sits below them'
  - Output: a scroll table per page and device: share reaching each block, with the 1 line finding. Next: feeds 07-02 (fix the stop block) and 07-03 (recordings to watch).
  - Watch: share of mobile paid sessions reaching the first proof block (7 days, Clarity), then product page conversion rate, mobile, then revenue.
- **07-02 Find the one block on each money page where most mobile shoppers stop, move the proof, price or buy button above it on a page copy, and test it** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every 2 weeks, one page at a time. Enablers: behaviour-map, theme-twin, test-reader.
  - Inputs: scroll table per page (enabler, behaviour-map); 20 recordings of shoppers who stopped at that block (setup, Microsoft Clarity); theme copy to build on (connected, store platform); sessions and orders per page (connected, GA4 and store orders).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the page with the most paid sessions lost at one block 2) Watch 20 recordings at that block and write what shoppers do there 3) Draft the move: which block goes up, which goes down 4) Build it on a theme copy and walk the buying flow on a phone 5) Launch as a test with a set run time
  - Output: one fixed page per 2 weeks, each with the evidence and a running test. Next: results go to 07-19 (standard or fail note).
  - Watch: add to cart rate on the changed page, mobile (14 days, GA4), then product page conversion rate, then revenue.
- **07-03 Watch 30 recordings of paid mobile shoppers who left the product page, tag each with a reason (rage click, dead click, size doubt, price shock, slow load, popup in the way) and count them** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every 2 weeks. Enablers: behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: recordings filtered to paid, mobile, left without add to cart (setup, Microsoft Clarity); a fixed reason list with 1 example each (enabler, behaviour-map); popup, chat and cookie bar settings (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Filter recordings to paid mobile sessions that left the product page 2) Watch 30 and tag each with 1 main reason 3) Count reasons, and stop early if the same 2 reasons repeat 4) Screenshot 3 clear examples per top reason 5) Hand the top reason to its owner: page, speed, popup or offer
