  - Output: a ranked reason list with counts and screenshots, in place of evenings spent watching. Next: popup findings go to 07-02; slow load goes to 08-17; doubts go to 07-11.
  - Watch: the top reason's share of tagged exits (14 days, Clarity recording tags), then product page conversion rate, mobile, then revenue.
- **07-05 Ask one question on the product page ('What stopped you today?') and one after purchase ('What nearly stopped you?'), and code 200 answers into themes** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: always on, read monthly. Enablers: behaviour-map, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: an on-page poll tool (setup, Clarity or a free poll app); a post-purchase survey (setup, post-purchase survey app); theme list from the objection ladder (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Set the page poll to show after 30 seconds or on exit, on mobile product pages only 2) Add the post-purchase question with an open text box 3) Collect 200 answers 4) Code each answer to a theme and count them in code 5) Put the top 5 themes next to the ticket themes from 07-11
  - Output: a ranked list of stopping reasons in shoppers' words, from both sides of the purchase. Next: feeds 07-11 (coverage matrix) and 07-16 (idea scoring evidence).
  - Watch: answers collected (30 days, poll and survey tools), then product page conversion rate, then revenue.
### T2. When a shopper taps an ad and lands on a page that says something else, help me make the page keep the ad's promise, starting with the ads that spend most, so I stop paying for clicks that leave feeling tricked

Metric: ad to page match score on the top 20 ads by spend, and bounce rate from paid clicks

- **07-07 Fix the landing page for the top 5 ads by spend first: put the ad's hook in the headline, keep the offer and price the same, reuse the ad's first frame, and ship each on a page copy** Score 42, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every 2 weeks, top 5 ads by spend. Enablers: congruence-check, theme-twin, page-library.
  - Inputs: ranked mismatch table (enabler, congruence-check); the ad's hook, offer, first frame and media (connected, Meta Ads); allowed claims and offer rules (brain, claims and offers shelves); page templates and blocks (enabler, page-library).
  - Steps: 1) Take the top 5 ads with the lowest match score 2) Write the page headline from the ad's hook, word for word where it fits 3) Match offer, price and guarantee exactly from ad to page to cart 4) Use the ad's first frame or product shot as the hero image 5) Build on a page copy, point that ad to it, and read for 14 days against the old page 6) Write what the best ad and page pairs share as a rule for new pages
  - Output: 5 fixed pages, each tied to its ad, with a 14 day read, and a rule on what the best pairs share. Next: wins go to 07-19 (page standard); the rule feeds 09-creative briefs.
  - Watch: conversion rate from the ad's clicks (14 days, GA4 and Meta Ads), then cost per purchase on the top 5 ads, then revenue.
- **07-06 Rank ads by 30 day spend, pair each with its landing page, and score the match 1 to 5 on promise, offer, price, visual and awareness level** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every Monday after the ad pull. Enablers: congruence-check, awareness-map, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: ads with destination URL, spend, clicks and purchases (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); landing page text and a mobile screenshot of the first screen (connected, store platform); hook, offer and awareness tags per ad (enabler, creative-tags); a scoring rubric with 2 examples per score (enabler, congruence-check).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the top 20 ads by spend with their landing URLs 2) Screenshot the first mobile screen of each page 3) Score promise, offer, price, visual and awareness match from 1 to 5 each 4) Spot check 5 scores by hand and fix the rubric where it is wrong 5) Sort by spend times the gap, so a big-spend ad with a weak page comes first
  - Output: a table: ad, spend, page, 5 match scores, and the line that breaks the promise. Next: feeds 07-07 (fix pages for the top ads).
  - Watch: ad to page match score, spend weighted, top 20 ads (weekly, congruence-check), then bounce rate and conversion rate from paid clicks, then revenue.
- **07-08 When a new ad angle starts winning, build a page within 5 days that continues its story from the library blocks, and send only that angle's ads to it** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when a new angle beats the account average on cost per purchase for 7 days. Enablers: page-library, congruence-check.
  - Inputs: the winning angle, its ads and its tags (enabler, creative-tags); page blocks the store can build (enabler, page-library); proof and claims for that angle (brain, messaging and claims shelves); a page builder or theme access (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Write a 1 page brief: angle, avatar, awareness level, offer, the doubts to answer 2) Assemble the page from library blocks in the order the brief sets 3) Walk it on a phone and check every claim against the claims shelf 4) Point only that angle's ads to it 5) Read cost per purchase for 14 days against the old page
  - Output: a live angle page in 5 days, with its ads pointed to it and a 14 day read. Next: feeds 07-10 (page library entry) and 11-paid-social scaling.
  - Watch: cost per purchase on the angle's ads (14 days, Meta Ads and store orders), then new customers from the winning angle, then revenue.
- **07-09 Send ad clicks to a page that fits their awareness: unaware and problem aware clicks to a story or advertorial page, product aware and most aware clicks to the product page with the offer up top** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once to set up, then when a new top of funnel ad launches. Enablers: awareness-map, congruence-check, page-library.
  - Inputs: awareness level tag per ad (enabler, awareness-map); landing URL per ad (connected, Meta Ads); a story or advertorial page template (enabler, page-library); founder story, maker's choices and proof (brain, messaging shelf (story bank)).
  - Steps: 1) List ads by awareness level and where each lands today 2) Flag unaware and problem aware ads landing on a bare product page 3) Build one story page for the top cold angle: problem, why old fixes fail, how ours works, proof, offer 4) Point the cold ads to it and keep warm ads on the product page 5) Read new customer cost for each group over 28 days
  - Output: a routing table by awareness level and one live story page for cold traffic. Next: feeds 11-paid-social top of funnel budget and 09-creative briefs.
  - Watch: cost per new customer from unaware and problem aware ads (28 days, Meta Ads and store orders), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **07-10 List every landing page that got paid clicks in the last 90 days with its ads, sessions, conversion and stock status, and mark each keep, fix or retire** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: page-library.
  - Inputs: landing pages by paid sessions, 90 days (connected, GA4); ads and their destination URLs (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); stock per product on each page (connected, store platform); orders with product cost (founder, store platform plus founder unit costs).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every landing page with paid sessions 2) Join each to its ads, conversion and profit per visitor, computed in code 3) Flag pages with sold-out products or 404s still getting clicks 4) Mark keep, fix or retire and pick where each retired page's ads should go 5) Redirect retired pages and update the ads
  - Output: a page library with keep, fix or retire on each page and no paid click to a dead page. Next: feeds 07-06 (pairs to score) and 11-paid-social URL fixes.
  - Watch: paid clicks landing on sold-out or retired pages, count (7 days, GA4 and Meta Ads), then wasted ad spend, then revenue.
### T3. When the same questions and doubts stop shoppers, help me answer each one on the page, next to the choice it blocks, in the customer's own words, so I get fewer tickets and more buyers

Metric: pre-sale tickets per 1,000 sessions, and product page conversion rate

- **07-12 Write answers for the top 10 blank cells in the customer's own words and put each next to the choice it blocks: fit under the size picker, delivery date under the price, returns next to the buy button** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly, after the matrix refresh. Enablers: question-matrix, review-miner.
  - Inputs: ranked blank cells (enabler, question-matrix); customer phrases for each doubt (brain, customers shelf (language bank)); true delivery days and return policy (brain, operations and offers shelves); theme copy (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Take the top 10 blanks 2) Write each answer in 1 or 2 short lines, using a customer phrase where one fits 3) Place each next to the choice it blocks, not in a FAQ at the bottom 4) Check each answer against policies and claims 5) Ship on a theme copy and read tickets and add to cart for 28 days
  - Output: 10 answer blocks live beside the choices they unblock. Next: feeds 07-19 (standard if it wins).
  - Watch: pre-sale tickets on those questions, count (28 days, helpdesk), then product page conversion rate, then revenue.
- **07-11 Pull every pre-sale question from tickets, chat, reviews, site search and Amazon questions, map each to the page block that should answer it, and rank the blank cells by how often it is asked** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: question-matrix.
  - Inputs: pre-sale tickets and chats, 12 months (connected, helpdesk); reviews with text (connected, reviews app); site search terms (connected, GA4 or store search); Amazon customer questions, if they sell there (setup, Amazon Seller Central); text of product pages, FAQ and policy pages (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every question and group near-duplicates into one 2) Count how often each is asked, by product 3) Check each page and FAQ for an answer and mark answered, weak or blank 4) Rank blanks by count times the traffic of the page that should answer 5) Spot check 20 matches by hand
  - Output: a coverage matrix: questions by page, with blanks ranked by how much they cost. Next: feeds 07-12 (write the answers), 14-10 (answers in emails) and 17-seo article briefs.
  - Watch: top 20 questions answered on the page they belong to, share (30 days, question-matrix), then pre-sale tickets per 1,000 sessions, then revenue.
- **07-13 Add a fit helper on size-dependent products built from return reasons and reviews that mention fit, and show 'runs small' or 'runs large' notes where reviews say so** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once per sized product, then quarterly. Enablers: question-matrix, review-miner.
  - Inputs: return reasons by product and size (setup, returns app); reviews that mention fit or size (connected, reviews app); size chart and measurements (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Count returns for fit by product and size 2) Pull review lines about fit and group them: runs small, true, runs large 3) Write a 2 question fit helper (usual size, fit preference) from the chart 4) Show the fit note under the size picker 5) Read fit returns and conversion for 60 days
  - Output: a fit helper and fit notes on each sized product. Next: feeds 14-03 (fit email before delivery).
  - Watch: returns for fit, share of orders (60 days, returns app and store orders), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **07-14 Give a new product page enough honest proof before reviews arrive: the maker's reason for each choice, a real test result, reviews of the sibling product labeled as such, and the guarantee** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 2 weeks before each launch. Enablers: brain-graph, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: product facts, test results and proof (brain, products and claims shelves); reviews of the closest existing product (connected, reviews app); questions shoppers asked about the sibling product (enabler, question-matrix).
  - Steps: 1) List what a first buyer doubts, from the sibling product's questions 2) Write one proof line per doubt from the products and claims shelves 3) Add sibling reviews with a clear label, never as reviews of the new product 4) Put the guarantee next to the buy button 5) Ask the first 50 buyers for a review 5 days after delivery
  - Output: a launch page with proof per doubt and a review ask set up. Next: feeds 14 launch sends.
  - Watch: conversion rate on the new product page, first 30 days (30 days, GA4), then launch revenue, then revenue.
- **07-15 Turn the 'which one should I get' tickets into a 3 question chooser on the collection page that ends on one product** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when the range changes. Enablers: question-matrix.
  - Inputs: 'which one' tickets and chats with the answer support gave (connected, helpdesk); products with what each is for and who it is for (brain, products shelf); a quiz or chooser block (setup, store platform or a quiz app).
  - Steps: 1) Read 50 'which one' tickets and list the questions support asks back 2) Keep the 3 questions that split the range best 3) Map each answer path to one product with a 1 line reason 4) Build the chooser on the collection page and log every path 5) Read chooser completion and orders from chooser users against others
  - Output: a live chooser with every path ending on one product and a reason. Next: path data feeds 02-customers-avatars.
  - Watch: 'which one' tickets per 1,000 sessions (30 days, helpdesk), then collection page to product view rate, then revenue.
### T4. When a test is proposed or called a winner, help me rank ideas by money and evidence, read results on profit per visitor, and promote only real wins to the page standard, so I stop rolling out wins that fade or lose money

Metric: shipped page changes with a measured profit per visitor gain, share

- **07-20 Rewrite the pack picker so the bigger pack shows its saving in dollars and per unit, preselect the pack most buyers should take, and test it against today's picker** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once per product with packs, then when prices change. Enablers: test-reader.
  - Inputs: orders by pack size and product (connected, store platform); margin per pack (brain, finance shelf (unit economics)); pack and discount rules (brain, offers shelf); theme copy (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the share of orders per pack and the margin of each, in code 2) Write the saving in dollars and per unit for each pack 3) Preselect the pack with the best margin that buyers also pick often 4) Test against today's picker for whole weeks 5) Read profit per visitor and units per order
  - Output: a new pack picker in test, with the saving in dollars on every pack. Next: feeds 07-19 and 04-offers-pricing.
  - Watch: units per order (28 days, store orders), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **07-16 Score every test idea on one sheet (money at stake on that page, evidence from recordings, polls and tickets, ease, and whether traffic can power it) and run the top one** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every 2 weeks. Enablers: test-reader, behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: test ideas with steps (have, idea bank ideas/v1 07-LPG and 09-CRO); sessions, orders and profit per visitor per page (connected, GA4 and store orders); evidence from scroll, recordings and polls (enabler, behaviour-map); past wins and fails (enabler, win-loss-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) List ideas from the team, the idea bank and the evidence 2) Drop ideas already logged as a fail 3) Score money at stake, evidence, ease and traffic, the same way for everyone 4) Check the page has the traffic for a test in 4 weeks or less; if not, send it to 07-18 5) Run the top idea and park the rest in rank order
  - Output: a ranked test list with scores and the reason for each, and one test running. Next: feeds 07-17 (set up and read the test).
  - Watch: share of tests that win (90 days, win-loss-ledger), then profit per visitor, then gross profit.
- **07-19 Promote every page test winner to the page standard (T0) that new pages start from, write a fail note for every loser, and recheck each standard after 90 days and whether its buyers came back** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after every test read, and on each recheck date. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, test-reader.
  - Inputs: test reads (enabler, test-reader); repeat orders of buyers from each version, 60 to 90 days (connected, store platform); the page standard list (brain, history shelf (experiment registry)).
  - Steps: 1) For a win, write the standard in 1 line with the number and date 2) For a loss, write the fail note with why and 'do not rerun unless' 3) At 60 days, check whether buyers from the winning version reordered as often 4) Add standards to the page brief and the library blocks 5) On each recheck date, retest or mark the standard expired
  - Output: a page standard list with proof and recheck dates, and a fail list. Next: feeds 07-16 (drop repeat fails), 07-08 (new pages start from T0) and 05-09.
  - Watch: new pages built from the current standard, share (90 days, page-library), then profit per visitor on new pages, then gross profit.
- **07-17 Before a test starts, set the sample size, the run time in whole weeks and profit per visitor as the main number; after it ends, read it with a daily plot so a 5 day spike is not called a win** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: at the start and end of every test. Enablers: test-reader.
  - Inputs: sessions and orders on the tested page (connected, GA4 and store orders); product cost per order (brain, finance shelf (unit economics)); a test tool or a theme split (buy, an A/B test app); promo and holiday dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the sample size and weeks needed from weekly traffic, in code 2) Check the calendar for sales or holidays inside the window 3) Run whole weeks only, and do not peek to stop early 4) Read profit per visitor, then conversion and order value, with a daily plot 5) Call win, loss or no difference, with the numbers
  - Output: a test plan before and a 1 page read after, on profit per visitor. Next: feeds 07-19 (standard or fail note).
  - Watch: tests stopped before their planned end, count (90 days, test-reader), then profit per visitor, then gross profit.
- **07-18 When traffic is too low to test, ship the change and read an honest before and after: same weekdays, same traffic mix, 4 weeks each side, with the caveat printed** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: for each shipped change on a page with too little traffic to test. Enablers: test-reader.
  - Inputs: sessions, orders and traffic mix by day (connected, GA4 and store orders); the change and its date (connected, store platform activity log); ad spend by campaign (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Record the ship date and what changed 2) Wait 4 weeks 3) Compare the same weekdays before and after, split by paid and organic 4) Flag anything else that moved: a new campaign, a sale, a stock-out 5) Write the read with a plain caveat: 'likely helped, not proven'
  - Output: a before and after read with the caveat, filed with the change. Next: feeds 07-19 as a soft result.
  - Watch: conversion rate on the changed page, same weekdays (4 weeks each side, GA4), then profit per visitor, then gross profit.

## 08-site-speed Site and speed

### T1. When a shopper taps our ad on a phone on a slow connection and waits, help me get the product, price and buy button on screen in under 2.5 seconds on the pages that sell, so I stop paying for clicks that leave before they see what they came for

Metric: share of mobile visits with the main image and price painted in under 2.5 seconds (field LCP p75) on the top 10 paid landing pages, 28 days

- **08-03 Make the product image, price and buy button the first things to paint on mobile product pages: preload the main image at phone size, stop lazy loading it, and show text while fonts load** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once per template, then on each theme change. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: the element that paints last per product template (enabler, speed-lab); theme code and a theme copy (connected, store platform); speed fix steps (have, idea bank ideas/v1 08-SPD).
  - Steps: 1) Find the main image on the product template and preload it at phone width 2) Remove lazy loading from the first image only 3) Set fonts to show fallback text at once 4) Test on the theme copy: same 3 lab runs, same phone profile 5) Merge if the image and price paint sooner and nothing else broke
  - Output: a product template that paints image and price first, with the before and after. Next: result logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: field LCP p75 on product pages, mobile (28 days, Chrome field data), then conversion rate from paid mobile sessions, then revenue.
- **08-01 Measure real mobile load for home, 2 collections, the top 5 product pages, the top ad landing pages, cart and checkout, from field data and a mid-range phone on 4G, and save a dated baseline with filmstrips** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then the 1st of each month. Enablers: speed-lab.
  - Inputs: field data per page: main image paint (LCP), tap response (INP), layout shift (CLS), 28 days (setup, PageSpeed Insights and Chrome field data API); pages ranked by paid and organic sessions (connected, GA4); top ad landing URLs (connected, Meta Ads); a lab tool with a mid-range Android profile on 4G (setup, WebPageTest or PageSpeed lab).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the pages by sessions and by ad spend, not by what is easy 2) Pull 28 day field data for each, on mobile 3) Run 3 lab runs per page on a mid-range phone profile and keep the middle one 4) Save the filmstrip: what the shopper sees at 1, 2, 3 and 5 seconds 5) Write the baseline table with the date and the element that paints last
  - Output: a dated baseline table per page with field and lab numbers and a filmstrip each. Next: feeds 08-08 (revenue model), 08-17 (rank fixes) and every before and after in 08-14.
  - Watch: pages with a dated baseline, share of pages that get paid traffic (1 day, speed-lab), then decisions on speed made from our numbers, then revenue.
- **08-02 Open the top 5 ad landing pages inside the Instagram and Facebook in-app browser on a mid-range phone, film the load, and compare it with Chrome and Safari** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then after each theme change. Enablers: speed-lab.
  - Inputs: top 5 ads by spend with their URLs (connected, Meta Ads); share of sessions from in-app browsers (connected, GA4); a mid-range Android and an iPhone (setup, team).
  - Steps: 1) Send each ad preview to both phones and tap through inside the app 2) Screen record the load and note seconds to image, price and buy button 3) Repeat in Chrome and Safari for the same page 4) List what loads slower in the app: fonts, video, pop-ups, scripts that block 5) Hand the top gap to 08-03 as the first fix
  - Output: a side by side of in-app vs browser load for 5 pages, with the worst gap named. Next: feeds 08-03 and 08-17.
  - Watch: seconds to price on screen in the Instagram in-app browser (7 days, screen recordings), then conversion rate from Meta clicks, then revenue.
- **08-05 Resize and compress every product and banner image over 300 KB, and set a rule that blocks new uploads over that size without making the product look worse** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once as a sweep, then on every upload. Enablers: speed-lab.
  - Inputs: every image with its file size and where it shows (connected, store platform); photo rules and approved product shots (brain, content and brand kit shelf); an image compression app or CDN setting (setup, image app).
  - Steps: 1) List images over 300 KB by page views 2) Resize to the largest size shown and compress, keeping the original 3) Compare 5 products side by side on a phone with the founder 4) Turn on compression for new uploads, including banners and blog images 5) Check the list monthly for new heavy images
  - Output: a catalog with no image over 300 KB on money pages, and a rule for new uploads. Next: logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: total image weight on the top 10 pages (7 days, speed-lab), then field LCP on product and collection pages, then revenue.
- **08-06 Make add to cart, the size picker and the cart drawer answer a tap in under 200 milliseconds on a mid-range phone, by finding the scripts that block the tap** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then after any app that touches the product form or cart. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: field tap response (INP) per page (setup, PageSpeed Insights and Chrome field data API); a performance trace of tapping size and add to cart (setup, Chrome DevTools on a phone profile); apps that hook into the product form or cart (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Record a trace of picking a size and tapping add to cart 2) List the scripts that run on the tap and how long each takes 3) Delay or remove the ones that are not needed for the tap, on a theme copy 4) Retest the tap and walk the buying flow 5) Merge if the tap answers in under 200 milliseconds and cart still works
  - Output: a product form and cart that respond at once, with the scripts that blocked them named. Next: logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: field INP p75 on product pages, mobile (28 days, Chrome field data), then add to cart rate, mobile, then revenue.
- **08-04 Stop the page jumping: reserve space for review stars, banners, the sticky cart and app blocks, and measure layout shift before and after** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once per template. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: layout shift per page and the element that moves (enabler, speed-lab); theme code and a theme copy (connected, store platform); recordings with misclicks near moving blocks (setup, Microsoft Clarity).
  - Steps: 1) List the blocks that load late and push content down 2) Set a fixed height for each on the theme copy 3) Run the same tests on both themes 4) Watch 10 recordings for misclicks after the change 5) Merge if layout shift drops under 0.1 and nothing broke
  - Output: templates with no visible jump, and layout shift under 0.1 on product pages. Next: logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: field CLS p75 on product pages, mobile (28 days, Chrome field data), then add to cart rate, then revenue.
### T2. When apps, scripts and old code pile up on the pages, help me cut what earns nothing and put a dollar figure on what stays, so I pay for speed only where it makes money

Metric: script weight and main thread blocking time on product pages, and what slowness costs per month in our own dollars

- **08-10 Delay chat, pop-up, reviews carousel and non-purchase tags until the shopper scrolls or taps, and keep any that sell after a with and without test** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then when a widget is added. Enablers: app-audit, theme-twin, speed-lab.
  - Inputs: widgets and tags on product pages (enabler, app-audit); orders or leads each widget brings (connected, store platform and app dashboards); theme copy (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) List widgets that load before the product: chat, pop-up, reviews carousel, tags 2) Set each to load on first scroll or tap on the theme copy 3) For widgets that claim sales, like reviews, run a with and without test 4) Keep the widget if its sales beat the speed cost from the model 5) Merge and log
  - Output: a product page where the product loads first, and a keep or cut call on each widget with its numbers. Next: logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: main thread blocking time on product pages (7 days, speed-lab), then field LCP and INP on product pages, then revenue.
- **08-08 Build our own speed revenue model: conversion rate by load time from our sessions, then what 1 second faster on money pages is worth per month in dollars** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: speed-lab, app-audit.
  - Inputs: sessions with a load time event and orders (setup, GA4 with a load time event); sessions per page type, paid and organic (connected, GA4); average order value and margin (brain, store platform and finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Add a load time event to GA4 if missing and wait 14 days 2) Bucket sessions by load time and compute conversion per bucket, in code 3) Fit the drop in conversion per extra second 4) Multiply by sessions per page type and gross profit per order 5) Write the dollar figure per second per page type, with the range
  - Output: a table: dollars per month per second saved, by page type, from our own data. Next: feeds 08-17 (rank fixes), 06-12 (dollars per app) and 08-11 (cost of a new app).
  - Watch: model built with 14 days of our own data (14 days, GA4), then speed budget spent where it pays, then gross profit.
- **08-07 List every script that loads on product pages, the app or tag it belongs to, its size and blocking time, and whether that app is still installed and used** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: app-audit, speed-lab.
  - Inputs: scripts per page with size and blocking time (enabler, speed-lab); installed apps and billing history (connected, store platform); theme code with app snippets (connected, store platform); which apps the team uses (founder, founder or ops lead).
  - Steps: 1) Record every script request on the product page 2) Match each to an app, a tag or the theme 3) Mark scripts from apps no longer installed as leftovers 4) Rank by blocking time 5) Hand leftovers to 08-09 and unused apps to 06-10
  - Output: a script table: owner, size, blocking time, still used or leftover. Next: feeds 08-09, 08-10 and 06-10.
  - Watch: third party script weight on product pages (7 days, speed-lab), then field LCP and INP on product pages, then revenue.
- **08-09 Remove leftover code from uninstalled apps and last season's banners, countdowns and gift apps on a theme copy, and log the seconds and dollars saved** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after each app removal, and after every peak season. Enablers: app-audit, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: leftover scripts and snippets (enabler, app-audit); seasonal apps and banners still live (connected, store platform); dollars per second by page type (enabler, speed-lab).
  - Steps: 1) List leftovers and seasonal extras still loading 2) Remove them on a theme copy 3) Run the same speed tests on both themes and walk the buying flow 4) Publish if it passes 5) Log seconds saved and dollars per month from the model
  - Output: a lighter theme and a line in the log: 'removed 4 dead apps, 0.6 seconds, about $1,900 a month'. Next: logged in 08-14.
  - Watch: mobile load time on product pages (7 days lab, 28 days field, speed-lab), then conversion rate on product pages, then revenue.
- **08-11 Before any new app, page-builder page or autoplay video goes live, test its speed cost on a theme copy and show the founder the dollars at stake** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: before each new app, page-builder page or hero video. Enablers: speed-lab, app-audit, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: the app, page or video to add (founder, the requester); theme copy (connected, store platform); dollars per second by page type (enabler, speed-lab).
  - Steps: 1) Add it to a theme copy only 2) Run the same tests on both themes 3) Convert the added seconds to dollars per month 4) Show the founder: what it adds, what it costs, a lighter option 5) Log the go or no-go decision
  - Output: a go or no-go with the speed cost in dollars, before anything reaches the live theme. Next: go decisions feed 08-15 (budget check).
  - Watch: new apps or pages added without a speed test, count (90 days, store platform activity log), then mobile load time on money pages, then revenue.
### T3. When anyone ships a speed fix or a site change, help me test it on a copy of the theme, check tracking and the buying flow, and merge only if it wins, so I never lose sales to a speed fix

Metric: speed changes with a measured before and after and no break after publish, share

- **08-12 Duplicate the live theme, apply one speed change to the copy, run the same speed tests on both themes 3 times, walk the buying flow on the copy, and merge only if the copy wins on both** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: for every speed change. Enablers: theme-twin, speed-lab.
  - Inputs: theme access and the duplicate theme feature (connected, store platform); the same page list and lab settings as the baseline (enabler, speed-lab); a buying flow checklist: product, size, add to cart, cart, discount, checkout (have, idea bank ideas/v1 05-SET).
  - Steps: 1) Duplicate the live theme and name it with the date and change 2) Make one change on the copy 3) Run 3 lab runs on each theme for each page, same phone and network 4) Walk the buying flow on the copy on 2 phones 5) Merge only if the copy is faster and the flow passes; keep the old theme for 14 days
  - Output: a merged change with a side by side table, or a rejected change with the reason. Next: feeds 08-13 (tracking check) and 08-14 (log).
  - Watch: speed changes that broke cart, reviews or size picker after publish, count (90 days, change-log), then days lost to a speed fix gone wrong, then risk.
- **08-13 After every speed change, check the Meta pixel, Conversions API and GA4 still record add to cart and purchase, with a test order and 3 days of counts against store orders** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after every speed or script change. Enablers: event-check.
  - Inputs: purchases and add to carts by day (connected, Meta Ads (Events Manager) and GA4); orders by day (connected, store platform); the change and its date (connected, store platform activity log).
  - Steps: 1) Place a test order on the live theme right after the merge 2) Check the test order shows in Meta and GA4 within 1 hour 3) For 3 days, compare Meta and GA4 purchases with store orders 4) Flag a gap over 10 percent and roll back the script change if it caused it 5) Log the date tracking was confirmed
  - Output: a pass or fail on tracking for each change, with a date. Next: failures go to 22-measurement and back to 08-12 for rollback.
  - Watch: GA4 and Meta purchases as a share of store orders (3 days, event-check), then cost per purchase that Meta optimises on, then risk.
- **08-16 Two weeks before a peak or big drop, load test the top pages at 10 times normal traffic, turn off nonessential apps for the day, and have the host plan and rollback ready** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: 2 weeks before each peak or drop. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: peak dates (brain, calendar shelf); normal and last peak's sessions per hour (connected, GA4); a load test tool (buy, a load test service); host or platform plan limits (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Take last peak's busiest hour and multiply by 3 to 10 for the target 2) Load test the home, top product and cart pages on a theme copy 3) List apps to switch off for the day and switch them off on the copy 4) Confirm the plan limits and who to call at the host 5) Publish the peak theme 2 days before and keep the normal one ready
  - Output: a peak theme tested at target traffic, and a named rollback. Next: links to 06-19 (readiness list).
  - Watch: server response time and mobile load in the peak hour (peak days, speed-lab), then peak revenue against plan, then revenue.
- **08-15 Set a speed budget per page type (image weight, script weight, main image paint time) and check every theme or app change against it the same day** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once to set, then on every logged change. Enablers: speed-lab, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: baseline per page type (enabler, speed-lab); store changes with date and owner (connected, store platform activity log); the speed standard list (brain, audits shelf (speed audit)).
  - Steps: 1) Set the budget per page type from the baseline and the standard 2) On each change, run the lab test on the affected page types 3) Flag any change that breaks the budget, with the owner 4) Owner fixes or rolls back within 2 days 5) Report budget breaks monthly
  - Output: a budget per page type and a same-day flag on any change that breaks it. Next: feeds 06-07 and 08-12.
  - Watch: changes that broke the speed budget, count (30 days, speed-lab), then field LCP on money pages, then revenue.
- **08-14 Log every speed change with a same-day lab before and after and a 28 day field read, mark it kept, reverted or no effect, and promote what worked to the speed standard (T0)** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at each merge, then 28 days later. Enablers: speed-lab, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: baseline and after runs (enabler, speed-lab); field data 28 days after (setup, PageSpeed Insights and Chrome field data API); conversion by page type before and after (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Write the change, the page type and the lab before and after on merge day 2) At 28 days, pull field data and conversion for the page type 3) Mark kept, reverted or no effect 4) Promote a kept fix to the speed standard every new page must meet 5) Write a fail note for fixes that moved the score but not real shoppers
  - Output: a speed log with before, after and a verdict per change, and a speed standard list. Next: feeds 08-15 (budget) and 08-19 (judge agency claims).
  - Watch: fixes with a field read at 28 days, share (28 days, speed-lab), then dollars spent on fixes that did nothing, then gross profit.
### T4. When I pay a developer or an agency for speed, help me brief only the fixes that change what shoppers see on the pages that sell, so I pay for results, not scores

Metric: dollars spent on speed per second saved on money pages, field data

- **08-17 Rank speed fixes by money: sessions on the page type times seconds the fix saves times dollars per second, and hand the top 3 to the developer** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 1st of each month. Enablers: speed-lab, app-audit.
  - Inputs: candidate fixes with estimated seconds saved (enabler, speed-lab); dollars per second by page type (enabler, speed-lab); developer hours available (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) List fixes from the baseline, the script table and the idea bank 2) Estimate seconds saved per fix on its page type 3) Compute dollars per month per fix, in code 4) Divide by developer hours to get dollars per hour of work 5) Pick the top 3 that fit the hours
  - Output: a ranked fix list with dollars per month and per hour of work. Next: feeds 08-18 (developer briefs).
  - Watch: dollars saved per developer hour, estimated (per month, speed-lab), then field LCP on the pages that sell, then revenue.
- **08-18 Write each developer brief with the page, the change, the target number on a mid-range phone, the test that proves it, the tracking check and the way back** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: for each fix in the top 3. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: the fix and its page type (enabler, speed-lab); baseline numbers and filmstrip (enabler, speed-lab); who the developer is and their rate (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Name the page, the element and the change in plain words 2) Set the target: for example, main image painted by 2.5 seconds on a Moto G profile 3) Say the test: 3 lab runs on both themes, then 28 days of field data 4) Say the checks: buying flow and tracking (08-13) 5) Say the way back: the theme copy stays for 14 days
  - Output: a 1 page brief per fix that pays for a result, not for hours. Next: done fixes go to 08-12 and 08-14.
  - Watch: briefed fixes that hit their target, share (28 days, speed-lab), then dollars spent per second saved, then gross profit.
- **08-19 Review an agency speed quote line by line and mark each fix as changes what shoppers see, score only, or skip, using our field data and page types** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when a speed quote arrives. Enablers: speed-lab.
  - Inputs: the agency quote with each fix and price (founder, founder); field and lab data per page type (enabler, speed-lab); pages that already pass (enabler, speed-lab).
  - Steps: 1) List each quoted fix with its price 2) Check which page type it touches and whether that page already passes in field data 3) Mark changes what shoppers see, score only, or skip 4) Price the kept fixes against dollars per second 5) Send the agency the trimmed scope with the target numbers
  - Output: a trimmed quote with each line marked and the dollars saved. Next: kept fixes go to 08-18.
  - Watch: quoted dollars cut from fixes that would not move field data (per quote, founder), then dollars spent per second saved, then gross profit.
- **08-20 Decide whether to keep patching the old theme or move to a lighter one: count patch hours in the last year, load our top product on the lighter theme's demo, and compare** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once a year, or when patch hours pass 40 in a year. Enablers: speed-lab, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: speed work and developer hours over 12 months (founder, founder); baseline per page type (enabler, speed-lab); a candidate theme to try on a copy (connected, store platform theme store).
  - Steps: 1) Total patch hours and cost for speed in the last 12 months 2) Install the lighter theme as an unpublished copy with our top product and 1 collection 3) Run the same tests on both 4) List what the move would cost: build hours, apps to rewire, risk 5) Write keep or move with the numbers for the founder
  - Output: a keep or move call with patch cost, speed gap and move cost side by side. Next: a move goes to 06-08 and 08-12 for a staged switch.
  - Watch: mobile load on the top product page, current vs lighter theme (7 days, speed-lab), then speed spend per year, then gross profit.

## 09-creative Creative

### T1. When Monday numbers land, help me see which ads are winning and losing and why, broken into hook, message, proof and offer, so I stop feeding ads that look busy and do not sell and the next brief starts from what we learned

Metric: share of spend on ads at or under target cost per purchase, 7 day

- **09-03 Mark every live ad scale, watch or cut each Monday, name the part that made it win or lose (hook rate, hold rate, click rate or page), and move ads under 3 times target spend to 'not read yet'** Score 48, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday after the ad pull, and any day spend jumps 20 percent. Enablers: creative-archive, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: last 7 and 28 days of ad-level spend, impressions, 3 second views, thruplays, clicks, purchases, value (connected, Meta Ads); target cost per purchase per product (brain, finance shelf); store orders by landing page and UTM (connected, Shopify); tags per ad (enabler, creative-tags).
  - Steps: 1) Compute per ad in code: hook rate (3 second views / impressions), hold rate (thruplays / 3 second views), click rate, page conversion, cost per purchase 2) Put every ad under 3 times target spend in 'not read yet', not in cut 3) Mark scale if cost per purchase is under target for 7 days, cut if over 1.5 times target after 3 times target spend, else watch 4) For each cut, name the weakest link against the account median: hook, hold, click or page 5) If click rate is fine and page conversion is under half the site rate, send it to 09-05 as a page problem, not a creative one 6) Hand the list to the media buyer with dollars at stake per ad
  - Output: a Monday table: every live ad with scale, watch, cut or not read yet, the weak link and the dollars at stake. Next: feeds 11-01 (ad set cut, hold, scale) and 09-05 (postmortem of cuts).
  - Watch: share of spend on ads at or under target cost per purchase (7 day, Meta Ads), then blended cost per new customer, 28 day, then gross profit.
- **09-05 Run a postmortem on every ad that spent over 3 times target without hitting it: who saw it by age, gender and placement, where people dropped, the likely cause, and a fix and relaunch plan or a kill note** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, for each ad marked cut in 09-03. Enablers: creative-postmortem, segment-map.
  - Inputs: ad-level breakdowns by age, gender, placement and region with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); landing page sessions, add to cart and orders by ad UTM (connected, GA4); scroll depth on the landing page (setup, Clarity); tags and the winner cards (enabler, principle-matcher).
  - Steps: 1) Lay out the drop chain: impressions, 3 second views, thruplays, clicks, page views, add to cart, purchases 2) Find the step where this ad falls furthest below the account median 3) Split spend and purchases by age, gender and placement; mark cells that sold near target 4) If the page is the break, compare the ad's promise with the page headline and send it to 10-11 5) Write the cause in one line and pick one fix: new first frame, new hook, new page, or narrow to the good cell 6) Write a relaunch plan with a spend cap, or a kill note with the tag that failed
  - Output: a one page postmortem per cut ad with the drop chain, the segment table, the cause and a relaunch or kill note. Next: feeds 09-06 (fail note) and 11-04 (relaunch in the good segment).
  - Watch: share of relaunched ads that reach target cost per purchase (14 day, Meta Ads), then hit rate of ads and wasted spend per month, then gross profit.
- **09-01 Load every ad we ran in the last 12 months into one archive with the video or image, copy, dates, spend, purchases and placement, so any past ad can be found in 10 seconds** Score 36, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then a daily pull. Enablers: creative-archive.
  - Inputs: 12 months of Meta ads at ad level with media, copy, spend, purchases and value (connected, Meta Ads); TikTok ad history with media, when the brand runs TikTok (setup, TikTok Ads); a naming rule: product, concept, angle, format, date (brain, ads shelf); a list of ads the founder remembers as big wins or big losses (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every ad with its media, copy, headline, landing URL, dates, spend, purchases and value 2) Keep ad-level rows even when 2 ads share one image; group them under one creative id 3) Flag dynamic and mixed creatives so their results are not read as one ad 4) Rename in the archive (not in Meta) to the naming rule: product, concept, angle, format, date 5) Add filters for product, date, spend band, cost per purchase band and status 6) Ask the founder to star the 5 ads they remember and check the archive agrees with the numbers
  - Output: a searchable archive of every ad with media, copy and results, dynamic ads flagged. Next: feeds 09-02 (tag every ad) and 09-03 (Monday verdict).
  - Watch: share of last 12 months of spend covered by archived ads with media (12 month, Meta Ads), then hit rate of new ads, since briefs start from real past results, then gross profit.
- **09-04 Write why each of the top 5 winners of the last 90 days worked: match its tags to the idea bank, the knowledge cards and library ads with the same tags, name the principle, and write a test that repeats it** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: on the 1st of each month, and when a new ad passes 5 times target spend under target. Enablers: principle-matcher, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: top 5 ads by spend that held target for 14 days (enabler, creative-archive); tags per ad (enabler, creative-tags); idea bank filtered to paid social creative and ad copy (have, ideas/v1); knowledge cards on hooks, angles and formats (have, data/library-v3); winner board and loser board (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the 5 ads with the most spend that held target cost per purchase for 14 days 2) Find ideas and cards whose tags match each winner's hook, angle, awareness level and format 3) Find our own losers with the same tags; if the tag also loses, it is not the reason 4) Write the principle in one sentence, e.g. 'names the pain in the buyer's words in the first 2 seconds' 5) Write one test that repeats the principle on a new angle, with a pass line 6) Flag any claim in the winner we cannot prove, before it is copied into new ads
  - Output: 5 winner cards: the principle, the proof it is the reason, the repeat test and any claim flag. Next: feeds 09-08 (near and far variants), 09-10 (other formats) and 10-05 (copy batch).
  - Watch: hit rate of ads briefed from a winner card vs ads briefed without one (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **09-06 Promote each creative test winner to the T0 standard that every new brief starts from, write a fail note for each loser, and set a date to recheck each standard** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a creative test is read, and the 1st of each month for rechecks. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: test results with the winner definition written before launch (brain, history shelf); winner cards and postmortems (enabler, creative-postmortem); who approves a new standard (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) For each test, compare the result with the winner definition written before launch 2) Win: write the rule in one line (e.g. 'open on the problem, not the product') and add it to the T0 list 3) Loss: write a fail note with what was tried, the spend, the tag and the likely reason 4) Inconclusive (under 3 times target spend): mark as not tested, never as failed 5) Set a recheck date 90 days out for each standard; on that date run 1 ad that breaks the rule 6) Founder signs new standards before they reach the brief template
  - Output: a T0 list of creative rules with proof and recheck dates, and a fail list nobody repeats. Next: feeds every new brief in 09-08, 09-12 and 09-15.
  - Watch: share of new briefs that cite a T0 rule (28 day, win-loss-ledger), then hit rate of new ads, then gross profit.
- **09-02 Tag every ad in the archive by hook type, angle, pain point, avatar, awareness level, format, length, first frame, proof, offer, CTA and talent, and check 20 tags by hand each week** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each new ad within a day of launch. Enablers: creative-tags, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: the archive with media and copy (enabler, creative-archive); tag list with a plain definition and 3 examples per tag (enabler, creative-tags); avatars and pain points (brain, customers shelf); angles library (brain, messaging shelf); a person for 20 minutes a week to check tags (founder, founder or creative lead).
  - Steps: 1) Write the tag list: 13 parts, each with a plain definition and 3 examples from this brand's own ads 2) Tag the top 100 ads by spend first, then the rest 3) Read the first 3 seconds of each video and the headline of each static for hook and first frame tags 4) Pull 20 tags at random each week; the checker marks right or wrong 5) Fix the definition of any tag wrong 3 times or more and re-tag ads with that tag 6) Show a coverage table: how many ads and how much spend per tag value
  - Output: every ad tagged on 13 parts, with a weekly accuracy score and a coverage table. Next: feeds 09-04 (why winners won), 09-08 (variants) and 09-11 (awareness heat map).
  - Watch: tag accuracy on the weekly check of 20 (weekly, creative-tags), then share of spend on ads at or under target cost per purchase, then gross profit.
### T2. When the 2 or 3 ads carrying the account start to tire, help me have fresh variants of the proven winners ready to take over, so I do not see costs jump the week they fade

Metric: share of new ads launched in 28 days that reach target cost per purchase (hit rate)

- **09-09 Put 5 new openings on the body of a worn out winner within a week, each changing one lever (angle, offer, avatar or format) across all 3 hook layers: sound, on-screen text and the first picture** Score 46, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: when 09-07 labels a winner worn out. Enablers: variant-stack, script-builder.
  - Inputs: the worn out ad and its drop chain (enabler, fatigue-watch); hook types that won and lost for this brand (brain, ads shelf); hook ideas from the idea bank (have, ideas/v1); raw footage or new clips for the first 3 seconds (brain, content and brand kit shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Cut the old hook off and keep the body from the first second the product appears 2) Write 10 hooks: 3 new angles, 2 new avatars, 2 new offers, 3 new formats (text on screen, talking head, product demo) 3) Pick 5 that do not sell in the first line and do not name the product 4) Build each hook in 3 layers: what is heard, what is read, what is seen 5) Use new footage in the first frame; a recoloured old frame is not a new hook 6) Launch the 5 in a new ad set beside the old ad and read at 3 times target spend
  - Output: 5 new openings on a proven body, live within 7 days of the wear flag. Next: feeds 09-06 (promote the best opening) and 11-15 (test read).
  - Watch: hook rate of new openings vs the worn out ad's first 7 days (7 day, Meta Ads), then cost per purchase on the refreshed concept, then gross profit.
- **09-08 Make 5 variants of each of last month's top 3 winners, 3 near (new hook, new first frame, new length) and 2 far (new angle, new avatar or new format), all keeping the winning principle, and launch each set as one test** Score 42, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: on the 1st of each month, and when 09-07 flags a winner. Enablers: variant-stack, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: top 3 winners with tags and winner cards (enabler, principle-matcher); voice rules and allowed claims (brain, voice and claims shelves); real product photos and clips with rights (brain, content and brand kit shelf); an editor or designer, in house or freelance (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) Write the principle each winner followed at the top of its variant sheet 2) Near 1 to 3: keep the body; change only the hook line, the first frame, or cut to a new length 3) Far 1 and 2: keep the principle; change the angle, the avatar or the format (static to video, UGC to founder) 4) Label each variant near or far and name the one thing it changes 5) Check each against claims and use the real product photo, not a redrawn one 6) Launch the 5 as one ad set beside the winner, never inside it, with the read spend set to 3 times target
  - Output: 15 variants a month, each labelled near or far with the one change, launched as 3 tests. Next: feeds 11-15 (test launch) and 09-06 (win or fail note).
  - Watch: hit rate of near vs far variants (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **09-07 Check each ad with over 20 percent of spend every Monday for wear: frequency, hook rate and cost per purchase over 14 days, and label it worn out, starved of budget, or out of new people** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and any day the top ad's cost per purchase rises 15 percent. Enablers: fatigue-watch, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: daily ad-level frequency, reach, impressions, 3 second views, spend and purchases for 28 days (connected, Meta Ads); share of reach that is new vs seen before, per ad set (connected, Meta Ads); target cost per purchase (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List ads with over 20 percent of account spend in the last 7 days 2) For each, plot hook rate, click rate, frequency and cost per purchase by day for 14 days 3) Worn out: hook rate down 25 percent from its first 7 days while frequency rises 4) Starved: hook and click rate steady but spend cut by the platform or a budget change 5) Out of new people: reach flat, frequency over 3 on cold ad sets, cost rising for every ad in the set 6) Send worn out ads to 09-09 for new openings; send out of new people to 11-10
  - Output: a weekly wear list: each big ad labelled worn out, starved or out of new people, with the chart behind it. Next: feeds 09-09 (fresh openings) and 11-10 (funnel stage split).
  - Watch: days between a wear flag and a replacement ad going live (per flag, fatigue-watch), then cost per purchase on the top 3 ads, then gross profit.
- **09-10 Turn each winning principle into 4 other formats (static, carousel, UGC video and a native article ad), and send the matching block to the landing page and the email team** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when 09-04 writes a winner card. Enablers: format-translator, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: the winner card with its principle (enabler, principle-matcher); format specs per placement: sizes, safe zones, text limits (enabler, format-translator); formats already live and their share of spend (enabler, creative-tags); brand kit and product photos (brain, content and brand kit shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List formats the account already runs and their share of spend; pick the 4 with the least 2) Rewrite the principle for each format: one frame for a static, 5 cards for a carousel, a 30 second script for UGC, 600 words for a native article 3) Keep the same promise, proof and awareness level in all 4 4) Send the page block version to 07-landing-pages-cro and the email version to 14-email-messaging 5) Launch the 4 formats in one ad set and compare cost per purchase by format
  - Output: 4 new format versions of one proven idea, plus a page block and an email block. Next: feeds 11-15 (launch) and 10-14 (carry the line everywhere).
  - Watch: share of spend on the account's top format (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer and risk of one ad carrying the account, then revenue.
### T3. When cost per new customer climbs and most new ads speak to people who already know us, help me aim more of them at shoppers who have never heard of us, using what rivals and buyers teach us, so I grow with new buyers

Metric: share of spend on unaware and problem aware ads, 28 day

- **09-11 Tag every live ad and 3 rivals' active ads by awareness level, and show spend share per level for us next to each rival** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every Monday after the ad pull. Enablers: awareness-map, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: last 90 days of our ads with media, copy, spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); 3 to 5 competitor page ids (brain, competitors shelf); rivals' active ads with start dates (connected, Meta Ad Library); awareness rubric with 5 examples per level (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) Pull our ads and 3 rivals' active ads 2) Tag each ad with one awareness level (unaware, problem aware, solution aware, product aware, most aware) and one funnel stage, using the rubric 3) Spot check 20 tags by hand and fix the rubric where it is wrong 4) Weight our levels by spend; weight rivals' by days live, since their spend is not public 5) Compute cost per new customer by level for our ads, from store new customer orders 6) Write the 1 line gap: the level where we spend least and rivals run most
  - Output: a heat map: 5 awareness levels by 4 brands, share in each cell, cost per new customer by level, and the gap line. Next: feeds 09-12 (brief top of funnel ads) and 11-11 (prospecting budget split).
  - Watch: share of spend on unaware and problem aware ads (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, and cost per new customer, then revenue.
- **09-12 Brief 4 top of funnel ads for the thinnest awareness level: pull 10 references at that level from the swipe file, keep 3 principles, and write a one page brief for each ad** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every other Monday, after 09-11. Enablers: awareness-map, swipe-file, script-builder.
  - Inputs: the gap line from the heat map (enabler, awareness-map); swipe file filtered by awareness level (brain, inspiration shelf); pain points and the words buyers use for the problem (brain, customers shelf); T0 creative rules (enabler, win-loss-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Load the brain core page, the claims shelf and the voice shelf before any brief 2) Filter the swipe file to the gap level and pick 10 ads; note why each stops the scroll 3) Reduce the 10 to 3 principles in one line each; never copy text, logos, products or faces 4) Write 4 briefs: avatar, the problem in their words, the hook, the story, the proof, the page it lands on 5) For unaware and problem aware ads, point the ad to an article or quiz page, not the product page 6) Judge these ads on cost per new customer over 7 day click, not last click return
  - Output: 4 one page briefs for top of funnel ads, each tied to a principle and a landing page. Next: feeds 09-15 (AI video stack) and 11-11 (budget for top of funnel).
  - Watch: new customer share of purchases from these ads (28 day, Shopify), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **09-13 Pull rivals' ads and organic posts every Monday, keep those live 30 days or more as signals, write the base principle of each in one line, and turn the best 3 into our own script, static and video** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: competitor-feed, swipe-file, script-builder.
  - Inputs: competitor page ids and social handles (brain, competitors shelf); rivals' ads with start dates (connected, Meta Ad Library); rivals' top organic posts by views, saved by hand or a cheap tool (setup, Instagram and TikTok); our claims and voice (brain, claims and voice shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Pull each rival's active ads and last 30 organic posts; sort ads by days live, posts by views 2) Save the top 10 to the swipe file with a note: what it does and which tag or gap it fills 3) Write the base principle behind each, e.g. 'shows the old way failing before the product appears' 4) Pick 3 principles that fit a gap in 09-11 and that we have not tested 5) Write our own script, one static and one video brief per principle, in our voice with our proof 6) Check nothing copies their words, logo, product or faces
  - Output: a weekly note of 10 rival signals with principles, and 3 of our own scripts built from them. Next: feeds 09-15 (AI video stack) and 09-16 (static test first).
  - Watch: hit rate of ads built from rival principles (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **09-14 Find the 10 moments when buyers hire our product from reviews, tickets and a post-purchase question, and write 2 hooks per moment in their own words** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once a quarter, and when the team runs out of ideas. Enablers: customer-jobs, review-miner.
  - Inputs: all reviews with text and rating (connected, reviews app); last 6 months of tickets and chats (connected, helpdesk); a post-purchase question: what was going on when you decided to buy (setup, post-purchase survey); 5 customer calls of 20 minutes (founder, founder or CX lead).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every sentence that names a moment: a trip, a new job, a gift, a problem that got worse 2) Group them into 10 moments and count how many buyers name each 3) For each moment note what they used before and what nearly stopped them 4) Write 2 hooks per moment using their exact words, not ours 5) Mark moments no live ad speaks to and send the top 3 to the brief in 09-12
  - Output: a moments table: 10 moments with counts, the old way, the doubt, and 20 hooks. Next: feeds 09-12 (briefs), 10-17 (moment copy) and 02-customers-avatars.
  - Watch: hook rate of moment hooks vs account median (7 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.
### T4. When we can make 50 new ads a week with AI and editors, help me make the ones that deserve test budget and know what each cost to make, so I do not pay for ads that never pay back

Metric: production cost per winning ad, 90 day

- **09-15 Make one AI video stack a week: write the script from a winning principle and the awareness gap, write 3 variations of the hook and angle, cut each to 15, 30 and 30 to 45 seconds, render, and pick one cut per stack to launch** Score 38, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: every Tuesday, after the Monday read. Enablers: video-stack, script-builder, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: access to a video tool such as Higgsfield (buy, video tool subscription); the winning principle and the awareness gap (enabler, principle-matcher); real product photos and clips (brain, content and brand kit shelf); allowed claims and voice rules (brain, claims and voice shelves); past winning scripts (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write the script: hook, problem, product in use, proof, CTA, with shot notes 2) Write 3 variations that change the hook and the angle, not just words 3) Cut each variation to 15, 30 and 30 to 45 seconds, so 9 cuts 4) Render with the video tool; use the real product clip wherever the product is on screen 5) Check every cut: product looks like what arrives, no invented claim, captions readable at phone size 6) Pick one cut per variation by the strongest first 3 seconds, and launch the 3 as one test
  - Output: a grid of 9 cuts from 1 script, 3 picked and launched as one test. Next: feeds 11-15 (launch and read the stack) and 09-06 (which length wins for this brand).
  - Watch: cost per test and hook rate of AI cuts vs filmed ads (7 day, Meta Ads), then ads launched per week and hit rate, then gross profit.
- **09-16 Test each new angle as 3 cheap statics before booking a video shoot, then write the shoot list only for angles that beat the account's median hook and click rate** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: before any shoot or video job over $1,000 is booked. Enablers: format-translator, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: the new angles with a one line brief each (brain, messaging shelf); product photos and brand kit (brain, content and brand kit shelf); account median click rate and cost per purchase for statics (connected, Meta Ads); shoot budget and date (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Make 3 statics per angle: a headline on a product photo, a problem picture, a review quote 2) Launch each angle as its own ad set with the same budget for 5 days 3) Keep angles whose click rate and cost per purchase beat the static median 4) Write the shoot list for kept angles only: 5 openings, product in use, proof shots, each at 9:16 5) Drop or rework angles that lost, and log them in 09-06
  - Output: angles proven on statics and a shot list that covers only those angles. Next: feeds the shoot, then 09-08 (variants from the footage).
  - Watch: share of shoot budget spent on angles that passed a static test (90 day, production-ledger), then production cost per winning ad, then gross profit.
- **09-17 Log what each finished ad cost to make and whether it paid back, by source (agency, creator, AI, in house), and show hit rate and cost per winning ad for each source every month** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a creative invoice arrives, and the 1st of each month. Enablers: production-ledger, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: creative invoices and retainer fees by month (setup, a Google Sheet kept by the founder); which source made each ad (brain, ads shelf naming rule); spend, purchases and gross profit per ad (enabler, margin-view).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each ad in the archive with its source and its cost to make 2) Split retainers across the ads delivered that month 3) Per source compute in code: ads made, ads that reached target, hit rate, cost per winning ad, gross profit after ad spend 4) Size next month's ad count: spend divided by spend per winner, divided by hit rate 5) Write one line per source: keep, cut or change the brief
  - Output: a monthly source table: cost per ad, hit rate, cost per winning ad, verdict per source, and next month's ad count. Next: feeds the agency review on 11-09 and 23-team-ops.
  - Watch: cost per winning ad by source (90 day, production-ledger), then creative spend per dollar of gross profit, then gross profit.
### T5. When I decide which creators to brief again, help me see who drives purchases, not just views, and keep their rights in order, so I put the creator budget into people whose videos sell

Metric: cost per purchase on creator and partnership ads vs brand handle ads, 28 day

- **09-18 Rank every creator we paid by purchases per 1,000 views and cost per purchase on their ads, and mark each rebrief, renew or cut** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 1st of each month, and before any creator renewal. Enablers: creator-roster, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: creator list with fees, deliverables and handles (brain, creators shelf); ad-level results for ads using each creator's content, including partnership ads (connected, Meta Ads); discount code and link sales per creator (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Map each ad and code to the creator whose content it uses 2) Compute in code per creator: views, purchases per 1,000 views, cost per purchase, fee per purchase 3) Split results by partnership ad (their handle) vs brand handle ad for the same video 4) Mark rebrief when views are high and purchases low, renew when both are strong, cut when both are weak 5) Write the one line reason for each verdict
  - Output: a creator table ranked by purchases, not views, with a verdict and reason per creator. Next: feeds 09-19 (brief the next round) and 11-16 (partnership ads).
  - Watch: cost per purchase on creator content (28 day, Meta Ads), then creator spend per new customer, then gross profit.
- **09-20 List every live ad that uses a creator's face, a customer's content or licensed music with its rights end date, and pause or replace it 14 days before the date** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: creator-roster, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: rights terms and end dates per creator and per music track (brain, creators shelf); live ads with media (connected, Meta Ads); UGC library with permission status (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Match each live ad's media to a creator, a customer post or a music track 2) Flag ads with no rights record at all as a risk now 3) List ads whose rights end in 14 days, with their spend 4) For each: renew the rights, cut a version without that face or track, or plan the pause 5) Hand pauses to the media buyer; the agent does not pause ads
  - Output: a rights calendar of live ads with end dates, and a plan for each ad within 14 days of its end. Next: feeds 11-14 (launch preflight rights check).
  - Watch: live ads past their rights end date (weekly, creator-roster), then ads taken down or claimed, then risk.
- **09-19 Brief the next 5 creators with a concept, not just 'make UGC': the principle, the hook options, the claims they may and may not say, the rights terms, and a partnership ad request** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each creator round, after 09-18. Enablers: creator-roster, script-builder, claim-guard.
  - Inputs: creator candidates with niche, audience and past results (brain, creators shelf); winning principles and T0 rules (enabler, principle-matcher); allowed and forbidden claims (brain, claims shelf); rights terms: usage length, paid use, partnership ad access (founder, founder); creator fees (buy, creators).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 5 creators: 3 renewals from 09-18 and 2 new ones whose audience matches the best segment 2) Write one concept per creator: the principle, the moment, 3 hook options, what to show 3) Add a claims box: 5 lines they may say, 5 they must not 4) Set rights in writing: 90 day paid use, partnership ad access, the end date 5) Ask each for raw footage too, so new hooks can be cut later
  - Output: 5 signed creator briefs with concept, claims box, rights and partnership access. Next: feeds 11-16 (run partnership ads) and 09-20 (rights dates).
  - Watch: share of creator videos that reach target cost per purchase (28 day, Meta Ads), then hit rate of creator content, then gross profit.

## 10-copy Copy

### T1. When our best ad is getting more expensive and the media buyer needs new words by Friday, help me get copy built from what buyers say, for the buyers who convert best, that wins on cost per purchase and not on clicks, so I launch fresh ads without paying more for each sale

Metric: cost per purchase by copy set, 7 day click, first 14 days live

- **10-05 Write the Friday copy batch: 10 headline candidates for each chosen avatar and pain pair, cut to 3 truly different angles, each with a written reason and a pass line** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Wednesday for a Friday launch slot, and when the top ad's cost per purchase rises 15 percent. Enablers: copy-decomposer, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: the pair to write for (enabler, copy-decomposer); winner cards from creative (enabler, principle-matcher); language bank and objection ladder (brain, customers shelf); ad copy playbook v1.2 and ad copy ideas (have, playbooks/ad-copy-v1.2 and ideas/v1).
  - Steps: 1) Write the buying question: what the reader believes now and what they must believe to buy 2) Write 10 headlines, each with a true specific: a number, a time, a count or a price 3) Cut to 3 that differ in angle, not synonyms; name the angle of each 4) Write the primary text for each: first line under 14 words, one idea, one proof 5) Write the reason each might win and the cost per purchase that counts as a pass 6) Run 10-09 on the 3 before they go to the media buyer
  - Output: 3 copy sets with angle names, reasons and pass lines, ready for Friday. Next: feeds 10-09 (voice check) then 11-14 (launch preflight).
  - Watch: cost per purchase by copy set, 7 day click (14 day, Meta Ads), then blended cost per new customer, 28 day, then gross profit.
- **10-06 Refresh a tiring winner's words without losing its appeal: keep the promise and the proof, and change only the first line, the headline and the form (list, story or question)** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when fatigue-watch labels a winner worn out. Enablers: variant-stack, copy-decomposer.
  - Inputs: the worn out ad and its split into promise, proof and CTA (enabler, copy-decomposer); wear label and dates (enabler, fatigue-watch); voice rules (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write the promise and proof of the winner in one line each; these do not change 2) Write 3 new forms: a numbered list, a short customer story with a turn, a question the buyer asks 3) Write 3 new first lines and 3 new headlines per form 4) Pick 3 full sets and run them through 10-09 5) Launch beside the old ad, not in place of it
  - Output: 3 refreshed copy sets on the same promise, ready to launch. Next: feeds 11-15 (test launch).
  - Watch: cost per purchase of refreshed sets vs the old ad's last 7 days (14 day, Meta Ads), then life of a winning message in weeks, then gross profit.
- **10-01 Split every ad we ran in 12 months into avatar, pain point, promise, proof and CTA, rank each avatar and pain pair by spend and cost per purchase, and list the pairs no ad speaks to** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every new ad within a day of launch. Enablers: copy-decomposer, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: 12 months of ad copy, headlines and results at ad level (connected, Meta Ads); up to 4 avatars and their pain points (brain, customers shelf); landing page text per ad URL (brain, site shelf); a person to check 20 splits a week (founder, founder or copy lead).
  - Steps: 1) Pull primary text, headline, description and URL for every ad with spend 2) Tag each ad with one avatar, one pain point, the promise, the proof type and the CTA 3) Check 20 at random by hand and fix the avatar and pain definitions that are wrong 4) Compute in code per pair: ads, spend, purchases, cost per purchase, share of new customers 5) Build a grid of avatars by pains; mark cells with spend, cells that win, and empty cells
  - Output: an avatar by pain grid with spend, cost per purchase and empty cells marked. Next: feeds 10-04 (copy per segment) and 10-05 (Friday copy batch).
  - Watch: share of spend on the 3 cheapest avatar and pain pairs (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **10-04 Write 3 copy sets for each of the 2 cheapest segments by age, gender and placement, using their pain and their words, so the ad reads as if it were written for them** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every other Monday, and before each launch slot. Enablers: segment-map, copy-decomposer.
  - Inputs: purchases and cost per new customer by age, gender and placement, 28 days (connected, Meta Ads); avatar and pain grid (enabler, copy-decomposer); language bank (brain, customers shelf); placement text limits and safe zones (enabler, format-translator).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the 2 cells with the lowest cost per new customer and at least 30 purchases, e.g. women 35 to 44 on Reels 2) Match each cell to the avatar that most often buys there, from order and survey data 3) Write 3 sets per cell: primary text, headline, on-screen line, sized to that placement 4) Use the words that cell uses in reviews; drop lines that only fit the other cell 5) Hand the sets to the media buyer with the cell each is for
  - Output: 6 copy sets, each labelled with the segment and placement it is written for. Next: feeds 11-12 (ad sets for the best segment).
  - Watch: cost per purchase in the target segment vs generic copy (14 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **10-03 Move proven words between channels each month: turn the top 20 Google search terms, Search Console queries and Amazon questions into 10 Meta hooks, and turn the 5 best Meta hooks into Google headlines and article titles** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 1st of each month. Enablers: signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: Google Ads search terms with purchases, 90 days (connected, Google Ads); Search Console queries with clicks and position (connected, Search Console); Amazon questions and review phrases (setup, Amazon Seller Central); site search terms (connected, GA4); Meta hooks with cost per purchase (enabler, copy-decomposer).
  - Steps: 1) List the 20 search terms with the most purchases and the 20 fastest rising queries 2) Add the 10 most asked Amazon questions and site searches 3) Rewrite 10 of them as Meta hooks in the searcher's words, e.g. a query 'socks that do not slip' becomes the first line 4) Take the 5 Meta hooks with the lowest cost per purchase and write 3 Google headlines each within 30 characters 5) Send 3 article titles built from winning hooks and rising queries to 17-seo 6) Tag each line with its source so its result is traced back
  - Output: a monthly bridge sheet: 10 Meta hooks from search, 15 Google headlines from Meta, 3 article titles. Next: feeds 11-13 (launch the bridge tests), 12-google-ads and 17-seo.
  - Watch: cost per purchase of search-sourced Meta hooks vs account median (14 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer across Meta and Google, then revenue.
- **10-02 Mine our reviews and 3 rivals' Amazon and site reviews for what buyers love, hate and wish for, and build a bank of the 20 most used phrases per theme with counts** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then on the 1st of each month. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: all our reviews with text, rating and product (connected, reviews app); rivals' reviews from Amazon and their sites, 3 rivals (buy, a review export tool or a paid data source); our Amazon reviews and questions, when we sell there (setup, Amazon Seller Central); return reasons (connected, Shopify).
