  - Steps: 1) Pull 5 star and 1 star reviews first, then the rest 2) Group sentences into themes: loved, hated, wished for, surprised by, used instead 3) Count each theme per brand and keep the exact phrases, with source 4) Mark where rivals fail most (1 star themes) and we are praised for the same thing 5) Write the top 20 phrases per theme into the language bank; forum quotes are never testimonials
  - Output: a language bank: themes with counts for us and each rival, and 20 verbatim phrases per theme. Next: feeds 10-05 (copy batch), 10-16 (retargeting objections) and 03-competitors.
  - Watch: click rate and cost per purchase of ads using bank phrases vs not (14 day, Meta Ads), then cost per purchase on new copy, then revenue.
### T2. When new ads are due to go live tomorrow and I do not know if a line breaks Meta's rules or promises something we cannot back, help me check every claim, quote and boast first and cut drafts to our voice, so I launch without a rejection and stop rewriting every line myself

Metric: ads rejected per 100 launched, and share of drafts approved without a rewrite

- **10-10 Fix a rejected ad within the hour: find the words that triggered it, write 3 rewrites that keep the angle and pass, and log the rule so it is checked next time** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when Meta rejects an ad or warns the account. Enablers: claim-guard.
  - Inputs: rejection notice and the rejected ad (connected, Meta Ads); past rejections and the rewrites that passed (enabler, claim-guard); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Read the policy named in the notice and mark the lines and images that fit it 2) Write 3 rewrites that keep the angle and drop the trigger 3) Run them through 10-07 4) The media buyer submits one; request review only if the ad was clearly within the rules 5) Add the trigger and the passing line to the rejection log
  - Output: 3 passing rewrites and a new line in the rejection log. Next: feeds 10-07 (the check learns the rule).
  - Watch: hours from rejection to the ad running again (per rejection, Meta Ads), then days of delivery lost and account warnings, then risk.
- **10-07 Check every line in tomorrow's ads against the claims shelf and Meta's rules: health and before and after claims, personal attributes, fake scarcity, review quotes and guarantees, and return a pass, fix or block per line** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: before every launch, the day before. Enablers: claim-guard.
  - Inputs: the ads due tomorrow with all text and on-screen text (connected, launch sheet or Meta drafts); allowed claims with proof files, and forbidden claims (brain, claims shelf); Meta ad rules for the category, written as checks (enabler, claim-guard); review quotes with permission status (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Split each ad into single lines, including on-screen text and captions 2) Match each line to an allowed claim and its proof file; lines with no match go to fix 3) Flag rule risks: 'you' plus a body or money problem, before and after, cures, invented scarcity 4) Check every quote is real, permitted and not changed in meaning 5) Write a passing rewrite for each fix; block lines with no proof 6) A person signs the block list before launch
  - Output: a line by line check with pass, fix (and the rewrite) or block, signed before launch. Next: feeds 11-14 (launch preflight).
  - Watch: ads rejected per 100 launched (28 day, Meta Ads), then days of delivery lost to rejections, and account health, then risk.
- **10-09 Score each batch of freelancer or AI drafts in code for reading level, sentence length, banned words and voice rules, and cut 20 drafts to the 5 worth the founder's time** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when a batch of drafts arrives. Enablers: voice-check.
  - Inputs: the drafts (founder, writer or AI tool); voice rules, 20 liked and 20 disliked examples (brain, voice shelf); readability and banned word checks (enabler, voice-check); the founder's past ratings of copy (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Score reading level in code; fail anything above grade 8 2) Fail sentences over 20 words, banned words, em dashes and vague words 3) Compare each draft with liked and disliked examples and give a voice score 4) Rank by score and by how different the angle is from the others 5) Send the top 5 with one line on why each passed; log the founder's pick to the ratings
  - Output: 5 drafts that pass voice and reading checks, with scores, from a batch of 20. Next: feeds 10-07 (claim check) and the voice shelf ratings.
  - Watch: share of drafts the founder approves without a rewrite (28 day, history shelf), then founder hours spent on copy per week, then customer satisfaction.
- **10-08 Swap every boast in live and draft copy (best, premium, amazing, world class) for a number, a rating or a test result we can back, and list the boasts with no proof to replace** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then on every draft batch. Enablers: voice-check, claim-guard.
  - Inputs: live ad copy and product page copy (connected, Meta Ads and site crawl); proof per product: ratings, review counts, tests, specs (brain, products shelf); banned words (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Scan all lines for a list of 40 boast words and banned words 2) For each hit, find a true specific from the products shelf, e.g. '4.8 stars from 12,000 reviews' 3) Rewrite the line with the specific; never invent a number 4) List boasts with no proof to the founder as proof to find or lines to drop
  - Output: a swap list of boasts to specifics, and a short list of claims that need proof. Next: feeds 10-09 (voice check) and the claims shelf.
  - Watch: share of live lines with a specific number or proof (28 day, claim-guard), then click rate and cost per purchase, then revenue.
### T3. When the ad promises one thing and the page it sends to says another, or people visit and do not buy, help me find which live ads no longer match their page and which doubt costs the most sales, so I stop paying for clicks that land on the wrong message

Metric: conversion rate of paid sessions on the top 10 ads' landing pages

- **10-11 Score the top 10 ads by spend for match with their landing page on promise, offer, picture and awareness level, and rewrite the page headline of the worst 3 to mirror the ad within a day** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and after any page or offer change. Enablers: congruence-check, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: top 10 ads with URL, copy and media (connected, Meta Ads); landing page text and a screenshot per URL (brain, site shelf); paid sessions and conversion by landing page and ad (connected, GA4); awareness level per ad (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) For each ad, write its promise, offer and awareness level in one line each 2) Read the top of the landing page as a phone user sees it and write the same 3 3) Score 0 to 3: promise matches, offer matches, picture matches the ad 4) For the 3 lowest scores, write a new headline and sub line that repeat the ad's promise 5) Send the change to 07-landing-pages-cro and compare conversion 14 days before and after
  - Output: a match score for the top 10 ads and 3 page headline rewrites. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro and 11-17 (ad-matched page test).
  - Watch: conversion rate of paid sessions on the 3 fixed pages (14 day, GA4), then cost per purchase on those ads, then revenue.
- **10-13 List every question buyers ask per hero product from tickets, Amazon questions, reviews and site search, mark where each is answered, and write the answer line for the 3 unanswered doubts that cost the most sales** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once a month per hero product, and when a product page's add to cart rate drops. Enablers: question-matrix, review-miner.
  - Inputs: tickets and chats tagged by product, 6 months (connected, helpdesk); Amazon questions per product (setup, Amazon Seller Central); product page, FAQ and ad text (brain, site shelf); scroll depth on the product page (setup, Clarity).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every question and group into 15 doubts: fit, size, wash, ship time, returns, price, and so on 2) Count each doubt and mark if it is answered on the page, in the FAQ, in ads, in email 3) Rank blank cells by count times the product's traffic 4) Write a short true answer for the top 3, placed where Clarity shows buyers still read 5) Add the top doubt's answer as a line in one retargeting ad
  - Output: a doubt by channel matrix per product and 3 answer lines placed on page and in one ad. Next: feeds 10-16 (retargeting), 07-landing-pages-cro and 15-customers-cx.
  - Watch: add to cart rate on the product page (14 day, GA4), then product page conversion and tickets per 100 orders, then revenue.
- **10-12 When a sale ends or a product sells out, list every live ad, email, page line and listing that still says it, and hand over the fixes before midnight** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on each sale end date and each sellout. Enablers: claim-guard, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: live ads with text (connected, Meta Ads); live flows and scheduled campaigns (connected, email platform); offer end dates and stock state (brain, offers shelf and Shopify); site crawl text (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Take the ended offer or the sold out product name 2) Search every live line for the code, the percent, the product name and 'ends' 3) List each hit with where it runs and who owns it 4) Write the replacement line for each; pausing ads stays with the media buyer 5) Recheck the next morning that no hit is still live
  - Output: a sweep list of stale lines with owners and replacement lines, rechecked next day. Next: feeds 11-18 (move spend off sold out products).
  - Watch: live lines still naming an ended offer or sold out product (24 hours, claim-guard), then tickets about wrong prices and trust, then customer satisfaction.
- **10-14 Carry each winning ad line to the product page, the welcome email and the listing within a week, and log where it now runs** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a copy test is read as a win. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, congruence-check.
  - Inputs: the winning line and its test result (enabler, win-loss-ledger); the page, email and listing where the product is sold (brain, site and email shelves); Amazon listing access, when they sell there (setup, Amazon Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) Write the line as a page headline, an email subject and first line, and a listing bullet 2) Check each fits the place's length limit and awareness level 3) Send each to its owner with the test result as the reason 4) Log where the line runs in the messaging shelf with the date
  - Output: one proven line live on page, email and listing, logged in the messaging shelf. Next: feeds 10-11 (the next match score).
  - Watch: conversion rate on the product page and welcome email click rate (14 day, GA4 and email platform), then revenue per visitor, then revenue.
### T4. When retargeting shows the same ad again and new ads only speak to people who already know us, help me write a different argument for each awareness level and each buying moment, so I win back the doubters and reach buyers our normal ads miss

Metric: cost per new customer by awareness level of the copy

- **10-15 Tag all ad copy by awareness level, and for the thinnest level write 3 copy sets at the right story depth: long native text for unaware, the problem named for problem aware, the method for solution aware** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every other Monday, after the awareness heat map updates. Enablers: awareness-map, copy-decomposer.
  - Inputs: heat map with the gap line (enabler, awareness-map); problem words and moments from buyers (brain, customers shelf); the story bank (brain, messaging shelf); top of funnel copy ideas (have, ideas/v1).
  - Steps: 1) Read the gap level from the heat map, e.g. problem aware at 6 percent of spend 2) Write the buyer's state at that level in one line: what they know and do not know 3) Write 3 sets: open on the problem, not the product; name the product only after the reason 4) For unaware, write 150 to 300 words of native text that reads like a post, not an ad 5) Point each set to a page at the same level, not straight to checkout
  - Output: 3 copy sets at the gap level, each with its matching landing page. Next: feeds 11-11 (top of funnel budget) and 09-12 (visual briefs).
  - Watch: new customer share of purchases from these sets (28 day, Shopify), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **10-16 Write one retargeting ad per top objection for people who visited in the last 30 days and did not buy, each with a different argument and its proof, never the same ad again** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once a month, and when retargeting frequency passes 4. Enablers: copy-decomposer, review-miner.
  - Inputs: objection ladder ranked by sales lost (brain, customers shelf); top unanswered doubts per product (enabler, question-matrix); proof per product: reviews, tests, guarantee (brain, products and claims shelves); retargeting ad sets and their frequency (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Take the top 3 objections, e.g. 'will it fit', 'is it worth the price', 'can I return it' 2) Write one ad per objection: name it, answer it, show the proof 3) Use a different picture from the cold ad the visitor already saw 4) Keep the same argument across the follow-up ads in the sequence 5) Read each by cost per purchase on 7 day click, not by view-through
  - Output: 3 retargeting ads, one per objection, with proof. Next: feeds 11-10 (retargeting slice of the funnel mix).
  - Watch: cost per purchase in retargeting ad sets (14 day, Meta Ads), then conversion of site visitors who did not buy, then revenue.
- **10-17 Write copy for each buying moment our ads miss, starting with gift buyers 3 weeks before a gifting date: who it is for, why it is a safe gift, when it arrives, how returns work** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: quarterly from the moments table, and 3 weeks before each gifting date. Enablers: customer-jobs, copy-decomposer.
  - Inputs: the moments table with counts (enabler, customer-jobs); gifting dates and ship cut-offs (brain, calendar shelf); gift options: wrap, notes, gift returns (brain, offers shelf); share of orders shipped to a different address (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the moment no live ad speaks to that has the most buyers 2) Write who buys, for whom, and the doubt at that moment 3) Write 3 sets; for gifts, answer 'will they like it' and 'will it arrive in time' with dates 4) Add the order-by date in digits for gifting sets 5) Send each set to the media buyer with its start and stop date
  - Output: 3 copy sets per moment, with start and stop dates for seasonal ones. Next: feeds 11-19 (peak ramp plan).
  - Watch: orders shipped to a different address than billing (28 day, Shopify), then revenue in gifting weeks, then revenue.
### T5. When a proven ad starts to cost 30 percent more per sale and the buyer wants to cut the price, help me change the headline, the price line or the offer in the right order and give reasons to buy that are not a discount, so I save the ad without giving away margin

Metric: gross profit per order on ads with refreshed copy

- **10-19 Turn each approved sale or bundle into one offer line (what, how much, until when) and check it against the live code, the price and the end date in the store before it ships** Score 42, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when an offer is approved, 7 days before it starts. Enablers: claim-guard.
  - Inputs: the approved offer with rules (brain, offers shelf); discount codes, prices and product eligibility (connected, Shopify); the offer calendar (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write the offer as one line with digits: '20 percent off 3 packs, ends Sunday 11:59 pm ET' 2) Check the code exists, applies to the right products and ends at the stated time 3) Write the ad, email and page versions of the same line 4) Put the end date into the sweep list for 10-12
  - Output: one checked offer line in 3 versions, with the end date scheduled for the sweep. Next: feeds 10-12 (sweep on end date) and 14-email-messaging.
  - Watch: tickets about a wrong price or code in the sale week (7 day, helpdesk), then sale revenue and trust, then customer satisfaction.
- **10-18 When a proven ad's cost per purchase rises 30 percent, test in order: a new headline first, then a new price line, then a new offer, one at a time, before anyone cuts the price** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when a winner's cost per purchase is up 30 percent for 7 days. Enablers: copy-decomposer, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: the ad's daily results for 28 days (connected, Meta Ads); margin per product and the discount floor (brain, finance and offers shelves); wear label (enabler, fatigue-watch).
  - Steps: 1) Rule out wear first: if hook rate has fallen, send it to 09-09 instead 2) Step 1: test 3 new headlines on the same ad for 7 days 3) Step 2, only if step 1 fails: restate price as cost per day or per use, or add a bundle saving line 4) Step 3, only if step 2 fails: propose an offer inside the discount floor, for founder sign-off 5) Log each step's result in the ledger
  - Output: a 3 step rescue test with a result at each step, and a price cut only as the last resort. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing when step 3 is reached.
  - Watch: cost per purchase on the rescued ad (7 day, Meta Ads), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **10-20 Write 5 reasons to buy each hero product that are not a discount (proof, fit, method, guarantee, cost per use) and test the best 2 against the current discount ad** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once a quarter, and when discount orders pass 50 percent of orders. Enablers: review-miner, copy-decomposer, margin-view.
  - Inputs: share of orders with a discount code and margin per order (enabler, margin-view); praise themes and phrases (enabler, review-miner); product proof and guarantee terms (brain, products and claims shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Compute in code the share of orders and gross profit that came with a code 2) Write 5 reasons from praise themes and proof, e.g. 'lasts 2 years of weekly wear' 3) Pick 2 with the most review mentions 4) Run each as an ad with no code against the current discount ad, same audience and budget 5) Compare gross profit per 1,000 impressions, not just cost per purchase
  - Output: 5 no discount reasons per hero product and a test of the top 2 against the discount ad. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing and 14-email-messaging.
  - Watch: share of orders with a discount code (28 day, Shopify), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.

## 11-paid-social Paid social

### T1. When the weekly numbers land on Monday with spend up and revenue flat, help me know by noon which ad sets and placements to cut, hold or scale and by how much, so I stop the week's budget leaking into losers

Metric: share of spend in ad sets above target cost per purchase after spending 3 times that target, 7 day click

- **11-01 Rank every ad set each Monday by dollars spent above target, and mark each cut, hold or scale with a dollar amount, judging only ad sets that spent at least 3 times target cost per purchase** Score 48, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday by 10am, and any day spend jumps 20 percent. Enablers: spend-rules, margin-view.
  - Inputs: ad set spend, purchases and value, 7 and 28 days, 7 day click (connected, Meta Ads); target cost per purchase per product from margin (brain, finance shelf); approval rules: what a buyer may change without the founder (brain, decisions and goals shelf); the creative verdicts for the same week (enabler, creative-archive).
  - Steps: 1) Compute in code per ad set: spend, purchases, cost per purchase, dollars above target 2) Put ad sets under 3 times target spend in 'not read yet' 3) Cut: over 1.5 times target for 7 days. Scale: under target for 7 days. Hold: the rest 4) Write the dollar change for each: cut to zero, hold, or raise by the step in 11-03 5) Mark changes that need the founder under the approval rules 6) Log every call with the reason so next Monday can check it
  - Output: a Monday list: every ad set with cut, hold, scale or not read yet, the dollar change and the reason. Next: feeds 11-03 (scale steps), 11-04 (postmortem of cuts) and 00-today.
  - Watch: share of spend in ad sets above target after 3 times target spend (7 day, Meta Ads), then blended cost per new customer, 28 day, then gross profit.
- **11-02 Tell the buyer for each new ad after 2 days whether to kill it or wait, by showing how far it is from the read spend and whether its hook and click rate are already below the kill line** Score 46, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: daily for ads launched in the last 7 days. Enablers: spend-rules.
  - Inputs: daily ad-level spend, impressions, 3 second views, clicks, purchases (connected, Meta Ads); account medians for hook rate and click rate (enabler, creative-archive); target cost per purchase (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Show each new ad's spend as a share of 3 times target 2) Kill early only if hook rate is under half the account median after 2,000 impressions 3) Otherwise wait, and show the date it will reach read spend at the current pace 4) If it will never reach read spend, flag the budget, not the ad
  - Output: a daily new ad list: kill, wait with a read date, or budget too small. Next: feeds 11-15 (test reads) and 09-03 (Monday verdict).
  - Watch: share of killed ads that had reached read spend (28 day, Meta Ads), then hit rate of new ads, then gross profit.
- **11-03 Raise the budget of each ad set that beat target for 7 days in steps sized to its read window, check cost per purchase after each step, and step back if it breaks** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when 11-01 marks an ad set scale. Enablers: spend-rules.
  - Inputs: ad set daily spend and cost per purchase, 14 days (connected, Meta Ads); the ceiling on cost per new customer (enabler, margin-view); monthly budget cap (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Set the step so the ad set can spend 3 times target in the next read window 2) Raise once, then wait for the read window before the next step 3) After each step, compare cost per purchase with the ceiling 4) Step back to the last good budget if it goes over the ceiling for 3 days 5) Fork new creative into a new ad set; never edit the winning ad set's ads
  - Output: a scale log per ad set: each step, its date and cost per purchase after it. Next: feeds 11-09 (monthly review) and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: cost per purchase in the 7 days after each budget step (7 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week at or under the ceiling, then revenue.
- **11-04 Break every cut ad set's spend down by age, gender, placement and region, find the cells that still bought near target, and relaunch the best creative only in those cells with a capped budget** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, for ad sets marked cut. Enablers: creative-postmortem, segment-map.
  - Inputs: breakdowns by age, gender, placement and region with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads); postmortem of the ads in the set (enabler, creative-postmortem); target cost per purchase (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Lay out a grid of spend and cost per purchase by age and gender, then by placement 2) Mark cells under target with at least 10 purchases 3) Mark cells that took over 20 percent of spend with no purchases 4) Relaunch the ad in the good cells with a new first frame if the postmortem says hook 5) Cap the relaunch at 3 times target and read it like a new test
  - Output: a segment grid per cut ad set and a capped relaunch in the cells that bought. Next: feeds 11-12 (segment ad sets) and 09-06 (fail note).
  - Watch: cost per purchase of relaunched ad sets (14 day, Meta Ads), then wasted spend per month, then gross profit.
- **11-05 Read spend and purchases by placement over 28 days, and remove placements that take over 5 percent of spend with cost per purchase above 2 times target** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 1st of each month. Enablers: segment-map, spend-rules.
  - Inputs: placement breakdown with spend, purchases and value, 28 days (connected, Meta Ads); TikTok placement report, when the brand runs TikTok (setup, TikTok Ads); target cost per purchase (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute in code cost per purchase and share of spend per placement 2) Flag placements over 5 percent of spend at over 2 times target 3) Check the ads there have a format made for that placement before removing it 4) Exclude the placement or make a format for it, and log which 5) Recheck in 28 days
  - Output: a placement table with the placements removed or given their own format. Next: feeds 09-10 (formats for placements that should work).
  - Watch: share of spend in placements over 2 times target (28 day, Meta Ads), then blended cost per purchase, then gross profit.
### T2. When Meta shows a great week but the store orders and the bank do not agree, help me see what the ads really made and set how much a new customer may cost, so I do not scale on a number that is not real

Metric: gap between Meta-reported purchases and store orders from paid social, and cost per new customer from store data

- **11-06 Compare Meta-reported purchases and value with store orders from paid social each week, per campaign, and show the gap and the store-side cost per new customer** Score 46, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and before any budget raise over 20 percent. Enablers: margin-view, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: Meta purchases and value by campaign, 7 day click and 1 day view split (connected, Meta Ads); orders with UTMs, new or returning, and gross profit (connected, Shopify); sessions by source and campaign (connected, GA4); numbers not to trust and since when (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Match store orders to campaigns by UTM; count new customers apart from repeat buyers 2) Put Meta purchases next to store orders per campaign and compute the gap in percent 3) Show 1 day view purchases as a separate column, not added in 4) Compute store-side cost per new customer and marketing efficiency (store revenue divided by total ad spend) 5) If the gap moves over 20 points in a week, flag tracking to 22-measurement
  - Output: a weekly table per campaign: Meta purchases, store orders, gap, new customers and store-side cost per new customer. Next: feeds 11-07 (ceiling), 11-09 (agency check) and 22-measurement.
  - Watch: gap between Meta purchases and store orders from paid social (7 day, Meta Ads and Shopify), then budget decisions made on store numbers, then gross profit.
- **11-07 Set the most a new customer may cost for each product line from first order gross profit and 12 month repeat buying, and put it in every scale and cut rule** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter or after a price change. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders with product, discount, shipping and returns, 12 months (connected, Shopify); landed cost per SKU (founder, finance shelf); how long the founder can wait to earn back a customer (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) Compute gross profit on the first order per product line 2) Compute repeat gross profit per customer at 90 days and 12 months by first product 3) Set two ceilings: break even on the first order, and break even within the founder's payback window 4) Founder picks one per line and signs it 5) Write the ceilings into the finance shelf and into 11-01 and 11-03
  - Output: a signed ceiling for cost per new customer per product line. Next: feeds 11-01, 11-03 and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: share of spend in ad sets above their line's ceiling (28 day, Meta Ads), then contribution per new customer, then contribution margin.
- **11-08 Split each product's revenue from ads by gross margin and set a return on spend target per product, so low margin products stop getting the same target as high margin ones** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once a quarter, and when catalog ads run over 30 percent of spend. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: product-level ad spend and value, including catalog ads (connected, Meta Ads); gross margin per product (enabler, margin-view); product sets in the catalog (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Compute in code break even return on spend per product: 1 divided by gross margin 2) Group products into 3 margin bands 3) Build a product set per band and give each its own target 4) Show spend share per band before and after
  - Output: a target per margin band and the catalog split into 3 product sets. Next: feeds 11-01 (targets per band).
  - Watch: share of spend on the low margin band (28 day, Meta Ads), then gross profit from catalog ads, then gross profit.
- **11-09 Check the agency's changes each month against store profit: list every budget, audience and ad change from the account log and what cost per new customer did in the 14 days after** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 3rd of each month, before the agency call. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: account activity log of changes (connected, Meta Ads); store-side cost per new customer by week (enabler, funnel-mix); the agency's monthly report and fee (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every change with its date and who made it 2) Group into budget, audience, ad and setting changes 3) Show store-side cost per new customer 14 days before and after each group 4) Compare with what the report claims 5) Write 3 questions for the agency call
  - Output: a monthly change log with before and after store numbers and 3 questions for the agency. Next: feeds 26-reports and 23-team-ops.
  - Watch: store-side cost per new customer month on month (28 day, Shopify and Meta Ads), then agency fee per new customer, then gross profit.
### T3. When revenue from ads looks strong but new customers are flat, help me move spend toward people who have never bought, at the right awareness level and in the segments that buy, so I grow the customer base instead of paying for repeat orders

Metric: new customers per week from paid social, and share of spend reaching past buyers

- **11-10 Split spend each week into prospecting, retargeting and past buyers, show how much reaches people who already bought, and exclude recent buyers from every cold campaign** Score 42, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and when new customers are flat 2 weeks while revenue rises. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: campaigns, ad sets and audiences with spend (connected, Meta Ads); new vs returning orders by campaign (connected, Shopify); customer list for exclusions, refreshed weekly (connected, Shopify); the repurchase cycle per product (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Label each ad set prospecting, retargeting or past buyers 2) Compute spend share and new customer share per label 3) Find the share of cold ad set purchases that were returning customers 4) Add or refresh the buyer exclusion, sized to the repurchase cycle 5) Keep past buyer spend in one campaign so it cannot bleed into cold
  - Output: a funnel split: spend and new customers per stage, and exclusions in place. Next: feeds 11-11 (awareness budget) and 14-email-messaging (past buyers).
  - Watch: share of cold purchases from returning customers (14 day, Shopify), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **11-12 Give the 2 best segments by age, gender and placement their own ads written for them, and list the segments with high reach where no ad speaks to them** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every other Monday. Enablers: segment-map.
  - Inputs: breakdowns by age, gender, placement and region, 28 days (connected, Meta Ads); copy sets written for each segment cell (10-04) (enabler, segment-map); avatars and share of orders per avatar (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Rank segment cells by cost per new customer, with at least 30 purchases 2) Add the tailored copy and creative for the top 2 cells to the live broad ad set, not a narrow one 3) List cells with over 10 percent of reach and under half their share of purchases 4) Send those cells to 10-copy as the next brief 5) Compare cost per purchase in each cell before and after 14 days
  - Output: tailored ads live for the 2 best cells and a list of cells nobody speaks to. Next: feeds 10-04 (next segment copy) and 02-customers-avatars.
  - Watch: cost per purchase in the top 2 segment cells (14 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **11-11 Move 15 percent of prospecting spend to unaware and problem aware ads for 4 weeks as a test, reading new customers and store-side cost per new customer against the old split** Score 36, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: when the heat map shows under 20 percent of spend on unaware and problem aware ads. Enablers: awareness-map, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: the heat map and gap line (enabler, awareness-map); at least 4 live top of funnel ads from the 09-12 briefs (enabler, awareness-map); new customer orders by week (connected, Shopify); founder sign-off on a 4 week test (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Read spend share and cost per new customer by awareness level from the heat map 2) Put the top of funnel ads in their own ad set with 15 percent of prospecting budget 3) Point them to pages at the same level, not the checkout 4) Read weekly on new customers and store-side cost per new customer, not on last click return 5) At 4 weeks, keep, grow or roll back, and log it in the ledger
  - Output: a 4 week budget test with a keep, grow or roll back call. Next: feeds 09-06 (T0 rule on awareness mix) and 22-measurement.
  - Watch: share of spend on unaware and problem aware ads (28 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week and cost per new customer, then revenue.
- **11-13 Each month, launch the 10 search-sourced hooks as a Meta test, and send the 3 Meta angles with the lowest cost per new customer to Google Ads and the SEO list** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: on the 1st of each month, after the bridge sheet is written. Enablers: signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: the monthly bridge sheet (enabler, signal-bridge); Meta angles with store-side cost per new customer (connected, Meta Ads and Shopify); Google search terms and Amazon review phrases (connected, Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Launch the 10 search-sourced hooks in one ad set on the best performing body 2) Read at 3 times target spend and keep the hooks that beat the account median 3) Send the 3 best Meta angles to 12-google-ads as headline tests and to 17-seo as article topics 4) Log each line's source and result so the bridge learns which source works
  - Output: one Meta test of search-sourced hooks and 3 angles handed to Google and SEO. Next: feeds 12-google-ads, 17-seo and 10-03 (next month's bridge).
  - Watch: hit rate of search-sourced hooks vs other hooks (28 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer across channels, then revenue.
### T4. When new ads, creators or a new product go live next week, help me check every link and rule before spend starts and set each test up to end with a clear yes or no, so I do not waste launch day or pay for tests that end with not sure

Metric: share of tests read as a clear win or loss, and launch-day errors

- **11-14 Check every ad in tomorrow's launch before spend starts: the link opens and has UTMs, the pixel fires, the product is in stock, the offer is live, the claims passed and the rights are valid** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: the day before every launch. Enablers: launch-preflight, claim-guard.
  - Inputs: ads in draft with URLs and UTMs (connected, Meta Ads); stock per product (connected, Shopify); pixel and conversion API event health (connected, Meta Ads); claim check results and rights end dates (enabler, claim-guard).
  - Steps: 1) Open each URL on a phone and check it loads the right product or page 2) Check UTMs follow the naming rule 3) Check the purchase event fired in the last 24 hours with value 4) Check each product has over 14 days of stock and each offer is live in the store 5) Check claims passed 10-07 and rights run past 30 days 6) Return a pass or a list of fixes; nothing launches with a fail
  - Output: a launch sheet with pass or fix per ad, signed before launch. Next: feeds 11-15 (test launch).
  - Watch: ads launched with a broken link, no stock or a failed claim (per launch, launch-preflight), then launch day spend wasted, then risk.
- **11-15 Launch each creative test, such as the weekly AI video stack, as one ad set per concept with the winner line and read date written first, cap each ad's share, and log the result as a win, a loss or not tested** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every launch, and every Tuesday for the AI video stack. Enablers: spend-rules, win-loss-ledger, video-stack.
  - Inputs: the 3 picked cuts or variants (enabler, video-stack); target cost per purchase and budget (brain, finance shelf); the testing campaign (connected, Meta Ads); past test results (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write the winner line before launch, e.g. 'under $32 per purchase at $96 spend' 2) Put the concept in its own ad set in the testing campaign, never in scaling 3) Set the budget so each ad can reach 3 times target in 7 days 4) Read on the read date; under read spend is logged as not tested 5) Move winners to the scaling campaign as a new ad set; log losers with a fail note 6) Note which cut length won, 15, 30 or 30 to 45 seconds
  - Output: a launched test with a written winner line, a read date and a logged result. Next: feeds 09-06 (T0 or fail note) and 11-03 (scale).
  - Watch: share of tests read as a clear win or loss (28 day, win-loss-ledger), then hit rate of new ads, then gross profit.
- **11-17 Test the top ad sending to a page that repeats its promise against the same ad sending to the product page, split 50 and 50, for 14 days** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when 10-11 scores a top 10 ad 1 or less for match. Enablers: congruence-check.
  - Inputs: the match scores for the top 10 ads (enabler, congruence-check); a page built to repeat the ad's promise (setup, page builder in the store theme); page conversion by ad (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Duplicate the ad with the new URL into the same ad set 2) Split budget evenly and cap each ad's share 3) Read cost per purchase and page conversion on each URL at 14 days 4) Keep the winner; if the matched page wins, add the rule to T0 and brief pages for the next 3 ads
  - Output: a page test result for the top ad and a rule on which page each ad type should send to. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro and 09-06.
  - Watch: conversion rate of paid sessions on the tested page (14 day, GA4), then cost per purchase on the top ad, then revenue.
- **11-16 Run the best creator videos as partnership ads through the creator's handle, and compare cost per purchase with the same video run from the brand's handle** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when a creator video beats the account median hook rate, and monthly. Enablers: creator-roster.
  - Inputs: partnership ad permission from each creator (founder, creators); creator ranking by purchases (enabler, creator-roster); the video, run from the brand handle, with results (connected, Meta Ads); rights end dates (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the 3 creator videos with the best purchases per 1,000 views 2) Request partnership access on each creator's handle 3) Launch each video twice in one ad set: creator handle and brand handle 4) Read both at 3 times target spend 5) Keep the handle that wins and put it in the creator's record
  - Output: 3 handle tests with a result per creator, logged on the roster. Next: feeds 09-18 (creator ranking) and 09-19 (next briefs).
  - Watch: cost per purchase of creator handle vs brand handle (14 day, Meta Ads), then cost per new customer from creator content, then revenue.
### T5. When a best seller has 10 days of stock left, a sale is coming, or slow stock ties up cash, help me move spend to what we can ship and plan the ramp and the stop, so I do not pay for buyers who cannot buy

Metric: ad spend on products with under 14 days of stock

- **11-18 Each morning, list products with under 14 days of stock and the ad spend behind them, move that spend to products that can ship, and plan the restart for when stock returns** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: daily. Enablers: ad-stock-link.
  - Inputs: stock per SKU and sales per day, 28 days (connected, Shopify); ad spend by product, including catalog ads (connected, Meta Ads); incoming stock dates (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute days of stock per SKU in code: units on hand divided by sales per day 2) List SKUs under 14 days with the ad spend and ads behind them 3) Propose moving the spend to the nearest product with over 45 days of stock 4) Pull the SKU from catalog product sets when it is under 5 days 5) When stock lands, restart the paused ads in their old ad sets and watch cost for 3 days
  - Output: a daily stock and spend list with the moves to make and a restart plan. Next: feeds 10-12 (sold out line sweep) and 19-supply-inventory.
  - Watch: ad spend on SKUs with under 14 days of stock (7 day, Meta Ads and Shopify), then revenue lost to sellouts, then revenue.
- **11-19 Plan each peak sale 3 weeks out: the budget ramp by day, the ads ready and tested, the stop date, and the cut-back the morning after the sale ends** Score 40, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 3 weeks before each peak on the calendar. Enablers: spend-rules, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: peak dates and what went wrong last year (brain, calendar shelf); last year's daily spend and cost per purchase in the same weeks (connected, Meta Ads); the sale offer and its end time (brain, offers shelf); stock for the sale products (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Set a daily budget curve: warm up 10 to 14 days before, peak on sale days, stop at the end time 2) Test sale ads 2 weeks before so winners are known on day 1 3) Grow the retargeting and email lists in the warm up weeks 4) Check stock covers the planned orders at the ramp budget 5) Schedule the cut-back for the morning after and the sale line sweep
  - Output: a dated peak plan: budget per day, ads ready, stock check and the stop date. Next: feeds 10-17 (gift copy), 10-12 (sweep) and 00-today.
  - Watch: cost per purchase in the sale week vs plan (sale week, Meta Ads), then sale revenue and gross profit, then revenue.
- **11-20 Point a capped share of spend at slow stock with a bundle or clearance ad, and stop when margin per order falls below the floor or stock reaches 60 days of cover** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: when a SKU has over 180 days of stock. Enablers: ad-stock-link, margin-view.
  - Inputs: days of stock per SKU (connected, Shopify); margin per order and the floor (brain, finance shelf); a bundle or clearance offer the founder approves (founder, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List SKUs with over 180 days of stock and the cash tied in each 2) Build a bundle with a best seller, or a clearance offer inside the discount floor 3) Run it in its own ad set capped at 10 percent of spend 4) Stop at the margin floor or 60 days of cover 5) Report cash freed and gross profit per order
  - Output: a capped slow stock campaign with cash freed and margin per order tracked. Next: feeds 21-finance-cash and 19-supply-inventory.
  - Watch: units sold of the slow SKUs per week (7 day, Shopify), then cash tied in stock, then cash.

## 12-google-ads Google Ads

### T1. When Google reports a 6 times return and I suspect most of it is people typing our name, help me see what searches without our name really return and how many new customers they bring, so I judge Google on the buyers it finds, not the ones who were coming anyway

Metric: non-brand return on ad spend and new customers from Google non-brand, per month

- **12-01 Split last 90 days of Search, Shopping and Performance Max into brand and non-brand, and show spend, sales, return and new customer share for each** Score 42, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once at onboarding, then on the 1st of each month. Enablers: search-term-sorter, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: search terms and Performance Max search insights, last 90 days (connected, Google Ads); brand name, product names and common misspellings (brain, products shelf); orders marked new or returning buyer (connected, Shopify); brand and non-brand sorting of every term (enabler, search-term-sorter).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 90 days of search terms per campaign type 2) Tag each term brand or non-brand, and list the 20 biggest brand terms found inside non-brand campaigns 3) Sum spend, sales, return and new customer share for each side 4) Put the dollars of brand spend hiding in cold campaigns on one line 5) Founder reads the split and signs the non-brand return floor
  - Output: a 2 row table (brand, non-brand) by campaign type, with spend, sales, return and new customer share, plus the brand leak in dollars. Next: feeds 12-04 (cost per new customer ceiling) and 12-18 (Monday check).
  - Watch: non-brand return on ad spend, brand excluded (30 day, Google Ads), then new customers from Google non-brand per month, then gross profit.
- **12-04 Set a cost per new customer ceiling for Google non-brand from first order margin, and flag mid-month when it is crossed** Score 42, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once, then checked on the 15th of each month. Enablers: margin-view, order-match.
  - Inputs: gross profit per first order by product (brain, finance shelf); how much of a first order's profit the founder will spend to win it (founder, decisions and goals shelf); non-brand spend and store-matched new customers month to date (enabler, order-match); monthly Google budget plan (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the ceiling: first order profit times the share the founder will spend 2) Each 15th, divide month to date non-brand spend by store-matched new customers 3) Project where spend and cost per new customer will land at month end 4) If over the ceiling, list the 3 campaigns driving it and the budget change for each 5) Founder approves any cut over $500
  - Output: a ceiling number and a mid-month flag with 3 named budget changes. Next: feeds 12-18 (Monday check) and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: non-brand cost per new customer vs the ceiling (month to date, Google Ads), then customers bought at a profit, then contribution margin.
- **12-02 Match the purchases Google claims each week to store orders, and mark each as new or returning, kept or returned** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: order-match.
  - Inputs: Google Ads conversions by campaign with click ids (connected, Google Ads); orders with UTM, click id, new vs returning and refunds (connected, Shopify); GA4 purchases by source (connected, GA4); matching rules by click id, UTM and date (enabler, order-match).
  - Steps: 1) Pull last week's Google conversions and store orders 2) Match by click id first, then UTM and date 3) Count matched, Google only, store only, returned and returning buyer orders per campaign 4) Flag any campaign where store-matched sales are 25 percent off Google's count 5) Check 10 matches by hand and log any rule that is wrong
  - Output: a weekly table per campaign: Google says, store sees, new buyers, returned, gap in percent. Next: feeds 12-03 (brand test), 12-19 (scaling gates) and 22-measurement.
  - Watch: gap between Google purchases and store-matched purchases (7 day, Google Ads), then trust in Google's return and the right bids, then gross profit.
- **12-03 Test whether brand search buys sales we would get anyway: pause brand search in half of matched regions for 4 weeks and compare orders** Score 32, ready 4/5, weeks.
  - When: once, when brand spend is over 20 percent of Google spend and few rivals bid on our name. Enablers: funnel-mix, order-match.
  - Inputs: auction insights on brand terms: who bids and how often (connected, Google Ads); orders by state or region, last 12 weeks (connected, Shopify); organic clicks on brand queries by region (connected, Search Console); founder sign-off on a 4 week test (founder, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Read auction insights: if a rival shows on our name over 20 percent of the time, stop and defend instead 2) Pick 2 groups of regions with matching order trends over 12 weeks 3) Pause brand search in one group only 4) After 4 weeks, compare total orders and brand organic clicks in both groups 5) Write the verdict: keep, cut or bid lower, with dollars saved or lost per month
  - Output: a 4 week holdout read with orders lost or kept, and a keep or cut decision. Next: feeds 12-19 (scaling gates) and 13-12 (affiliates on brand terms).
  - Watch: orders in paused regions vs control regions (28 day, Shopify), then spend saved on clicks we would get free, then gross profit.
### T2. When the Google bill arrives and I cannot see what I paid for, help me find the searches, products and Performance Max corners that take money and never sell, so I stop paying for traffic that was never going to buy

Metric: share of Google spend on search terms and products with no sale in 30 days

- **12-05 Pull every search term with spend and no sale in 90 days, sort by cost, and add the clear misses as negatives with a reason** Score 42, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once, then every 2 weeks. Enablers: search-term-sorter.
  - Inputs: search terms with cost, clicks and purchases, 90 days (connected, Google Ads); current negative keyword lists (connected, Google Ads); products we sell and do not sell (brain, products shelf); junk and wrong-intent sorting (enabler, search-term-sorter).
  - Steps: 1) Pull terms with 10 or more clicks and 0 purchases 2) Sort by cost and tag each: wrong product, job seeker, free, how to, rival, maybe 3) A person checks the top 50 by hand so no term that sells elsewhere is blocked 4) Add clear misses as phrase or exact negatives with the reason and date 5) Leave the maybes for 30 more days and recheck
  - Output: a negative list with reasons, and dollars of 90 day waste it would have stopped. Next: feeds 12-18 (Monday check) and 12-09 (funnel stage split).
  - Watch: spend on terms with no sale (30 day, Google Ads), then non-brand return on ad spend, then gross profit.
- **12-06 Map where Performance Max money goes (brand, past buyers, video, display, auto-made assets) and turn off the settings nobody chose** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: funnel-mix, search-term-sorter.
  - Inputs: Performance Max channel report and search insights (connected, Google Ads); account settings: search partners, display expansion, auto-apply, location option (connected, Google Ads); customer list for new customer goal and exclusions (connected, Shopify); Google Ads ideas on default settings and Performance Max (have, ideas/v1/12-GAD-google-ads.json).
  - Steps: 1) Pull Performance Max spend by channel and the share of search insights that are brand terms 2) List every default that leaks: search partners, display expansion, auto-apply, presence or interest, auto-made text and video 3) Price each leak in 30 day spend and sales 4) Add brand exclusions and the new customer goal where the numbers say so 5) Founder approves the change list before anyone clicks
  - Output: a one page map: Performance Max spend by channel and brand share, plus a priced list of settings to switch. Next: feeds 12-07 (margin split) and 12-19 (scaling gates).
  - Watch: share of Performance Max spend on brand terms and past buyers (30 day, Google Ads), then new customers per dollar in Performance Max, then gross profit.
- **12-08 Pull Google spend off any product with under 14 days of stock and put it back the day stock lands** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: daily after the stock sync. Enablers: feed-doctor.
  - Inputs: stock and 14 day sales rate per SKU (connected, Shopify); restock dates (brain, operations shelf); product-level Shopping spend (connected, Google Ads); stock custom label kept current (enabler, feed-doctor).
  - Steps: 1) Compute days of stock per SKU from the 14 day sales rate 2) Label products under 14 days as low stock in the feed 3) Exclude low stock products from Shopping and Performance Max listing groups 4) Remove the label when stock lands and log the dates 5) Send a weekly list of spend saved and restocks waiting
  - Output: a daily low stock label and a weekly note of spend held back. Next: feeds 19-supply-inventory.
  - Watch: spend on products under 14 days of stock (7 day, Google Ads), then no ad dollars on products we cannot ship, then cash.
- **12-07 Label every Shopping product by margin tier and by sell or no-sell, then split campaigns so money moves from 20 no-sale products to the ones that sell** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: margin-view, feed-doctor.
  - Inputs: product-level Shopping and Performance Max report, 30 and 90 days (connected, Google Ads); unit cost and margin per SKU (brain, finance shelf); stock per SKU (connected, Shopify); custom labels written into the feed (enabler, feed-doctor).
  - Steps: 1) Mark each product high, mid or low margin 2) Mark products with 50 or more clicks and no sale in 30 days as no-sale 3) Write both labels into the feed as custom labels 4) Split into campaigns by label, with a return target per tier from its margin 5) Read in 4 weeks: spend share and profit per tier
  - Output: a labelled feed and a campaign split by margin tier, with no-sale products capped. Next: feeds 12-08 (stock pull) and 12-18 (Monday check).
  - Watch: share of Shopping spend on high margin products (28 day, Google Ads), then gross profit from Shopping, then gross profit.
### T3. When almost all Google spend sits on our name and ready-to-buy searches, help me split spend by top, middle and bottom of funnel and move what sells on Meta and in search between the two, so I reach buyers before they know our name

Metric: share of Google spend on top and middle of funnel searches, and new customers from them

- **12-11 Turn the top 20 non-brand search terms that sell into 20 Meta hooks in the searcher's own words** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, before the Meta creative round. Enablers: signal-bridge, search-term-sorter, copy-decomposer.
  - Inputs: non-brand search terms with purchases, 90 days (connected, Google Ads); Search Console queries with clicks to product pages (connected, Search Console); avatars and pain points (brain, customers shelf); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the top 20 non-brand terms by purchases and the top 20 queries by clicks 2) Group them into pain, use, feature and comparison 3) Map each group to one avatar and pain pair 4) Write 2 hooks per group in the searcher's words, checked against allowed claims 5) Founder strikes any hook that does not sound like us 6) Hand the sheet to 11-paid-social as one hook test batch
  - Output: a hook sheet: 10 groups, 20 hooks, each with its source term and Google conversion rate. Next: feeds 11-paid-social (hook test) and 17-10 (article briefs).
  - Watch: thumb stop rate and cost per purchase of search-born hooks vs control (14 day, Meta Ads), then new customers from Meta, then revenue.
- **12-09 Tag every Google search term by funnel stage (problem, category, comparison, product, brand) and show spend and new customer share per stage next to Meta's split** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then on the 1st of each month. Enablers: awareness-map, funnel-mix, search-term-sorter.
  - Inputs: search terms with spend and purchases, 90 days (connected, Google Ads); orders marked new or returning (connected, Shopify); Meta spend by awareness level (enabler, awareness-map); funnel rubric: 5 example terms per stage (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each term: problem words are top, category and 'best' or 'vs' are middle, product and brand are bottom 2) A person checks the 50 highest spend tags and fixes the rubric 3) Sum spend, sales and new customer share for top, middle and bottom 4) Lay Google's split next to Meta's awareness split 5) Write the gap line: the stage where we spend least and new customers are cheapest
  - Output: a funnel table: 3 stages by spend, sales, return and new customer share, beside Meta's, plus the gap line. Next: feeds 12-10 (top of funnel test), 12-11 (terms into Meta hooks) and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: share of Google spend on top and middle of funnel terms (28 day, Google Ads), then new customers from Google per month, then revenue.
- **12-13 Rewrite headlines on the top 10 non-brand ad groups to lead with product, price or offer where rivals show clearer prices, and check the landing page says the same** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then when auction insights show a new rival. Enablers: congruence-check, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: auction insights and top of page rate for top 10 terms (connected, Google Ads); mobile screenshots of the results page for the top 10 terms (setup, a person, 30 minutes); live prices, offers and shipping (brain, offers shelf); landing page per ad group (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Screenshot the results page for the top 10 terms on a phone 2) Note each rival's price, offer and shipping line 3) Write 3 headlines per group with product and price or offer first 4) Check each landing page shows the same price and offer above the fold, and fix it if not 5) Launch as ad variations and read at 3 weeks
  - Output: 10 ad groups with new headlines and matched landing pages. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro (congruence fixes).
  - Watch: click rate on the top 10 non-brand terms (21 day, Google Ads), then non-brand sales at the same spend, then revenue.
- **12-12 Bring the 5 angles that won on Meta in the last 60 days into Google search ads and Performance Max assets, as a 50/50 test** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly, after the Meta round is read. Enablers: signal-bridge, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: Meta winners with angle and hook tags (enabler, creative-tags); responsive search ad asset ratings (connected, Google Ads); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf); voice rules (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List the 5 Meta angles with the lowest cost per new customer 2) Write 3 headlines of 30 characters and 2 descriptions per angle 3) Replace only assets rated low, keep the best 4) Run as a 50/50 ad variation in the top 3 non-brand ad groups 5) Read at 4 weeks on click rate and conversion rate
  - Output: an ad variation test live in 3 ad groups, with the angle each headline came from. Next: feeds 12-13 (price and offer headlines) and 17-06 (title rewrites).
  - Watch: click rate and conversion rate of non-brand search ads (28 day, Google Ads), then non-brand sales, then revenue.
- **12-10 Run one top of funnel test on YouTube or Demand Gen with the 3 best problem aware videos from Meta, past buyers excluded, read at 4 weeks on brand search lift and new customers** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once Search and Shopping pass the scaling gates in 12-19. Enablers: funnel-mix, awareness-map, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: Meta videos tagged unaware or problem aware with hold rate and cost per new customer (enabler, creative-archive); brand search impressions baseline, 8 weeks (connected, Search Console); budget cap for tests (brain, finance shelf); customer list to exclude (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Stop if the gates in 12-19 are not met and write why 2) Pick 3 Meta videos tagged unaware or problem aware with the best hold rate 3) Recut the first 5 seconds so the product shows by second 3 and the problem is said out loud 4) Set a weekly budget of at least 10 times the target cost per new customer, past buyers excluded 5) Founder approves the budget and the stop line 6) At 4 weeks, read brand search lift and store new customers, not Google's last click
  - Output: a live top of funnel test with a budget, a stop line and a read date. Next: feeds 11-paid-social (which videos travel) and 12-19 (next budget step).
  - Watch: brand search impressions vs the 8 week baseline (28 day, Search Console), then new customers who did not know us, then revenue.
### T4. When best sellers drop out of Shopping for a missing detail or a price mismatch, help me catch it the same day and fix the feed, so I stop losing sales I never see

Metric: share of top 50 products approved and serving in Shopping

- **12-14 Check the Merchant Center feed every morning for disapprovals, price and stock mismatch and missing details, ranked by each product's sales, and fix best sellers the same day** Score 42, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: daily. Enablers: feed-doctor.
  - Inputs: Merchant Center diagnostics (connected, Merchant Center via Google Ads); live prices, sale prices and stock (connected, Shopify); sales per product, 30 days (connected, Shopify); feed check rules (enabler, feed-doctor).
  - Steps: 1) Pull disapprovals, warnings and limited items 2) Compare feed price, sale price and stock with the live site 3) Rank problems by the product's 30 day sales 4) Send the top 5 fixes with the exact field to change 5) Recheck the next morning and log the time to fix
  - Output: a morning list of feed problems ranked by sales at stake, with the field to fix. Next: feeds 12-18 (Monday check) and 18-19 (AI shopping feed).
  - Watch: share of top 50 products approved and serving (7 day, Merchant Center), then Shopping sales, then revenue.
- **12-17 Check rival Shopping prices on our top 20 products each week and pull back spend where a rival is 20 percent cheaper** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: feed-doctor, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: price competitiveness report (connected, Merchant Center via Google Ads); rival list (brain, competitors shelf); margin per SKU and lowest price allowed (brain, finance shelf); click rate and sales per product (connected, Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull benchmark prices for the top 20 products 2) Flag any product where a rival is 20 percent or more cheaper 3) For flagged products, cut the bid or move to the low tier if click rate or sales fell 4) Send the list to the founder for a price or offer call where margin allows 5) Log each call with the date
  - Output: a weekly price gap list with the action taken on each product. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing.
  - Watch: cost per sale on flagged products (14 day, Google Ads), then profit on Shopping, then gross profit.
- **12-15 Rewrite the Shopping titles of 20 products that sell on Meta but get few Shopping views, to lead with the words buyers type** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: feed-doctor, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: Shopping impressions and clicks per product (connected, Google Ads); search terms per product (connected, Google Ads); Search Console queries to product pages (connected, Search Console); Meta sales per product (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) List 20 products with good Meta sales and low Shopping impressions 2) For each, pull the terms that already bring clicks and the ones rivals use 3) Write the title as type, key feature, size or colour, brand, in under 70 characters for the front 4) A person checks each title is true to the product 5) Push to the feed as a supplemental file and read impressions at 3 weeks
  - Output: 20 new titles in the feed, each with the terms it now leads with. Next: feeds 17-13 (rename pages in buyers' words).
  - Watch: Shopping impressions for the 20 products (21 day, Google Ads), then Shopping sales, then revenue.
- **12-16 Switch on the free Merchant Center extras we never used: free listings, promotions, shipping and returns, product ratings and store rating** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then checked each quarter. Enablers: feed-doctor.
  - Inputs: Merchant Center settings (connected, Merchant Center via Google Ads); reviews app product ratings feed (connected, reviews app); return window and shipping times (brain, offers shelf); live promotions calendar (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List each free extra and whether it is on 2) Connect the product ratings feed from the reviews app 3) Enter shipping and return policy so badges can show 4) Load the next month's promotions 5) Recheck in 30 days that stars, badges and promos show
  - Output: a checklist of extras switched on, with the date each went live. Next: feeds 17-20 (stars and prices in search).
  - Watch: free listing clicks (28 day, Merchant Center), then Shopping click rate at no cost, then revenue.
### T5. When I run Google myself or check the freelancer, help me look at 5 numbers in 20 minutes each Monday and add money only when the account has earned it, so I keep it safe without becoming an expert

Metric: weeks the Monday check was done, and budget changes made with a written reason

- **12-18 Run a 20 minute Monday check on 5 numbers (non-brand return, new customers, spend vs plan, wasted terms, feed errors) and make one change at most** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: funnel-mix, order-match, feed-doctor.
  - Inputs: last week's spend, sales and search terms (connected, Google Ads); store-matched new customers (enabler, order-match); monthly budget plan (brain, finance shelf); feed error count (enabler, feed-doctor).
  - Steps: 1) Read the 5 numbers with last week and 4 week average side by side 2) Mark any number that moved more than 20 percent 3) Find the one cause behind the biggest move before touching bids 4) Make one change, write why, and set the date to read it 5) Log it in the decisions list
  - Output: a one page Monday card with 5 numbers, the one change and its read date. Next: feeds 26-reports and 00-today.
  - Watch: weeks the Monday check was done (4 week, decisions log), then steady non-brand return and fewer panic changes, then risk.
- **12-19 Pass 5 scaling gates before adding $5,000 a month to Google: tracking matches, non-brand over the floor for 8 weeks, feed clean, search impression share lost to budget, stock to cover** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: before any budget rise over 20 percent. Enablers: order-match, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: store-matched return and new customers, 8 weeks (enabler, order-match); impression share lost to budget per campaign (connected, Google Ads); stock cover for top products (connected, Shopify); floors and caps (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Check each of the 5 gates and mark pass or fail with the number 2) If any fail, write the fix and stop 3) If all pass, add money in steps of 20 percent a week to campaigns losing share to budget 4) Read cost per new customer each week and stop at the ceiling from 12-04
  - Output: a gate sheet with pass or fail and, if passed, a 4 week budget ramp. Next: feeds 12-10 (top of funnel test).
  - Watch: non-brand cost per new customer during the ramp (7 day, Google Ads), then more new customers at a profit, then revenue.
- **12-20 Get Google ready 3 weeks before Black Friday: budget ramp, promotions in the feed, stock cover, and landing pages that match the ads** Score 38, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 21 days before each peak on the calendar. Enablers: feed-doctor, congruence-check.
  - Inputs: peak dates and offers (brain, calendar shelf); offer terms (brain, offers shelf); stock for top 30 products (connected, Shopify); last year's peak spend and sales by day (connected, Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Set the daily budget ramp from last year's curve and this year's cap 2) Load promotions into Merchant Center 10 days ahead for approval 3) Check stock cover for the top 30 products and label low ones 4) Check each ad's offer matches its landing page 5) Founder signs the plan; a person checks it live on day 1
  - Output: a signed peak plan with budget by day, approved promotions and checked pages. Next: feeds 14-email-messaging and 11-paid-social peak plans.
  - Watch: Google sales on peak days vs plan (peak window, Google Ads), then peak revenue, then revenue.

## 13-affiliate-marketplace-ads Affiliate, marketplace and retail media

### T1. When the affiliate payout is due Friday, help me see which commissions are for orders that were returned, cancelled, fake or from past buyers, so I pay only for new sales that happened and stayed

Metric: commission paid on returned, cancelled or returning-buyer orders, per month

- **13-01 Match every commission to its store order before Friday's payout, and hold those for returned, cancelled, test or duplicate orders** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every week, 2 days before payout. Enablers: partner-ledger.
  - Inputs: commission report for the payout period (setup, affiliate network such as Impact, ShareASale or Refersion); orders with refunds, cancels and codes (connected, Shopify); return window and payout terms (brain, offers shelf); order match and hold rules (enabler, partner-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the commission report and the matching orders 2) Mark each row: kept, returned, cancelled, test or duplicate 3) Hold pay on rows still inside the return window 4) Reverse commissions on orders refunded after the last payout 5) A person reviews any hold over $200 before it is sent
  - Output: a payout file with held and reversed rows and the dollars saved. Next: feeds 13-04 (rates by new buyer share) and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: commission paid on returned or cancelled orders (30 day, affiliate network), then commission cost per kept order, then contribution margin.
- **13-03 Find creator codes leaked to coupon sites and stop paying commission on buyers who found the code at checkout** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly scan, and when code orders jump. Enablers: partner-ledger.
  - Inputs: orders with discount codes (connected, Shopify); a scan of top coupon sites for our codes (setup, a person, 20 minutes, or a code tool); code owner and terms (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List every code used in the last 30 days with orders and new buyer share 2) Search our brand on the top 5 coupon sites and note which codes appear 3) For leaked codes, retire them and give the creator a new unique code or link 4) Set commission on leaked-code orders to zero from the leak date if the terms allow 5) Tell the creator in plain words and log it
  - Output: a list of leaked codes retired and commission saved per month. Next: feeds 13-04 (rates).
  - Watch: orders on leaked codes (30 day, Shopify), then discount and commission leak, then contribution margin.
- **13-02 Check any partner whose orders jump 3 times in a week before paying: order source, click to order time, new vs returning, repeated address or card** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a partner's weekly orders are 3 times their 8 week average. Enablers: partner-ledger.
  - Inputs: partner clicks and orders by day (setup, affiliate network); orders with address, payment and new vs returning (connected, Shopify); fraud signs list (enabler, partner-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Flag the partner and freeze their pending commission 2) Check click to order time: under 10 seconds on many orders points to cookie stuffing 3) Check repeated addresses, cards or emails and the share of returning buyers 4) Ask the partner where the traffic came from and log the answer 5) Release, cut or remove, and write the reason
  - Output: a checked partner with a release or cut decision and the reason. Next: feeds 13-05 (who to keep).
  - Watch: commission paid to flagged partners before checks (30 day, affiliate network), then commission only on real orders, then risk.
- **13-05 Catch affiliates bidding on our brand name in Google, stop them and claw back the commission** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly. Enablers: partner-ledger, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: auction insights on brand terms (connected, Google Ads); brand search screenshots on phone and desktop (setup, a person, 10 minutes); affiliate terms on brand bidding (brain, creators shelf); partner orders from paid search (setup, affiliate network).
  - Steps: 1) Search our brand name and top product names and screenshot any ad not ours 2) Match the display URL or tracking link to a partner 3) Send the terms and ask them to stop in 48 hours 4) Reverse commission on orders from those clicks if the terms allow 5) Recheck the next week and log
  - Output: a log of brand bidders caught, stopped and commission recovered. Next: feeds 12-03 (brand search test).
  - Watch: partner ads on brand terms (7 day, Google Ads), then brand clicks we stop paying twice for, then gross profit.
- **13-04 Set commission rates by who brings new buyers: more for creators and content partners with high new buyer share, less for coupon and loyalty sites** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: partner-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: 90 days of partner orders with new vs returning (enabler, partner-ledger); first order profit per product (brain, finance shelf); current commission terms (brain, creators shelf); rate change sign-off (founder, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute new buyer share and profit after commission per partner 2) Group partners into 3 tiers by new buyer share 3) Set a rate per tier that keeps first order profit above the floor 4) Founder signs the table 5) Send 30 days notice with the new terms and the reason
  - Output: a 3 tier commission table with the profit check for each tier. Next: feeds 13-05 (who to keep) and 16-18 (creator seeding).
  - Watch: share of commission paid on new buyers (90 day, affiliate network), then new customers per commission dollar, then contribution margin.
### T2. When a creator's post outsells our studio ads, help me run it as a partnership ad, find more creators like them and pay a fair rate with the rights in writing, so I get proven ads cheaper than a new shoot

Metric: cost per new customer of partnership ads vs our own brand ads

- **13-11 Check the rights end date on every live creator ad each week and renew or pause 14 days before it runs out** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: creator-roster.
  - Inputs: live ads using creator content (connected, Meta Ads); rights terms and end dates (brain, creators shelf); a person who owns renewals (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) List live ads with creator content 2) Match each to its rights end date 3) Flag any ending within 14 days 4) Renew the winners and pause the rest before the date 5) Log each renewal with the new end date
  - Output: a weekly rights list with renew or pause on each ad. Next: none.
  - Watch: live creator ads past their rights date (7 day, Meta Ads), then no takedowns or surprise bills, then risk.
- **13-07 Run the 5 best creator posts as partnership ads through the creator's handle, head to head with our best brand ad** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: creator-roster, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: creator posts with written usage rights and partnership ad permission (setup, the creator); our best brand ad of the last 30 days (connected, Meta Ads); test budget cap (brain, finance shelf); allowed claims to check the creator's caption (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 5 posts with the best organic hold rate and saves 2) Get written rights for 60 or 90 days and the partnership ad permission 3) Check each caption against allowed claims and fix what is not true 4) Launch in one ad set with our best brand ad as control, same budget each 5) Read at 14 days on cost per new customer 6) Move winners into the main ad set and log the result on the roster
  - Output: a live partnership ad test with the winner named at day 14. Next: feeds 11-paid-social (scale winners) and 13-09 (roster).
  - Watch: cost per new customer, partnership ads vs brand ad (14 day, Meta Ads), then new customers at a lower cost, then revenue.
- **13-12 Check every partner post for claims we cannot back and wrong logo use, before it goes live and 48 hours after** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each partner post. Enablers: creator-roster, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: the draft or live post (setup, the creator); allowed and forbidden claims with proof (brain, claims shelf); brand kit rules (brain, content and brand kit shelf); FTC paid partner label rule (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Read the caption and script against allowed claims 2) Check the paid partner label is there 3) Check the logo and product shots follow the brand kit 4) Send exact fixes before posting, or within 48 hours if already live 5) Log any repeat problem on the roster
  - Output: a checked post with fixes sent and logged. Next: none.
  - Watch: partner posts with an unbacked claim or no label (30 day, creators shelf), then no platform strike or FTC letter, then risk.
- **13-06 Find 30 people already posting about us for free and rank them by fit, engagement and whether they bought more than once** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: creator-roster.
  - Inputs: tagged posts and mentions on Instagram (setup, Instagram via Meta Business); TikTok mentions (setup, TikTok); reviews with photos or video (connected, reviews app); customers with 2 or more orders (connected, Shopify); creator fit rules: niche, tone, audience (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the last 90 days of tags, mentions and photo reviews 2) Match names to customers to see who bought and how often 3) Score each on fit, engagement rate and repeat buying 4) A person watches the top 30 posts and drops any off-brand ones 5) Add the top 15 to the roster with the reason
  - Output: a ranked list of 30 free fans, top 15 on the roster with notes. Next: feeds 13-07 (partnership ads) and 16-18 (seeding).
  - Watch: creators on the roster who already buy (30 day, creators shelf), then cheaper, more believable partner content, then revenue.
- **13-09 Write one creator brief per winning principle: the hook, the pain, the proof, the do-not-say list, 3 example openings and cuts at 15, 30 and 45 seconds with raw footage** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: before each creator round. Enablers: principle-matcher, script-builder, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: winning principles from our ads (enabler, principle-matcher); avatars and pain points (brain, customers shelf); allowed and forbidden claims (brain, claims shelf); briefs that worked and failed before (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the 3 principles behind our best ads this quarter 2) Write one brief per principle with 3 example opening lines 3) Add the do-not-say list and the proof they may show 4) Ask for 15, 30 and 45 second cuts plus raw clips we can recut 5) Founder reads the brief once before it goes out
  - Output: 3 creator briefs, each tied to a principle that already won. Next: feeds 13-07 (partnership ads) and 16-18 (seeding).
  - Watch: share of creator videos usable as ads without a reshoot (per round, creators shelf), then partner content that sells, then revenue.
- **13-10 Track each creator's posts, code orders, partnership ad results and rights end date on one roster, and renew or cut each month** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
