  - When: on the 1st of each month. Enablers: creator-roster, partner-ledger.
  - Inputs: partnership ad results by creator (connected, Meta Ads); code and link orders per creator (connected, Shopify); fees paid and content delivered (brain, creators shelf); commission rows per creator (enabler, partner-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Update each creator's posts delivered, orders, new buyers and fees 2) Compute cost per new customer per creator across organic and ads 3) Mark renew, test again or cut 4) List rights ending in the next 30 days 5) Send renewals and cut notes
  - Output: a monthly roster with renew, retest or cut on every creator. Next: feeds 13-11 (rights check) and 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: cost per new customer per creator (30 day, creators shelf), then money moves to creators who sell, then revenue.
- **13-08 Price a creator's quote against what the same reach and clicks would cost in paid ads, and send a fair offer** Score 30, ready 2/5, hours.
  - When: each time a creator sends a quote. Enablers: creator-roster, margin-view.
  - Inputs: the creator's last 10 posts with views, saves and link clicks, from their insights screenshots (setup, the creator); audience country, age and fake follower check (buy, an audit tool or a manual look); our cost per 1,000 views and cost per new customer for that audience (connected, Meta Ads); first order profit (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Take the median views of the last 10 posts, not the best one 2) Check the audience is in our countries and age band, and not bots 3) Price the same views at our paid cost per 1,000 4) Add value for usage rights if we will run it as an ad 5) Send an offer with a base fee and a bonus per sale on their code
  - Output: a priced offer for each creator quote, with the math on one line. Next: feeds 13-09 (roster).
  - Watch: fee paid per 1,000 real views vs paid media (per deal, creators shelf), then fair creator fees, then contribution margin.
### T3. When the Amazon agency reports record ad sales but the payout barely moved, help me see what ads cost against profit after Amazon fees and stop the spend that loses money, so I stop growing sales that lose money

Metric: Amazon ad spend as a share of Amazon profit before ads, per ASIN

- **13-16 Pause ads on hero ASINs when FBA stock falls under 3 weeks or when another seller holds the buy box** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: daily. Enablers: marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: FBA stock and 14 day sales rate (setup, Seller Central); buy box owner per ASIN (setup, Seller Central); inbound shipments and dates (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute weeks of FBA stock per hero ASIN 2) Lower bids at 3 weeks and pause at 10 days, unless an inbound lands first 3) Pause ads on any ASIN where we lost the buy box 4) Resume when stock or the buy box is back 5) Log each pause with dollars not spent
  - Output: a daily pause and resume list with the reason for each. Next: feeds 19-supply-inventory.
  - Watch: ad spend on ASINs with no buy box or low stock (7 day, Amazon Ads), then rank kept through stock gaps, then cash.
- **13-13 Compute Amazon ad profit per ASIN after fees, product cost and ads, and cut bids where ads lose money** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then every 2 weeks. Enablers: marketplace-plan, margin-view.
  - Inputs: Sponsored Products and Brands spend and sales by ASIN (setup, Amazon Ads); Amazon fees per unit and payout (setup, Seller Central); landed cost per unit (brain, finance shelf); total Amazon sales per ASIN, ad and organic (setup, Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 30 days of ad spend and total sales per ASIN 2) Compute profit per unit after fees and cost, then after ads 3) Set a break-even ad cost per sale per ASIN 4) Cut bids on campaigns over break-even and raise on those well under 5) Founder sees the list of ASINs that lose money with ads on
  - Output: a per-ASIN table: profit before ads, ad cost, profit after ads, and the bid action. Next: feeds 13-15 (bid ceiling) and 20-marketplaces.
  - Watch: Amazon profit after ads per ASIN (30 day, Seller Central), then Amazon payout, then contribution margin.
- **13-14 Harvest Amazon search terms: move terms that sell from auto campaigns into exact match, and block terms with 15 clicks and no sale** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every 2 weeks. Enablers: marketplace-plan, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: search term report, 60 days (setup, Amazon Ads); profit per unit per ASIN, for the break-even line (brain, finance shelf); products we do not sell (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull search terms with clicks, orders and cost 2) Add terms with 2 or more orders under break-even to exact match campaigns 3) Add terms with 15 or more clicks and no order as negative exact 4) A person checks the negatives do not block a real product use 5) Send the top 10 converting terms to the signal bridge for Meta and Google
  - Output: an updated exact list, a negative list, and 10 terms passed to other channels. Next: feeds 12-11 (Meta hooks) and 17-10 (article briefs).
  - Watch: Amazon ad spend on terms with no sale (30 day, Amazon Ads), then Amazon ad profit, then contribution margin.
- **13-15 Split Amazon ad spend into brand defence and new shoppers, and set a bid ceiling from profit per unit for Prime Day and Q4** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and 3 weeks before Prime Day and Black Friday. Enablers: marketplace-plan, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: search terms and campaigns (setup, Amazon Ads); brand and product name list (brain, products shelf); profit per unit per ASIN (brain, finance shelf); peak dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each term brand or non-brand 2) Show spend, sales and new-to-brand orders for each side 3) Set a bid ceiling per ASIN from profit per unit for peak weeks 4) Cap brand defence bids where no rival shows on our name 5) Read after the peak: profit per unit, not ad sales
  - Output: a brand vs new shopper split and a peak bid ceiling per ASIN. Next: feeds 20-marketplaces peak plan.
  - Watch: new-to-brand orders share (30 day, Amazon Ads), then new Amazon buyers at a profit, then gross profit.
- **13-17 Feed Amazon reviews, questions and search terms into Meta hooks and site copy, and send Meta's winning angles into Amazon images and A+ content** Score 34, ready 1/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: signal-bridge, review-miner.
  - Inputs: our Amazon reviews and questions (setup, Seller Central); rival Amazon reviews for 3 top listings (buy, review export tool); Meta winners with angle tags (enabler, creative-tags); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Group our reviews and questions by love, hate, wish and doubt 2) Do the same for 3 rivals and list where they fail 3) Write 10 Meta hooks from the top themes in buyers' words 4) Rework the first 3 listing images and A+ blocks around the 2 best Meta angles 5) Track Meta hook results and Amazon conversion rate for 4 weeks
  - Output: a theme sheet, 10 hooks for Meta and a new image and A+ set for the hero listing. Next: feeds 11-paid-social (hooks) and 20-marketplaces (listing).
  - Watch: Amazon unit session percentage on the hero listing (28 day, Seller Central), then sales on Amazon and Meta from the same words, then revenue.
### T4. When the month closes and ad spend sits across 6 platforms and new ones pitch for budget, help me see one true total against budget and bills, and test a new channel only with a stop line, so I pay only for what works

Metric: invoice gaps found and recovered, and spend vs budget across all platforms

- **13-20 Say yes or no to a new ad channel pitch (Walmart, Target, programmatic, Instacart) by checking 4 gates, and set a test budget with a stop line** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each time a new channel pitches. Enablers: spend-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: current retargeting reach and frequency on Meta and Google (connected, Meta Ads, Google Ads); profit per unit on the channel after its fees (brain, finance shelf); the offer and credits in writing (founder, the channel rep); budget cap for tests (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Check the 4 gates: core ads profitable, retargeting not full, margin covers fees, stock covers demand 2) If a gate fails, write a no with the reason 3) If all pass, set a 6 week test budget, a stop line and the store number that judges it 4) Founder signs 5) Read at 6 weeks against the stop line
  - Output: a yes or no memo, and for a yes a 6 week test plan with a stop line. Next: feeds 20-marketplaces.
  - Watch: store sales per dollar on the new channel (6 week, spend-ledger), then no money on channels that cannot pay back, then risk.
- **13-18 Match each month's ad invoices from Meta, Google, TikTok and Amazon to reported spend and budget before they are paid, and chase every credit owed** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on the 3rd of each month. Enablers: spend-ledger.
  - Inputs: spend by day per ad account (connected, Meta Ads, Google Ads, TikTok Ads, Amazon Ads); invoices and card statements (founder, finance inbox or accounting tool); budget per channel (brain, finance shelf); invalid click credits promised (connected, Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull spend per platform for the month 2) Match each invoice or card charge to spend 3) Flag gaps over 2 percent and any spend over budget 4) Open a ticket for each gap or missing credit 5) Follow each open ticket weekly until paid, and log the dollars back
  - Output: a monthly spend and invoice table with open disputes and dollars recovered. Next: feeds 21-finance-cash and 26-reports.
  - Watch: invoice dollars not matched to spend (monthly, spend-ledger), then one true paid media total, then cash.
- **13-19 Read how much Meta spend lifts Amazon: compare Amazon brand searches and sales in weeks Meta spend rose or fell, then hold out one region to confirm** Score 30, ready 4/5, weeks.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: spend-ledger, funnel-mix, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: Meta spend by week and region (connected, Meta Ads); Amazon brand search volume and sales by week (setup, Amazon Brand Analytics); site orders by week (connected, Shopify); founder sign-off on a 4 week region holdout (founder, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Line up 26 weeks of Meta spend with Amazon brand searches and sales 2) Mark weeks where Meta spend moved more than 30 percent 3) Compare Amazon sales in those weeks with the weeks around them 4) If the link looks real, run a 4 week region holdout on Meta 5) Write the share of Amazon sales Meta seems to drive, with its range
  - Output: a halo read with a range, and a keep or cut note for Meta budget. Next: feeds 11-paid-social and 22-measurement.
  - Watch: Amazon brand search volume in holdout vs control regions (28 day, Amazon Brand Analytics), then total sales across site and Amazon, then revenue.

## 14-email-messaging Email and messaging

### T1. When I paid to win a first-time buyer and most never order again, help me reach them at the moment they usually reorder, with the right use, fit and reorder messages, so I make the ad cost pay back

Metric: second order rate within 90 days of the first order

- **14-03 Split the post-purchase flow for first-time buyers: thank you on day 0, a use or fit email before the parcel lands, a review ask 5 days after delivery, and a cross-sell on the reorder clock** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: flow-map, buy-cycle.
  - Inputs: fulfilment and delivery events (setup, store platform and shipping app); top return reasons and use mistakes (setup, returns app and helpdesk); review request timing in the reviews app (connected, reviews app); voice rules for email (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Map today's post-purchase messages and remove any that clash with the reviews app 2) Write the use or fit email from the top 3 return and ticket causes 3) Move the review ask to 5 days after delivery, not after dispatch 4) Add the cross-sell on the buy-cycle day 5) Read returns for fit, review rate and second orders over 60 days
  - Output: a first-time buyer flow with 4 timed messages and a 60 day read. Next: review counts feed 07-14 (proof for new products); fit causes come from 06-15.
  - Watch: returns for fit or wrong use, share of first orders (60 days, store refunds and returns app), then second order rate and review count, then revenue.
- **14-01 Work out the median days between first and second order per first product, and set each post-purchase and win-back flow to fire on that day, not the default 90** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: buy-cycle, flow-map.
  - Inputs: orders with customer, product and date, 24 months (connected, store platform); current flow timing and filters (setup, email platform); how long each product lasts in use, where it wears out or runs out (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Compute median and 75th percentile days to second order per first product, in code 2) Group products with a similar clock 3) Set the cross-sell email a few days before the median and the win-back after the 75th percentile 4) Hold out 10 percent of new buyers for 60 days 5) Read second order rate against the holdout
  - Output: a timing table per product group and flows set to it, with a holdout running. Next: feeds 14-02 (replenishment) and 14-06 (holdout read).
  - Watch: second order rate within 90 days (90 days, store orders), then repeat revenue, then revenue.
- **14-02 Build a replenishment reminder per consumable or wear-out product, timed to when it runs out or wears out, with a one tap reorder link to the same size and colour** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once per product, fired on each buyer's clock. Enablers: buy-cycle, flow-map.
  - Inputs: reorder gaps per product (enabler, buy-cycle); variant, size and colour per past order (connected, store platform); offer rules for repeat buyers (brain, offers shelf); email and SMS platform (setup, email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Pick products bought 2 or more times by at least 10 percent of buyers 2) Set the reminder a week before the median reorder day 3) Build a reorder link that fills the cart with the same variant 4) Write 2 messages: a plain reminder, then a nudge with a reason to buy now that the offer rules allow 5) Read reorders from the flow against a 10 percent holdout
  - Output: live reminders per product with a one tap reorder, and a holdout read. Next: feeds 14-06.
  - Watch: reorder rate per product from the reminder (60 days, store orders), then repeat revenue, then revenue.
- **14-04 When a return is logged, send a save note that offers the right size or an exchange first, then store credit, and track who buys again within 60 days** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: on every return. Enablers: flow-map.
  - Inputs: return events with reason and item (setup, returns app); fit notes and size chart (brain, products shelf); exchange and credit rules (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Trigger on the return with the reason 2) For fit, suggest the right size using the fit notes 3) For other reasons, offer an exchange, then store credit 4) Send a plain note from a named person, not a promo 5) Read the share who buy again in 60 days against returners before the flow
  - Output: a return save flow with one note per reason. Next: none.
  - Watch: returners who buy again within 60 days, share (60 days, store orders), then revenue kept from returns, then revenue.
### T2. When the email platform says email made a big share of revenue, help me see what each flow and send really made from store orders, fix the flows that give margin away and keep only proven wins, so I pay for real results

Metric: email revenue from store orders within 1 day of a click, and repeat orders gained against a holdout

- **14-05 Rebuild email and text revenue from store orders placed within 1 day of a click, per flow and per campaign, and put it next to the platform's own number** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly, and before an agency renewal. Enablers: flow-map.
  - Inputs: clicks, opens and sends per message (setup, email platform); orders with UTM source and time (connected, store platform); sessions and purchases by source (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Tag every link with flow or campaign in the UTM 2) Match orders to a click within 1 day, in code 3) Sum per flow and campaign, and set it next to the platform's 5 day open-based number 4) Split new and repeat buyers 5) Write the gap in 1 line for the founder
  - Output: a flow and campaign table with our number next to the platform's, split by new and repeat buyers. Next: feeds 14-06 and the agency review in 26-reports.
  - Watch: email revenue from store orders, share of total (30 days, store orders), then what we pay for email tools and agency against what it earns, then revenue.
- **14-07 Reorder the cart and checkout flows: a plain reminder first, answers to the top checkout doubts second, and a discount only in the last message and only for first-time buyers** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: flow-map, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: cart and checkout flow messages and results (setup, email platform); top checkout doubts from tickets and polls (enabler, question-matrix); discount rules and margin floor (brain, offers and finance shelves); repeat vs first-time buyer flag (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Split into added to cart and checkout started flows 2) Message 1: the cart, the product image, no discount 3) Message 2: answer the top 2 doubts (shipping cost, returns, fit) 4) Message 3: a discount only for first-time buyers, within the discount rules 5) Read recovered orders and profit per recipient against the old flow
  - Output: a 3 step cart and checkout flow that gives a discount only when needed. Next: feeds 14-08.
  - Watch: profit per recipient in cart and checkout flows (30 days, store orders), then discount cost as a share of revenue, then gross profit.
- **14-06 Hold out 10 percent of new buyers from the post-purchase and win-back flows and 10 percent of text subscribers from texts for 60 days, and read the gap in repeat orders and profit** Score 36, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once, read at 60 days, then yearly. Enablers: flow-map, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: flow entry filters (setup, email platform); text subscribers and costs (setup, SMS platform); repeat orders and margin (brain, store platform plus unit costs from the finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Add a random 10 percent holdout tag to each key flow's entry 2) Do the same for texts 3) Wait 60 days without changing the flows 4) Compare repeat orders and gross profit per person, in code 5) Keep, cut or change each flow and text program on the gap, and log it
  - Output: a lift read per key flow and for texts, with a keep or cut call. Next: feeds 14-08 (standards) and 21-finance-cash (text bill).
  - Watch: repeat orders per 100 people, flow vs holdout (60 days, store orders), then repeat revenue and profit, then gross profit.
- **14-08 Judge every email and text test on orders and profit per recipient, promote the winner to the flow standard (T0) that new flows copy, and write a fail note for losers with a date to recheck** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after each test, and on each recheck date. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, flow-map.
  - Inputs: test arms with sends, clicks and orders (setup, email platform and store orders); unit costs for profit per recipient (brain, finance shelf); the email standard list (brain, history shelf (experiment registry)).
  - Steps: 1) Set the main number before the test: profit per recipient, not opens or clicks 2) Run to the planned sample, not to a date 3) For a win, write the standard with the number, like 'plain text founder note ends every flow, +11 percent orders' 4) For a loss, write the fail note and 'do not rerun unless' 5) Recheck each standard after 6 months
  - Output: an email standard list with proof and recheck dates, and a fail list. Next: feeds 05-09 (brand standards) and every new flow build.
  - Watch: tests judged on orders or profit, share (90 days, win-loss-ledger), then revenue per recipient across flows, then gross profit.
### T3. When new sign-ups come in every day, help me turn them into first buyers within 72 hours with messages that fit what they already know and answer what stops them, so I do not waste the sign-up I paid for

Metric: sign-up to first order rate within 7 days

- **14-12 When a sold-out product comes back, alert everyone on its waitlist within an hour, only once stock is really live, in order of sign-up** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on each restock. Enablers: flow-map, send-guard.
  - Inputs: waitlist sign-ups per product and variant (setup, email platform or back in stock app); live stock per variant (connected, store platform); restock dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Trigger on stock going above 0 for the variant, not on a planned date 2) Check stock covers at least 20 percent of the waitlist, or send in batches 3) Send email and text to the waitlist within 1 hour 4) Send a second note after 24 hours only if stock remains 5) Read orders per 100 on the waitlist
  - Output: a restock alert that fires on real stock, with orders per 100 waiting. Next: none.
  - Watch: orders per 100 waitlist sign-ups within 48 hours (48 hours, store orders), then revenue from restocks, then revenue.
- **14-11 Rank sign-up sources (pop-up, quiz, footer, checkout, ad lead forms) by first orders within 30 days and cost per buyer, and tune the pop-up's timing and offer on that** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: flow-map.
  - Inputs: sign-ups by source and form (setup, email platform); first orders by customer (connected, store platform); lead ad spend (connected, Meta Ads); site conversion with and without the pop-up (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Count sign-ups and first orders in 30 days per source, in code 2) Add cost where a source has spend, like lead ads 3) Check site conversion for sessions that saw the pop-up vs not 4) Move pop-up timing to 8 seconds or exit, never on load, and test the offer 5) Grow the sources with the lowest cost per buyer
  - Output: a source table with first orders and cost per buyer, and a tuned pop-up. Next: feeds 14-09.
  - Watch: first orders within 30 days per 100 sign-ups, by source (30 days, store orders), then new customers from sign-ups, then revenue.
- **14-09 Split the welcome flow by what the sign-up already knows: cold sign-ups from a quiz or content get a problem and story series, warm ones who viewed a product get that product, proof and the offer inside 72 hours** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: awareness-map, flow-map.
  - Inputs: sign-up source and pages viewed before sign-up (setup, email platform with onsite tracking); awareness level of each sign-up source and landing page (enabler, awareness-map); founder story, angles and proof (brain, messaging shelf); welcome offer rules (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each sign-up source by awareness level 2) Branch the welcome flow: cold path and warm path 3) Cold path: problem, why old fixes fail, how ours works, proof, then the offer on day 3 4) Warm path: the product they viewed, reviews, the offer in the first email, a reminder at 48 hours 5) Read first orders in 7 days per path against the old single flow
  - Output: a 2 path welcome flow tied to awareness level, with a 7 day order read per path. Next: feeds 14-11 (source ranking).
  - Watch: sign-up to first order rate within 7 days, per path (30 days, store orders), then new customers from email sign-ups, then revenue.
- **14-10 Put the answers to the top 5 pre-sale questions from the coverage matrix into the welcome and browse emails, one question per email, in the customer's own words** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then when the matrix changes. Enablers: question-matrix, flow-map.
  - Inputs: top pre-sale questions with counts (enabler, question-matrix); customer phrases (brain, customers shelf (language bank)); true policies: delivery, returns, fit (brain, operations and offers shelves); welcome and browse flow messages (setup, email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Take the top 5 questions by ticket count 2) Write a short answer email for each, headline in the customer's words 3) Place them in the welcome and browse flows where the doubt shows up 4) Check each against the claims and policies 5) Read click to order rate per email and pre-sale tickets from subscribers
  - Output: 5 answer emails live in the welcome and browse flows. Next: feeds 14-08 if tested.
  - Watch: pre-sale tickets from subscribers per 1,000 sign-ups (30 days, helpdesk), then sign-up to first order rate, then revenue.
### T4. When my top customers bring most of the profit, help me make them feel noticed with a personal letter and quiet early access, so I keep them buying and talking about us

Metric: 12 month repeat rate and gross profit of the top 2 percent of customers against a held-back group

- **14-14 Give the top customers quiet early access to new products and restocks 48 hours before the list, by private link, and keep them out of discount blasts** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before each launch and restock. Enablers: vip-list, flow-map.
  - Inputs: the VIP segment (enabler, vip-list); launch and restock dates (brain, calendar shelf); a hidden product link or password page (connected, store platform); email and text platform (setup, email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Build the private link or hidden collection 3 days before launch 2) Send a plain note from the founder 48 hours early, no mention of 'VIP' or a tier 3) Remove the VIP segment from discount campaigns and cap them at 2 sends a week 4) Give a named reply-to person, not the no-reply address 5) Read early orders and sell-through from the group
  - Output: a quiet early access send per launch, and a VIP segment kept out of discount blasts. Next: feeds 14-15.
  - Watch: orders from VIPs in the 48 hour window (per launch, store orders), then launch sell-through and repeat rate of top customers, then gross profit.
- **14-13 Rank customers by 12 month gross profit and recency, pick the top 2 percent, and have the founder sign a short personal letter to each, mailed in a plain envelope with no discount code** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: twice a year, and after each peak. Enablers: vip-list.
  - Inputs: orders and customers, 24 months (connected, store platform); unit costs for gross profit (brain, finance shelf); open complaints or refunds to skip (connected, helpdesk); the founder's letter and signature (founder, founder); a print and mail service for letters (buy, a letter mailing service).
  - Steps: 1) Rank customers by gross profit and last order date, in code 2) Take the top 2 percent and drop anyone with an open complaint 3) Hold back 20 percent of them at random for the read in 14-15 4) Founder writes a 5 line letter: thanks, one true detail about what they bought, a way to reach them 5) Mail it and tag each customer with the date 6) Say nothing about it in public channels
  - Output: letters mailed to the top 2 percent, each customer tagged, and a held-back group. Next: feeds 14-14 (early access list) and 14-15 (read).
  - Watch: repeat orders from letter recipients vs held back (90 days, store orders), then 12 month repeat rate of top customers, then gross profit.
- **14-15 Read the top customer program against the held-back group at 90 and 180 days, on repeat orders, gross profit and referrals, and keep it only if it pays** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at 90 and 180 days after each letter round. Enablers: vip-list, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: recipients and held-back group (enabler, vip-list); orders and referral codes (connected, store platform); cost of letters, gifts and time (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Compare repeat orders and gross profit per person, recipients vs held back 2) Count referrals and reviews from each group 3) Subtract the cost per person 4) Write keep, change or stop with the numbers 5) Log it in the ledger with a recheck date
  - Output: a 1 page read of the program with a keep, change or stop call. Next: feeds 14-08 standards and 15-customers-cx.
  - Watch: gross profit per top customer, recipients vs held back (180 days, store orders), then profit from top customers, then gross profit.
### T5. When a send could land in spam, break a law, promise something false or go unanswered, help me check before it goes and catch it after, so I keep the inbox and the customer's trust

Metric: spam complaint rate and inbox placement on the main sending domain

- **14-17 Check SPF, DKIM and DMARC on the sending domain, spam rate in Google Postmaster and bounce rate per send each week, and run the repair steps when spam passes 0.3 percent** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday, and on any open rate drop across all sends. Enablers: send-guard.
  - Inputs: DNS records for the sending domain (setup, domain registrar or DNS host); spam rate and domain reputation (setup, Google Postmaster Tools); bounces, complaints and clicks per send (setup, email platform).
  - Steps: 1) Check SPF, DKIM and DMARC pass, and fix any that fail 2) Read spam rate and reputation in Postmaster 3) If spam passes 0.3 percent, send only to people who clicked or bought in 90 days 4) Widen the audience again step by step as spam falls under 0.1 percent 5) Log each step and the date
  - Output: a weekly domain health line and a repair log when needed. Next: feeds 14-19 and 14-20.
  - Watch: spam complaint rate, Gmail (7 days, Google Postmaster Tools), then inbox placement and email revenue, then risk.
- **14-18 Before every send, check consent, text quiet hours for the buyer's state or country, and that every stock, deadline and offer line is true against the store and the brand record** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: before every campaign and every new flow. Enablers: send-guard, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: the send: segment, time, copy (setup, email platform and SMS platform); live stock and live discounts (connected, store platform); claims, offers and per-market rules (brain, claims, offers and market shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Check every contact in the segment has consent for that channel 2) Check text send time against quiet hours in each recipient's time zone 3) Check 'last chance', 'almost gone' and deadline lines against real stock and end dates 4) Check claims against the claims shelf 5) Block or fix, and log the result
  - Output: a pass or block per send with the line to fix. Next: blocked claims go to 05-12.
  - Watch: sends blocked before going out, and complaints after, count (30 days, send-guard log), then legal and trust incidents, then risk.
- **14-19 Cap each person at 1 email and 1 text a day across flows and campaigns, and list who got 3 or more messages in a day last week** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once to set, then weekly. Enablers: send-guard, flow-map.
  - Inputs: sends per person per day (setup, email platform and SMS platform); unsubscribes by day (setup, email platform); flow priority order (brain, email shelf (flow map)).
  - Steps: 1) Count messages per person per day for last week, in code 2) List people who got 3 or more and which flows overlapped 3) Set flow priority so a cart email pauses the browse email 4) Turn on the platform's frequency cap for campaigns 5) Read unsubscribes per 1,000 sends before and after
  - Output: a frequency cap, a flow priority order and a weekly overlap list. Next: none.
  - Watch: unsubscribes per 1,000 sends (14 days, email platform), then list size and email revenue, then revenue.
- **14-16 Route every reply to a marketing email or text into the helpdesk, answer within 1 business day, and track time to first reply and follow-ups owed** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: always on, reviewed weekly. Enablers: ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: reply-to address and SMS inbox (setup, email platform and SMS platform); helpdesk queues and tags (connected, helpdesk); support tone and macros (brain, operations shelf (support)).
  - Steps: 1) Set the reply-to to a real inbox that feeds the helpdesk 2) Tag replies by flow or campaign 3) Set a 1 business day first reply target 4) Report weekly: replies, time to first reply, follow-ups owed, orders from repliers 5) Turn repeated reply questions into email copy fixes
  - Output: every reply answered inside 1 business day, with a weekly report. Next: repeated questions feed 07-11 (coverage matrix).
  - Watch: time to first reply on marketing email replies (7 days, helpdesk), then customer satisfaction and orders from repliers, then customer satisfaction.
- **14-20 Stop mailing contacts with no click, site visit or order in 180 days after a 2 email goodbye, and watch the platform bill and spam rate fall** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: send-guard.
  - Inputs: contacts with last click, visit and order dates (setup, email platform and store platform); platform price tiers (setup, email platform billing); opens are not trusted alone because of mail privacy features (brain, trust and access shelf (numbers not to trust)).
  - Steps: 1) Build the segment: no click, no visit and no order in 180 days 2) Send 2 plain goodbye emails 5 days apart with a one-click stay button 3) Suppress everyone who did not click 4) Record the list size, bill tier and spam rate before and after 5) Re-run monthly
  - Output: a smaller, active list and a lower platform bill. Next: feeds 14-17 (spam rate).
  - Watch: contacts billed and spam rate (30 days, email platform), then platform cost and inbox placement, then gross profit.

## 15-customers-cx Customers and CX

### T1. When a month of new buyers came in on paid ads and 90 days later most never came back, help me find why and fix what they get in the first weeks, so I stop paying full price for every sale

Metric: second order rate within 90 days, by month of first order

- **15-03 Write the first 14 days after delivery: take the top 10 how-to questions new buyers ask (use, wash, size, fit) and answer them in the delivery email, a day 5 email and a card in the box** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when a new question enters the top 10. Enablers: question-matrix, reason-tagger.
  - Inputs: tickets sent within 14 days of delivery (connected, helpdesk joined to Shopify orders); product care, use and size rules (brain, products shelf (FAQ per product)); email tool with shipping and delivery flows (setup, Klaviyo or similar); a small print run of insert cards (buy, local printer).
  - Steps: 1) Pull tickets sent within 14 days of delivery and tag each by question 2) Rank the top 10 questions by count per 100 orders 3) Write one short answer per question, with a photo or a 20 second clip 4) Put answers 1 to 3 in the delivery email, 4 to 6 in a day 5 email, and the rest on the insert card and the help page 5) Founder checks every answer against the product and the claims shelf 6) Compare how-to tickets and returns for 30 days before and after
  - Output: 2 live emails, 1 insert card and a help page, each answering named questions. Next: feeds 15-06 (fills blank cells in the matrix) and 14-email-messaging.
  - Watch: how-to tickets in the first 14 days after delivery, per 100 orders (30 day, helpdesk joined to Shopify orders), then returns from unsure buyers, and second order rate, then customer satisfaction.
- **15-04 Find the reorder window for each top product and send a reminder 5 days before it runs out, with no discount on the first touch, tested against a 20 percent holdout** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once per product, then read each month. Enablers: cohort-view, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: orders with repeat purchases per product (connected, Shopify); email or SMS tool (setup, Klaviyo or similar); brand voice and banned words (brain, voice shelf); discount rules (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) For each of the top 10 products, compute the median days from first to second order 2) Set the reminder at that median minus 5 days, and a second touch at plus 10 days 3) Write the reminder in brand voice: what they bought, when it usually runs out, one tap to reorder 4) Hold out 20 percent of customers who get nothing 5) After 60 days compare reorder rate and margin with the holdout, and log it as a win or a fail
  - Output: live reorder flows per product, with a 60 day readout against a holdout. Next: feeds 25-experiments (win and loss ledger) and 14-email-messaging.
  - Watch: reorder rate within 60 days, flow versus holdout (60 day, Shopify orders and the email tool), then repeat revenue, then revenue.
- **15-01 Build the cohort grid: second order rate at 30, 60 and 90 days by month of first order, split by first product, channel and first discount, and name the 2 cohorts that fell most** Score 34, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then the first Monday of each month. Enablers: cohort-view.
  - Inputs: 24 months of orders with customer ids, refunds and discount codes (connected, Shopify); order source for each first order, from link tags (connected, GA4 and Shopify order source); product cost per SKU (brain, finance shelf); what happened each month: sales, stockouts, new ads (brain, calendar and history shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 24 months of orders and tie each to one customer by email or phone 2) Mark each customer's first order month, first product, first channel and first discount code 3) Count who ordered again by day 30, 60 and 90 in each cohort, from totals 4) Split each cohort by first product, channel and discount, and hide cells under 50 customers 5) Name the 2 cohorts that fell most against the 6 month average, and the split that explains it 6) Check with the founder what changed that month: a sale, a new ad, a stockout
  - Output: a cohort grid (first order month by day 30, 60 and 90) with splits, plus 2 lines on the cohorts that fell and why. Next: feeds 15-02 (why they hire and leave), 15-04 (reorder reminders) and 21-13 (payback by cohort).
  - Watch: second order rate within 90 days, by month of first order (90 day, Shopify orders), then repeat revenue per customer, and blended cost per order, then revenue.
- **15-02 Find why customers hire the product and why one-time buyers never order again, from 200 reviews, 200 tickets and a 1 question survey on the thank you page** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: customer-jobs, reason-tagger.
  - Inputs: reviews with star rating and product (connected, reviews app); tickets from one-time buyers and from repeat buyers (connected, helpdesk joined to Shopify orders); a thank you page question: what made you buy today (setup, post-purchase survey); list of one-time and repeat customers (enabler, cohort-view); voice of customer quotes already filed (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 200 reviews and 200 tickets, half from repeat buyers and half from one-time buyers 2) Tag each by the moment that made them buy, the result they wanted, what they used before and what nearly stopped them 3) Add the 1 question survey on the thank you page and read the first 100 answers 4) Compare repeat buyers with one-time buyers: which moments and doubts differ 5) Write the top 3 hiring moments and the top 3 reasons for no second order, with 2 quotes each 6) Founder reads it and marks what rings true and what is wrong
  - Output: a 1 page jobs map: 3 hiring moments, 3 reasons there is no second order, quotes, and what each suggests we change. Next: feeds 15-03 (first 14 days), 10-copy (hooks) and 02-customers-avatars.
  - Watch: second order rate of the cohort after the fix ships (90 day, Shopify orders), then repeat rate, and new angles for ads, then revenue.
- **15-05 Ask customers who went quiet where they went: send a 2 question exit survey to anyone past 2 times their reorder window, and count the reasons and the rivals they name** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: first Monday of each month. Enablers: reason-tagger, review-miner.
  - Inputs: lapsed customers past 2 times their product's reorder window (enabler, cohort-view); a short survey form sent by email (setup, email tool or a free form); tickets and reviews that name a rival or say they stopped (connected, helpdesk and reviews app); competitor names and brands to match (brain, competitors shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List customers past 2 times their product's reorder window with no order 2) Send 2 questions: did you stop or switch, and if you switched, to what and why 3) Add tickets and reviews that name a rival or say they stopped 4) Tag every answer with one reason and the rival named, and check 20 tags by hand 5) Report the top 3 reasons and the top 2 rivals with counts and 3 quotes, and send product reasons to the product owner
  - Output: a monthly list: reasons for leaving by count, rivals named, 3 quotes. Next: feeds 03-competitors and 15-14 (causes sent to an owner).
  - Watch: lapsed customers with a stated reason, by reason and rival (90 day, exit survey and helpdesk tags), then win-back rate and repeat rate, then revenue.
### T2. When the inbox fills up and replies and follow-ups slip, help me answer faster, keep every promise and remove the questions at the source, so no customer waits and I stop paying to answer the same question

Metric: tickets per 100 orders, and median time to resolution

- **15-07 Put a clock on every ticket: first reply time, time to resolution, follow-ups owed and reopens, with the order value at stake, and list every breach each morning** Score 38, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every morning at 9. Enablers: ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: tickets with every status change, tag and agent (connected, helpdesk); orders and lifetime spend per customer (connected, Shopify); reply and resolution targets per ticket type (brain, operations shelf (support policies)); team hours and who covers weekends (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every ticket opened or still open in the last 30 days, with each status change 2) Compute first reply time and time to resolution in business hours, per ticket and per agent 3) Mark tickets over target, tickets waiting on us, and follow-ups promised but not sent 4) Add the customer's lifetime spend and any open order value to each late ticket 5) Send one morning list: breaches first, sorted by value at stake, each with an owner
  - Output: a daily breach list, and a 30 day table of median first reply and resolution time by reason and agent. Next: feeds 15-08 (follow-up rules) and 23-team-ops (staffing).
  - Watch: median time to resolution in hours (7 day, helpdesk), then repeat rate of customers who had a ticket, and bad reviews, then customer satisfaction.
- **15-08 Give every promise made in a ticket a dated follow-up: when an agent writes we will reship, refund or check, create a task, check it happened, and write back to the customer** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: on every ticket closed with a promise, checked each day. Enablers: ticket-clock, work-board.
  - Inputs: closing replies and tags (connected, helpdesk); list of promise phrases and the macros that use them (brain, operations shelf (support macros)); refunds, reships and tracking numbers (connected, Shopify); owner for each promise type (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Scan closing replies for promise phrases: reship, refund, replace, check with the warehouse, get back to you 2) Create a follow-up task with a due date and an owner 3) On the due date check the order: was the refund paid, did the reship get a tracking number 4) Send the customer a short note: done, with proof, or a new date and why 5) Report each week: promises made, kept on time, late, and the reopen rate for each
  - Output: a follow-up list with every promise, its owner, its due date and whether it was kept. Next: feeds 15-07 (ticket clock) and 23-team-ops.
  - Watch: reopen rate of tickets closed with a promise (30 day, helpdesk), then repeat rate of customers who had a problem, then customer satisfaction.
- **15-06 Build the question coverage matrix: every question from tickets, chat, Amazon questions, reviews and site search in rows, every place it could be answered in columns, then fill the 10 biggest blank cells** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every 2 weeks. Enablers: question-matrix, reason-tagger.
  - Inputs: 6 months of tickets and chat (connected, helpdesk); Amazon customer questions and buyer messages (setup, Amazon Seller Central, when they sell there); site search terms (connected, Shopify search and GA4); reviews that ask or answer a question (connected, reviews app); product page, FAQ and email text (brain, site shelf and email shelf); claims we may make (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every question and merge the ones that mean the same thing 2) Count each question per 100 orders and mark the product it is about 3) Check each place (product page, FAQ, shipping email, ad, Amazon listing, help centre) and mark answered, half answered or blank 4) Rank blank cells by count and by how close the question is to buying: size, fit, delivery and returns first 5) Write the answer for the top 10 blanks, and a person checks each against the claims shelf 6) Publish the answers and log the date, so the next run shows tickets before and after
  - Output: a matrix of questions by places, with the top 10 blanks filled and dated. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro (page blocks), 17-seo (articles) and 20-15 (Amazon listing).
  - Watch: tickets on the 10 filled questions, per 100 orders (30 day, helpdesk tags), then tickets per 100 orders, and product page conversion, then customer satisfaction.
- **15-09 Tag the last 90 days of tickets with one reason from a fixed list of 15, find the reason growing fastest, and put a dollar cost on each reason** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then every Monday. Enablers: reason-tagger, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: tickets with text and handle time (connected, helpdesk); refunds, reships and store credit (connected, Shopify); support cost per hour (brain, finance shelf); reason list with 3 examples each (enabler, reason-tagger).
  - Steps: 1) Draft the 15 reasons from 300 sample tickets and write 3 examples for each 2) Tag every ticket, then check 40 tags by hand and fix the list where it is wrong 3) Count each reason per 100 orders this week against the last 8 weeks 4) Cost each reason: agent minutes times cost per minute, plus refunds, reships and credits 5) Write the top 3 by cost and the 1 growing fastest, each with an owner who can fix the cause
  - Output: a reason table with count per 100 orders, trend and cost, plus 3 named fixes. Next: feeds 15-10 (early delivery notes), 15-12 (returns) and 23-team-ops.
  - Watch: share of tickets by top 5 reasons (30 day, helpdesk tags), then support cost per order, then contribution margin.
- **15-10 Send delivery updates before customers ask: each morning find orders past their dispatch date or stuck in transit for 3 days, and send a 2 line note with a new date** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every morning. Enablers: ticket-clock, reason-tagger.
  - Inputs: fulfilment status per order (connected, Shopify); carrier tracking scans (setup, tracking app (AfterShip or similar)); promised dispatch and delivery days per region (brain, operations shelf (fulfilment)); email or SMS tool to send the note (setup, Klaviyo or similar); order status tickets (connected, helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) List open orders past the promised dispatch date, and shipped orders with no scan for 3 days 2) Group them by cause: not packed, carrier delay, address problem 3) Send each customer a 2 line note with the cause in plain words and a new date 4) Send address problems to a person to call or text the same day 5) Compare order status tickets per 100 orders for 4 weeks before and after
  - Output: a daily list of late orders, and a note sent for each. Next: feeds 19-16 (carrier scorecard) and 15-07.
  - Watch: order status tickets per 100 orders (30 day, helpdesk joined to Shopify orders), then support cost per order, and bad reviews, then customer satisfaction.
- **15-11 Write the support rulebook for a new hire or an AI agent: tone, what each may refund or reship alone and up to what amount, when to hand to a person, and 20 replies for the top reasons** Score 30, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when a rule is broken or a new person starts. Enablers: ticket-clock, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: dollar caps for refund, reship, discount and credit (founder, founder); tone and banned words (brain, voice shelf); current macros, policies and escalation rules (brain, operations shelf (support)); top reasons with example tickets (enabler, reason-tagger); AI agent action log, if one is used (connected, helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) Ask the founder for limits: refund, reship, discount and credit, each with a dollar cap 2) Write 20 replies for the top reasons in brand voice, each with the facts to check first 3) Write the hand-over rules: angry customer, chargeback threat, press or creator, VIP, anything over the cap 4) A person loads the rules into the helpdesk and the AI agent settings 5) Each week read 20 closed tickets (AI and human), mark any action over a cap, and fix the rule
  - Output: a 2 page rulebook, 20 replies in the helpdesk, and a weekly check of 20 tickets. Next: feeds 23-team-ops and 15-07.
  - Watch: share of tickets answered by someone other than the founder (30 day, helpdesk), then founder hours on support, and AI agent mistakes, then risk.
### T3. When returns, defects or bad reviews start to rise on a product, help me catch the cause early and send it to the person who can fix it, so the next 500 orders ship right

Metric: return rate by product and reason, 60 day

- **15-13 Catch a bad batch in days: count tickets, reviews and returns that name the same defect per product and purchase order, alert at 5 in 7 days, and list the unshipped orders from that batch** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every day. Enablers: reason-tagger.
  - Inputs: tickets with customer photos (connected, helpdesk); new reviews (connected, reviews app); returns with reasons (connected, Shopify); purchase order and date received for each batch (brain, operations shelf (supply)); stock by batch or date received (setup, 3PL or warehouse system).
  - Steps: 1) Tag each defect mention with product, defect type and delivery date 2) Map the delivery date to the batch received just before it 3) Alert when 5 mentions of the same defect hit one batch within 7 days 4) List orders not yet shipped from that batch and hold them for a person to check 5) Send the supplier the count, photos and order numbers, and log the claim
  - Output: a defect alert with batch, count, photos and a hold list. Next: feeds 19-14 (supplier score) and 19-15 (inspection before paying).
  - Watch: tickets and reviews with the same defect, per batch (14 day, helpdesk tags, reviews app and purchase order records), then refunds and bad reviews, then gross profit.
- **15-12 Build the return table for each product and size: return rate and top reason over 60 days, the full cost of each return, and a flag on any product at 1.5 times its 6 month rate** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: reason-tagger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: returns and refunds with reason codes and notes (connected, Shopify returns, or the returns app); full cost of a return: postage both ways, restock, write-off (founder, founder interview, question 7); product cost per SKU (brain, finance shelf); size chart and product page text (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 60 days of returns and join each to product, size, colour and order date 2) Clean the reason codes into the fixed list and read 20 free text notes by hand 3) Compute return rate per product and size from totals, and the full cost of each return 4) Flag products at 1.5 times their 6 month rate, and name the size or reason behind it 5) Write 1 fix per flag: a size chart line, a photo, a product note, or a supplier call
  - Output: a return table by product and size with cost, flags and 1 fix each. Next: feeds 15-13 (batch check), 07-landing-pages-cro (size chart) and 21-07 (margin).
  - Watch: return rate by product and return reason (60 day, Shopify returns), then gross profit per order, then gross profit.
- **15-14 Answer every 1 and 2 star review within 5 days with a fix, and send each cause to the person who can change the product or the page, tracking how many weeks it stays open** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: daily for replies, every Monday for open causes. Enablers: review-miner, work-board.
  - Inputs: new reviews with rating (connected, reviews app); the reviewer's orders and tickets (connected, Shopify and helpdesk); reply tone (brain, voice shelf); owner for each cause type (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull new 1 and 2 star reviews each day with the reviewer's order and tickets 2) Draft a reply that names the problem and the fix, and a person approves it 3) Contact the customer privately to make it right, and log what was done 4) Tag the cause and put it on the work board with an owner and a due date 5) Each Monday list causes open for over 2 weeks and how many reviews each caused
  - Output: replies posted within 5 days, and a list of causes with owner and weeks open. Next: feeds 15-12 (returns) and 07-landing-pages-cro.
  - Watch: share of 1 and 2 star reviews answered within 5 days (30 day, reviews app), then star rating on best sellers, and conversion, then revenue.
- **15-15 Mine 300 reviews of the top 3 rivals on Amazon and their own sites: list what buyers love, hate and wish for, mark the complaints we already solve, and hand 5 lines to copy and 3 notes to product** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: the 3 rivals with product links (brain, competitors shelf); rival reviews from Amazon and their sites (buy, a review export tool, or a person copies them by hand); our own reviews (connected, reviews app); proof we hold for each claim (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 100 reviews per rival, weighted to 1 to 3 stars 2) Group them into love, hate and wish themes and count each 3) Mark each hate theme: we solve it, we have the same problem, or unknown 4) Write 5 lines for ads and pages from the themes we solve, each with our proof 5) Send the 3 themes we share with rivals to the product owner 6) Founder checks every claim before it goes to copy
  - Output: a theme table for 3 rivals, 5 approved lines and 3 product notes. Next: feeds 10-copy, 09-creative and 03-competitors.
  - Watch: cost per new customer on ads that use the new lines (14 day, Meta Ads), then conversion on paid traffic, then revenue.
- **15-16 Compare each product's site rating with its Amazon rating, and list what Amazon reviewers complain about that site reviewers do not** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: first Monday of each month, for sellers on Amazon. Enablers: review-miner, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: site reviews and ratings (connected, reviews app); Amazon reviews on our listings (setup, Amazon Seller Central); Amazon return reasons (setup, Amazon Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) Pull ratings and reviews per product from both places 2) For each product with a gap of 0.3 stars or more, group the Amazon complaints 3) Split complaints into product, listing (wrong expectation), delivery, and fake or reseller units 4) Write the fix per group: listing copy, image, size note, or a seller report 5) Recheck the gap 60 days later
  - Output: a gap table with the Amazon-only complaints and a fix per product. Next: feeds 20-20 (fix the rating) and 20-15 (Amazon question matrix).
  - Watch: star rating per product on site and on Amazon (90 day, reviews app and Amazon Seller Central), then Amazon buy rate, then revenue.
### T4. When I want to keep my best customers and stop paying ads to reach people who already buy, help me see who makes money and treat them quietly well, so they stay without learning to wait for a sale

Metric: repeat rate and gross profit of the top 2 percent of customers, 365 day

- **15-19 Count how many Meta purchases come from people who already bought from us, per campaign, work out the cost per truly new customer, and give the ads team a list of campaigns that should exclude past buyers** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: campaigns with spend and purchases (connected, Meta Ads (read only)); orders with link tags and customer history (connected, Shopify); which campaigns are prospecting and which retargeting (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Match orders with a Meta link tag to the customer's order history 2) For each campaign, split purchases into first orders and repeat orders 3) Compute cost per new customer as spend divided by first orders, from totals 4) List prospecting campaigns where over 25 percent of buyers were past customers 5) Send the list to the person who runs ads to add a past buyer exclusion; ecomOS does not change the account
  - Output: a campaign table with new and repeat share and cost per new customer, plus an exclusion list. Next: feeds 22-13 (funnel mix) and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: share of Meta purchases from existing customers (30 day, Meta Ads and Shopify orders), then cost per new customer, then revenue.
- **15-17 Rank customers by gross profit over 365 days after returns, shipping and discounts, take the top 2 percent, and drop heavy returners and buyers who only order with a code** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: vip-list, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders, refunds and discounts per customer (connected, Shopify); product and shipping cost (brain, finance shelf); tickets and reviews per customer (connected, helpdesk and reviews app).
  - Steps: 1) Compute gross profit per customer for 365 days from orders, refunds, shipping and discounts 2) Rank and take the top 2 percent, with order count, first product and last order date 3) Drop customers with 3 or more returns, or with a code on every order 4) Add notes from tickets and reviews: what they bought and what they said 5) Founder reads the list and removes anyone who should not be on it
  - Output: a VIP list of about 2 percent of customers, with profit, history and notes. Next: feeds 15-18 (letters) and 14-email-messaging (a quiet segment).
  - Watch: repeat rate and gross profit of the top 2 percent (365 day, Shopify orders), then profit from repeat customers, then gross profit.
- **15-18 Mail a personal letter to the top 50 customers with quiet early access to the next launch through a private link, never announced, and compare what they do in 60 days with 50 similar customers who got nothing** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: before each launch, at most once a quarter. Enablers: vip-list.
  - Inputs: the VIP list (enabler, vip-list); the founder's voice and signature (founder, voice shelf and founder); next launch date and units set aside (brain, calendar shelf and products shelf (roadmap)); letter printing or a handwriting service, and postage (buy, a letter service); a hidden product page or a code that only works for these 50 (setup, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 50 VIPs, and hold out 50 with the same profit and recency who get nothing 2) Write the letter in the founder's voice, with 1 line from each customer's own history 3) A person reads every letter before it is printed 4) Set up the hidden page or code with access 7 days before the public launch 5) Mail the letters, and log who got what and when 6) At 60 days compare orders, spend and referrals with the holdout, and log it as a win or a fail
  - Output: 50 letters sent, a private access page, and a 60 day readout against the holdout. Next: feeds 25-experiments (win or fail note) and 15-17 (next quarter's list).
  - Watch: orders and spend from letter recipients versus the holdout (60 day, Shopify orders), then repeat rate of top customers, and referrals, then gross profit.
- **15-20 List customers with 3 or more returns in 180 days, show their share of all refunds, and draft a fair rule for the founder to sign, such as exchange only after the third return** Score 24, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: reason-tagger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: returns and refunds per customer (connected, Shopify); full cost of a return (founder, founder interview, question 7); current return policy (brain, operations shelf (returns)).
  - Steps: 1) Count returns and refund value per customer for 180 days 2) Compute the share of all refunds from customers with 3 or more returns, and their reasons 3) Separate fit and defect returns from wear and return 4) Draft 2 rule options with the money each saves and the sales each could cost 5) Founder picks one, and it goes into the returns app and the support rulebook
  - Output: a list of serial returners, their share of refunds, and a signed rule. Next: feeds 15-11 (rulebook) and 21-07 (margin).
  - Watch: share of refund value from customers with 3 or more returns (180 day, Shopify returns), then refunds per order, then gross profit.

## 16-organic-content Organic and content

### T1. When a post gets 400,000 views and the store sees no bump, help me see which posts, products and awareness levels actually sold to strangers, so I make content for buyers, not views

Metric: orders and new customers from organic posts per month

- **16-02 Split each post's views into followers and strangers, and list the 10 posts that reached strangers who then bought** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: post-ledger.
  - Inputs: views from followers vs non-followers per post (setup, Instagram and Facebook insights via Meta Business); TikTok For You page share per video (setup, TikTok); post to order join (enabler, post-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Pull follower and non-follower views per post 2) Rank posts by stranger views and by new customers 3) List the 10 posts that did both 4) Write what the 10 share: hook, product, format, length 5) Plan 4 posts next month that repeat the shared parts
  - Output: a top 10 list of posts that reached and sold to strangers, with what they share. Next: feeds 16-08 (turn into a series) and 16-16 (boost as ads).
  - Watch: share of views from non-followers (28 day, Instagram insights), then new customers from organic, then revenue.
- **16-01 Tag every post of the last 90 days by product, pillar, format, hook and awareness level, and join each to link clicks, orders and new customers** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every Monday. Enablers: post-ledger, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: posts with views, reach, saves and link clicks (setup, Instagram and Facebook insights via Meta Business); TikTok and YouTube post stats (setup, TikTok and YouTube); orders with UTM, link in bio and codes (connected, Shopify); tag list with definitions (enabler, creative-tags).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 90 days of posts from each platform 2) Tag each post with product, pillar, format, hook type and awareness level 3) A person checks 20 tags and fixes the tag rules 4) Join each post to clicks, orders and new customers in the 72 hours after it 5) Rank posts by new customers, not views
  - Output: a post table ranked by orders and new customers, with tags on every row. Next: feeds 16-02, 16-04 and 16-08.
  - Watch: orders tied to organic posts (28 day, Shopify), then new customers from organic, then revenue.
- **16-05 Set a monthly scorecard for the social manager or agency on posts shipped, stranger reach, link clicks, orders and new customers** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once, then on the 1st of each month. Enablers: post-ledger.
  - Inputs: post results (enabler, post-ledger); monthly fee (brain, finance shelf); targets agreed with the manager (founder, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 5 numbers and a target for each with the manager 2) Fill the card from the ledger on the 1st 3) Add cost per order from organic: fee divided by tracked orders 4) Write one line on what to change next month 5) Founder reads it in 2 minutes and signs keep, change or cut
  - Output: a one page scorecard with 5 numbers, the fee per order and a keep or cut note. Next: feeds 26-reports.
  - Watch: orders per month from organic (30 day, Shopify), then judge the team on money, not likes, then gross profit.
- **16-03 Find the one platform where our buyers come from, using 90 days of sessions, orders and new customers per platform plus a 'where did you hear about us' question** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: post-ledger.
  - Inputs: sessions and orders by social source (connected, GA4); post-purchase survey with a 'where did you hear' question (setup, post-purchase survey app); hours spent per platform per week (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) Add the survey question if it is not live and wait 2 weeks for answers 2) Sum sessions, orders and new customers per platform for 90 days 3) Add survey answers per platform next to the tracked numbers 4) Divide by the team's hours on each platform 5) Pick 1 platform to lead, and set the others to repost only
  - Output: a platform table with orders per hour spent, and a 1 platform decision. Next: feeds 16-05 (scorecard).
  - Watch: orders per team hour on the lead platform (90 day, GA4), then team hours on the channel that sells, then gross profit.
- **16-04 Map our organic posts and 3 rivals' posts by awareness level and fill the level where we are thin with 4 posts next month** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: awareness-map, competitor-feed, post-ledger.
  - Inputs: our posts with awareness tags (enabler, post-ledger); 3 rivals' organic posts, last 60 days (enabler, competitor-feed); rival handles (brain, competitors shelf); awareness rubric with 5 examples per level (enabler, awareness-map).
  - Steps: 1) Tag rivals' posts by awareness level with the same rubric 2) Show the share of posts and views per level for us and each rival 3) Name the thin level, most often unaware or problem aware 4) Filter the swipe file by that level and pick 4 ideas 5) Brief 4 posts for next month
  - Output: a heat map of 5 levels by 4 brands, plus 4 briefs for the thin level. Next: feeds 16-06 (idea pipeline) and 09-creative.
  - Watch: share of posts at unaware and problem aware (28 day, post-ledger), then new people who meet the brand, then revenue.
### T2. When it is Monday and the team has nothing ready to post, help me pick ideas from what worked for us, what works for rivals and what customers keep asking, so I stop starting from zero every week

Metric: posts shipped per week from the plan, and share that name a product

- **16-06 Save 20 ideas a week into the swipe file from Muse and from rivals' organic posts, each tagged with the principle behind it, the awareness level and one of our products** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every week. Enablers: swipe-file, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: Muse, for Meta content ideas (setup, Muse); rival handles on Instagram and TikTok (brain, competitors shelf); content pillars (brain, content and brand kit shelf); tag list for principle and awareness level (enabler, creative-tags).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 10 ideas from Muse for our category and 10 top posts from rivals this week 2) Write one line on why each worked: the principle, not the content 3) Tag each with awareness level, format and one of our products 4) Drop any idea that needs a claim we cannot prove 5) Pick 5 for next week's plan
  - Output: 20 tagged ideas a week in the swipe file, 5 picked for the plan. Next: feeds 16-07 (rival breakdown) and 16-10 (shoot plan).
  - Watch: posts shipped from swipe file ideas (28 day, post-ledger), then posts shipped each week, then revenue.
- **16-07 Break a rival's breakout post into its parts (hook, format, pacing, angle) and write our own version in our words** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a rival post gets 5 times their usual views. Enablers: competitor-feed, principle-matcher, script-builder.
  - Inputs: rival posts with views vs their average (enabler, competitor-feed); our voice rules (brain, voice shelf); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf); organic ideas in the idea bank (have, ideas/v1/13-ORG-organic-and-content.json).
  - Steps: 1) Flag the post and save it to the swipe file 2) Write its parts: first line, first frame, length, pacing, angle 3) State the principle in one sentence 4) Write 2 scripts that use the principle with our product and our words, never their text or faces 5) Post one within 7 days and compare to our median
  - Output: a principle note and 2 scripts, 1 posted within a week. Next: feeds 16-08 (series) and 09-creative.
  - Watch: views and link clicks vs our median (7 day, post-ledger), then reach to new buyers, then revenue.
- **16-09 Take the 10 most asked questions with no post (size, care, shipping, which one) from the question coverage matrix and answer each in a short video or carousel** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: question-matrix, format-translator.
  - Inputs: tickets and chat questions (connected, helpdesk); repeated comment and DM questions (setup, Instagram and Facebook insights via Meta Business); review questions and doubts (connected, reviews app); where each question is answered today (enabler, question-matrix); true answers (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the matrix and sort by how often each question is asked 2) Pick 10 questions with no post answer 3) Write each answer in under 30 seconds of talk or 5 slides 4) A person checks each answer against the product facts 5) Post over 3 weeks, pin the best to the profile and mark the matrix cell filled
  - Output: 10 answer posts live and 10 matrix cells filled. Next: feeds 15-customers-cx (fewer tickets) and 18-08 (answer blocks).
  - Watch: tickets on the 10 questions (30 day, helpdesk), then doubt removed before the buy, then revenue.
- **16-08 Turn the post that beat the rest into a series: name the format, write 6 more scripts with the same structure, and film them in one batch** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when a post does 3 times our median on orders or stranger views. Enablers: principle-matcher, script-builder.
  - Inputs: the winning post and its tags (enabler, post-ledger); products that fit the format (brain, products shelf); voice rules (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Name the format in 5 words and write its structure 2) Write 6 scripts with the same structure on 6 products or questions 3) Film all 6 in one session 4) Post 2 a week for 3 weeks 5) Read orders and stranger views per episode and keep or stop the series
  - Output: a named series with 6 filmed episodes and a keep or stop read. Next: feeds 16-16 (boost as ads).
  - Watch: median views and orders per episode (21 day, post-ledger), then a recipe the team can repeat, then revenue.
- **16-11 Build a 2 week buffer of ready posts before any launch or peak so posting never stops** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 3 weeks before each launch or peak on the calendar. Enablers: script-builder, work-board.
  - Inputs: peaks and launches (brain, calendar shelf); stock for featured products (connected, Shopify); posts already filmed (brain, content and brand kit shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List the posting days from now to 2 weeks after the event 2) Check which slots have a filmed post and which need a script 3) Check stock for every product in a slot 4) Film or edit the gaps and schedule all posts 5) Keep 4 evergreen posts in reserve
  - Output: a scheduled calendar with 2 weeks covered and 4 spares. Next: feeds 16-14 (spike alarm).
  - Watch: days without a post (30 day, post-ledger), then reach kept through busy weeks, then revenue.
- **16-13 Turn the 3 best Meta ads and 2 best SEO articles into native posts, and the 3 best posts into ad drafts** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: format-translator, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: Meta winners of the month (connected, Meta Ads); articles with the most organic orders (connected, Search Console); top posts by orders (enabler, post-ledger); format specs per platform (brain, content and brand kit shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 3 ads, 2 articles and 3 posts by orders 2) Rewrite each ad as a post that does not look like an ad 3) Cut each article into a 5 slide carousel or a 30 second answer 4) Hand the 3 posts to the ad team as ad drafts 5) Track results in both places for 3 weeks
  - Output: 5 native posts and 3 ad drafts, each linked to its source. Next: feeds 11-paid-social and 17-seo.
  - Watch: orders from reworked posts vs our median (21 day, post-ledger), then more output from ideas already proven, then revenue.
- **16-10 Plan one shoot day to leave with 2 weeks of posts: a shot list of 14 posts across 3 pillars in one location** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once a month. Enablers: script-builder, swipe-file.
  - Inputs: picked ideas from the swipe file (enabler, swipe-file); products to feature and stock (brain, products shelf); shoot budget (brain, finance shelf); who is on camera (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 14 ideas across 3 pillars and at least 2 awareness levels 2) Write a 1 line script and 1 line shot note for each 3) Order the shots by setup so the day has few changes 4) Film 2 takes of each opening line 5) Log the files in the asset library with rights
  - Output: 14 filmed posts from one day, logged with rights. Next: feeds 16-11 (buffer).
  - Watch: posts shipped per shoot day (per shoot, post-ledger), then cost per post, then gross profit.
- **16-12 Test 3 formats that work without the founder on camera (team member, customer, hands-only product demo) for 3 weeks** Score 28, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once, when the founder is the only face that sells. Enablers: post-ledger, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: founder on camera: yes, no and how much time (brain, voice shelf); team members or customers willing to appear (founder, team and customers); baseline founder post results (enabler, post-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 3 formats that do not need the founder 2) Make 3 posts per format 3) Post them over 3 weeks on the lead platform 4) Compare stranger views and orders with founder posts 5) Keep any format within 70 percent of the founder baseline
  - Output: a format test with 1 or 2 formats kept to run without the founder. Next: feeds 16-18 (creator seeding).
  - Watch: orders per post, non-founder vs founder (21 day, post-ledger), then content that does not depend on one person, then risk.
### T3. When a post takes off and the comments fill with 'where can I buy this', help me answer fast with a working link and stock ready, and put money behind the posts that sell, so I catch buyers before the moment passes

Metric: orders within 72 hours of a spike post

- **16-14 Set a spike alarm: when a post passes 3 times its usual views, check stock and the link and pin a working link in the comments within 1 hour** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every hour while a post is live under 72 hours. Enablers: post-ledger.
  - Inputs: views per post by hour (setup, Instagram and Facebook insights via Meta Business); stock for the product shown (connected, Shopify); a person on call (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) Compare views per hour with the account's median 2) At 3 times median, alert the person on call 3) Check the product is in stock and the link works on a phone 4) Pin a comment with the link and update the bio link 5) If stock is short, add a waitlist and tell the team
  - Output: an alert, a pinned working link and a stock check within 1 hour. Next: feeds 16-15 (comment replies) and 16-16 (boost).
  - Watch: orders within 72 hours of a spike post (72 hour, Shopify), then buyers caught while the post is hot, then revenue.
- **16-15 Sort comments and DMs into buyer questions, complaints, praise and spam, and reply to buyers within 2 hours with a link and an answer from the matrix** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: twice a day, and hourly on a spike. Enablers: question-matrix, post-ledger.
  - Inputs: comments and DMs (setup, Instagram and Facebook insights via Meta Business); tested answers per question (enabler, question-matrix); reply tone and escalation rules (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull new comments and DMs 2) Sort each into buyer question, complaint, praise or spam 3) Reply to buyer questions first with the answer and a link 4) Move complaints to support within 1 hour and reply in public that we are on it 5) Add any new question to the matrix
  - Output: a sorted inbox with buyer replies sent and new questions logged. Next: feeds 16-09 (answer posts) and 15-customers-cx.
  - Watch: median reply time to buyer questions (7 day, Instagram inbox), then sales not lost in public, then customer satisfaction.
- **16-16 Boost the organic posts that sold to strangers as ads, head to head against our best studio ad** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every 2 weeks. Enablers: post-ledger, creative-archive.
  - Inputs: posts ranked by stranger reach and orders (enabler, post-ledger); our best studio ad (connected, Meta Ads); rights for any creator or customer in the post (brain, creators shelf); test budget cap (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 3 posts with the most stranger orders 2) Check rights for everyone in them 3) Run them as ads from the brand handle next to the best studio ad, same budget 4) Read at 14 days on cost per new customer 5) Move winners into the main ad set and log the result
  - Output: a live test of 3 boosted posts vs studio, with a winner at day 14. Next: feeds 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: cost per new customer, boosted posts vs studio ad (14 day, Meta Ads), then cheaper tested ads, then revenue.
- **16-17 Turn social reach into emails and phone numbers: add a list offer to the bio link and 1 post a week that asks for sign-ups** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then weekly. Enablers: post-ledger.
  - Inputs: email and SMS sign-up form with source tracking (setup, email platform); the list offer allowed (brain, offers shelf); bio link tool (setup, link in bio page).
  - Steps: 1) Add a sign-up offer at the top of the bio link 2) Tag sign-ups by social source 3) Post 1 sign-up post a week: early access, a guide or a drop alert 4) Count sign-ups per post and per platform 5) Keep the post type that brings the most sign-ups
  - Output: a live bio sign-up, a weekly sign-up post and a count by source. Next: feeds 14-email-messaging (welcome flow).
  - Watch: new email and SMS sign-ups from social (28 day, email platform), then buyers we can reach again for free, then revenue.
### T4. When we send free product to 40 creators and only a handful post, help me pick the right people, brief them well and reuse customer proof with rights, so I get content that sells without paying for bots

Metric: posts delivered and orders per product sent

- **16-20 Check every health, comparison or 'best' claim and every paid post label before it goes out** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
