  - When: before each post. Enablers: brain-graph, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: allowed and forbidden claims with proof (brain, claims shelf); FTC paid partner label rule (brain, claims shelf); the draft post (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) Read the draft for any health, comparison or 'best' claim 2) Find the proof for each claim in the brain, or cut it 3) Check paid or gifted posts carry a clear label 4) Mark the post checked with the date 5) Log any cut claim so the team learns
  - Output: a checked post and a log of claims cut. Next: none.
  - Watch: posts with an unbacked claim (30 day, post-ledger), then no strike, FTC letter or refund wave, then risk.
- **16-19 Turn the 10 best reviews and customer photos of the month into posts, with written permission** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: review-miner, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: reviews with photos and ratings (connected, reviews app); top praised themes (enabler, review-miner); permission request template (brain, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 5 star reviews with photos from the month 2) Pick 10 that match a top praised theme 3) Ask each customer for permission and log the answer 4) Make a post or carousel from each approved one 5) Tag the post in the ledger as customer proof
  - Output: 10 proof posts with permission logged. Next: feeds 16-16 (boost) and 09-creative.
  - Watch: link clicks on proof posts vs our median (28 day, post-ledger), then trust before the buy, then revenue.
- **16-18 Seed 40 creators with a tracked plan: pick by audience fit, send with a short brief and a unique code, and score who posted and sold 30 days later** Score 28, ready 1/5, weeks.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: creator-roster.
  - Inputs: creator candidates with audience checks (brain, creators shelf); fake follower and audience check (buy, an audit tool or a manual look); unique codes and links (connected, Shopify); product units and shipping budget (brain, finance shelf); one-page brief (enabler, script-builder).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 40 creators by fit, real engagement and audience country 2) Send product with a 1 page brief and a unique code 3) Log each send on the roster 4) At 30 days, score each: posted or not, views, code orders, content we can reuse 5) Send the next box only to those who posted and sold
  - Output: a seeding roster with 40 rows scored at 30 days. Next: feeds 13-10 (roster) and 13-07 (partnership ads).
  - Watch: share of seeded creators who posted (30 day, creators shelf), then orders per product sent, then revenue.

## 17-seo SEO

### T1. When organic revenue falls 20 percent in a week and no one knows why, help me see within a day which pages and search terms lost it, and what organic really earns from our own orders, so I fix it before a month of sales is gone

Metric: organic revenue from store orders, week on week

- **17-03 Find the cause of a 20 percent organic drop within a day: which pages and terms lost clicks, and whether it was ranking, click rate, demand, tracking or indexing** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when organic revenue or clicks drop 20 percent week on week. Enablers: search-feed, crawl-watch.
  - Inputs: daily clicks, impressions and positions per page (enabler, search-feed); crawl and indexing changes (enabler, crawl-watch); deploy and app change log (brain, audits shelf); Google update dates (have, public notes).
  - Steps: 1) Compare the last 7 days with the 7 before per page 2) Sort the loss into: lower rank, lower click rate, lower demand, tracking break or pages dropped from the index 3) Check the change log and update dates for the same days 4) Name the 5 pages with the most revenue lost and the cause for each 5) Write the fix and the owner, and recheck in 7 days
  - Output: a 1 page cause note with 5 pages, the cause of each and the fix. Next: feeds 17-17 (change checks).
  - Watch: organic clicks to the 5 pages (7 day, Search Console), then organic revenue recovered, then revenue.
- **17-04 Check an SEO agency report or a link building pitch against our own clicks, orders and past results before paying** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, and each time someone pitches. Enablers: search-feed.
  - Inputs: the agency report or pitch (founder, agency); organic orders by page (connected, GA4); rank and click history (enabler, search-feed); past SEO work and results (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List every claim in the report or pitch 2) Check each against our own clicks and orders for the pages named 3) Mark true, false or cannot tell 4) Write 5 questions the founder should ask 5) Log the verdict in the agency history
  - Output: a claim check with true, false or cannot tell on each line, and 5 questions. Next: feeds 21-finance-cash.
  - Watch: SEO fees per organic order (90 day, Shopify), then money spent only on work that moves sales, then gross profit.
- **17-02 Tie organic to store orders each month: sessions, orders, revenue and new customers by landing page, non-brand apart from brand** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 1st of each month. Enablers: search-feed.
  - Inputs: organic sessions and orders by landing page (connected, GA4); orders with UTM and new vs returning (connected, Shopify); brand vs non-brand query split (connected, Search Console).
  - Steps: 1) Pull organic sessions and orders by landing page for the month 2) Check GA4 organic orders against Shopify's organic count and note the gap 3) Split brand queries from non-brand by landing page 4) Rank the 20 pages that earn the most non-brand revenue 5) Write the 2 minute summary: grew or not, from which pages, what needs the founder
  - Output: a 2 minute monthly card with organic revenue, new customers and the top 20 pages. Next: feeds 17-04 (agency check) and 26-reports.
  - Watch: non-brand organic revenue (30 day, Shopify), then organic judged on money, then revenue.
- **17-01 Connect Search Console and an SEO data source such as OpenSEO through the MCP, and pull queries, pages, positions and clicks every day into one table** Score 32, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then daily. Enablers: search-feed.
  - Inputs: Search Console queries and pages, 16 months (connected, Search Console); an SEO data source for volumes, rival ranks and links, such as OpenSEO (buy, OpenSEO or similar); keyword universe with intent and priority (brain, seo shelf); organic landing page sessions and orders (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Connect Search Console and the SEO source through the MCP, read only 2) Pull 16 months of queries and pages as the baseline 3) Tag each query brand or non-brand and by intent 4) Join pages to GA4 organic sessions and orders 5) Check 10 rows by hand against the Search Console screen
  - Output: a daily table of query, page, position, clicks, impressions and orders. Next: feeds every job on this screen and 18-02.
  - Watch: days with a complete pull (30 day, search-feed), then organic decisions on fresh numbers, then revenue.
### T2. When money pages sit at position 8 to 20 or guides lose half their clicks, help me fix the pages Google already trusts before writing new ones, so I get the fastest organic growth for the least work

Metric: clicks to money pages from Search Console

- **17-05 List money pages at position 8 to 20 with the most impressions and write the fix for each: title, internal links and the content the top 3 have and we lack** Score 36, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: search-feed.
  - Inputs: pages with position and impressions per query (connected, Search Console); page revenue (connected, GA4); top 3 results for each target term (buy, OpenSEO or similar); SEO ideas on near-page-1 pages (have, ideas/v1/15-SEO-search.json).
  - Steps: 1) Pull pages at position 8 to 20 with over 500 impressions a month 2) Rank by impressions times conversion rate 3) For the top 10, read the top 3 results and list what they have that we lack 4) Write the fix: title, 3 internal links from strong pages, missing blocks 5) Ship 5 fixes and read positions at 4 weeks
  - Output: 10 pages with a written fix, 5 shipped, with a read date. Next: feeds 17-08 (intent check).
  - Watch: average position of the 10 pages (28 day, Search Console), then organic clicks to money pages, then revenue.
- **17-06 Rewrite titles and meta descriptions of 20 pages with high impressions and low click rate, using the words that win in Google Ads and Meta hooks** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: search-feed, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: pages with impressions, clicks and position (connected, Search Console); Google Ads headlines and search terms that convert (connected, Google Ads); Meta hooks with the best click rate (connected, Meta Ads); allowed claims (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List 20 pages in the top 10 with click rate below the norm for their position 2) Pull the paid headlines and hooks that win for the same topic 3) Write a new title and description led by the buyer's term and the winning angle 4) A person checks each is true and fits the page 5) Ship and read click rate at 4 weeks
  - Output: 20 new titles and descriptions, each linked to the paid ad or hook it borrows from. Next: feeds 12-12 (the reverse direction).
  - Watch: click rate of the 20 pages (28 day, Search Console), then more clicks without a rank change, then revenue.
- **17-08 Check each money page answers what its searchers wanted, compared with the top 3 results, and add the missing blocks such as size chart, comparison and FAQ** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each quarter, and when a page ranks but does not sell. Enablers: search-feed, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: queries per page with conversion rate (connected, Search Console); page behaviour: scroll and exits (setup, GA4 and Clarity); unanswered questions per product (enabler, question-matrix); page text and blocks (brain, site shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List pages with good rank and low conversion 2) Read the queries that bring buyers to each 3) Compare the page with the top 3 results for the lead query 4) Add the missing blocks with true answers 5) Read conversion rate at 4 weeks
  - Output: a list of pages with the blocks added and a read date. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro.
  - Watch: conversion rate of organic visits to the pages (28 day, GA4), then orders from search visits, then revenue.
- **17-07 Refresh the 10 guides that lost half their clicks: new facts, current products, the questions now asked, and a check for AI answers on top** Score 30, ready 1/5, days.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: search-feed, article-brief.
  - Inputs: clicks per page, this year vs last (connected, Search Console); current products and facts (brain, products shelf); questions buyers ask now (enabler, question-matrix); results page view per target term (buy, OpenSEO or similar).
  - Steps: 1) List guides with clicks down 50 percent year on year 2) Check each: lost rank, lost to an AI answer, or lost demand 3) Skip pages lost to demand; refresh the rest 4) Update facts and products, add 3 new questions, add a product block 5) Read clicks at 6 weeks
  - Output: 10 refreshed guides with the cause of loss noted on each. Next: feeds 18-08 (answer blocks).
  - Watch: clicks to the 10 guides (6 week, Search Console), then organic traffic won back, then revenue.
- **17-09 List 20 sites that link to 2 rivals and not to us, and pitch the 5 our buyers read with a real reason** Score 20, ready 2/5, weeks.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: search-feed, competitor-feed.
  - Inputs: backlinks of us and 3 rivals (buy, OpenSEO or similar); rival list (brain, competitors shelf); stories and data we can offer (brain, messaging shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull sites linking to 2 or more rivals and not us 2) Drop spam and paid link farms 3) Pick the 5 our buyers read 4) Write a pitch per site with a real reason: data, a story, a sample 5) Log replies and links won
  - Output: 5 pitches sent and a list of 20 target sites. Next: feeds 18-10 (lists assistants cite).
  - Watch: new links from relevant sites (90 day, OpenSEO or similar), then rank on money terms, then revenue.
### T3. When buyers search for things we sell and we have no page for them, help me write pages and articles from what they type, ask, buy on our ads and say in reviews, so I catch buyers early with pages that sell

Metric: new pages in the top 10 and orders from them

- **17-11 Pick FAQ and article work from the question coverage matrix: the blank cells asked most become answer blocks on product pages or full articles** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: question-matrix, article-brief.
  - Inputs: tickets and chat questions (connected, helpdesk); Amazon customer questions (setup, Seller Central); site search terms (connected, GA4); where each question is answered (enabler, question-matrix).
  - Steps: 1) Sort blank cells by how often each question is asked 2) Short answers go on the product page FAQ; long ones become articles 3) Write the top 10 with facts from the products shelf 4) Mark each matrix cell filled with the page URL 5) Read tickets and conversion on those products at 4 weeks
  - Output: 10 answers live and the matrix updated with URLs. Next: feeds 18-08 (answers assistants quote) and 15-customers-cx.
  - Watch: tickets on answered questions (28 day, helpdesk), then fewer doubts, more orders, then revenue.
- **17-10 Build 10 article briefs from 4 sources: Search Console gaps, Google Ads terms that sell, Meta's winning angles, and review and ticket questions** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: article-brief, signal-bridge, review-miner.

  - Steps: 1) Pull queries with 200 or more impressions and no page in the top 20 2) Match each to a paid search term that sells and a Meta angle that wins 3) Add the review and ticket words buyers use for the same problem 4) Write 10 briefs: the question, the angle, the proof, the product link 5) Founder picks 4 to write this month
  - Output: 10 briefs, each with its 4 sources named, 4 picked to write. Next: feeds 17-12 (buyer guides) and 16-13 (posts from articles).
  - Watch: impressions and orders from the new articles (90 day, Search Console), then new buyers from search, then revenue.

  - Inputs: queries that reach each product page (connected, Search Console); Google Ads and Amazon terms that sell (connected, Google Ads); product names in use (brain, products shelf); language bank of customer words (brain, customers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List pages whose title uses a name no one searches 2) Pull the terms buyers use for that product 3) Write title and H1 as the buyer's term, then our name 4) Founder approves name changes 5) Read impressions at 4 weeks
  - Output: renamed titles and H1s for product and collection pages. Next: feeds 12-15 (Shopping titles).
  - Watch: impressions of renamed pages (28 day, Search Console), then pages found by the words buyers use, then revenue.

  - Output: a list of new ad groups and hooks, each tied to an organic winner. Next: feeds 12-11 and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: orders from new ad groups and hooks (28 day, Google Ads), then paid ads built on proven words, then revenue.
- **17-15 Get seasonal pages live and refreshed 8 weeks before each season, with links from the home page** Score 30, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 8 weeks before each season on the calendar. Enablers: search-feed, article-brief.
  - Inputs: seasonal terms and last year's clicks by week (connected, Search Console); season dates and offers (brain, calendar shelf); stock for seasonal products (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) List last year's seasonal pages and their clicks by week 2) Refresh each with this year's products and offers 3) Build any missing page for a rising seasonal term 4) Link them from the home page and top collections 5) Check indexing 2 weeks later
  - Output: seasonal pages live and indexed 6 weeks before the season. Next: feeds 12-20 and 14-email-messaging peak plans.
  - Watch: clicks to seasonal pages vs last year (season, Search Console), then peak revenue from search, then revenue.
- **17-12 Write 5 'best X for Y' and 'X vs Y' guides that lead to a product, with an honest comparison** Score 28, ready 1/5, weeks.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: article-brief, review-miner.
  - Inputs: comparison and 'best' queries with volume (buy, OpenSEO or similar); rival reviews: where they win and fail (enabler, review-miner); battlecards (brain, competitors shelf); proof per product (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 5 comparison or 'best' terms with volume and buying intent 2) Read the top 3 results for each 3) Write each guide with a fair table, who each option is for, and our product where it truly fits 4) A person checks every claim about a rival is true and dated 5) Link each guide to the product and read orders at 90 days
  - Output: 5 published guides with a checked comparison table. Next: feeds 18-12 (comparison page for assistants).
  - Watch: orders from the 5 guides (90 day, GA4), then buyers caught while still choosing, then revenue.
- **17-14 Pull category search demand quarter by quarter for 3 years, compare it with our organic growth, and list the 10 terms rising fastest** Score 26, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: once a quarter. Enablers: search-feed, market-pulse.
  - Inputs: search volume by month for 50 category terms (buy, OpenSEO or similar); Google Trends for the category (setup, Google Trends); our organic clicks and orders by quarter (connected, Search Console).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 3 years of volume for the category terms 2) Sum by quarter and compute year on year change 3) Put our organic clicks and orders next to it 4) Write whether we grew faster or slower than the category 5) List the 10 rising terms and whether we have a page for each
  - Output: a quarter by quarter chart of category vs us, and 10 rising terms. Next: feeds 17-15 (seasonal pages) and 01-market-research.
  - Watch: our share of category clicks (quarter, Search Console), then budget set on the real trend, then revenue.
### T4. When a developer ships a change, a new app goes in or we move themes, help me catch anything that blocks Google or breaks pages the same day, so I do not lose years of search traffic in a week

  - Output: a list of pages with the action on each. Next: feeds 19-supply-inventory.
  - Watch: clicks kept on affected pages (28 day, Search Console), then ranking kept through stock gaps, then revenue.

## 18-answer-engines Answer engines

### T1. When a customer says 'ChatGPT told me your product does X' and it does not, help me find what assistants say about us, where they learned it, and fix it at the source everywhere the fact lives, so I stop selling a promise we cannot keep

Metric: share of prompt checks that state a wrong fact about us

- **18-01 Write the facts register: every fact buyers ask (price, sizes, materials, care, return window, warranty, shipping, founder story), its true value, and every place it lives** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then on every product or policy change. Enablers: facts-register, brain-graph.
  - Inputs: product facts and proof (brain, products shelf); offers, returns and shipping terms (brain, offers shelf); the facts page draft (brain, seo shelf); Amazon and other listings (setup, Seller Central); founder sign-off on each fact (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) List the 40 facts buyers ask most, from the question matrix and tickets 2) Write the true value for each from the brain 3) Crawl the site, feeds and listings for every place each fact appears 4) Founder signs the true values 5) Publish a plain facts page on the site from the register
  - Output: a signed register of 40 facts with every place each lives, and a live facts page. Next: feeds 18-03, 18-04, 18-05 and 18-15.
  - Watch: places where a fact differs from the register (30 day, facts-register), then assistants and support saying the same true thing, then risk.
- **18-05 Before a price, policy or shipping change, list every place the old fact lives and update all of them on switch day** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.

  - Steps: 1) Pull every place the fact lives from the register 2) Assign each place to an owner 3) Change all on switch day, including feeds, listings, FAQ and macros 4) Check each place the next day 5) Rerun 5 related prompts after 30 days
  - Output: a switch-day checklist done, with every place checked the next day. Next: none.
  - Watch: places still showing the old fact after 7 days (7 day, facts-register), then no customer arguments over an old promise, then customer satisfaction.
- **18-03 Fix each wrong fact at its source: find the page the assistant learned it from (our old page, a listing, a review site, a thread), correct or answer it, and recheck in 30 days** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when the monthly check finds a wrong fact. Enablers: facts-register, citation-map.

  - Steps: 1) For each wrong fact, open the cited links and find the wrong text 2) Fix it where we own the page; ask the owner or reply publicly where we do not 3) Log the source, the fix and the date 4) Rerun the same prompts after 30 days 5) Mark fixed, partly fixed or still wrong
  - Output: a fix log per wrong fact with its source and the 30 day recheck. Next: feeds 18-17 (proving what worked).
  - Watch: wrong facts still stated after 30 days (30 day, prompt checks), then fewer disputes over promises we never made, then customer satisfaction.
- **18-04 Find every place a fact differs, such as 60 day returns on the site and 30 on Amazon, and align them all to the register** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.

  - Steps: 1) Pull each fact from site, feed and listings 2) Mark every place that differs from the register 3) Fix the owned places first, then the listings 4) Log each fix with the date 5) Rerun the related prompts next month
  - Output: a diff list with every mismatch fixed or owned. Next: feeds 20-marketplaces.
  - Watch: facts that differ across places (30 day, facts-register), then one true answer everywhere, then risk.
- **18-02 Run 40 buyer prompts each month across ChatGPT, Gemini, Perplexity and Google AI answers, and log whether we are named, where, which facts are stated and which sources are cited** Score 30, ready 1/5, days.

  - Steps: 1) Run each prompt in each assistant from a clean session, 2 times 2) Log named or not, position, rivals named, facts stated and links cited 3) Mark each stated fact right or wrong against the register 4) A person checks 10 answers by hand 5) Compare with last month and write 3 lines on what moved
  - Output: a monthly prompt table with mention share, wrong facts and cited sources. Next: feeds 18-03 (fix at source), 18-09 (citation map) and 18-13 (rival claims).

## 19-supply-inventory Supply and inventory

### T1. When a best seller is on track to run out before a peak or a promo, help me see it while there is still time to order and know how many to buy, so I never lose the biggest sales weeks

Metric: days of stock left against supplier lead time plus buffer, for the top 20 products by gross profit

- **19-01 Compute days of stock left for every product and size against supplier lead time plus a 2 week buffer, and list what must be ordered this week, ranked by gross profit at risk** Score 44, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: stock-cover.
  - Inputs: stock on hand by variant and location (connected, Shopify inventory); stock at a 3PL or warehouse system, if used (setup, 3PL or WMS); 90 days of sales by variant (connected, Shopify orders); lead time and minimum order per supplier (brain, operations shelf (supply), founder interview question 23); open purchase orders with landing dates (brain, operations shelf (supply)); gross profit per unit (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull stock on hand and on order per variant 2) Compute sales per day from the last 28 and 90 days, and use the higher rate for best sellers 3) Days left = stock plus orders that land in time, divided by sales per day 4) Flag variants where days left is under lead time plus 14 days 5) Rank by gross profit per day that would be lost, and a person checks the top 10 against the shelf
  - Output: a weekly order list: variant, days left, lead time, and profit at risk. Next: feeds 19-02 (order size) and 21-03 (cash test for the order).
  - Watch: top 20 products by gross profit with days of stock under lead time plus buffer (weekly, Shopify inventory and purchase order records), then stockout days on best sellers, then revenue.
- **19-02 Size each reorder: forecast sales over lead time plus the cover target, subtract stock and open orders, round to the minimum order and case pack, and show the cash it needs and when** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when 19-01 flags a product. Enablers: stock-cover, po-ledger.
  - Inputs: the weekly order list (enabler, stock-cover); peaks and promos in the next 16 weeks (brain, calendar shelf and offers shelf); minimum order, case pack, price breaks and payment terms (brain, operations shelf (supply)); cash floor (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Forecast weekly sales for lead time plus 8 weeks, lifted for known peaks and promos 2) Subtract stock on hand and open orders, and round up to the minimum order and case pack 3) Check the next price break: is ordering more cheaper per unit and still safe for cash 4) Show deposit and balance dates and amounts 5) Founder approves or changes the number, and the order is logged in the purchase order ledger
  - Output: a draft purchase order per product with units, cost and payment dates, approved by the founder. Next: feeds 21-03 (cash test) and 19-10 (landed cost).
  - Watch: products below their reorder point with no open purchase order (weekly, Shopify inventory and purchase order records), then stockouts and overstock, then cash.
- **19-03 Build the Black Friday stock plan 12 weeks out: target units per top product from last year's peak weeks, this year's growth and planned promos, the gap to stock and open orders, and the last safe order date per supplier** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, 12 weeks before each peak. Enablers: stock-cover.
  - Inputs: last year's orders by week and variant (connected, Shopify orders); peak dates and when prep starts (brain, calendar shelf); promo plan for the peak (brain, offers shelf); lead times per supplier (brain, operations shelf (supply)); 3PL and carrier cut-off dates and peak capacity (setup, 3PL).
  - Steps: 1) Take last year's 6 peak weeks by variant and adjust for this year's growth rate 2) Add the lift from each planned promo, based on the last 3 promos 3) Subtract stock and open orders to get the gap per product 4) Work back the last safe order date from each supplier's lead time 5) Ask the 3PL and carriers for peak cut-offs and capacity, and log them 6) Founder signs the plan and the buys
  - Output: a peak plan: target, gap and last order date per top product, plus 3PL cut-offs. Next: feeds 19-02 (order size) and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: top products with stock on hand against the peak target (weekly, Shopify inventory), then peak week revenue, then revenue.
- **19-04 List every ad and shopping product spending on a product with under 14 days of stock, and send the list to the ads team to move budget before the spend runs** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday and Thursday. Enablers: stock-cover.
  - Inputs: ads with landing page and 7 day spend (connected, Meta Ads (read only)); Shopping spend by product (connected, Google Ads); days of stock per variant (enabler, stock-cover); which page and product id each product uses (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Map each ad's landing page or product id to a product 2) Join the last 7 days of spend to days of stock left 3) List ads on products under 14 days of stock, with spend and days left 4) Suggest the in-stock product each ad could point to instead 5) Send to the ads owner; the person changes the account, ecomOS does not
  - Output: a twice weekly list of ads on low stock, with spend and a swap. Next: feeds 11-paid-social and 12-google-ads.
  - Watch: ad spend on products with under 14 days of stock (weekly, Meta Ads, Google Ads and Shopify inventory), then wasted spend, and cancelled orders, then contribution margin.
- **19-05 Split stock cover between our warehouse and Amazon, list products where one side runs dry in under 21 days while the other has spare, and say how many units to move and by when** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday, for sellers on Amazon. Enablers: stock-cover, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: our stock and site sales (connected, Shopify); Amazon stock, inbound units and sales (setup, Amazon Seller Central); Amazon restock limits (setup, Amazon Seller Central); transit days from our warehouse to Amazon (brain, operations shelf (fulfilment)).
  - Steps: 1) Compute days of cover per product on each channel from its own sales rate 2) Flag products under 21 days on one channel and over 60 days on the other 3) Units to move = sales per day times 45 days, capped by the spare and by Amazon's limit 4) Draft the inbound shipment, and a person books it
  - Output: a transfer list: product, units, from, to, and ship-by date. Next: feeds 20-14 (Amazon stock plan).
  - Watch: stock cover in days per product, by channel (weekly, Shopify inventory and Amazon Seller Central), then Amazon rank and sales lost to stockouts, then revenue.
### T2. When the bank is low and the warehouse is full, help me find the cash stuck in stock that does not sell and turn it back into money, so I can pay for ads and the products that sell

Metric: value of stock with no sale in 180 days, at landed cost, monthly

- **19-06 List every product with no sale in 180 days or over 120 days of cover, value it at landed cost, and show the total cash stuck on one line** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: first Monday of each month. Enablers: stock-cover, po-ledger.
  - Inputs: stock and last sale date per variant (connected, Shopify); landed cost per unit (enabler, po-ledger); products being discontinued or seasonal (brain, products shelf (roadmap)).
  - Steps: 1) Pull stock and the last sale date per variant 2) Mark variants with no sale in 180 days, and those with over 120 days of cover 3) Value each at landed cost, and add storage cost per month 4) Total the cash stuck, split by product line 5) Founder marks which are discontinued, seasonal, or held on purpose
  - Output: a dead stock list with value, storage cost and a founder mark. Next: feeds 19-07 (clearance) and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: value of stock with no sale in 180 days, at landed cost (monthly, Shopify inventory and purchase order records), then cash free for ads and best sellers, then cash.
- **19-07 Pick a clearance route for each dead product: bundle with a best seller, VIP-only offer, outlet price, liquidator quote or donation, with the cash each returns against landed cost** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after 19-06 each month. Enablers: margin-view, stock-cover.
  - Inputs: the dead stock list (enabler, stock-cover); margin per best seller for bundles (enabler, margin-view); a liquidator quote (founder, founder); brand rules on discounts (brain, offers shelf).
  - Steps: 1) For each product, estimate units that would sell in 60 days on each route 2) Compute cash back after fees and shipping for each route 3) Drop routes the brand rules do not allow 4) Founder picks one route per product 5) Set a 60 day check on units cleared and cash back
  - Output: a clearance plan per product with route, cash back and a check date. Next: feeds 14-email-messaging (VIP offer) and 04-offers-pricing (bundles).
  - Watch: cash recovered from cleared stock against landed cost (60 day, Shopify orders), then cash, then cash.
- **19-08 Count returned units waiting at the warehouse, how long they have waited and how many are sellable, set a 5 day restock rule, and cut reorders that returns already cover** Score 30, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: stock-cover.
  - Inputs: returns received (connected, Shopify returns); warehouse returns log with grading (setup, 3PL); this week's reorder list (enabler, stock-cover).
  - Steps: 1) Pull returns received and their restock status 2) Compute days from received to back in stock, per product 3) Count the share graded sellable, and the reasons for the rest 4) Agree a 5 day restock rule with the warehouse 5) Take returned sellable units off this week's reorder list
  - Output: a returns backlog table and a trimmed reorder list. Next: feeds 19-02 (order size).
  - Watch: days from return received to back in stock (30 day, 3PL returns log and Shopify returns), then units bought that we already own, then cash.
- **19-09 Compare storage cost with gross profit per product at the 3PL and at Amazon over 90 days, and list products that cost more to store than they earn, plus Amazon units close to the aged stock fee** Score 26, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, and 60 days before Amazon's aged stock dates. Enablers: po-ledger, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: storage invoice by SKU (setup, 3PL); Amazon storage and stock age reports (setup, Amazon Seller Central); gross profit per product (brain, finance shelf); size per unit (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Split storage cost per product by the space it uses 2) Set it against 90 day gross profit 3) List products where storage is over 25 percent of gross profit 4) List Amazon units over 150 days old 5) Draft a move, removal or price cut for each, and the founder picks
  - Output: a storage table and one action per product. Next: feeds 19-07 (clearance) and 20-14 (Amazon stock plan).
  - Watch: storage cost against gross profit per product (90 day, 3PL and Amazon storage reports), then storage fees, then contribution margin.
### T3. When freight, duty, raw material or a supplier moves, help me know what each unit really costs to land and which suppliers are slipping, so I reprice or renegotiate before the next order

Metric: landed cost per unit against the cost used in the margin plan, per purchase order

- **19-10 Build landed cost for every purchase order: unit price, freight, duty, insurance, packaging and inbound handling split per unit, and flag any product 5 percent above the cost in the margin plan** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: when each order lands, and once for the last 12 months. Enablers: po-ledger.
  - Inputs: supplier invoices (founder, founder, or the accounting tool); freight, customs and duty bills (founder, founder); product cost used today (connected, Shopify cost field and finance shelf); units received per order (setup, 3PL receiving log).
  - Steps: 1) Enter each order with units and unit price 2) Add freight, duty, insurance and inbound bills; split freight per unit by volume and duty by value 3) Compare with the cost in the store and in the margin plan 4) Flag products 5 percent or more above plan, and a person approves the new cost in the store 5) Recompute gross margin per product at the new cost
  - Output: a landed cost table per order and product, with updated margins and flags. Next: feeds 21-07 (margin by product) and 04-offers-pricing.
  - Watch: landed cost per unit against the margin plan (per purchase order, purchase order records and Shopify cost field), then gross margin per product, then gross profit.
- **19-11 When a duty rate changes, recompute landed cost and margin per product at the new rate, list products that fall under the margin floor, and lay out reprice, absorb or move options** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a tariff change is announced. Enablers: po-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: tariff code and country of origin per product (founder, founder); the new duty rate (setup, public tariff schedule, typed into a sheet); landed cost per unit (enabler, po-ledger); margin floor (brain, finance shelf); how sales moved after past price changes (connected, Shopify orders).
  - Steps: 1) Recompute duty per unit at the new rate 2) Compute the new gross margin per product against the floor 3) For each product under the floor, show the price needed to hold margin and the sales lost at past price response 4) Add the option to move production, with lead time and cost from the supply shelf 5) Founder picks one option per product
  - Output: a table: product, old and new margin, price needed, and the founder's choice. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: gross margin per unit at the new duty rate (per purchase order, purchase order records), then gross profit per unit, then gross profit.
- **19-12 Log the price of the main raw material (such as cotton) every month next to the price last agreed with the supplier, and draft a cost-down ask when it falls 10 percent** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: market-pulse, po-ledger.
  - Inputs: the 1 or 2 materials that drive each top product's cost (brain, products shelf); a public price index for each material (setup, a monthly entry in a sheet); last agreed price and its date (enabler, po-ledger); supplier contract and price lock dates (brain, operations shelf (supply)).
  - Steps: 1) Name the 1 or 2 materials that drive each top product's cost 2) Log the index each month since the last price was agreed 3) Compute the change since then 4) When it falls 10 percent or more, draft a cost-down letter with the numbers 5) When it rises 10 percent or more, tell finance the likely new price and when it lands
  - Output: a monthly input cost line per top product, and a draft letter when it moves. Next: feeds 19-14 (supplier score) and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: input cost index against the price when cost was last agreed (monthly, public index logged in the brain), then cost per unit, then gross profit.
- **19-13 Match every supplier invoice to its purchase order and the units received before paying, and list mismatches by count and value** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when each supplier invoice arrives. Enablers: po-ledger.
  - Inputs: the purchase order (brain, operations shelf (supply)); units received (setup, 3PL receiving log); supplier invoice (founder, founder, or the accounting tool).
  - Steps: 1) Line up ordered units and price, received units, and invoiced units and price 2) Flag any line that differs by 1 unit or 1 percent 3) Hold payment on flagged lines and draft the note to the supplier 4) Log the outcome and the value saved
  - Output: a match table per invoice, with holds and notes. Next: feeds 19-14 (supplier score) and 21-04 (which bills to pay).
  - Watch: purchase orders where invoice, order and goods do not match, by value (monthly, purchase order records), then cash paid for stock that never came, then cash.
- **19-14 Score each supplier over 90 days on on-time delivery, defects found at receiving and price drift, and list products that depend on one supplier or one country with no backup** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each quarter, and when a supplier is late again. Enablers: po-ledger.
  - Inputs: promised and actual arrival dates per order (enabler, po-ledger); defects found at receiving (setup, 3PL receiving log); returns tagged defective (connected, Shopify returns); supplier and country per product (brain, operations shelf (supply)); backup suppliers already vetted (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) For each supplier, compute on-time share, defect rate and price change per unit 2) Mark suppliers getting worse over their last 3 orders 3) Compute each supplier's and country's share of units bought in 12 months 4) List top products with over 50 percent from one source and no vetted backup 5) Founder picks 1 product to get a second quote for this quarter
  - Output: a supplier scorecard and a single-source risk list. Next: feeds 19-15 (inspection) and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: supplier on-time delivery and defect rate per order (rolling 90 day, purchase order records), then stockouts and defects, then risk.
- **19-15 Book a third party inspection before paying the balance on any new supplier or any order over $20,000, with a checklist built from past defect tickets and return reasons** Score 28, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: when a factory says an order is ready to ship. Enablers: po-ledger, review-miner.
  - Inputs: top defect reasons for the product over 12 months (connected, helpdesk tags and Shopify returns); product spec and tolerances (brain, products shelf); an inspection service, about $300 a visit (buy, inspection company); order value and balance date (enabler, po-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the top defect reasons for the product over 12 months 2) Turn them into a checklist with photos of good and bad units 3) Book the inspection 5 days before the ship date 4) Read the report: pass, fix and re-inspect, or refuse 5) Pay the balance only on a pass, and log the result on the supplier score
  - Output: an inspection checklist, the report, and a pay or hold decision. Next: feeds 19-14 (supplier score) and 15-13 (bad batch alert).
  - Watch: defect rate found at receiving, per purchase order (per quarter, purchase order records), then returns and refunds for defects, then gross profit.
### T4. When parcels arrive late, damaged or go back, help me see which carrier, region or warehouse fails and what it costs, so I set a promise I can keep and stop paying for their mistakes

Metric: on time share and cost per delivered parcel, by carrier and region, 30 day

- **19-17 Set the delivery date shown on product and checkout pages from real door to door times, the 90th percentile by region and carrier, not the carrier's brochure** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: carrier-scorecard.
  - Inputs: order to delivery times (setup, Shopify fulfilment and the tracking app); the delivery promise shown today (brain, site shelf); order status tickets (connected, helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) Compute median and 90th percentile days from order to delivery by region and carrier 2) Compare them with the date shown on the site today 3) Draft the new promise per region, and a person updates the page and checkout 4) Watch order status tickets and conversion for 4 weeks
  - Output: a delivery promise per region, live on the site, with a before and after. Next: feeds 15-10 (early delivery notes) and 07-landing-pages-cro.
  - Watch: share of orders delivered by the date shown to the buyer (30 day, Shopify fulfilment and the tracking app), then order status tickets and refunds, then customer satisfaction.
- **19-20 List delivery exceptions every morning (wrong address, failed attempt, held at depot) with the hours left to act, and have a person contact each customer the same day** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every morning. Enablers: carrier-scorecard.
  - Inputs: open tracking exceptions (setup, tracking app); customer phone and email (connected, Shopify); reply templates for each exception (brain, operations shelf (support)).
  - Steps: 1) Pull open exceptions and the hours left before the parcel goes back 2) Sort by hours left and order value 3) Text or email each customer with the step they need to take 4) A person updates the carrier and logs the outcome
  - Output: a daily exception list with each one closed or returned. Next: feeds 15-10 (early delivery notes).
  - Watch: open delivery exceptions with under 24 hours left to act (daily, tracking app), then return to sender cost and lost customers, then customer satisfaction.
- **19-16 Build the carrier scorecard for the last 30 days: on-time share, first attempt delivery, damage and loss, and cost per delivered parcel by carrier and region, and name the worst lane with a cheaper option** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly, and when failed deliveries jump in a region. Enablers: carrier-scorecard.
  - Inputs: fulfilment events per order (connected, Shopify); carrier tracking scans (setup, tracking or shipping app (AfterShip, ShipStation)); carrier invoices (setup, carrier portal export); damage and loss tickets (connected, helpdesk); rate cards from 2 other carriers (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Join each order's tracking scans to its region and carrier 2) Compute on-time share against the date shown, first attempt success, and damage or loss rate 3) Divide the carrier bill by delivered parcels for each lane 4) Name the worst lane on cost and on service 5) Price the same lane with 2 other carriers, and draft the switch for the founder
  - Output: a carrier by region table, and a switch proposal for the worst lane. Next: feeds 19-17 (delivery promise), 19-18 (invoice check) and 21-10 (shipping cost).
  - Watch: cost per delivered parcel and on-time share, by carrier and region (30 day, shipping app and carrier invoices), then shipping cost per order, and delivery tickets, then contribution margin.
- **19-19 Measure the warehouse's real dispatch time against the contract, from order to label to first carrier scan, and send them a monthly scorecard with pick errors from tickets** Score 30, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: weekly, with a scorecard each month. Enablers: carrier-scorecard.
  - Inputs: order time, label time and first scan (setup, Shopify fulfilment and the tracking app); 3PL contract terms and credits (founder, founder); wrong item and missing item tickets (connected, helpdesk tags).
  - Steps: 1) Compute hours from order to label and to first scan, per day 2) Compute the share shipped within the promised time, and the worst days 3) Count wrong item and missing item tickets per 1,000 orders 4) Send the warehouse a 1 page scorecard with the numbers and any credits owed under the contract
  - Output: a monthly warehouse scorecard and a credit request. Next: feeds 19-16 (carrier scorecard) and 23-team-ops.
  - Watch: share of orders shipped within the promised dispatch time (weekly, Shopify fulfilment and the tracking app), then late delivery tickets, then customer satisfaction.
- **19-18 Check each carrier invoice against the expected charge from shipped weight, box size and zone, list overcharges, surcharges and late delivery refunds owed, and file the claims** Score 26, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, when the carrier invoice arrives. Enablers: carrier-scorecard.
  - Inputs: carrier invoice lines (setup, carrier portal export); shipped weight and box size per order (setup, shipping app); the agreed rate card (founder, founder); lost and damaged parcels (connected, helpdesk tags).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the expected charge per parcel from the rate card 2) Flag parcels billed 5 percent or more over the expected charge 3) Add lost and damaged parcels not yet claimed 4) Draft claims with order numbers and proof, and a person files them 5) Log money recovered each month
  - Output: a claim file per carrier and a monthly recovered total. Next: feeds 21-10 (shipping cost).
  - Watch: carrier invoice amount against expected charge (monthly, carrier invoices and shipping app), then shipping cost per order, then contribution margin.

## 20-marketplaces Marketplaces and quick commerce

### T1. When I sell on Amazon or TikTok Shop, or am asked to, help me decide which products go where, at what price and with what ads, so each marketplace adds profit and not just sales

Metric: contribution margin per unit by marketplace and product, after all fees, trailing 30 days

- **20-05 Cut Amazon campaigns that spend more than the product earns: set each product's break-even ad cost of sale from its margin, list campaigns and search terms above it for 14 days, and draft negatives and bid cuts** Score 36, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: marketplace-plan, margin-view.
  - Inputs: campaigns and search terms with spend and sales (setup, Amazon Ads); margin per unit before ads (enabler, margin-view); brand terms to protect (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Break-even ad cost of sale = margin before ads divided by price 2) List campaigns over break-even for 14 days, with spend and sales 3) List search terms with spend over 2 times the product's cost per sale and no sale, as negatives 4) Draft bid cuts and negatives in a sheet, and a person applies them 5) Recheck in 14 days and log the change
  - Output: a draft list of negatives and bid cuts per campaign, applied by a person. Next: feeds 13-affiliate-marketplace-ads.
  - Watch: ad cost of sale per campaign against the product's break-even (14 day, Amazon Ads), then Amazon profit, then contribution margin.
- **20-04 Check marketplace prices against site prices for the top 20 products every week, counting coupons and resellers, and flag any gap more than 5 percent below the site** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: listing-watch, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: site prices (connected, Shopify); our marketplace prices and coupons (setup, Amazon Seller Central and TikTok Shop); reseller offers on our listings (setup, Amazon Seller Central); price floors (enabler, marketplace-plan).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the final price a buyer pays on each channel, after coupons 2) Compute the gap to the site price 3) Flag gaps over 5 percent and say who set them: us, a coupon, or a reseller 4) Draft the fix: raise the coupon floor, end the deal, or report the reseller
  - Output: a weekly price gap table with a fix for each flag. Next: feeds 20-09 (reseller removal) and 04-offers-pricing.
  - Watch: price gap between site and each marketplace per top product (weekly, store catalogue and marketplace listings), then site conversion and marketplace margin, then contribution margin.
- **20-06 Say yes or no to a Prime Day or Lightning Deal invite with one number: margin per unit at the deal price after the deal fee and ads, units needed, and stock left after** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a deal invite arrives. Enablers: marketplace-plan, margin-view.
  - Inputs: deal price and deal fee (setup, Amazon Seller Central); margin per unit (enabler, margin-view); Amazon stock (setup, Amazon Seller Central); results of the last deals (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute margin per unit at the deal price after fees and ads 2) Compute units needed to cover the fee, and stock left for 30 days after 3) Compare with the last 2 deals' lift and the dip that followed 4) Write yes or no with the number, and the founder decides
  - Output: a yes or no note with margin per unit, units needed and stock left. Next: feeds 19-05 (stock split).
  - Watch: contribution margin per unit at the deal price (per event, fee and ad reports), then deal profit, then contribution margin.
- **20-01 Write the Amazon plan per product: margin per unit after referral fee, fulfilment fee, storage, returns and ads, next to site margin, then mark each product list, bundle only or do not list, with a price floor** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once before listing, then each quarter. Enablers: marketplace-plan, margin-view.
  - Inputs: landed cost per unit (brain, finance shelf); fee preview and size tier per product (setup, Amazon Seller Central); ad cost of sale on Amazon today (setup, Amazon Ads); site margin per product (enabler, margin-view); marketplace rules and category limits (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List each product with price, landed cost, size tier and weight 2) Compute margin per unit after every fee and ads at today's cost of sale 3) Set it next to site margin and a 30 percent floor from the idea bank, which the founder can change 4) Mark each product: list, list only in a bundle, or do not list 5) Set a price floor per product that keeps the Amazon price at or above the site price 6) Founder signs the plan
  - Output: a signed product by channel plan with margin per unit and price floors. Next: feeds 20-05 (ad cuts), 20-04 (price check) and 19-05 (stock split).
  - Watch: contribution margin per unit by marketplace and product, after all fees (30 day, settlement and fee reports), then marketplace profit, then contribution margin.
- **20-02 Write the TikTok Shop plan: pick 3 to 5 products that sell on impulse, set shop price and creator commission inside the margin, send samples to 20 creators, and keep or cut each creator after 30 days on sales** Score 30, ready 2/5, weeks.
  - When: once to launch, then a 30 day read. Enablers: marketplace-plan, creator-roster, margin-view.
  - Inputs: TikTok Shop seller account (setup, TikTok Shop); margin per product (enabler, margin-view); creator fit rules and past creators (brain, creators shelf); sample budget and units (founder, founder); product clips with usage rights (brain, content and brand kit shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 3 to 5 products under about $40 that show well in 15 seconds 2) Set the shop price and a commission of 10 to 20 percent that keeps margin above the floor 3) List 40 creators by fit, audience quality and past shop sales, and a person picks 20 4) Send samples with a 1 page brief: the hook that works on Meta, the claim rules, the offer 5) Track videos posted, views, orders and margin per creator for 30 days 6) Keep the top 5, cut the rest, and log what the winning videos share
  - Output: a live shop with 3 to 5 products, 20 creators seeded, and a 30 day keep or cut list. Next: feeds 09-creative (creator hooks) and 22-measurement (TikTok Shop in total sales).
  - Watch: TikTok Shop orders and margin per creator (30 day, TikTok Shop seller center), then new customers from TikTok, then revenue.
- **20-03 Write a 1 page go or no go memo before adding a marketplace: 90 days of fees, stock needed, staff hours and the site sales it may take, next to what the current marketplaces earn per staff hour** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when someone proposes a new marketplace. Enablers: marketplace-plan, margin-view.
  - Inputs: margin per live marketplace (enabler, margin-view); team hours spent in each seller portal (founder, founder); fee schedule of the new marketplace (setup, public fee page typed into a sheet); goals and red lines (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute margin per staff hour for each live marketplace from last quarter 2) Model the new one at 3 sales levels: fees, ads, stock and hours 3) Estimate site sales it may take, from price gaps and buyer overlap 4) Write the memo: the number, the risk, and what we stop doing to make time 5) Founder decides, and the decision is logged
  - Output: a 1 page memo with a go or no go, and the founder's logged decision. Next: feeds 20-02 (TikTok Shop plan), or none.
  - Watch: contribution margin per marketplace per staff hour (quarterly, settlement reports and team time log), then time and cash spent on channels that pay, then contribution margin.
### T2. When a health warning, a suppressed listing or a reseller hits a top product, help me see it the same day and fix it, so I do not lose the account, the Buy Box or a week of sales

Metric: order defect rate against the limit, suppressed top listings, and Buy Box win rate

- **20-07 Check account health every day (order defect rate, late shipment, cancel rate, valid tracking and policy notices) against the limits, alert at half the limit, and draft the reply with cause and fix** Score 40, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: daily, and on every notice. Enablers: listing-watch.
  - Inputs: account health and performance pages (setup, Amazon Seller Central and Walmart Seller Center); marketplace orders with tracking (setup, Amazon Seller Central); policy rules for the category (brain, market shelf); who may send replies and change the account (brain, decisions and goals shelf (approval rules)).
  - Steps: 1) Pull each health number and its limit 2) Alert when any number passes half its limit, or when a notice lands 3) Find the orders behind the number: late, cancelled, no tracking, claim 4) Draft the plan of action: the cause, the fix done, and the fix that stops it again 5) A named person reviews and sends it
  - Output: a daily health line and a drafted reply for each notice. Next: feeds 20-08 (listing problems) and 19-19 (warehouse speed).
  - Watch: order defect rate and late shipment rate against the limits (60 day, Amazon Seller Central account health), then account safety and money held, then risk.
- **20-08 Each morning list every top product that is suppressed, has an open listing error or holds the Buy Box under 90 percent, with the seller who holds it and the ad spend still running** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every morning. Enablers: listing-watch.
  - Inputs: suppressed and errored listing reports (setup, Amazon Seller Central); Buy Box share by product (setup, Seller Central business reports); hourly Buy Box checks, if wanted (buy, a Buy Box tracker); ad spend by product (setup, Amazon Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull suppressed and errored listings and read each error 2) Pull yesterday's Buy Box share per product 3) Join ad spend per product 4) Write the fix for each problem: the missing field, the image, or the seller to report 5) A person makes the fix and pauses ads on products with no Buy Box
  - Output: a morning list: product, problem, fix, and ad spend at risk. Next: feeds 20-09 (reseller removal).
  - Watch: suppressed top listings, and Buy Box win rate per advertised product (7 day, Amazon Seller Central), then marketplace sales, then revenue.
- **20-09 Find every seller on our listings each week, test buy from any selling below our floor, and file a Brand Registry report with the proof** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: listing-watch.
  - Inputs: offers and sellers on our listings (setup, Amazon Seller Central); Brand Registry access (setup, Amazon Brand Registry, needs a trademark); list of authorised sellers (founder, founder); price floors (enabler, marketplace-plan); a small test buy budget (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) List sellers and prices on each of our listings 2) Mark authorised sellers from the founder's list 3) Test buy from the worst unauthorised seller and photograph what arrives 4) A person files the report with the photos, the order and the listing 5) Recheck 7 days later
  - Output: a seller list, test buy proof, and filed reports. Next: feeds 20-04 (price gap).
  - Watch: unauthorised sellers on our listings (weekly, Amazon Seller Central and Brand Registry), then Buy Box share, and bad reviews from fakes, then risk.
- **20-10 Keep a register of every safety certificate, test report and label each product needs on each marketplace, with expiry dates, and alert 60 days before one runs out** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, and when a listing is pulled. Enablers: listing-watch.
  - Inputs: documents per product (founder, founder); category requirements per marketplace (brain, market shelf); compliance notices (setup, Amazon Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) List each product and marketplace, and the documents each needs 2) Link each document with its issue and expiry date 3) Mark documents missing or expiring within 60 days 4) Send the list to the owner with the lab or supplier to ask
  - Output: a document register with gaps and owners. Next: none.
  - Watch: listings with a missing or expired compliance document (monthly, brain document register and marketplace notices), then listings pulled, then risk.
### T3. When the payout lands short or fees creep up, help me find every dollar owed and claim it before the window closes, so the money comes back

Metric: settlement shortfall and reimbursement owed but not claimed, monthly

- **20-11 Rebuild each Amazon, Walmart and TikTok Shop payout from orders, fee by fee, compare it with what landed, and list every gap over $50 with the line it sits on** Score 34, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: on every settlement. Enablers: settlement-check.
  - Inputs: settlement and fee reports (setup, Amazon Seller Central and TikTok Shop); orders and refunds per marketplace (setup, Amazon Seller Central and TikTok Shop); expected fee per product (enabler, marketplace-plan); reserve and payout rules (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the settlement and every order, refund and fee in it 2) Compute the expected payout from orders and the fee schedule 3) Line up expected and actual, fee by fee 4) List gaps over $50 with the order and fee line, plus any reserve held and its release date 5) A person opens a case for each gap and logs the outcome
  - Output: a reconciliation per settlement with gaps, reserves and cases opened. Next: feeds 21-02 (payout check) and 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: settlement shortfall: expected minus actual payout (per settlement, marketplace settlement reports), then cash back, then cash.
- **20-14 Plan Amazon stock each week: days of cover against restock lead time, units over 150 days old, and the storage limit, and choose which products get the space before Q4** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday, and when Amazon changes the storage limit. Enablers: marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: Amazon stock, stock age and storage limits (setup, Amazon Seller Central); lead time to get stock into Amazon (brain, operations shelf (fulfilment)); margin per product (brain, finance shelf); Q4 targets (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute days of cover per product at Amazon against lead time 2) List units over 150 days old and the date their fee starts 3) Rank products by profit per cubic foot 4) Give the space to the top products, and plan removal or price cuts for aged units 5) A person books the shipments
  - Output: a weekly Amazon stock plan: send, hold or remove, per product. Next: feeds 19-05 (stock split).
  - Watch: top products with fewer days of stock at Amazon than lead time (daily, Amazon restock and stock reports), then Amazon stockouts and storage fees, then revenue.
- **20-12 Reconcile Amazon warehouse units sent, received, sold, returned and on hand each month, and list units lost, damaged or refunded but never returned, with the claim deadline for each** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: settlement-check.
  - Inputs: shipments sent in (setup, Amazon Seller Central); inventory ledger and past reimbursements (setup, Amazon Seller Central); refunds and returns (setup, Amazon Seller Central); unit cost (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Line up units per product through each step 2) Flag gaps: received short, lost in the warehouse, damaged, refunded with no return in 45 days 3) Value each gap and mark its claim deadline 4) Draft each claim with the shipment id and proof, and a person files it 5) Log money back each month
  - Output: a monthly claim list with value and deadlines, and money recovered. Next: feeds 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: reimbursement owed and not yet claimed (monthly, Amazon inventory and reimbursement reports), then cash back, then cash.
- **20-13 Check each product's Amazon size tier and weight against a measured box, dispute wrong tiers, and redesign packaging where one size tier down saves more than it costs** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once, then on every new product or fee change. Enablers: settlement-check.
  - Inputs: measured size and weight of 3 units per product (founder, founder, with a tape and a scale); Amazon's recorded size and fee per product (setup, Amazon Seller Central); a quote for a smaller box (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Measure 3 units per product and log the numbers 2) Compare them with Amazon's size and fee 3) Open a case wherever Amazon's numbers are bigger 4) For products within 1 inch of the next tier down, price a smaller box against the fee saved per year
  - Output: a size tier table, cases opened, and a packaging change list. Next: feeds 19-10 (landed cost).
  - Watch: change in fulfilment fee per unit, per product (30 day, Amazon fee reports), then fee per unit, then contribution margin.
### T4. When marketplace shoppers ask, review and complain in their own words, help me use those words to fix listings and feed our ads, and bring ad winners back to the listing, so every channel sells with what buyers already told us

Metric: unit buy rate and star rating per top product, trailing 30 days

- **20-19 When marketplace sales fall, split units into visits and buy rate per top product week on week, then check rank on the top 10 search terms, price, Buy Box, reviews and stock, and write the 1 line cause** Score 40, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when units fall 15 percent week on week. Enablers: listing-watch.
  - Inputs: visits and unit buy rate per product (setup, Seller Central business reports); rank on top search terms (setup, Brand Analytics, or a rank tracker); price, Buy Box and stock history (setup, Amazon Seller Central); ad spend and impressions (setup, Amazon Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Split the drop into fewer visits or a lower buy rate 2) If visits fell, check rank on the top terms, ad spend and impressions 3) If buy rate fell, check price changes, Buy Box share, new bad reviews and sizes out of stock 4) Write the cause in 1 line with the fix, and a person owns it
  - Output: a 1 line cause and fix per product, with the numbers behind it. Next: feeds 20-08 (listing problems) or 20-05 (ad cuts).
  - Watch: visits and unit buy rate per top product (weekly, Seller Central business reports), then marketplace revenue, then revenue.
- **20-16 Turn Amazon reviews into Meta ads: pull 4 and 5 star reviews, take the buyers' exact words for before and after, tag each by avatar and awareness level, and write 10 hooks and 3 review ads to launch as one test** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once a month. Enablers: signal-bridge, review-miner, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: our Amazon reviews (setup, Amazon Seller Central); avatars and allowed claims (brain, customers shelf and claims shelf); awareness levels where we run fewest ads (enabler, awareness-map); Meta results to judge the test (connected, Meta Ads (read only)).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 4 and 5 star reviews from the last 12 months 2) Cut each into the before state, the result and the exact phrase, and tag by avatar and awareness level 3) Pick phrases for the awareness level where we run the fewest ads 4) Write 10 hooks in the buyer's words and 3 static review ads that show the star rating as it is on Amazon 5) Founder approves every claim and quote 6) A person launches them on Meta as one test, and the result goes to the win and loss ledger
  - Output: 10 hooks and 3 approved ads, launched as one test. Next: feeds 09-creative, 11-paid-social and 25-experiments.
  - Watch: cost per new customer of the review ads against the account average (14 day, Meta Ads), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **20-15 Build the Amazon question coverage matrix: every question from customer questions, buyer messages, reviews and our helpdesk in rows, and title, bullets, images, A+ page and FAQ in columns, then fill the 10 biggest blanks** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: once per top product, then monthly. Enablers: question-matrix, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: Amazon customer questions and buyer messages (setup, Amazon Seller Central); reviews on our listings (setup, Amazon Seller Central); helpdesk tickets about the product (connected, helpdesk); current listing text and images (setup, Amazon Seller Central); claims we may make (brain, claims shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the questions, merge duplicates and count them 2) Mark where each is answered in the listing: title, bullets, images, A+ page, or nowhere 3) Rank blanks by count and by how close to buying they are 4) Write each answer as a bullet line or an image caption, and a person checks it against the claims shelf 5) Update the listing and log the date to compare buy rate
  - Output: a matrix per product, and a listing update with 10 answers placed. Next: feeds 20-19 (sales drop check) and 15-06 (site question matrix).
  - Watch: unit buy rate per top product (30 day, Seller Central business reports), then marketplace conversion, then revenue.
- **20-20 When a top product's Amazon rating slips, read the reviews since the slide, find the 1 complaint behind it and the return reasons that match, and send the fix to product or listing** Score 30, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when a top product drops 0.2 stars in 30 days. Enablers: review-miner.
  - Inputs: reviews since the drop (setup, Amazon Seller Central); marketplace return reasons (setup, Amazon Seller Central); batch dates (brain, operations shelf (supply)); helpdesk tickets on the product (connected, helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) Pull reviews since the drop and group them 2) Match them to return reasons and batch dates 3) Name the 1 complaint behind most of the slide 4) Write the fix: a listing line, an image, a size note, or a supplier call 5) Reach unhappy buyers only through Amazon's allowed path, and never tie a refund to a review
  - Output: a 1 page cause and fix note with an owner. Next: feeds 15-13 (bad batch alert) and 20-15 (Amazon question matrix).
  - Watch: star rating per top product and share of new reviews citing the top complaint (30 day, marketplace reviews), then marketplace buy rate, then revenue.
- **20-18 Mine the 1 to 3 star reviews of the top 5 rival products on Amazon, group the complaints, mark which ones we solve, and add a comparison image to our listing and 3 lines to our ads** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then each quarter. Enablers: review-miner, signal-bridge.
  - Inputs: rival product links (brain, competitors shelf); rival reviews (buy, a review export tool, or copied by hand); our proof for each claim (brain, claims shelf); listing images (setup, Amazon Seller Central).
  - Steps: 1) Pull 100 low star reviews per rival 2) Group the complaints and count them 3) Mark each: we solve it (with proof), same problem, or unknown 4) Draft a comparison image on 3 solved complaints, with no rival names or logos 5) Write 3 ad lines, and the founder approves every claim
  - Output: a complaint table for 5 rivals, 1 comparison image and 3 ad lines. Next: feeds 10-copy and 03-competitors.
  - Watch: unit buy rate on our listing (30 day, Seller Central business reports), then marketplace and paid social conversion, then revenue.
- **20-17 Move Meta winners onto Amazon: take the top 3 Meta angles by cost per new customer, rewrite the listing's title, first bullet and 2 images around them, and run an A/B test in Amazon's listing test tool** Score 26, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: signal-bridge, creative-tags.
  - Inputs: top Meta ads by cost per new customer over 90 days (connected, Meta Ads (read only)); each winner's angle and hook (enabler, creative-tags); Brand Registry and the listing test tool (setup, Amazon Seller Central); product photos with rights (brain, content and brand kit shelf); marketplace image and title rules (brain, market shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the top 3 angles on Meta by cost per new customer over 90 days 2) Write a title and first bullet for each, inside Amazon's rules 3) Design 2 listing images per angle from real product photos 4) A person sets up the test for 8 weeks 5) Keep the winner, and send the result to the win and loss ledger
  - Output: a live listing test with 2 versions, and a readout. Next: feeds 25-experiments.
  - Watch: unit buy rate on the tested product (8 week, Amazon listing test report), then marketplace conversion, then revenue.

## 21-finance-cash Finance and cash

### T1. When the bank looks thin ahead of a supplier deposit and payroll, help me see the lowest cash day in the next 13 weeks and what to move, so I never scramble for a panic loan

Metric: lowest projected cash balance in the next 13 weeks against the cash floor, weekly

- **21-01 Build the 13 week cash forecast: bank balance today, payouts by day, bills, payroll, supplier deposits, ad plan and loan payments, and mark the lowest cash day against the floor** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then every Monday. Enablers: cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: bank balance and 90 days of transactions (setup, a read-only bank feed or the accounting tool); store payout schedule (connected, Shopify Payments); other payouts: PayPal, marketplaces, installment lenders (setup, each processor or seller portal); open bills, payroll and loan payments (setup, accounting tool (QuickBooks, Xero) or founder); purchase orders with deposit and balance dates (brain, operations shelf (supply)); ad budget per week and the cash floor (founder, finance shelf (budgets) and founder).
  - Steps: 1) Start from today's bank balance 2) Add expected payouts by day, from the sales run rate and each processor's payout delay 3) Subtract bills, payroll, rent, supplier deposits and balances, loan payments and planned ad spend, by week 4) Mark the lowest day and the gap to the floor 5) Founder checks the big lines and adds anything missing 6) Each Monday compare last week's forecast with what happened and note the miss
  - Output: a 13 week table with the lowest cash day, the gap to the floor, and last week's forecast error. Next: feeds 21-03 (order test), 21-04 (which bills first), 21-05 (financing) and 19-02 (order size).
  - Watch: lowest projected cash balance in the next 13 weeks against the floor (weekly, bank feed and the forecast), then cash runway, then cash.
- **21-02 Check every payout each day against what was expected from orders and processor terms, and list the late or short ones with the reason: reserve, hold, chargeback or bank holiday** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: every day. Enablers: money-match.
  - Inputs: Shopify Payments payouts, disputes and reserves (connected, Shopify); PayPal, Stripe, installment lender and marketplace payouts (setup, each processor or seller portal); bank deposits (setup, a read-only bank feed).
  - Steps: 1) Compute the expected payout per source per day from orders, fees and payout delay 2) Match each to a bank deposit by amount and date 3) List late or short payouts with the reason given in the processor report 4) Draft the chase note for each, and a person sends it
  - Output: a daily payout table with gaps and reasons. Next: feeds 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: payouts late or short against expected, by source (daily, processor reports and bank feed), then cash on hand, then cash.
- **21-03 Test a big purchase order against cash before signing: add its deposit and balance dates to a 26 week forecast, show the lowest point, and price 3 fixes: split shipment, longer terms, smaller first order** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before any order over 20 percent of monthly revenue. Enablers: cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: the draft purchase order (brain, operations shelf (supply), or 19-02); the cash forecast (enabler, cash-forecast); supplier payment terms (brain, operations shelf (supply)); sales plan for the stock (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Add the order's cash out dates, and the sales it brings in, by week 2) Show the lowest cash point over 26 weeks 3) Price 3 fixes: 2 shipments, 30/70 terms, 70 percent of units first 4) Founder picks one, and the ask to the supplier is drafted
  - Output: a cash test for the order with 3 options and the founder's pick. Next: feeds 19-02 (order size).
  - Watch: lowest cash in 26 weeks with the order, against the floor (per purchase order, the forecast), then cash runway, then cash.
- **21-04 When several bills fall in a short week, rank them by late fee, supplier importance and grace days, and set the pay order that keeps cash above the floor** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: weekly, when the forecast dips under the floor. Enablers: cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: open bills with due dates (setup, accounting tool); late fees and grace terms (founder, founder); suppliers we cannot upset (brain, operations shelf (supply)).
  - Steps: 1) List bills due in the next 2 weeks with amount, due date, late fee and grace days 2) Mark the must-pay bills: payroll, tax, critical suppliers 3) Order the rest by cost of paying late 4) Show cash each day under the plan, and the founder approves
  - Output: a pay now, pay later list with dates. Next: feeds 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: bills paid late and late fees paid (monthly, books and bank feed), then supplier trust and fees, then cash.
- **21-05 Price every financing offer in plain terms: the true yearly cost of Shopify Capital, a revenue loan or a credit line, with the repayments dropped into the forecast to show the lowest cash with and without** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when an offer arrives. Enablers: cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: offer terms: amount, fee, holdback share (founder, founder); the cash forecast (enabler, cash-forecast); daily sales run rate (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Turn the fee and holdback into a yearly cost, from how fast sales would repay it 2) Model repayments by day from the sales run rate 3) Show the lowest cash with and without the loan 4) Show what the money would buy (stock, ads) and the profit it should return 5) Founder decides, and the reason is logged
  - Output: a 1 page note per offer: yearly cost, cash effect, profit case. Next: none.
  - Watch: true yearly cost of each offer, and lowest cash with repayments (per offer, offer terms and the forecast), then cost of money, then cash.
- **21-06 Each month set aside estimated income tax, and show the owner the amount they can safely draw without breaking the cash plan** Score 26, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: profit to date (setup, accounting tool); tax rate estimate and payment dates (founder, founder, from the accountant); the cash forecast (enabler, cash-forecast).
  - Steps: 1) Estimate tax due on profit to date and the next payment date 2) Move the set-aside amount out of free cash in the forecast 3) Safe draw = lowest cash in 13 weeks, minus the floor, minus the tax set aside, minus a margin 4) Founder confirms the draw, and it goes into the forecast
  - Output: a monthly tax set-aside and a safe draw number. Next: feeds 21-01 (cash forecast).
  - Watch: tax set aside against estimated tax due (monthly, books and the forecast), then surprise tax bills, then cash.
### T2. When revenue is up but the bank is not, help me see what each product, channel and sale really made after every cost, so I grow the parts that make money

Metric: contribution margin per product and channel after marketing, monthly

- **21-08 Before a sale runs, compute the extra orders it needs to break even after product cost, shipping, fees and returns, and set that next to the lift the last 3 sales really got** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before each promo. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: promo depth, products and dates (brain, offers shelf); margin per product (enabler, margin-view); orders from the last 3 sales and the weeks after (connected, Shopify); return rate on sale orders (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Compute contribution per order at full price and at the sale price 2) Break-even lift = how many more orders the sale needs to make the same profit 3) Measure lift from the last 3 sales, after the dip in the weeks that followed 4) Write go, change the depth, or no, with the numbers 5) Founder decides
  - Output: a 1 page promo check with break-even lift and past lift. Next: feeds 21-09 (sale readout).
  - Watch: profit per order at the sale price against break-even (per promo, orders and costs), then promo profit, then gross profit.
