- **21-07 Build contribution margin by product and channel every month: sales after discounts and returns, minus landed cost, shipping, packing, payment and marketplace fees, and ad spend, and mark the products that lose money** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: monthly, in the first week. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders, discounts and refunds (connected, Shopify); landed cost per unit (brain, finance shelf); shipping and packing cost per order (setup, shipping app, or founder); marketplace fees (setup, seller portals); ad spend by campaign and landing page (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Compute each cost per order line 2) Assign ad spend to products by landing page and to channels by campaign 3) Compute contribution per product and channel from totals 4) Mark products with contribution under zero or under the floor 5) Founder reviews each loser: fix, reprice, or cut
  - Output: a monthly margin table by product and channel, with a founder note on each loser. Next: feeds 21-12 (spend line), 04-offers-pricing and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: contribution margin per product and channel after marketing (monthly, orders, costs and ad spend), then profit per order, then contribution margin.
- **21-10 Count shipping honestly: net shipping cost per order (carrier bill minus shipping charged) by order size, and test moving the free shipping line to where most orders clear it with profit** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: margin-view.
  - Inputs: carrier bills (setup, shipping app or carrier portal); shipping charged per order (connected, Shopify); order value spread (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Compute net shipping cost per order and as a share of revenue 2) Split by order value band 3) Model 2 new free shipping lines: orders lost, order value gained, shipping saved 4) Founder picks one to test for 4 weeks
  - Output: a shipping cost table, and a free shipping line test. Next: feeds 04-offers-pricing and 25-experiments.
  - Watch: net shipping cost as a share of revenue (monthly, carrier invoices and orders), then contribution per order, then contribution margin.
- **21-09 Read each big sale 6 weeks after it ends: profit after returns, the dip in the weeks after, and the share of buyers who were new, next to a normal month** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 6 weeks after each sale. Enablers: margin-view, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: orders and returns (connected, Shopify); ad spend during the sale (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); costs per product (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute contribution for the sale weeks after returns and ad spend 2) Compare the 4 weeks after with a normal month to size the dip 3) Split buyers into new and returning 4) Write the verdict: made money or not, and what to change next time 5) Log it as a win or a fail in the ledger
  - Output: a sale readout with profit, the dip after, new buyer share, and a win or fail note. Next: feeds 25-experiments and 21-08 (next promo check).
  - Watch: contribution margin of the sale period after returns (6 weeks after the sale, orders, returns and ad spend), then the next sale plan, then contribution margin.
- **21-11 Each month, explain in 5 lines why profit and cash moved differently: stock bought, payouts in transit, loan principal, bills paid or owed, and tax** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, after the books close. Enablers: money-match, cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: profit and loss for the month (setup, accounting tool); bank balance at start and end (setup, a read-only bank feed); change in stock value (connected, Shopify inventory at landed cost); loan schedule (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Take profit for the month 2) Add and subtract: stock change, payouts not yet landed, loan principal, change in unpaid bills, tax paid 3) Check that the result equals the change in the bank, and name any gap left 4) Write 5 lines the founder can read in 1 minute
  - Output: a monthly profit to cash bridge in 5 lines. Next: feeds 26-reports.
  - Watch: gap between monthly profit and change in cash, with named causes (monthly, books and bank feed), then founder trust in the numbers, then cash.
### T3. When the agency asks for more ad budget, help me set the most I can spend and still make money, and see whether we are buying new customers or only rebuying old ones, so I say yes or no with a number

Metric: total sales divided by total ad spend against the break-even line, weekly

- **21-12 Set the ad spend line from real margin: compute the break-even and target ratio of total sales to total ad spend, check it weekly, and answer the agency's next budget ask with it** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then weekly. Enablers: margin-view, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: total store sales (connected, Shopify); marketplace sales, if any (setup, seller portals); total ad spend on every platform (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); contribution before ads per order (enabler, margin-view); profit goal (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute contribution margin before ads as a share of sales 2) Break-even ratio = 1 divided by that share; the target ratio leaves the profit goal 3) Compute the weekly ratio from totals and set it next to both lines 4) Split by new and returning customer sales, so repeat sales do not hide a weak first order 5) Write the budget answer: the most weekly spend that still clears the line
  - Output: a weekly chart with break-even and target lines, and a 1 line budget answer. Next: feeds 11-paid-social and 00-today.
  - Watch: total store sales divided by total ad spend against break-even (weekly, store orders and ad platforms), then profit after ads, then contribution margin.
- **21-13 Split profit into new and returning customers: first order contribution per new customer minus the cost to get them, and the months each cohort takes to pay back** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: funnel-mix, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders flagged new or returning (connected, Shopify); ad spend by month (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); costs per product (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Cost per new customer = total ad spend divided by new customers, from totals 2) Compute first order contribution per new customer 3) Add up contribution by month per cohort until it passes the cost to get them 4) Mark cohorts that never pay back within 12 months 5) Set the most we can pay for a new customer, and log it on the finance shelf
  - Output: a payback table by cohort, and a cap on cost per new customer. Next: feeds 21-12 (spend line), 11-paid-social and 15-01 (cohort grid).
  - Watch: months for a new customer cohort to pay back its cost (quarterly, orders by cohort and ad spend), then growth that funds itself, then cash.
- **21-14 Find where the next $10,000 of marketing earns the most profit: read the last 12 weeks of spend steps per channel and the profit that followed, and say which channel to add to, or whether to keep the cash** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, or when extra budget appears. Enablers: funnel-mix, margin-view.
  - Inputs: weekly spend per channel (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); TikTok spend, if used (setup, TikTok Ads); weekly new customers and contribution (enabler, margin-view); tests running in those weeks (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) For each channel, pair weeks where spend rose with the change in new customers and contribution 2) Estimate profit per extra dollar with a range, and mark weak reads 3) Compare with keeping the cash for stock or debt 4) Write 1 recommendation and a small test to confirm it
  - Output: a table of profit per extra dollar per channel, and 1 recommendation. Next: feeds 22-09 (holdout test) and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: profit from added spend per channel (30 day, ad spend and store orders), then marketing profit, then contribution margin.
- **21-15 Forecast next quarter's sales in 2 parts, returning sales from past cohorts' repeat curves and new sales from planned spend and cost per new customer, and check last quarter's forecast against actual** Score 28, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: funnel-mix, cash-forecast.
  - Inputs: orders by cohort (connected, Shopify); planned ad spend (brain, finance shelf (budgets)); cost per new customer trend (connected, Meta Ads, Google Ads and Shopify); peaks and launches next quarter (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Project returning sales from each past cohort's repeat curve 2) Project new customers from planned spend divided by cost per new customer, with a range 3) Add peaks and launches from the calendar 4) Compare last quarter's forecast with actual and note the miss 5) Founder signs the number used for stock and hiring
  - Output: a quarterly forecast split into new and returning, with last quarter's error. Next: feeds 21-01 (cash forecast) and 19-03 (peak stock plan).
  - Watch: forecast against actual sales, new and returning (monthly, orders by cohort), then stock and hiring decisions, then cash.
### T4. When costs creep up one bill at a time, from apps nobody uses to payment fees to sales tax, help me find the leaks and what we owe, so profit stops leaking and no letter surprises me

Metric: overhead against plan by line, monthly, and states near their sales tax limit

- **21-16 List every app and subscription from the store admin and the card statement with its monthly cost, last use and owner, and cancel the ones nobody used in 60 days** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then each quarter. Enablers: app-audit, money-match.
  - Inputs: installed apps and app charges (connected, Shopify admin and billing); recurring card and bank lines (setup, a bank feed, or the founder's export); owner of each tool (brain, operations shelf (apps and stack)); last login or use per tool (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every app charge and recurring card line for 12 months 2) Match each to a tool, an owner and what it does 3) Mark last use, and whether another tool already does the same job 4) List cancels with monthly savings, and the founder approves each 5) A person cancels them and asks site speed to remove leftover code, and the saving is logged
  - Output: an app and subscription list with cost and use, and a signed cancel list. Next: feeds 21-17 (revenue lost to slow pages) and 08-site-speed.
  - Watch: monthly software cost (monthly, app charges and card feed), then overhead, then contribution margin.
- **21-17 Put a dollar number on revenue lost to app scripts, including code left behind by apps already deleted: time what each script adds to load, and turn the delay into lost orders using our own conversion by load time** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: after 21-16, and after every app install. Enablers: app-audit.
  - Inputs: theme code and scripts on the top 5 pages (connected, Shopify); load time with and without each script (setup, a free speed test tool on a copy of the theme); conversion by load time and device (connected, GA4); sessions and order value (connected, GA4 and Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Find every script on the top 5 pages and match it to an app, live or deleted 2) Test page load with and without each script on a copy of the theme 3) From GA4, read conversion by load time band on mobile 4) Lost revenue = sessions times the conversion drop for the added time, times order value 5) Rank apps by revenue lost plus monthly fee, and send the top 5 to site speed
  - Output: a table: app, fee, load time added, revenue lost per month, keep or remove. Next: feeds 08-site-speed (theme twin test).
  - Watch: revenue lost to app load time per month (28 day, GA4 and speed tests), then mobile conversion, then revenue.
- **21-19 Track sales and order counts by ship-to state over the last 12 months against each state's sales tax limit, and list states within 20 percent of the limit and states over it with no registration** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: tax-watch.
  - Inputs: orders by ship-to state (connected, Shopify); each state's sales and order count limit (setup, public limits typed into a sheet once, checked yearly); states where we are registered (founder, founder); marketplace orders where the marketplace collects the tax (setup, seller portals).
  - Steps: 1) Sum sales and order counts by state over the rolling 12 months, leaving out orders where the marketplace collects 2) Compare each state with its limit 3) List states over the limit with no registration, and states within 20 percent 4) Send the list to the accountant, who registers or confirms
  - Output: a monthly state list: over, near, registered. Next: none.
  - Watch: states within 20 percent of their limit, and states over it with no registration (monthly, orders by ship-to state), then back tax and penalties, then risk.
- **21-18 Each month group every bank and card line into 12 cost lines, compare them with plan, flag lines 10 percent over, and list every new recurring charge** Score 28, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: money-match.
  - Inputs: bank and card lines (setup, a read-only bank feed); budget per cost line (brain, finance shelf (budgets)); categories used in the books (setup, accounting tool).
  - Steps: 1) Put each line into one of 12 cost lines 2) Compare each line with plan and with last month 3) Flag lines 10 percent or more over plan 4) List charges that are new this month and repeat 5) Founder marks each flag: expected, cut, or ask
  - Output: an overhead against plan table, and a new charges list. Next: feeds 21-16 (app list).
  - Watch: overhead against plan by line (monthly, books and card feed), then profit leak, then contribution margin.
- **21-20 Show what each payment method costs per order (card, PayPal, Shop Pay, buy now pay later) next to its order value and repeat rate, and decide which to keep** Score 26, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: each quarter. Enablers: money-match, margin-view.
  - Inputs: fees and orders by payment method (connected, Shopify Payments); PayPal and buy now pay later fee reports (setup, each processor); checkout steps by payment method, where tracked (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Compute fees as a share of revenue by method 2) Compute order value and repeat rate by method 3) Estimate sales lost if a method were removed, from its share of checkouts 4) Founder decides, and any change is tested for 4 weeks
  - Output: a fee table by payment method, and a keep or drop decision. Next: none.
  - Watch: payment fees as a share of revenue, by method (monthly, processor reports), then fee per order, then contribution margin.

## 22-measurement Measurement and data health

### T1. When a site change or a new app quietly breaks tracking, help me catch it the same day, so I do not spend 3 weeks cutting ads that were working

Metric: tracked purchases per platform divided by store orders, daily

- **22-01 Match tracked purchases to store orders every morning (Meta, Google Ads and GA4 purchases, each divided by store orders) against their 28 day range, and alert when one falls out** Score 44, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every morning at 8. Enablers: tracking-check.
  - Inputs: yesterday's store orders (connected, Shopify); Meta purchases (connected, Meta Ads (read only)); Google Ads conversions (connected, Google Ads); GA4 purchases (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Pull yesterday's purchases from each platform and the store's orders 2) Divide each platform's count by store orders, on the same day and time zone 3) Compare each ratio with its 28 day range 4) When one falls out of range, alert with the platform, the size of the drop and the hour it started 5) A person places a test order to confirm before anyone changes ads
  - Output: a morning line per platform: in range or out, with the start time of any break. Next: feeds 22-05 (break report) and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: tracked purchases per platform divided by store orders (daily, Shopify, Meta Ads, Google Ads and GA4), then ads cut for the wrong reason, then risk.
- **22-02 Before any theme, checkout app or site change goes live, place a test order and check that view, add to cart, checkout and purchase each fire once, with the right value, on every platform** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before every site launch or app install. Enablers: tracking-check, theme-twin.
  - Inputs: a copy of the theme or a test store (setup, Shopify); a way to place test orders at no cost (founder, founder); the event checklist per platform (enabler, tracking-check); Meta's event test view (connected, Meta Ads (read only)).
  - Steps: 1) Write the checklist: events, platforms, value, currency, order id 2) Place a test order on the copy of the theme 3) Check each event fired once with the right value on Meta, Google Ads and GA4 4) Mark pass or fail, and hold the launch on any fail 5) After launch, repeat on the live site and cancel the test order
  - Output: a pass or fail sheet for each launch. Next: feeds 22-01 (daily match).
  - Watch: events passing the pre-launch check (per launch, tag test results), then tracking breaks after launches, then risk.
- **22-03 Check Meta's purchase event quality each week: browser and server events counted once, match quality, and the value sent (net sales, not with tax and shipping)** Score 38, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly, and after any change to the pixel or checkout. Enablers: tracking-check.
  - Inputs: Meta dataset quality and event counts (connected, Meta Events Manager (read only)); orders with net sales, tax and shipping split (connected, Shopify); who owns tracking changes (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull event counts, the share counted twice, and match quality for purchase 2) Compare Meta purchase value with store net sales for the same orders 3) Flag double counts, low match quality, and value that includes tax or shipping 4) Write the fix for the person who owns tracking; ecomOS changes nothing in Meta
  - Output: a weekly event quality line with fixes. Next: feeds 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: Meta purchases divided by store orders, and match quality (7 day, Meta Events Manager and Shopify), then Meta bidding on the right buyers, then revenue.
- **22-05 When tracking was broken, list the days it touched, the budget and creative decisions made in those days, and the true numbers from store orders, so the wrong calls can be reversed** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: after any tracking break. Enablers: tracking-check, metric-contract.
  - Inputs: break start and end (enabler, tracking-check); decisions log with dates (brain, decisions and goals shelf); store orders and ad spend for those days (connected, Shopify, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Mark the first and last day of the break 2) List every budget, pause and creative decision made in those days 3) Recompute each campaign's results from store orders by link tag 4) Mark decisions that would change with the true numbers 5) Founder reviews the list and a person reverses the wrong calls
  - Output: a break report: days, decisions to review, and the true numbers. Next: feeds 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: decisions made during a tracking break that were reviewed (per break, decision log), then ads wrongly cut or scaled, then revenue.
- **22-04 Find why GA4 shows fewer orders than the store each week (consent banner, checkout on another domain, duplicate tags, staff and test orders, or bots) and write the fix list** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly. Enablers: tracking-check.
  - Inputs: GA4 purchases and sessions by day, device and browser (connected, GA4); store orders with order ids (connected, Shopify); consent banner settings and change dates (brain, site shelf); staff and agency IP addresses, and test orders (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Divide GA4 purchases by store orders, by day, device and browser 2) Check the gap before and after any consent or checkout change 3) Match order ids to find purchases missing or counted twice 4) Filter out staff, agency, test traffic and bots, and recount 5) Write the fix list with an owner for each
  - Output: a weekly gap table and a fix list. Next: feeds 22-01 (daily match).
  - Watch: GA4 purchases divided by Shopify orders (weekly, GA4 and Shopify), then trust in channel and conversion reports, then risk.
### T2. When Meta and Google together claim more sales than the store made, help me see what each one really drove, so I do not scale on sales counted twice

Metric: sum of platform-claimed revenue divided by store revenue, same window, monthly

- **22-07 Before doubling a campaign after one great week, match its claimed purchases to store orders by link tag and time, split new and returning buyers and codes used, and hold the raise if under 60 percent match** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before any budget raise over 30 percent. Enablers: tracking-check, funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: the campaign's claimed purchases by day (connected, Meta Ads or Google Ads); orders with link tags, codes and customer history (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the campaign's claimed purchases for 7 days 2) Match them to store orders by link tag and order time 3) Split matched orders into new and returning buyers, and note discount codes from other channels 4) Write the verdict: real, partly real, or not, and hold the raise below 60 percent match
  - Output: a 1 line verdict per campaign: real, partly real, or not. Next: feeds 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: platform purchases matched to store orders for the campaign (7 day, Meta or Google and Shopify), then scale decisions, then revenue.
- **22-06 Each month add up the revenue every platform claims (Meta, Google, TikTok, email, affiliates) for the same window, and set it against store revenue to show how much is claimed twice** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and before any budget change over 20 percent. Enablers: tracking-check.
  - Inputs: Meta and Google claimed revenue (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); TikTok and email claimed revenue (setup, TikTok Ads and the email tool); store revenue (connected, Shopify); attribution window used per platform (enabler, metric-contract).
  - Steps: 1) Pull claimed revenue per platform for the same calendar window 2) Write down the attribution window each platform used 3) Divide the sum of claims by store revenue 4) Show which platforms claim most of the gap, and the orders more than one of them claim
  - Output: a claim sheet: claimed revenue by platform, store revenue, and the over-claim ratio. Next: feeds 22-07 (check a great week) and 21-12 (spend line).
  - Watch: sum of platform-claimed revenue divided by store revenue (monthly, ad platforms and Shopify), then scaling on sales counted twice, then revenue.
- **22-08 Check the agency's monthly report against the store: their revenue divided by store matched revenue, the window they used, and the numbers they left out, in a 1 page note** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, when the report arrives. Enablers: tracking-check, metric-contract.
  - Inputs: the agency report (founder, founder); ad platform results (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); store orders (connected, Shopify); the meaning of each number (enabler, metric-contract).
  - Steps: 1) Read each number in the report and the window it uses 2) Rebuild the same numbers from store orders 3) Divide the agency's revenue by store matched revenue 4) List what the report leaves out: returns, new customer share, margin 5) Write a 1 page note the founder can send back
  - Output: a 1 page note on the agency report. Next: feeds 26-reports.
  - Watch: agency-reported revenue divided by store matched revenue (monthly, agency report, ad platforms and Shopify), then trust, and fee talks with the agency, then risk.
- **22-10 Add a post-purchase question, where did you first hear about us, with 8 fixed answers, and count the answers each month next to what the trackers credit** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once to set up, then monthly. Enablers: tracking-check.
  - Inputs: a post-purchase survey on the thank you page (setup, post-purchase survey app); orders (connected, Shopify); channels we run, for the answer list (brain, ads shelf); last click source per order (connected, GA4 and Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Write 8 answers from the channels we run, plus other with a text box 2) Put the question on the thank you page 3) Each month count answers by channel 4) Set survey share next to last click share by channel 5) Flag channels the trackers miss, such as podcasts or word of mouth
  - Output: a monthly table: survey share against tracker share, by channel. Next: feeds 22-06 (claim sheet) and 21-14 (next $10,000).
  - Watch: share of orders answering the question, and answers by source (monthly, post-purchase survey and Shopify), then cutting channels that work, then revenue.
- **22-09 Run a holdout on brand search or retargeting: switch it off in a third of states for 3 weeks, compare store orders with the rest, and decide from the extra sales it really adds** Score 30, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: once per channel every 6 months, when a channel claims over 20 percent of sales. Enablers: holdout-test.
  - Inputs: brand and retargeting campaigns (connected, Google Ads and Meta Ads (read only; a person makes the change)); 12 weeks of orders by state (connected, Shopify); approval of the test and the sales at risk (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pick matched groups of states from 12 weeks of orders 2) Estimate the sales at risk, and the founder approves 3) A person switches the channel off in the test states for 3 weeks 4) Compare orders per state with the forecast from the control states 5) Write the extra sales per dollar and the decision, and log it in the ledger
  - Output: a holdout readout: extra sales per dollar with a range, and a keep, cut or trim decision. Next: feeds 21-14 (next $10,000) and 12-google-ads.
  - Watch: extra sales from the holdout, per channel (per test, store orders in test and holdout states), then spend on sales we already had, then contribution margin.
- **22-11 Audit every link in emails, SMS, bios, creator posts and affiliate pages for the right tracking tag, fix the broken ones, and publish one tag rule so direct and unknown orders fall** Score 28, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly. Enablers: metric-contract.
  - Inputs: links in email and SMS flows (setup, email tool); creator and affiliate links (brain, creators shelf); orders with no known source (connected, Shopify and GA4); the tag naming rule (enabler, metric-contract).
  - Steps: 1) List every live link by channel 2) Check each for source, medium and campaign tags 3) Fix missing or wrong tags, and a person updates flows and bios 4) Publish the tag rule for the team and agencies 5) Recount orders with no known source a month later
  - Output: a link audit with fixes, and one tag rule. Next: feeds 22-06 (claim sheet).
  - Watch: share of orders with no known source (monthly, Shopify and GA4), then fair credit per channel, then revenue.
- **22-12 Measure how much Meta ads lift Amazon sales: set weekly Meta spend against Amazon orders and brand searches on Amazon, and read it through Amazon Attribution links or a regional Meta pause** Score 26, ready 3/5, weeks.
  - When: each quarter, or before a big Meta cut. Enablers: holdout-test.
  - Inputs: weekly Meta spend (connected, Meta Ads); Amazon orders and brand search volume (setup, Amazon Seller Central and Brand Analytics); Amazon Attribution tags on some ads (setup, Amazon Attribution); approval for a regional pause (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Line up weekly Meta spend, Amazon orders and Amazon brand searches for 26 weeks 2) Tag a share of Meta ads with Amazon Attribution links 3) If the founder approves, a person pauses Meta in a few regions for 3 weeks 4) Estimate Amazon sales per Meta dollar, with a range 5) Write a rule for judging Meta with Amazon sales counted
  - Output: a halo estimate with a range, and a rule for judging Meta. Next: feeds 21-12 (spend line) and 20-marketplaces.
  - Watch: Amazon sales against Meta spend (weekly, Meta Ads and Amazon reports), then good ads cut for the wrong reason, then revenue.
### T3. When the ads look great but the store is not growing, help me see whether we are buying new customers or rebuying old ones, so spend goes to growth and not to people who would come back anyway

Metric: share of orders from new customers, and cost per new customer from store orders, 28 day

- **22-13 Split every ad dollar and every order by funnel stage (prospecting or retargeting on Meta, generic or brand search on Google, new or returning buyer in the store) in one weekly table** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: Meta campaigns, audiences and spend (connected, Meta Ads (read only)); Google campaigns and search terms (connected, Google Ads); orders marked new or returning (connected, Shopify); which campaign serves which stage (brain, ads shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Map each campaign to a stage, and a person checks the map once 2) Split spend by stage on each platform 3) Split orders into new and returning by customer history 4) Show the share of spend on prospecting and generic, and the share of orders from new customers, for 12 weeks 5) Write the 1 line answer: are we buying new customers or rebuying old ones
  - Output: a weekly funnel mix table with 12 weeks of trend, and the 1 line answer. Next: feeds 22-14 (cost per new customer), 11-paid-social and 12-google-ads.
  - Watch: share of orders from new customers, and share of spend on prospecting (28 day, Meta Ads, Google Ads and Shopify), then new customers per week, then revenue.
- **22-14 Count new customers from store orders (first order ever, by email or phone), set that against what Meta and Google call new, and publish one cost per new customer** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: funnel-mix, tracking-check.
  - Inputs: orders with full customer history (connected, Shopify); new customer counts the platforms claim (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); total ad spend (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Define new: first order ever, by email or phone, across all channels 2) Count new customers per month from the store 3) Compare with each platform's claim, and list the gap 4) Cost per new customer = total ad spend divided by store new customers, from totals 5) Write the meaning into the metric contract
  - Output: one cost per new customer, with the gap to platform claims. Next: feeds 21-13 (payback) and 15-19 (Meta buyers who already bought).
  - Watch: new customers from store orders against platform claims (monthly, Shopify and ad platforms), then bid caps and budget, then revenue.
- **22-15 Measure brand search inflation: the share of Google spend and conversions on brand terms, the share of those buyers who already bought, and what brand ads add that organic results would not** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: funnel-mix, holdout-test.
  - Inputs: search terms and campaigns (connected, Google Ads); brand clicks from organic search (connected, Search Console); orders with Google click ids and customer history (connected, Shopify).
  - Steps: 1) Split Google spend and conversions into brand and generic terms 2) Match brand search orders to customer history: new or returning 3) Set brand paid clicks next to brand organic clicks over 12 weeks 4) Write keep, trim or test, and send a test to 22-09 when brand spend is large
  - Output: a brand search table and a keep, trim or test note. Next: feeds 22-09 (holdout) and 12-google-ads.
  - Watch: share of Google conversions on brand terms from returning buyers (28 day, Google Ads and Shopify), then spend on buyers who would come anyway, then contribution margin.
- **22-16 Watch 90 day repeat rate by first order month and new customers per week side by side, and flag when either falls 2 months in a row** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: funnel-mix.
  - Inputs: orders with first order month per customer (connected, Shopify); weekly ad spend (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Compute 90 day repeat rate for each first order month 2) Count new customers per week and cost per new customer 3) Put both on one chart for 12 months 4) Flag when either falls 2 months in a row, and send the flag to the right screen
  - Output: a pair of trend lines and a flag. Next: feeds 15-01 (cohort grid) and 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: 90 day repeat rate by first order month (monthly, Shopify orders), then the quality of growth, then revenue.
### T4. When numbers disagree or move and nobody knows why, help me have one meaning, one source and one owner per number, fresh every morning, so the Monday meeting is spent deciding

Metric: share of headline numbers that passed the freshness check this morning

- **22-20 When revenue drops, split the change into visits, buy rate and order size by channel and device, check stock and late returns, and name the part that moved in 1 line** Score 42, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: when daily revenue moves 15 percent against the same day last week. Enablers: metric-contract.
  - Inputs: sessions and funnel steps (connected, GA4); orders, returns and order value (connected, Shopify); in-stock share of top page sizes and colours (connected, Shopify inventory); the change log (enabler, metric-contract).
  - Steps: 1) Split the revenue change into visits, buy rate and order size 2) Split each by channel and device, and find the biggest mover 3) Check the funnel step that moved: product view, cart, or checkout 4) Check stock on top pages, and returns landing from last month's sale 5) Check the change log for that day, then write the 1 line cause and an owner
  - Output: a 1 line cause with the numbers behind it, and an owner. Next: feeds 07-landing-pages-cro or 11-paid-social.
  - Watch: change in revenue split into visits, buy rate and order size (day and week, Shopify and GA4), then fixing the right thing, then revenue.
- **22-18 Check every feed each morning before the report goes out (last sync time, row counts against normal, missing days and time zone) and stamp each headline number fresh or stale** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every morning, before the report. Enablers: metric-contract.
  - Inputs: sync logs for the store, ad platforms and GA4 (connected, connected accounts); sync log for the email tool (setup, email tool); time zone and currency per tool (brain, trust and access shelf); normal row counts per feed (enabler, metric-contract).
  - Steps: 1) Read the last sync time and row count for each feed 2) Compare row counts with the normal range, and look for missing days 3) Check each tool's day ends at the same hour as the store's 4) Stamp each headline number fresh or stale, with the reason 5) Send stale feeds to the person who owns them
  - Output: a freshness strip on each report. Next: feeds 00-today and 26-reports.
  - Watch: share of headline numbers that passed the freshness check (daily, sync logs), then acting on half a day of data, then risk.
- **22-17 Write the metric contract for the top 15 numbers (name, meaning, formula, source, window, owner, and what shows when data is missing), fold marketplace and wholesale sales into total sales, and have the founder sign it** Score 34, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then when a number is added or disputed. Enablers: metric-contract.
  - Inputs: the numbers used in today's reports and meetings (founder, founder); connected sources and their health (brain, trust and access shelf); metric rules already written for the app (have, app/contracts metric contract); revenue outside the store and where it lives (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List the 15 numbers the team uses most 2) Write one meaning, formula, source, window and owner for each 3) Add sales from marketplaces and wholesale as their own source, so total sales counts every channel 4) Write what shows when a source is missing: unavailable, never zero 5) Founder signs, and every report links each number to the contract
  - Output: a signed contract of 15 numbers. Next: feeds 26-reports and every screen.
  - Watch: minutes of the weekly meeting spent on which number is right (weekly, meeting notes in the brain), then faster decisions, then risk.
- **22-19 Keep a change log of every theme publish, app install, tag edit, price change and campaign restructure with the date, and when a number shifts, list what changed that day** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on every change, read when a number moves. Enablers: metric-contract, work-board.
  - Inputs: theme publishes and app installs (connected, Shopify); account activity logs (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads (read only)); price changes (connected, Shopify); changes the team makes by hand (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull changes from each connected account every day 2) Add hand entries from the team through the work board 3) Keep one dated list with who made each change 4) When a number shifts, list every change in the 48 hours before
  - Output: a dated change log, and a what-changed list for any shift. Next: feeds 22-05 (break report) and 22-20 (revenue split).
  - Watch: hours to find the cause of a number shift (per event, change log in the brain), then time lost chasing numbers, then risk.

## 23-team-ops Team and ops

### T1. When I am working 70 hour weeks and still the bottleneck on every decision, help me see which work others can own and who should own it, so I get 10 hours back for the calls only I can make

Metric: decisions waiting on the founder over 48 hours, weekly

- **23-10 Run the Monday meeting from the board: 6 numbers, the cards waiting on approval, the late cards, and 3 calls to make, ending with an owner and a date for each call** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: work-board.
  - Inputs: 6 weekly numbers: sales, gross profit after ads, new customers, cost per new customer, stock days on top sellers, first reply time (connected, store platform, Meta Ads, Google Ads and helpdesk); cards waiting and late (enabler, work-board); decisions log (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Open the 6 numbers and the 3 biggest movers 2) Walk only the waiting and late cards, not every card 3) Pick 3 calls and decide each in the room 4) Give each call 1 owner and a date, and put it on the board 5) Log the calls to the decisions shelf 6) Count decisions made; if 0, cut the meeting to 30 minutes next week
  - Output: 3 logged decisions per week with owner and date, and a meeting that ends on time. Next: feeds 26-05 (decisions logged in the weekly pack).
  - Watch: decisions with an owner and date made in the weekly meeting (weekly, decisions log), then founder hours in meetings and speed of calls, then gross profit.
- **23-03 Log every decision waiting on the founder for more than 48 hours each Monday, and write an approval rule that lets someone else make the 3 most common kinds** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday before the team meeting. Enablers: work-board, owner-map.
  - Inputs: cards in the waiting on approval column with age in hours (enabler, work-board); current approval rules and budget caps (brain, decisions and goals shelf); the founder's red lines (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) List every card waiting on the founder and how long it has waited 2) Group them by kind: ad spend change, discount, refund over a limit, new vendor, creative sign-off 3) Pick the 3 kinds with the most cards 4) Draft a rule for each with a cap, for example refunds under $150 the CX lead approves 5) Founder signs or edits each rule 6) Write the rules to the decisions shelf and route matching cards to the new approver
  - Output: 3 written approval rules and a weekly count of decisions waiting over 48 hours. Next: feeds 23-10 (Monday meeting) and 26-05 (decisions logged).
  - Watch: decisions waiting on the founder over 48 hours (weekly, work board), then founder hours a week and launch dates held, then gross profit.
- **23-02 List every recurring task the founder did in the last 2 weeks from the calendar, inbox and board, and mark each one keep, hand off or drop, with a named owner and a check for each hand off** Score 36, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then every quarter and whenever the founder's week runs over 60 hours. Enablers: owner-map, work-board.
  - Inputs: the founder's calendar and sent mail for the last 14 days (founder, founder calendar and inbox); team list with skills and spare hours (founder, team list); open and done cards per person (enabler, work-board); what only the founder may decide (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every meeting, reply and task from the last 14 days and group repeats into recurring tasks 2) Log hours per recurring task and how often it comes back 3) Mark each task keep, hand off or drop, using the rule: keep only calls that need the founder's money, taste or name 4) Name 1 owner and 1 check (what good looks like) for each hand off 5) Founder confirms the list in a 20 minute call 6) Move each hand off to the board with a start date and a 2 week review
  - Output: a hand-off list: recurring tasks, hours a week each, new owner and the check, plus founder hours freed. Next: feeds 23-03 (approval rules) and 23-14 (written steps for each handed task).
  - Watch: founder hours a week on tasks with another named owner possible (weekly, task and decision log), then decisions waiting on the founder over 48 hours, then gross profit.
### T2. When a key person gives notice or I want 2 weeks off, help me see what only one person knows and holds, logins, contacts and steps, so the business does not stop when someone leaves

Metric: critical logins, contacts and tasks only one person can do, quarterly

- **23-15 When anyone leaves, close or move every account they can reach the same day from the security folder, and log what was closed and when** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the last working day of any staff member, freelancer or agency. Enablers: access-vault, owner-map.
  - Inputs: register of accounts and who has access (enabler, access-vault); admin pages on store, Meta, Google, email tool, helpdesk, bank (setup, each platform admin); the leaver's name and end date (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every account the person or agency can reach from the register 2) Remove or downgrade each one, starting with store owner, Meta Business, payouts and domain 3) Rotate any shared keys or app tokens they held 4) Log each change with time and who did it 5) Recheck 7 days later that nothing was re-added
  - Output: an offboarding log with every account closed on the day, and 0 open accounts for people who left. Next: feeds 24-04 (monthly stale access sweep).
  - Watch: accounts still open for people who left (monthly, security folder), then no lost data, spend or customers on the way out, then risk.
- **23-12 Run a 10 day handover when someone gives notice: list what they hold, record a 5 minute screen video per core task, move each login to a company account, and name the new owner** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: the day someone gives notice. Enablers: owner-map, access-vault.
  - Inputs: the leaver's tasks, logins and contacts (enabler, owner-map); screen recorder (Loom or the built-in one) (setup, screen recorder); who takes over each part (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull everything the person holds from the owner map and the access register 2) Book 3 sessions: tasks, contacts, logins 3) Record a 5 minute video per core task and file it with the written steps 4) Move logins and 2-step devices to company accounts 5) Name the new owner for each item and have them do each task once before day 10 6) Close the leaver's access on the last day (23-15)
  - Output: a handover pack: tasks with videos and new owners, logins moved, and contacts introduced. Next: feeds 23-15 (close access on the last day).
  - Watch: items handed over and tested by the new owner before the last day (per departure, owner map), then no stopped work after a departure, then risk.
- **23-11 Map every critical login, supplier contact, file and recurring task to the people who can do it today, and mark every one with only 1 person in red** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every quarter and when anyone gives notice. Enablers: owner-map, access-vault.
  - Inputs: account list with admins (enabler, access-vault); supplier, 3PL, agency and bank contacts (brain, operations shelf); recurring tasks with current doer (enabler, owner-map); who knows what, from each team member in a 10 minute survey (setup, team survey).
  - Steps: 1) List critical items: store owner login, Meta Business, payouts, domain, 3PL portal, factory contact, reorder file, payroll file 2) For each, ask who can do or reach it today 3) Mark items with 1 person red, 2 people amber, 3 or more green 4) Name a backup for each red item and a date to train them 5) Put each backup training on the board 6) Recount red items next quarter
  - Output: a single-person risk map with each red item's backup and training date. Next: feeds 23-12 (handover on notice) and 23-13 (cover for a founder break).
  - Watch: critical logins, contacts and tasks only one person can do (quarterly, owner map), then days of disruption when a person leaves, then risk.
- **23-13 2 weeks before a founder break, list the founder-only tasks and decisions due in the window, name a cover and a spend cap for each, and write what counts as an emergency** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 2 weeks before any founder break of 5 days or more. Enablers: owner-map, work-board.
  - Inputs: founder's recurring tasks and decisions (enabler, owner-map); calendar for the break window: launches, payouts, renewals (brain, calendar shelf); approval rules and caps (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List founder tasks and decisions due in the break window 2) Name a cover for each and a cap they can spend or approve alone 3) Write the emergency rule: account takeover, payout hold, stock-out on a top seller, press problem 4) Set alerts so only emergencies reach the founder 5) Cover runs 1 day of the founder's tasks before the break as a test
  - Output: a cover plan: each task, its cover, the cap, and a 4 item emergency list. Next: feeds 26-02 (away mode: only flags reach the founder).
  - Watch: founder-only tasks with a named cover (before each break, owner map), then founder switches off; no stalled decisions in the window, then risk.
- **23-14 Write step lists for the 10 core recurring tasks, like reorder, returns, daily ad check, payouts and sale setup, and test each by having someone else do it once from the page** Score 28, ready 5/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once, then every 6 months, and before each new hire starts. Enablers: owner-map.
  - Inputs: the 10 core recurring tasks (enabler, owner-map); support macros and policies (brain, operations shelf); screen videos from the current doer (setup, screen recorder).
  - Steps: 1) Pick the 10 tasks that stop the business if skipped for a week 2) Current doer writes the steps in under 1 page with screenshots 3) Someone else does the task once from the page and marks where they got stuck 4) Fix the page and date it 5) Set a review date 6 months out 6) For a new hire, tick off each task they run alone by week 8
  - Output: 10 tested step lists with a review date, and a week 8 checklist for new hires. Next: feeds 23-12 (handover) and 23-13 (break cover).
  - Watch: core roles with written steps reviewed in the last 6 months (quarterly, owner map), then weeks until a new hire runs their job alone, then gross profit.
### T3. When the warehouse, the factory, the agency or a software vendor does not deliver what we pay for, help me hold them to the contract with dates and numbers, so I get credits and fix the service

Metric: vendor misses against the contract, and credits claimed, monthly

- **23-16 Check every order's ship time against the 3PL's contract ship time each week, and daily during a sale, and total the late orders and the credits owed** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and daily when late-order tickets jump. Enablers: vendor-scorecard, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: orders with paid time and fulfilled time (connected, store platform orders); contract ship time, cut-off and credit terms (founder, 3PL contract); tickets tagged late or where is my order (connected, helpdesk); 3PL order export if fulfilled times do not sync (setup, 3PL portal export).
  - Steps: 1) Compute hours from paid to shipped for every order, using the contract's cut-off 2) Mark each order on time or late and total per day 3) Match late orders to where is my order tickets 4) Compute credits owed under the contract 5) Send the 3PL the list with order numbers and ask for credit and a fix date 6) Log the claim and check it lands on the next invoice
  - Output: a weekly ship time table, late orders by day, and a credit claim with order numbers. Next: feeds 23-18 (invoice audit) and 26-04 (service in the weekly pack).
  - Watch: orders shipped within the contract ship time (weekly, store platform orders), then late-order tickets and repeat rate of affected buyers, then customer satisfaction.
- **23-18 Check every line of the monthly 3PL invoice against the contract rates and the store's own order and unit counts in code, and list the lines to dispute** Score 38, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: when each 3PL invoice arrives. Enablers: vendor-scorecard, margin-view.
  - Inputs: the 3PL invoice as CSV or PDF (founder, 3PL invoice); contract rate card: pick, pack, storage, postage, extras (founder, 3PL contract); orders, units and shipments for the month (connected, store platform orders).
  - Steps: 1) Load the invoice lines into a table 2) Recompute each line from the rate card and the store's own counts in code 3) Mark lines that do not match, with the dollar gap 4) Add credits owed from 23-16 and 23-17 5) Send the dispute list before the payment date 6) Track the fulfilment cost per order by part month over month
  - Output: an invoice check: matched lines, disputed lines with dollars, and cost per order by part. Next: feeds 21 (finance and cash) and 23-19 (vendor renewal).
  - Watch: invoice lines that do not match contract rates or order counts (monthly, 3PL invoice), then fulfilment cost per order, then gross profit.
- **23-17 Tag wrong item and damaged tickets by order, compute the error rate per 1,000 orders each week, and send the 3PL the list with the credits owed** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: vendor-scorecard, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: tickets tagged wrong item or damaged, with order number and photo (connected, helpdesk); orders shipped per week (connected, store platform orders); contract error rate and credit terms (founder, 3PL contract).
  - Steps: 1) Pull wrong item and damaged tickets and link each to its order 2) Compute errors per 1,000 orders per week, split by product 3) Compare with the contract's allowed rate 4) List reship and refund cost per error 5) Send the 3PL the list and ask for credit and a root cause per product
  - Output: a weekly pick error rate, errors per product and a credit claim. Next: feeds 23-18 (invoice audit).
  - Watch: wrong or damaged item orders per 1,000 (weekly, helpdesk), then reship and refund cost, and review rating, then customer satisfaction.
- **23-19 Score the agency each month on deliverables due against delivered and late, and on contribution margin from its channel after its fee, and bring the scorecard to every renewal call** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and 30 days before the retainer renews. Enablers: vendor-scorecard, margin-view, work-board.
  - Inputs: the scope: deliverables per month (founder, agency contract); agency deliverables with due and delivered dates (enabler, work-board); ad spend and store orders from the agency's channel (connected, Meta Ads, Google Ads and store platform); product cost, shipping and fees (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List deliverables due this month from the scope 2) Mark each delivered on time, late or missed from board dates 3) Compute contribution margin from the channel after ad spend and the agency fee 4) Show 3 months side by side 5) Write the call: renew, renegotiate with named fixes, or leave
  - Output: an agency scorecard: on time share, missed items, and profit after fee for 3 months. Next: feeds 26-18 (invoice check against delivered work).
  - Watch: agency deliverables late or missed against the scope (monthly, work board), then contribution margin from the agency's channel after its fee, then gross profit.
- **23-09 Track each open purchase order through sample, materials, production, quality check and ship against the factory's plan every week, and show what a slip does to launch date and stock** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday while a purchase order is open, and when the factory goes quiet for 5 days. Enablers: vendor-scorecard, work-board.
  - Inputs: open purchase orders with units, dates and factory (brain, operations shelf); factory production steps and promised dates (founder, supplier emails or purchase order file); stock on hand and weekly sell rate per product (connected, store platform inventory and orders); launch dates tied to the stock (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List each open order with its 5 steps and promised dates 2) Ask the factory for a dated update every Monday and log it 3) Mark any step late against plan 4) Compute the new landing date and weeks of stock left at today's sell rate 5) Say what moves: launch date, ad spend on that product, or air freight for part 6) Put the call on the board with an owner
  - Output: a purchase order tracker with late steps in red and the stock-out date each slip causes. Next: feeds 19 (supply and inventory) and 23-04 (launch plan).
  - Watch: production steps late against plan, per open order (weekly, purchase order file), then stock-outs avoided and launch dates held, then revenue.
- **23-20 List every software charge on the card with its monthly cost, owner and last login, and cancel tools unused in 60 days before their renewal date** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly, and 30 days before any annual renewal. Enablers: app-audit, owner-map.
  - Inputs: card and bank statements for 3 months (founder, finance); installed store apps with cost (connected, store platform apps); last login per tool from each admin page (setup, each tool's admin); apps and stack with owner (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull every recurring charge from 3 months of statements 2) Match each to a tool, an owner and what it does 3) Check last login or usage for each 4) Mark cancel, keep or downgrade, and list renewal dates in the next 60 days 5) Owner confirms, then cancel and log the monthly saving
  - Output: a software list with cost, owner, last use and renewal date, plus a cancel list and the saving. Next: feeds 08-site-speed app audit and 21 (finance and cash).
  - Watch: monthly software cost for tools unused in 60 days (monthly, card statements), then operating cost as a share of revenue, then gross profit.
### T4. When the team says it is drowning or a peak is coming, help me see the load per person from tickets, tasks and orders, so I shift work or hire on numbers, not feelings

Metric: workload per person against the written hiring trigger, monthly

- **23-05 Count open tickets, follow-ups owed and time to resolution per support agent, plus open and late board cards per person, every Monday, and flag anyone 50 percent above the team median** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and daily during a sale. Enablers: ticket-clock, capacity-plan, work-board.
  - Inputs: tickets with agent, first reply, resolved time, reopens and follow-ups owed (connected, helpdesk); order value at stake per open ticket (connected, store platform orders); open and late cards per person (enabler, work-board); each person's weekly hours (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Pull last week's tickets per agent: open, resolved, follow-ups owed, median time to resolution, reopens 2) Pull open and late board cards per person 3) Divide each by the person's hours to get load per hour 4) Flag anyone 50 percent above the team median, and any ticket owed a follow-up for over 48 hours 5) Lead moves 2 cards or 1 queue off each flagged person 6) Log the moves and check the flag clears next Monday
  - Output: a load table per person with red flags, and a list of follow-ups owed with the order value at stake. Next: feeds 23-07 (hiring trigger) and 26-04 (service next to money in the weekly pack).
  - Watch: follow-ups owed over 48 hours, and people above 150 percent of median load (weekly, helpdesk and work board), then time to resolution and staff who stay, then customer satisfaction.
- **23-06 Forecast tickets and orders for the 5 Black Friday days from last year's peak and this year's plan, and turn them into agent and warehouse hours needed against hours booked** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: 6 weeks before Black Friday and any sale over 2x normal orders. Enablers: capacity-plan, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: last year's orders and tickets per day around the peak (connected, store platform and helpdesk); this year's sale plan and expected order lift (brain, calendar shelf); tickets an agent closes per hour, and orders packed per warehouse hour (enabler, capacity-plan); the 3PL's peak capacity and cut-off times (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull daily orders and tickets for last year's peak window 2) Scale by this year's plan to get orders and tickets per day 3) Divide by closes per agent hour and orders per pack hour 4) Set hours needed against hours booked per day and name the gap 5) Book temps or overtime, or add macros and a where is my order page to cut tickets 6) Check the plan against real volume each peak day
  - Output: a peak staffing table: 5 days, hours needed, hours booked, the gap, and the fix for each gap. Next: feeds 24-19 (peak readiness check).
  - Watch: staff hours planned against staff hours needed for peak days (peak window, helpdesk and store platform), then first reply time and ship time held on peak days, then customer satisfaction.
- **23-07 Write the hiring trigger per team as a number, like tickets per agent per day or orders per ops person per week, and check it each month against the last 8 weeks** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then monthly, and whenever someone asks to hire. Enablers: capacity-plan, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: tickets per agent per day, 8 weeks (connected, helpdesk); orders per week (connected, store platform orders); team list with roles and hours (founder, team list); the cost of a hire and the cash floor (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Compute load per person per team for the last 8 weeks 2) Founder sets the trigger per team, for example 80 tickets per agent per day for 4 weeks running 3) Check the upstream fixes first (23-08) and note what they would cut 4) Compare this month's load with the trigger and say hire, wait or fix upstream 5) Log the call and the numbers on the decisions shelf
  - Output: a hiring trigger per team and a monthly hire, wait or fix call with the numbers behind it. Next: feeds 26-20 (month close).
  - Watch: workload per person against the written hiring trigger (monthly, helpdesk and store platform), then hires made on time, and payroll as a share of gross profit, then cash.
### T5. When work sits across people, screens and vendors, help me see every task, owner and date on one board, so nothing is found missing the week before a launch

Metric: tasks late against plan, weekly

- **23-01 Put every open task from every screen, Slack thread and the founder's notes on one kanban board with 5 columns (to do, doing, waiting on approval, live, measured), each card with 1 owner, a due date and the metric it should move** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every task any screen or person creates lands on it. Enablers: work-board.
  - Inputs: tasks proposed by each screen and agent (enabler, work-board); team list with roles and weekly hours (founder, team list); approval rules: who approves what and spend caps (brain, decisions and goals shelf); a board tool if we do not host one yet (Trello, Notion or a Google Sheet) (setup, Google Sheet).
  - Steps: 1) Pull open tasks from the founder's inbox, Slack and last 2 meeting notes into one list 2) Merge duplicates and drop anything nobody will do in the next 60 days 3) Give each card 1 owner, a due date and the metric it should move 4) Place each card in one of the 5 columns 5) Founder checks the waiting on approval column and clears or rejects each card 6) Set a rule: no card moves to live without a metric, and none to measured without a before and after number
  - Output: a board with every open task, 1 owner and 1 due date each, and a count of cards with no owner (target 0). Next: feeds 23-04 (launch plan), 23-05 (load per person) and 26-03 (what shipped and what it did).
  - Watch: share of open tasks with 1 owner and a due date (weekly, work board), then tasks late against plan, and decisions waiting on the founder, then gross profit.
- **23-04 Build the launch plan 4 weeks out: every task, owner, vendor and date timed back from launch day, with the 5 tasks that block others marked red** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 4 weeks before every launch, sale or new product drop. Enablers: work-board.
  - Inputs: launch date, product and offer (brain, calendar shelf); stock arrival date and units (brain, operations shelf); vendor lead times: photo shoot, creators, agency, warehouse (brain, operations shelf); launch checklist from the last launch and what went wrong (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Copy the last launch checklist and add what was missing last time 2) Time each task back from launch day using real vendor lead times 3) Give each task 1 owner and put it on the board 4) Mark the 5 tasks that block others (stock landed, photos, page live, emails built, ads approved) 5) Check twice a week: any red task 2 days late goes to the founder 6) Log the launch outcome and what slipped for next time
  - Output: a dated launch plan on the board with owners and 5 red blocker tasks. Next: feeds 23-10 (Monday meeting) and 25-12 (look-back after the launch).
  - Watch: launch tasks late against plan (weekly, work board), then launch on the planned date with stock, page and ads ready, then revenue.
- **23-08 Rank ticket types by count per 100 orders and agent minutes, and give the top 3 an owner outside support with an upstream fix like a size chart, a tracking email or an FAQ line** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and when tickets per 100 orders rise 20 percent. Enablers: ticket-clock, question-matrix, work-board.
  - Inputs: tickets tagged by type with handle time (connected, helpdesk); orders per week (connected, store platform orders); where each question is answered today (enabler, question-matrix); support macros and policies (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Tag the last 30 days of tickets by type and count per 100 orders 2) Multiply by handle time to get agent hours per type 3) Pick the top 3 types and name the upstream cause for each 4) Give each fix an owner outside support (site, ops, email) and a date on the board 5) Check tickets per 100 orders for that type 30 days after the fix
  - Output: top 3 repeat ticket types with a named fix, owner and date, and the hours each costs a month. Next: feeds 07 (landing page fixes) and 15 (CX help center).
  - Watch: tickets per 100 orders for the top 3 types (30 day, helpdesk), then support cost per order, then contribution margin.

## 24-security Security

### T1. When I hear another founder lost their ad account and store to one phishing link, help me see which of our logins could be taken the same way and fix them this week, so we do not lose the store, the ads or a payout

Metric: admin logins across store, ads, email, bank and domain with 2-step login on, weekly

- **24-02 Turn on 2-step login with an app or a key for every admin on the 8 critical accounts, and replace every shared admin login with a named login at the lowest role each person needs** Score 44, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: this week, then for every new admin on the day they are added. Enablers: access-vault.
  - Inputs: red list from the security folder (enabler, access-vault); an authenticator app on each admin's phone, or 2 hardware keys for the founder (setup, authenticator app or keys); who needs which role (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Start with store owner, Meta Business, domain, bank and payouts 2) Turn on 2-step with an app or key; drop SMS codes where the platform allows 3) Create a named login for each person who shared one, at the lowest role 4) Retire the shared login and store backup codes offline 5) Recount: 2-step share and shared logins left
  - Output: every critical admin on 2-step, 0 shared logins, and backup codes stored offline. Next: feeds 24-05 (Meta takeover plan).
  - Watch: admin logins with 2-step on, and shared logins left (weekly, security folder), then account takeovers, then risk.
- **24-05 Every morning compare live Meta ads, admins and daily spend against yesterday and the work board, and alert on any ad, spend jump or admin not made by a known person** Score 44, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: daily at 7 am. Enablers: access-vault, work-board.
  - Inputs: live ads, ad sets, spend and account users (connected, Meta Ads); ad changes the team planned (enabler, work-board); store staff and settings changes, payout bank changes (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Pull yesterday's live ads, spend and admins 2) Diff against the day before 3) Match each new ad or admin to a board card or a named person 4) Alert the owner on any unmatched ad, admin, or spend 2x the 7 day average 5) Owner confirms or starts the takeover plan within 1 hour
  - Output: a daily change check with unmatched items flagged, and hours to confirm each. Next: feeds 24-04 (Meta takeover plan).
  - Watch: unapproved ad spend and unknown admins on ad accounts (daily, Meta Ads), then dollars spent on ads we did not make, then risk.
- **24-01 Build the security folder: list every account (store, Meta Business, Google Ads, email tool, helpdesk, bank, payment processor, domain), its owner, its admins, 2-step status and recovery email and phone, with no passwords stored** Score 42, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every quarter and whenever an account is added. Enablers: access-vault.
  - Inputs: list of every business account (founder, founder interview); admin and user lists from each platform's settings page (setup, each platform admin); roles and who sees what (brain, trust and access shelf); team list (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) List every account the business depends on, starting with the 8 that can stop sales 2) Export the admin and user list from each settings page 3) Record owner, admins, 2-step on or off, recovery email and phone, and whether it sits in a company account 4) Mark red: no 2-step, recovery on a personal email, or only 1 admin 5) Founder checks the list and names the owner for each red line 6) Put each red line on the board with a date this week
  - Output: a security folder: 1 row per account with owner, admins, 2-step and recovery, and a red list with owners. Next: feeds 24-02 (2-step sweep), 24-04 (stale access) and 23-15 (offboarding).
  - Watch: critical accounts with 2-step on, 2 admins and a company recovery email (weekly, security folder), then no lost store, ad account or payout, then risk.
- **24-04 Write and test the Meta takeover plan: 2 backup admins, where the business verification lives, how to pause all spend in 5 minutes, and the Meta support path** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then every 6 months. Enablers: incident-kit, access-vault.
  - Inputs: Meta Business admins and ad accounts (connected, Meta Ads); business verification documents (founder, founder); backup admins' names (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Confirm 2 trusted people are admins with 2-step on 2) File the verification documents and the support steps in the security folder 3) Write the 5 minute pause: who does it and from which login 4) Run a dry drill with a stopwatch; the Meta connection stays read only, a named person clicks 5) Fix any step that took too long
  - Output: a 1 page Meta takeover plan tested once, with a time to pause. Next: feeds 24-18 (incident plan).
  - Watch: minutes to pause all spend in the last drill (6 monthly, security folder), then ad dollars lost in a takeover, then risk.
- **24-03 Pull every staff member, agency, partner and app with access to store, Meta, Google, email and bank, and remove any unused in 60 days or tied to someone who left** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly, and the day any agency or partner relationship ends. Enablers: access-vault, app-audit.
  - Inputs: users and partner access per platform with last login (setup, each platform admin); installed apps with data scopes (connected, store platform apps); people and agencies who left (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Export users, partner businesses and apps with their last login or last use 2) Mark anyone unused in 60 days or no longer working with us 3) Remove or downgrade each, starting with Meta partner access and store staff 4) Remove apps with customer or order data that nobody uses, and rotate their keys 5) Log each removal in the security folder
  - Output: a monthly stale access list and a removal log. Next: feeds 24-01 (security folder refresh).
  - Watch: accounts and partner access unused in 60 days (monthly, security folder), then outside people able to spend our money or see our customers, then risk.
- **24-06 Show the team 4 real fake Meta policy emails, send 1 practice phishing email a quarter, and count who reports it instead of clicking** Score 30, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: quarterly, and when a new person joins. Enablers: incident-kit, access-vault.
  - Inputs: real examples of fake Meta and Shopify emails (have, knowledge cards and team inboxes); a free practice phishing tool or a hand-made test email (setup, practice tool); team list with emails (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Collect 4 fake emails the team got and mark the tells 2) Hold a 15 minute session: how to check a link, where to report 3) Send 1 practice email per quarter 4) Count reports and clicks per person 5) Rerun the session for anyone who clicked
  - Output: a quarterly phishing score: reported, clicked, ignored. Next: none.
  - Watch: staff who report a practice phishing email instead of clicking (quarterly, practice tool), then account takeovers from one click, then risk.
### T2. When chargebacks jump or fake orders slip through, help me see the cause, the cost and the rule to change, so the processor does not hold payouts and good buyers still get through

Metric: disputes as a share of orders, trailing 30 days, against the processor's warning line

- **24-07 Compute disputes as a share of orders over the last 30 days each day, against the processor's warning line, split by reason, product and first-time buyer, with the month's cost in fees and lost goods** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: daily, and the day a processor warning email lands. Enablers: fraud-watch, margin-view.
  - Inputs: disputes with reason code, amount and outcome (connected, store platform payments); orders per day with customer and product (connected, store platform orders); processor warning line (often 0.75 to 1 percent) (founder, processor terms); product cost and dispute fees (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull disputes and orders for the last 30 days 2) Compute the rate against the warning line and chart it daily 3) Split by reason code, product, first-time buyer and ship country 4) Name the top cause and the rule that would stop it 5) Total the month's cost: refunded value, goods, dispute fees
  - Output: a dispute rate chart with the warning line, the top cause, and the monthly cost. Next: feeds 24-08 (answer disputes) and 24-09 (hold risky orders).
  - Watch: disputes as a share of orders, trailing 30 days (daily, store platform payments), then payouts held and fraud losses, then risk.
- **24-09 Hold orders over $300 from new customers with a freight forwarder address, a billing and shipping mismatch or 3 cards tried, review each before it ships, and each month check how many held orders were good buyers** Score 36, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: on every order, reviewed within 4 working hours; rule check monthly. Enablers: fraud-watch.
  - Inputs: orders with risk score, address, billing, cards tried (connected, store platform orders); known freight forwarder addresses (enabler, fraud-watch); later disputes and repeat orders of held buyers (connected, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Set the hold rule and route held orders to one reviewer 2) Reviewer checks email age, phone, social presence and past orders, then ships or cancels 3) Log the call per order 4) Monthly, count held orders later confirmed good (paid, no dispute, bought again) and confirmed fraud 5) Loosen the rule that blocked the most good orders, or tighten the one fraud passed
  - Output: a daily hold queue, and a monthly table of good orders blocked against fraud stopped. Next: feeds 24-07 (dispute rate).
  - Watch: orders held for risk and the share later confirmed as fraud (weekly, store platform orders), then fraud losses and good sales turned away, then gross profit.
- **24-10 Check every discount code's use against its expected use each week, find codes posted on coupon sites, and cap or expire the leaked ones the same day** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday, and when one code passes 2x its expected use. Enablers: fraud-watch, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders by discount code (connected, store platform orders); expected use per code: partner, creator, email, customer service (brain, offers shelf); a check of the 5 big coupon sites for our brand (setup, coupon site check).
  - Steps: 1) Count orders per code this week against its expected use 2) Search coupon sites for our brand and list posted codes 3) Compute margin lost on each leaked code 4) Cap per customer, expire, or replace with single-use codes 5) Tell the partner whose code leaked
  - Output: a leaked code list with margin lost and the action taken. Next: feeds 04 (offers and pricing).
  - Watch: orders on partner codes above expected use (weekly, store platform orders), then discount cost per order, then gross profit.
- **24-08 Answer every not-received and not-as-described dispute within 3 days with tracking, the delivery scan, photos and the policy page, from a template per reason code, and track the win rate** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: within 3 days of each dispute opening. Enablers: fraud-watch.
  - Inputs: open disputes with reason code and deadline (connected, store platform payments); tracking and delivery scans (connected, store platform fulfilments); tickets from the same customer (connected, helpdesk); return and shipping policy text (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List open disputes by deadline 2) Pull tracking, delivery scan, customer tickets and the policy for each 3) Fill the template for the reason code 4) A person checks the proof and submits 5) Log the outcome and the win rate per reason
  - Output: every dispute answered in time with proof, and a win rate per reason code. Next: feeds 24-07 (dispute rate).
  - Watch: disputes answered in time and won (monthly, store platform payments), then money recovered from disputes, then gross profit.
- **24-11 List the top 1 percent of customers by returns and not-received claims cost, and set rules for them only, like signature on delivery or no free returns** Score 32, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: monthly. Enablers: fraud-watch, margin-view.
  - Inputs: orders, returns, refunds and claims per customer (connected, store platform); tickets claiming not received (connected, helpdesk); return cost per order (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Rank customers by return and claim cost over 12 months 2) Take the top 1 percent and check each by hand for honest bad luck 3) Tag the rest and set the rule for tagged customers only 4) Track their claim cost the next 3 months
  - Output: a tagged list with the cost each customer drove and the rule applied. Next: feeds 15 (customers and CX).
  - Watch: return and not-received claim cost from the top 1 percent of customers (monthly, store platform), then return and claim cost, then gross profit.
### T3. When traffic and add to carts jump but orders do not, help me see how much is bots, fake clicks and fake sign-ups, so I do not scale spend or keep a page because of fake visitors

Metric: share of sessions and add to carts scored as bots, weekly

- **24-12 Score last week's sessions and add to carts for bots (known crawlers, data center traffic, zero scroll, 1 second visits, add to cart with no page view) and show real conversion rate after removing them** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday, and whenever sessions jump 30 percent with flat orders. Enablers: bot-filter, behaviour-map.
  - Inputs: sessions, add to carts and orders by source, country and device (connected, GA4); scroll and session length per visit (setup, Clarity); firewall or hosting logs with bot scores (setup, Cloudflare or host logs).
  - Steps: 1) Pull sessions and add to carts by source, country, device and hour 2) Score each segment on the bot signs 3) A person checks the top 3 flagged segments in recordings 4) Show conversion rate and cost per session with and without bot segments 5) Mark any campaign or page whose win was mostly bots
  - Output: a weekly bot share per source and page, and clean conversion rates. Next: feeds 25-05 (test audit) and 22 (measurement).
  - Watch: share of sessions and add to carts scored as bots (weekly, GA4), then budget and page calls made on real visitors, then risk.
- **24-13 Watch small or failed payments per hour against normal, and when a card testing burst hits, turn on a checkout challenge, cancel the test orders and refund before fees stack up** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: hourly, with an alert at 5x the normal rate. Enablers: bot-filter, fraud-watch.
  - Inputs: payment attempts and failures per hour (connected, store platform payments); orders under $5 and new accounts per hour (connected, store platform orders); a checkout challenge or bot protection setting (setup, store platform or Cloudflare).
