  - Steps: 1) Set the normal rate of failed payments per hour from the last 30 days 2) Alert when the rate passes 5x 3) Turn on the challenge and block the source 4) Cancel test orders and log fees 5) Turn the challenge off 24 hours after the rate is normal
  - Output: an hourly alert, a 1 page response, and the cost of each attack. Next: feeds 24-07 (dispute rate).
  - Watch: small or failed payment attempts per hour against normal (hourly, store platform payments), then fees and processor alarms, then risk.
- **24-14 Find paid clicks and email sign-ups with no engagement from flagged sources, remove the fake sign-ups from the list, block the source, and ask the ad platform for credits** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: bot-filter.
  - Inputs: clicks and sessions by placement and region (connected, Google Ads and GA4); sign-ups by source, time and domain (setup, email tool); placement and click reports (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Pull clicks with sessions under 2 seconds by placement and region 2) Pull sign-ups in bursts from disposable domains or one source 3) Remove fake sign-ups and add a form challenge 4) Exclude bad placements and file for credits 5) Track the share each week
  - Output: a list of fake clicks and sign-ups removed, exclusions added and credits claimed. Next: feeds 12 (Google Ads) and 14 (email).
  - Watch: share of paid clicks and sign-ups flagged as fake (weekly, Google Ads, GA4 and email tool), then ad waste and email reach, then gross profit.
### T4. When a data wipe, a hack or a peak week is coming, help me have backups, a tested plan and a short checklist, so we recover in hours, not weeks

Metric: hours to restore a sample of products in the last test restore

- **24-17 Check each week that no browser or search engine marks the site unsafe, that the certificate is valid, and that the domain is locked and on auto-renew in a company account** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly, and the day a renewal email looks odd. Enablers: incident-kit, access-vault.
  - Inputs: Search Console security issues (connected, Search Console); domain registrar lock, auto-renew and owner (setup, domain registrar); certificate expiry date (setup, site check).
  - Steps: 1) Read the Search Console security report and a Safe Browsing check 2) Check the certificate end date 3) Check the domain is locked, on auto-renew, paid by a company card and held in a company account 4) Alert and fix any fail the same day
  - Output: a weekly pass or fail on 4 checks with an owner for any fail. Next: none.
  - Watch: days flagged as unsafe, and domains locked and on auto-renew (weekly, Search Console and registrar), then days the store is dark or flagged, then revenue.
- **24-15 Back up products, theme, pages, customers and orders every day, and each quarter restore 20 products to a copy store and time it** Score 34, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: daily backups; restore drill quarterly and 3 weeks before a launch. Enablers: incident-kit.
  - Inputs: a backup app for the store (setup, backup app); a copy or development store (setup, store platform); the list of what must come back first (brain, operations shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Install the backup app and check the first full backup 2) Pick 20 products, 2 pages and the theme to restore 3) Restore to the copy store and time it 4) Write the steps and file them in the security folder 5) Fix anything that did not come back
  - Output: daily backups and a timed restore drill with written steps. Next: feeds 24-16 (incident plan).
  - Watch: hours to restore a sample of products in the last test restore (quarterly, backup app), then hours of lost selling after a wipe, then revenue.
- **24-16 Write the 1 page incident plan (first 5 steps, who to call, how to pause ads and payouts, what to tell customers) and run a 30 minute drill with the peak checklist 3 weeks before Black Friday** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: once, then every 6 months and 3 weeks before each peak. Enablers: incident-kit, access-vault.
  - Inputs: security folder with owners and recovery paths (enabler, access-vault); support contacts for store, processor, Meta, domain (founder, founder); peak dates (brain, calendar shelf); the last restore drill result (enabler, incident-kit).
  - Steps: 1) Write the first 5 steps for takeover, data wipe, leak and site down 2) List who to call with numbers and support links 3) Add the peak checklist: 2-step, backups tested, fraud rules, traffic limits, stale access removed 4) Run a 30 minute drill with the team on one case 5) Log gaps and fix them before the peak
  - Output: a 1 page incident plan, a passed peak checklist, and a drill date. Next: feeds 23-13 (break cover emergency list).
  - Watch: days since the incident plan was last tested, and peak checks passed (before each peak, security folder), then revenue lost in the best week of the year, then risk.
### T5. When someone uses our name to cheat customers or a new script could read their cards, help me find it and shut it, so customers trust our store and our emails

Metric: lookalike domains and fake ads using our brand found and taken down, weekly

- **24-18 Search each week for lookalike domains and fake ads using our brand name, logo or products, and file takedowns with the registrar and Meta** Score 30, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: weekly, and the day a customer asks about an order we never took. Enablers: competitor-feed, incident-kit.
  - Inputs: our brand name and product names (brain, products shelf); ads using our name in the public Ad Library (connected, Meta Ad Library); new domains similar to ours (setup, lookalike domain check); tickets about orders we cannot find (connected, helpdesk).
  - Steps: 1) Search the Ad Library and new domains for our name and close spellings 2) Match against tickets about unknown orders 3) A person confirms each fake with screenshots 4) File takedowns and log the date 5) Warn customers with a short note on the site if the fake is spreading
  - Output: a weekly impersonation list with takedowns filed and outcomes. Next: feeds 27-15 (spike watch).
  - Watch: lookalike domains and fake ads using our brand found and taken down (weekly, Meta Ad Library and domain check), then customers cheated in our name, then customer satisfaction.
- **24-20 List every script on checkout and payment pages, check each against the approved list after any new app install, and remove any that can read card fields** Score 30, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: monthly, and on the day any app is added. Enablers: app-audit, incident-kit.
  - Inputs: scripts loaded on cart, checkout and thank you pages (setup, site scan); installed apps and their permissions (connected, store platform apps); approved script list (brain, trust and access shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Scan cart and checkout pages and list every script and where it loads from 2) Match each to an approved app 3) Flag unknown scripts and any with access to card fields 4) Remove or ask the vendor, and log the change
  - Output: an approved script list and a monthly diff with any unknown script removed. Next: feeds 08 (site speed app audit).
  - Watch: scripts on checkout and payment pages not on the approved list (monthly, site scan), then card data stolen through our store, then risk.
- **24-19 Set email authentication on our sending domain to the strict setting in 3 steps over 6 weeks, so nobody can send fake order emails in our name** Score 28, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once, then checked monthly. Enablers: access-vault.
  - Inputs: DNS access for the sending domain (setup, domain registrar); every tool that sends mail as us (email tool, helpdesk, store) (brain, operations shelf); a free report reader for the domain (setup, report reader).
  - Steps: 1) List every tool that sends as our domain and add each to the records 2) Set the policy to monitor and read reports for 2 weeks 3) Move to quarantine for 2 weeks, then to reject 4) Check real emails still land in inboxes at each step
  - Output: sending domain at the strict setting with every real sender passing. Next: feeds 14 (email deliverability).
  - Watch: sending domains with email authentication at the strict setting (monthly, report reader), then fake emails reaching customers, and inbox placement, then customer satisfaction.

## 25-experiments Experiments and memory

### T1. When a test passes its end date or wins, help me make the call on the rule we set and get the winner live for every shopper, so I stop paying for the losing version and bank the win

Metric: days from each test's planned read date to a recorded result, and share of winners live on 100 percent of traffic within 14 days

- **25-01 Every Monday, list tests past their read date with no result, read each against its written rule, and record win, loss, flat or unreadable with the number** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: test-reader, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: running tests with read date and rule (enabler, test-card); orders, revenue and sessions per version (connected, store platform and GA4); ad test results per version (connected, Meta Ads); product cost, shipping and discounts (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List every test whose read date has passed and has no result 2) Pull each version's visitors, orders, gross profit and guardrail metric 3) Compare with the rule written before launch 4) Mark win, loss, flat or unreadable, naming the missing field if unreadable 5) Founder confirms each call in the Monday meeting 6) Stop the losing version the same day
  - Output: a Monday read list: each test with the call, the number and the rule it was judged on. Next: feeds 25-02 (promote wins), 25-03 (fail notes) and 26-05 (tests in the weekly pack).
  - Watch: days from each test's planned read date to a recorded result (trailing 90 days, test registry), then money spent on losing versions, then gross profit.
- **25-04 14 days after each call, check the winner is live on 100 percent of traffic, the loser is off, and the win holds on phones as well as desktop** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 14 days after every called win. Enablers: test-reader, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: live page, theme and ad versions (connected, store platform and Meta Ads); results by device (connected, GA4); the call and the winning version (enabler, win-loss-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Check the live page or ad matches the winning version 2) Check the test tool is off and the loser is gone 3) Split the result by phone and desktop 4) If phones lost, open a follow-up test for phones only 5) Mark the win shipped with a date
  - Output: a shipped check per win: live, loser off, phone and desktop result. Next: feeds 26-20 (what each change moved at month close).
  - Watch: share of called winners live on 100 percent of traffic within 14 days (monthly, test registry), then gross profit banked from wins, then gross profit.
- **25-02 Promote each win to the T0 standard: write the rule it proves (for example 'first frame shows the product in use'), the proof, where it applies, and a recheck date 90 days out** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the day a test is called a win. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: the called win with numbers (enabler, test-reader); the ad or page tags that changed (enabler, creative-tags); current T0 standards (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Name the one change that won in plain words 2) Write it as a standard all new work starts from, with the proof and the scope (product, channel, awareness level) 3) Match it to the principle it followed from the idea bank 4) Set the recheck date and the number that would retire it 5) Send the new standard to the brief builders on creative, copy and landing pages
  - Output: a T0 standard: rule, proof, scope, principle and recheck date. Next: feeds 09 (creative briefs), 10 (copy) and 07 (landing pages); rechecked in 25-11.
  - Watch: share of wins turned into a written standard within 7 days (monthly, win and loss ledger), then win rate of new work built on standards, then gross profit.
- **25-06 Read every Meta ad test with the same rules as a site test: spend per version set before launch, cost per new customer as the metric, and a 7 day read** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at launch and at the read date of every ad test. Enablers: test-reader, creative-archive, test-card.
  - Inputs: ad results per version: spend, purchases, new customers, age, gender, placement (connected, Meta Ads); new versus returning buyers from the store (connected, store platform orders); the ad test card (enabler, test-card).
  - Steps: 1) Write the card before launch: versions, spend each, metric, read date 2) At the read, pull spend and results per version 3) Judge on cost per new customer, not clicks or platform ROAS alone 4) Check the top segment per version from the age, gender and placement split 5) Record the call in the ledger
  - Output: an ad test read with the call, the segment where it won, and the ledger entry. Next: feeds 25-02 (promote win) and 09 (creative variants).
  - Watch: share of ad tests read against a rule set before launch (monthly, test registry), then cost per new customer, then gross profit.
- **25-03 For each loss, write a fail note: what we tried, the number, the likely why, what it rules out, and the date or condition to retest** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the day a test is called a loss or flat. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, creative-postmortem.
  - Inputs: the called loss with numbers (enabler, test-reader); who saw it and where people dropped, for ad losses (enabler, creative-postmortem); the test card with the hypothesis (enabler, test-card).
  - Steps: 1) State the idea and the result in 2 lines 2) Name the likely why from the drop-off data, not a guess 3) Write what it rules out and what it does not 4) Set a retest date or condition, like a new season or a new price 5) Tag it so any similar proposal shows this note
  - Output: a fail note with the why, what it rules out and a retest date. Next: feeds 25-09 (proposal check) and 25-11 (recheck).
  - Watch: closed tests with a written lesson (monthly, win and loss ledger), then repeat tests of failed ideas, then gross profit.
- **25-05 Audit every win above 20 percent before rollout: split within 1 point of plan, purchases counted in both versions, no sale in the window, and no bot spike** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before rolling out any win above 20 percent. Enablers: test-reader, test-card.
  - Inputs: visitors and orders per version (connected, GA4 and store platform); sale and holiday dates (brain, calendar shelf); sessions by source, country and hour, to spot bot spikes (connected, GA4).
  - Steps: 1) Check the split ratio against plan 2) Check orders per version in GA4 match store orders 3) Check the window for sales, launches, emails and bot spikes 4) Mark the win clean or suspect 5) Rerun suspect wins for 2 more weeks
  - Output: a clean or suspect mark on every big win, with the reason. Next: feeds 25-04 (rollout).
  - Watch: share of wins above 20 percent checked before rollout (quarterly, test registry), then plans built on real wins, then gross profit.
### T2. When someone proposes an idea or a new marketer starts, help me see what this store already tried, what won, what failed and why, so we never pay for the same answer twice

Metric: tests launched that repeat a past idea with no new reason written, per quarter

- **25-08 Turn each creative postmortem into a registry entry: the ad, spend, who saw it, where people dropped, the cause and the fix, tagged so the next brief sees it** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every time an ad is killed after $200 or more spend. Enablers: creative-postmortem, creative-tags, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: ad spend, reach, age, gender, placement, hook and hold rates (connected, Meta Ads); landing page conversion for the ad's traffic (connected, GA4); the ad's tags (enabler, creative-tags).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the ad's numbers and the segments that saw it 2) Find the step where people dropped: thumb stop, hold, click, page or cart 3) Write the likely cause and the fix 4) Relaunch with the fix, or kill it with a note 5) File it in the ledger with tags so any brief with the same tags shows it
  - Output: a postmortem entry per killed ad, with the fix or a kill note, tagged. Next: feeds 09 (next creative brief) and 25-09 (proposal check).
  - Watch: killed ads with a postmortem on file (monthly, win and loss ledger), then spend on repeats of failed creative, then gross profit.
- **25-09 Check every new test proposal against the registry and the idea bank by tags, and show the 3 closest past tests with results before it goes on the queue** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: whenever anyone proposes a test. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, principle-matcher.
  - Inputs: the proposal with tags (enabler, test-card); past tests and fail notes (enabler, win-loss-ledger); idea bank ideas/v1 (have, idea bank).
  - Steps: 1) Tag the proposal: area, page, lever, awareness level 2) Find the 3 closest past tests and the closest idea bank entries 3) Show results and fail notes next to the proposal 4) Ask for a new reason if it repeats a past loss 5) Queue, change or drop it
  - Output: a proposal card with 3 past tests and a go, change or drop call. Next: feeds 25-18 (queue ranking).
  - Watch: tests launched that repeat a past idea with no new reason written (quarterly, test registry), then test money spent on known answers, then gross profit.
- **25-10 Test the principle behind each ad winner on a second product or format, and mark it 'holds for this brand' or 'does not' after 2 reads** Score 32, ready 4/5, weeks.
  - When: after each win promoted to T0. Enablers: principle-matcher, win-loss-ledger, format-translator.
  - Inputs: the winning ad's principle (enabler, principle-matcher); knowledge cards and idea bank (have, knowledge cards and ideas/v1); a second product or format to try it on (brain, products shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Name the principle the winner followed 2) Build 1 new ad on a second product or format that keeps the principle 3) Run it as an ad test with the same read rules 4) Mark the principle holds or does not after 2 reads 5) Update the brand's principle list
  - Output: a list of principles marked proven, not proven or failed for this brand. Next: feeds 09 (creative) and 10 (copy).
  - Watch: principles with 2 reads on file (quarterly, win and loss ledger), then win rate of new concepts, then gross profit.
- **25-11 Each month, pull T0 standards past their recheck date, check them against the last 90 days of numbers, and keep, retire or retest each** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the first Monday of each month, and each quarter for shipped winners. Enablers: win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: standards with recheck date and retire rule (enabler, win-loss-ledger); the metric each standard moved, last 90 days (connected, store platform, GA4 and Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) List standards past their recheck date 2) Pull the metric each one moved, last 90 days against the proof 3) Keep, retire or retest each 4) Set the next recheck date 5) Tell the screens that use a retired standard
  - Output: a monthly recheck list: kept, retired, retest, with dates. Next: feeds 26-20 (month close).
  - Watch: shipped winners still live and still ahead at 90 days (quarterly, win and loss ledger), then standards that still earn, then gross profit.
- **25-07 Load every past test from sheets, Slack and agency decks into one registry with hypothesis, dates, versions, result and lesson, marking missing fields as unknown** Score 30, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: once at onboarding, then every closed test adds a row. Enablers: win-loss-ledger, decision-log.
  - Inputs: past test notes, sheets and agency decks (founder, founder and agency files); ad history with results (enabler, creative-archive); experiment registry shelf (brain, history shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Collect every past test file and deck 2) Write one row per test: idea, dates, versions, metric, result, lesson 3) Mark unknown fields as unknown, never guessed 4) Tag each by area, page, awareness level and idea type 5) Founder reads the 10 biggest rows and fixes what is wrong 6) Write a 1 page 'what we know' per area for any new marketer
  - Output: a registry of past tests and a 1 page 'what we know' per area. Next: feeds 25-09 (proposal check) and 25-10 (principle check).
  - Watch: areas with a decision history updated in the last 90 days (quarterly, test registry), then repeat tests avoided, then gross profit.
- **25-12 Run the same 20 minute look-back after each launch, replatform, big sale or crisis, with 5 fixed questions, and file the answers** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: within 7 days after each launch, big sale, replatform or crisis. Enablers: decision-log, work-board.
  - Inputs: the plan and what happened (enabler, work-board); sales, profit and tickets for the window (connected, store platform and helpdesk); people who worked on it (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Ask: what did we plan, what happened, what went well, what went wrong, what we do next time 2) Pull the numbers for the window 3) Write 3 changes for next time 4) Add the changes to the checklist for the next launch 5) File the look-back
  - Output: a look-back note with 3 changes added to the next launch checklist. Next: feeds 23-04 (next launch plan).
  - Watch: big projects closed with a look-back on file (yearly, decision log), then fewer repeat mistakes at launch, then risk.
- **25-13 Bring parked ideas back when their reason runs out, like stock landing or the busy season ending, with the original note and the numbers since** Score 28, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: weekly check of return dates and conditions. Enablers: decision-log, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: parked ideas with a return date or condition (enabler, decision-log); stock arrivals and season dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) List parked ideas whose date passed or condition is met 2) Attach the original note and what changed 3) Founder decides: queue, park again with a new date, or drop
  - Output: a list of returning ideas with a new call each. Next: feeds 25-18 (queue ranking).
  - Watch: parked ideas past their return date with no new decision (monthly, decision log), then good ideas used, then gross profit.
### T3. When a test is about to go live, help me write the win rule, the orders it needs and the read date, and check the setup, so the result cannot be spun or spoiled

Metric: share of tests launched with metric, read date, stop rule and scale rule on file

- **25-17 Duplicate the live theme, make the speed or checkout flow change on the copy, walk the checkout on 3 phones, test both, and merge only after the read** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: for every theme, speed or checkout change. Enablers: theme-twin, test-card.
  - Inputs: the live theme and a copy (connected, store platform); page speed per template (connected, site speed data); 3 phones for a checkout walk (founder, team).
  - Steps: 1) Duplicate the live theme 2) Make the change on the copy only 3) Test speed on both, and walk checkout on 3 phones 4) Split traffic or run a timed switch, with a test card 5) Merge the copy only if it wins; log the result either way
  - Output: a tested theme copy merged or dropped, with the speed and conversion numbers. Next: feeds 08 (site speed) and 25-02 (promote win).
  - Watch: conversion rate and revenue per visitor, copy against live (test window, GA4), then site changes that do not break checkout, then revenue.
- **25-14 Write a test card before launch: hypothesis, primary metric (gross profit per visitor), guardrail, orders needed per version, read date, stop rule and scale rule, signed by the founder** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before every test. Enablers: test-card.
  - Inputs: the idea and the page, ad or email (enabler, test-card); traffic and conversion on the page (connected, GA4); approval rules (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Write the hypothesis: if we change X, Y moves because Z 2) Pick gross profit per visitor as the main metric, and a guardrail like return rate 3) Compute orders needed and the read date 4) Write the stop rule and the scale rule 5) Founder signs; no sign, no launch
  - Output: a signed test card on file for every test. Next: feeds 25-01 (Monday read).
  - Watch: share of tests launched with metric, read date, stop rule and scale rule on file (quarterly, test registry), then test reads nobody can spin, then gross profit.
- **25-15 Compute the orders and days each test needs from the page's traffic and conversion rate, and turn down any test that cannot finish in 6 weeks, offering a bolder change instead** Score 34, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before every test, and when planning the month. Enablers: test-card.
  - Inputs: weekly visitors and orders on the page (connected, GA4); the smallest lift worth finding (founder, founder).
  - Steps: 1) Pull weekly visitors and conversion rate for the page 2) Compute orders per version for the smallest lift worth finding 3) Turn it into days 4) If over 6 weeks, suggest a bolder change or a bigger page 5) Set how many tests the store can carry this month
  - Output: a sample size and days per test, and the month's test capacity. Next: feeds 25-14 (test card) and 25-18 (queue).
  - Watch: share of tests that reached their planned orders by the read date (quarterly, test registry), then tests that end in a clear call, then gross profit.
- **25-16 Before launch, check no other test shares the page area, audience or dates, and no sale, holiday or launch falls in the window, then check purchases count in both versions on day 1** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before every launch and on day 1. Enablers: test-card, test-reader.
  - Inputs: running tests with pages, audiences and dates (enabler, test-card); sale, holiday and launch dates (brain, calendar shelf); day 1 orders per version (connected, GA4 and store platform).
  - Steps: 1) List running tests that share the page area or audience 2) Check the calendar for sales and launches in the window 3) Move the date or mark the read 4) On day 1, check orders show in both versions and the split is within 1 point 5) Pause and fix if either fails
  - Output: a launch check passed or failed, with the clash or setup fault named. Next: feeds 25-01 (Monday read).
  - Watch: share of tests that passed a launch check (quarterly, test registry), then test windows lost to broken setups, then risk.
### T4. When the queue has 20 ideas and room for 2, help me pick the ones aimed at the biggest profit leak and judge every result on profit per visitor, so our few test slots go where the money is

Metric: share of tests launched from the top 5 of the ranked list, per quarter

- **25-18 Rank the test queue each month by money at stake (visitors on the page, times the gap to a benchmark, times margin) and pick the top 2, sending clear defects straight to a fix** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the first Monday of each month. Enablers: test-card, win-loss-ledger.
  - Inputs: the proposal queue with past-test checks (enabler, test-card); visitors and conversion per page (connected, GA4); gross margin per product (brain, finance shelf); idea bank ideas/v1 filtered by the brand's bottleneck (have, idea bank).
  - Steps: 1) Score each proposal by money at stake 2) Move clear defects like a broken button to a fix card, no test 3) Take the top 2 that fit the month's capacity 4) Founder approves 5) Park the rest with a return date
  - Output: a ranked queue with 2 picks, a defect fix list, and parked ideas. Next: feeds 25-14 (test card).
  - Watch: share of tests launched from the top 5 of the ranked list (quarterly, test registry), then gross profit per visitor, then gross profit.
- **25-19 Keep a 5 percent held-back group away from popups, win-back discounts and new apps, and compare orders and gross profit per person over 30 and 90 days** Score 32, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: set up once, read monthly and before any app renewal. Enablers: test-reader.
  - Inputs: customers and visitors with a held-back flag (setup, store platform and email tool); orders and gross profit per person (connected, store platform orders); app and discount costs (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 5 percent of visitors and subscribers at random and flag them 2) Exclude them from popups, discounts and the new app 3) Compare orders and gross profit per person after 30 and 90 days 4) Keep, cut or change each tactic on the number 5) Show the whole program's gain against the held-back group
  - Output: a held-back read per tactic, and the program's gain in dollars. Next: feeds 21 (finance, app renewals) and 26-20 (month close).
  - Watch: orders and gross profit per person, shown the offer against held back (30 day, store platform orders), then discounts paid to people who would buy anyway, then gross profit.
- **25-20 30 days after each win, compare returns, refunds and tickets from each version's buyers, and demote the win if gross profit per visitor flipped** Score 30, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 30 days after every called win. Enablers: test-reader, win-loss-ledger, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: orders per version with returns and refunds (connected, store platform); tickets per version's buyers (connected, helpdesk); product cost and discounts (brain, finance shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull each version's buyers 2) Count returns, refunds and tickets per 100 orders 3) Recompute gross profit per visitor after returns 4) Keep the win or demote it with a fail note
  - Output: a 30 day check per win: kept or demoted, with the numbers. Next: feeds 25-03 (fail note) and 25-11 (recheck).
  - Watch: return rate and refund rate of each version's buyers (30 days after the read, store platform), then wins that stay wins after returns, then gross profit.

## 26-reports Reviews and reports

### T1. When it is Sunday night and the Monday meeting is at 9, help me see what happened last week, why, and the 3 calls I need to make, so I walk in ready instead of up late in 6 tabs

Metric: minutes the founder spends preparing the weekly meeting, and decisions logged from it, weekly

- **26-01 Build the Monday pack each Sunday at 6 pm: 6 numbers with last week, the 4 week average and the normal range, the 3 biggest movers with a named cause, and 3 calls to make** Score 42, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: every Sunday at 6 pm. Enablers: review-pack, mover-explainer, number-book.
  - Inputs: sales, orders, new customers, refunds (connected, store platform); ad spend and results (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads); sessions and conversion (connected, GA4); product cost, shipping and fees (brain, finance shelf); the 6 numbers the founder cares about (brain, decisions and goals shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pull last week's data from each connected account 2) Compute the 6 numbers in code, marking any missing source as unavailable 3) Compare each with its 4 week average and normal range 4) Pick the 3 biggest movers and name a cause for each with the evidence 5) Write 3 calls with a suggested owner 6) Founder reads it in 10 minutes and edits the calls
  - Output: a 1 page Monday pack: 6 numbers, 3 movers with causes, 3 calls. Next: feeds 23-10 (Monday meeting) and 26-05 (decisions logged).
  - Watch: minutes the founder spends preparing the weekly meeting (weekly, founder log), then decisions made on time, then gross profit.
- **26-04 Put service next to money every week: first reply time, time to resolution, follow-ups owed, open backlog and the 30 day review rating, with the order value in open tickets** Score 40, ready 5/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Sunday with the pack. Enablers: ticket-clock, review-pack.
  - Inputs: tickets with reply, resolution and follow-up times (connected, helpdesk); review rating and count (connected, reviews app); order value per open ticket (connected, store platform orders).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the week's ticket times and backlog 2) Count follow-ups owed over 48 hours and the order value at stake 3) Add the 30 day review rating and the top new complaint 4) Flag service slipping while sales hold
  - Output: a service block in the Monday pack with red flags and dollars at stake. Next: feeds 23-05 (load per person).
  - Watch: first reply time in hours and open ticket backlog (weekly, helpdesk), then review rating and repeat rate, then customer satisfaction.
- **26-02 Flag any key number outside its normal range for 2 weeks running, with the start date and the likely cause checked against stock, price, ads, site changes and season, and send only these while the founder is away** Score 38, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: daily check, sent in the Monday pack, and as the only alerts while the founder is away. Enablers: mover-explainer.
  - Inputs: 12 months of weekly key numbers (connected, store platform, GA4, Meta Ads and Google Ads); stock by product (connected, store platform inventory); site changes, price changes and launches with dates (brain, work board and calendar shelf); sale and holiday dates (brain, calendar shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Set a normal range for each key number from the last 12 weeks, season adjusted 2) Mark any number outside it for 2 weeks running 3) Check the causes in order: stock, price, ad changes, site changes, season, tracking 4) Name the most likely cause with the evidence 5) Send the flag to the number's owner, or to the founder if it is an emergency
  - Output: a short list of numbers off for 2 weeks, each with a start date, a cause and an owner. Next: feeds 26-01 (Monday pack) and 26-15 (brands needing the lead).
  - Watch: key numbers outside their normal range for 2 or more weeks (weekly, store platform and ad accounts), then problems caught while small, then gross profit.
- **26-05 List tests due, ended and waiting on a call in the pack, decide each in the meeting, then send the team a 5 line note of what moved and what was decided, with owner and date** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every Monday. Enablers: review-pack, win-loss-ledger, work-board.
  - Inputs: tests due and ended (enabler, win-loss-ledger); calls from the meeting (brain, decisions and goals shelf); team list and channel (founder, team list).
  - Steps: 1) Add due and ended tests with the read to the pack 2) Decide each in the meeting 3) Write the 5 line note: 3 numbers that moved, the calls, owners and dates 4) Log each call on the decisions shelf and the board 5) Count opens of the note
  - Output: test calls made in the meeting, and a team note with owners and dates. Next: feeds 25-02 (promote wins) and 23-10 (Monday meeting).
  - Watch: test reads decided in the weekly meeting (weekly, test registry), then decisions per week and time to act, then gross profit.

  - Steps: 1) Pull cards that moved to live or measured last week 2) Pull each card's metric for 14 days before and since 3) Mark each as moved, flat or too early 4) List late cards with the owner
  - Output: a shipped list: each change, owner, metric before and after, and late cards. Next: feeds 26-20 (month close).

## 27-wild-bets-pr Wild bets and PR

### T1. When a bold idea gets a yes or a bet has run for weeks, help me set its cost cap, stop rule and read date and read it against a group left out, so a big swing never quietly sinks the quarter

Metric: spend to date on each live bet against its cap, and share of bets launched with a cap, stop rule, read date and comparison group on file

- **27-02 Set the quarter's total bet cap as a share of profit after ads, and show spend to date on each live bet against its cap every Monday** Score 38, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at quarter planning, then every Monday. Enablers: bet-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: profit after ads, last quarter and plan (brain, finance shelf); spend per bet: ads, product, fees, hours (enabler, bet-ledger); ad spend tagged to bets (connected, Meta Ads and Google Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Founder sets the cap, for example 5 percent of the quarter's profit after ads 2) Log every dollar and product unit to its bet 3) Show each bet's spend against its own cap and the total against the quarter cap 4) Flag any bet past 80 percent of its cap 5) Stop or re-sign any bet past its cap
  - Output: a Monday bet table: spend against cap per bet and in total. Next: feeds 26-01 (Monday pack).
  - Watch: total bet spend this quarter as a share of profit after ads (weekly, bet ledger), then cash kept safe from runaway bets, then cash.
- **27-01 Write a bet card before any money moves: the category habit it breaks, the most it can cost, the stop rule, the read date, the named metric and a group left out, signed by the founder** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before any bet, stunt, partnership or event gets money. Enablers: bet-ledger.
  - Inputs: the idea and its owner (founder, founder); the quarter's bet cap and cash floor (brain, finance shelf); past bets and lessons (brain, history shelf); wild bet ideas and stop rule examples (have, idea bank ideas/v1 (19-WLD)).
  - Steps: 1) Name the habit in our category the bet breaks 2) Write the most it can cost in cash, product and team hours 3) Write the stop rule and the read date 4) Pick 1 metric and a left-out group of similar customers 5) Check it against past bets for a repeat 6) Founder signs; no card, no spend
  - Output: a signed bet card with cap, stop rule, read date, metric and comparison group. Next: feeds 27-02 (cap tracking) and 27-03 (read).
  - Watch: share of bets launched with a cost cap, stop rule, read date and comparison group on file (quarterly, bet ledger), then bets that cannot become a crisis, then cash.
- **27-03 At each bet's read date, compare the bet group with the left-out group on the named metric, and call scale in steps, stop or rerun, testing any borrowed stunt on a small group first** Score 36, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: at each bet's read date. Enablers: bet-ledger, margin-view.
  - Inputs: the bet card (enabler, bet-ledger); orders, gross profit and repeat rate per group (connected, store platform); cost to date (enabler, bet-ledger).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the named metric for both groups 2) Compute the gain per customer and the gross profit per customer reached 3) Call scale to the next step, stop or rerun 4) For a scale, set the next step size and its check 5) Write the result and 1 lesson in the ledger
  - Output: a bet read with the call, the next step size, and a lesson on file. Next: feeds 25-03 (fail note) or 25-02 (promote win).
  - Watch: gain on the bet's named metric against the left-out group (at the read date, store platform), then gross profit per customer reached, then gross profit.
- **27-04 Launch the next new product as a pre-order or a 200 unit drop with a sales target set before launch, and order full stock only if the target is hit** Score 34, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: for every new product before the first full stock order. Enablers: bet-ledger, marketplace-plan.
  - Inputs: the product, price and landed cost (brain, products shelf); supplier minimum order and lead time (brain, operations shelf); email list and top buyers (connected, store platform customers); a pre-order setting or app (setup, store platform).
  - Steps: 1) Set the target and the window, for example 300 pre-orders in 14 days 2) Build the pre-order page with honest ship dates 3) Send to the list and the top buyers first 4) Count pre-orders daily against target 5) Order full stock, a smaller run, or refund and drop
  - Output: a pre-order result against target and a stock decision. Next: feeds 19 (supply and inventory).
  - Watch: pre-orders or drop sales against the target set before launch (launch window, store platform orders), then cash tied up in stock that does not sell, then cash.
- **27-05 Run a 90 day capped test on TikTok Shop or Amazon with 3 hero products, a price floor after fees, and a stop rule on profit per order** Score 30, ready 2/5, weeks.
  - When: once, when the founder wants a new channel; read at 30, 60 and 90 days. Enablers: marketplace-plan, bet-ledger.
  - Inputs: hero products with landed cost (brain, products shelf); channel fees, shipping and ad costs (enabler, marketplace-plan); a seller account on the channel (setup, TikTok Shop or Amazon Seller Central); creators for TikTok Shop videos (buy, creators shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Pick 3 hero products and set the price floor after fees 2) Set up listings with the best images and review proof 3) Cap spend and stock for 90 days 4) Read orders and profit per order at 30, 60 and 90 days 5) Scale, hold or close on the stop rule
  - Output: a 90 day channel test with profit per order and a call. Next: feeds 20 (marketplaces).
  - Watch: orders and profit per order in the test channel (90 day, channel seller account), then new revenue without a new business, then revenue.

  - Steps: 1) Read 12 months of posts for clashes with our values and category 2) Check audience match and fake follower share 3) Check news and reviews for problems 4) File pass or flag with the reason 5) Add an exit clause for flags that still go ahead
  - Output: a vetting note per partner with pass or flag. Next: feeds 27-17 (partner problem watch).
  - Watch: partners approved with an open reputation flag (quarterly, creator roster), then partners who embarrass the brand, then risk.
- **27-10 Run the ambassador program on a weekly routine: tasks sent Monday, posts checked by Wednesday, orders per code counted Friday** Score 28, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: every week while the program runs. Enablers: creator-roster, work-board.
  - Inputs: ambassador list with codes (brain, creators shelf); orders per code (connected, store platform orders); brand voice rules (brain, voice shelf).
  - Steps: 1) Send each ambassador 1 task on Monday 2) Check posts against voice and claims by Wednesday 3) Count orders per code on Friday 4) Drop anyone with 0 orders for 8 weeks
  - Output: a weekly ambassador sheet: tasks, posts checked, orders per code. Next: feeds 27-08 (term read).
  - Watch: orders from ambassador codes per month, and posts waiting past the review deadline (monthly, store platform orders), then founder hours on the program, then gross profit.
### T3. When a launch has a story or press lands, help me pitch the right writers and turn the wave of visitors into orders and lasting links, so PR sells and keeps selling

Metric: visits, orders and conversion rate from each press piece, 14 days

- **27-12 Before a press piece or planned spike lands, check stock on the featured product, a matching landing page, email capture and a retargeting audience, all within 24 hours** Score 40, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the day a piece is confirmed, and within 2 hours of a viral post. Enablers: press-tracker, congruence-check.
  - Inputs: featured product stock (connected, store platform inventory); the piece or post and what it promises (enabler, press-tracker); landing page and email capture (connected, store platform); audience for visitors from the piece (connected, Meta Ads).
  - Steps: 1) Check stock covers 3x a normal week of the featured product 2) Point the link to a page that matches the story 3) Turn on email capture for the visitors 4) Ask the media buyer to build a retargeting audience 5) Watch orders hourly for 72 hours
  - Output: a 24 hour readiness check passed, and hourly orders for 72 hours. Next: feeds 27-13 (count results).
  - Watch: orders in the 72 hours after a spike against a normal 72 hours (72 hours, store platform orders), then orders from each spike, then revenue.
- **27-13 Count visits, orders, sign-ups and new linking sites from each press placement over 14 days and over the retainer term, before the PR agency renews** Score 32, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: 14 days after each placement, and 30 days before the retainer renews. Enablers: press-tracker.
  - Inputs: visits and orders by referring site (connected, GA4 and store platform); new linking sites (connected, Search Console links); the retainer cost and term (founder, PR agency contract).
  - Steps: 1) List placements with dates 2) Pull visits, orders and sign-ups from each for 14 days 3) Pull new links from each 4) Total cost per order over the term 5) Renew, renegotiate or end on the numbers
  - Output: a placement table and a renewal call with cost per order. Next: feeds 26-11 (client report).
  - Watch: visits, new linking sites and orders from each placement (retainer term, GA4 and Search Console), then PR spend that pays, then revenue.
- **27-11 Find the angle a journalist would run for the next launch (data, founder story, trend or stunt), and build a list of 25 writers who covered that topic in the last 90 days** Score 28, ready 2/5, days.
  - When: 6 weeks before a launch or news. Enablers: press-tracker.
  - Inputs: the launch and what is new about it (brain, calendar shelf); our own data a writer could use, like survey or order trends (connected, store platform); writers and recent articles (buy, press database or manual search); founder story (founder, founder interview).
  - Steps: 1) Write 3 angles that are news, not ads 2) Pick 1 with our own data behind it 3) Find 25 writers who covered the topic in 90 days 4) Write a 5 line pitch per writer group 5) Track replies per pitch
  - Output: an angle, a checked list of 25 writers, and pitches with reply tracking. Next: feeds 27-12 (ready the site) and 27-13 (count results).
  - Watch: reply rate on pitches, per story (per story, press tracker), then press that sells, then revenue.
- **27-14 Ask every outlet that names us without a link to add one, and check 30 days later whether AI answers and search now name us for the topic** Score 26, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: within 7 days of each mention, and 30 days after. Enablers: press-tracker, search-feed.
  - Inputs: mentions of the brand without a link (enabler, mention-watch); search results and AI answers for the topic (enabler, search-feed).
  - Steps: 1) List new mentions and whether each links to us 2) Send a short thank you with the link request 3) Log each outcome 4) 30 days later, check AI answers and search for the topic
  - Output: linked mentions gained and a before and after on AI answers. Next: feeds 18 (answer engines) and 17 (SEO).
  - Watch: new sites mentioning or linking to the brand (quarterly, Search Console), then buyers from search and AI answers for a year, then revenue.
### T4. When a bad post spreads, a partner slips up, or we know a problem is coming, help me size it fast and reply first with a message I approved, so it stops in hours, not weeks

Metric: hours from a negative spike to an approved public reply

- **27-15 Watch reviews, social mentions and tickets for a spike in a negative theme, size it within 2 hours (posts, reach, orders at risk), and draft a reply for the founder to approve** Score 38, ready 3/5, days.
  - When: hourly check; alert when a negative theme doubles its daily average. Enablers: mention-watch, review-miner.
  - Inputs: new reviews with rating and text (connected, reviews app); tickets with tags (connected, helpdesk); public social mentions (setup, social listening tool); voice and claims rules (brain, voice and claims shelves).
  - Steps: 1) Group new reviews, tickets and mentions by theme 2) Alert when a negative theme doubles 3) Size it: posts, reach, orders from affected customers 4) Draft a reply and a fix in our voice 5) Founder approves; post and reply to each customer 6) Log hours from spike to reply
  - Output: an alert, a size estimate, and an approved reply within hours. Next: feeds 27-16 (prepared statements).
  - Watch: hours from a negative spike to an approved public reply (per spike, mention watch), then reputation damage and lost orders, then risk.
- **27-16 When a recall, delay or price rise is known, draft the customer email, the FAQ line and the post before customers find out, and send within 48 hours** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: the day the team knows. Enablers: mention-watch, question-matrix.
  - Inputs: affected orders and customers (connected, store platform orders); the facts and the fix (founder, founder); where each question is answered (enabler, question-matrix).
  - Steps: 1) List affected customers and orders 2) Draft the email, FAQ line, post and support macro 3) Founder approves 4) Send to affected customers first, then post 5) Track tickets and refunds for 14 days
  - Output: an approved message set sent within 48 hours, with tickets tracked. Next: feeds 15 (CX macros).
  - Watch: days from knowing a problem to telling affected customers (per event, helpdesk), then trust kept through bad news, then risk.
- **27-17 Watch each partner's and ambassador's public posts daily, and keep a written exit clause and a holding statement ready for each** Score 32, ready 3/5, hours.
  - When: daily while any partner is live. Enablers: mention-watch, creator-roster.
  - Inputs: live partners and handles (brain, creators shelf); contracts with exit clauses (founder, creator contracts); public posts (setup, social listening tool).
  - Steps: 1) List live partners and their handles 2) Scan daily for clashes or news 3) Keep a holding statement per partner 4) On a flag, pause ads through their handle and decide within 24 hours
  - Output: a daily partner watch with a holding statement per partner. Next: feeds 27-09 (vetting).
  - Watch: hours from a partner's public problem to our response (per event, mention watch), then partner problems that become ours, then risk.
### T5. When the top 2 percent of buyers drive a large share of profit, help me treat them as insiders with quiet perks and prove it against similar buyers left out, so we keep and grow them

Metric: repeat purchase rate of the inner circle against similar buyers left out, 90 days

- **27-18 Pick the top 2 percent of buyers by profit and recency, leave out anyone with an open complaint, send half a personal letter from the founder with private early access, and keep the other half as the comparison** Score 36, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: quarterly. Enablers: vip-list, bet-ledger, ticket-clock.
  - Inputs: customers ranked by profit and recency (connected, store platform customers); open tickets and complaints (connected, helpdesk); the founder's letter in their voice (brain, voice shelf); printing and mailing, or a handwritten note service (buy, mail service).
  - Steps: 1) Rank customers and take the top 2 percent 2) Drop anyone with an open ticket or a recent bad review 3) Split at random into letter and no letter 4) Send the letter with a private link and no discount code on the envelope 5) Keep it quiet: no public post about the program 6) Compare repeat rate and profit per customer after 90 days
  - Output: a VIP letter test: who got what, and repeat rate against the comparison group. Next: feeds 27-19 (inner circle drops) and 14 (email).
  - Watch: repeat orders, mailed group against not mailed (90 day, store platform orders), then profit from top customers, then gross profit.
- **27-19 Give the inner circle first access to the next drop through a private link 48 hours early, and compare their repeat rate and referrals with similar buyers left out after 90 days** Score 34, ready 4/5, hours. Start now.
  - When: before each drop or launch. Enablers: vip-list, bet-ledger.
  - Inputs: inner circle list (enabler, vip-list); the drop date and stock (brain, calendar shelf); orders by private link (connected, store platform orders).
  - Steps: 1) Set aside stock for the inner circle 2) Send the private link 48 hours early 3) Count orders and referrals from the link 4) Compare with left-out buyers at 90 days
  - Output: an inner circle read: orders, referrals and repeat rate against left-out buyers. Next: feeds 27-03 (bet read).
  - Watch: repeat purchase rate of the inner circle against similar buyers left out (90 day, store platform orders), then repeat revenue, then gross profit.
- **27-20 Find a reason worth telling a friend in the words happy buyers use in reviews, give top buyers a referral link with a product reward, and count orders per link** Score 32, ready 4/5, days. Start now.
  - When: once to set up, then monthly. Enablers: vip-list, review-miner.
  - Inputs: 5 star reviews and survey answers (connected, reviews app); top buyers (connected, store platform customers); a referral app or link tracking (setup, referral app).
  - Steps: 1) Pull the phrases happy buyers repeat most 2) Write the ask in those words 3) Send top buyers a link with a product reward, not a discount 4) Count orders and new customers per link monthly
  - Output: a referral ask in customers' words and orders per link. Next: feeds 14 (email) and 10 (copy).
